## Purpose

A full-depth critique of every live document in `docs/architecture` (82 files, ~76k lines; `_archive/` excluded), organized facet-by-facet. For each facet it answers four questions: What does the canon currently define? What does it already get right? Is the concept state-of-the-art against the strongest named systems, standards, and research of mid-2026 — and if not, what exactly is missing? And what should change: renames, strictly-better mechanisms, refactors of existing material, and red flags that could keep the architecture from succeeding.

## Method (summary — detail in Appendix B)

Thirty facet reviewers each read their assigned files completely and produced typed findings (`rename` / `better-way` / `refactor` / `red-flag` / `sota-gap` / `contradiction`), every finding anchored to a real `path:line` quote. Every facet report then passed an independent adversarial verification pass that re-read the cited regions, killed unsupported or already-addressed findings, amended inaccurate ones, and re-scored severity conservatively. Two cross-facet synthesizers then produced the program-level risk ranking (Part I) and the estate-wide naming program (Part II). Result: **446 verified findings** (4 killed in verification, 293 tightened during it), 12 systemic risks, and a 3-tier rename program.

Line numbers reference the working tree on branch `feat/outcome-room-aggregate` as of 2026-07-16 (including uncommitted modifications present that day).

## How To Read This

- **Part I — Program-level risk synthesis.** The twelve systemic risks, ranked by probability × damage, each with cross-facet evidence, a leading indicator to watch, and the cheapest credible mitigation. Read this first.
- **Part II — Naming-coherence program.** Seven systemic naming pathologies, a conflict-resolved rename table in three execution tiers, names to keep despite criticism, and a migration/alias strategy.
- **Part III — Per-facet deep review** (15 groups, 30 facets). Each facet: current shape → strengths → SOTA verdict with named anchors → gaps → severity-ordered findings with evidence and a concrete recommendation.
- **Appendix A** — coverage map (facet → files → verdict). **Appendix B** — method detail, statistics, and this review's own known gaps.

Findings marked *(confidence: medium)* survived verification but rest on judgment rather than mechanical fact; everything else was confirmed against the cited text.

## Executive Summary

**One-line diagnosis: the doctrine is unusually strong — often genuinely ahead of anything shipping — but the mechanisms that would make that doctrine buildable-as-specified and verifiable by outside parties are largely unspecified prose, and the gap between what the canon promises and what an implementer or auditor can mechanically check is the program's dominant risk.**

### Verdict distribution

Across 30 facets: **0 `sota` · 12 `near-sota` · 6 `divergent-by-design` · 12 `behind`**. The split is not random:

- **Ahead of the field (conceptual/governance layer):** the EvaluationExposureLedger (sealed-holdout exposure accounting beyond Inspect/Braintrust), accountable-actor-vs-resolver separation (beyond A2A/LangGraph), first-class negative/inconclusive/exploit results, the non-collapsing assurance ladder (`attested → … → settled`), consensus-as-deployment-profile with intelligence strictly upstream of the deterministic state machine, the Agentgres determinism rule (FoundationDB/TigerBeetle-class), Simplex-faithful physical-action safety, the Model Independence Test, and candidate-is-never-authority applied uniformly. The pack's claim-boundary honesty ("a receipt proves only its bound fact") is rarer and better than nearly all commercial agent-platform documentation.
- **Behind the field (operational substrate):** cryptographic mechanism (vs SLSA/in-toto/Sigstore/CT/RATS), machine-readable schema and status (vs Kubernetes/KEP/OpenTelemetry practice), distributed-systems timing/fencing physics (vs Chubby/Spanner/Raft), billing/dispute/settlement mechanics (vs Metronome/Stripe/FOCUS, Kleros/UMA, CoW/UniswapX), node OS lifecycle (vs Talos/Bottlerocket), and placement optimization (vs SkyPilot/Karpenter).

The exposure is asymmetric: the checks the doctrine has written — verifiable proof, fail-closed authority, honest attestation — are exactly the claims the product is sold on, and the unbuilt mechanism layer cannot yet cash them.

### The twelve systemic risks (digest — full analysis in Part I)

1. **[CRITICAL] The contract layer is prose, not schema — the master drift engine.** ~200 envelopes, ~180 receipt types, ~500 endpoints, ~374 URI schemes, and the status tracker are hand-maintained YAML-in-Markdown with zero machine validation; owner files already disagree with themselves (duplicate enums, 111 RFC-3986-invalid URI schemes, two conflicting `RoutingDecisionReceipt` schemas in one file).
2. **[CRITICAL] The proof/authority plane is not cryptographically verifiable as specified.** Signatures optional on receipts; no canonical serialization under any hash; no signing envelope, key lifecycle, transparency log, or offline verification; AuthorityGrant — the security keystone — is the thinnest object in the object model.
3. **[CRITICAL] Prompt injection and information-flow control are absent from the safety spine.** The #1 attack class for exactly this product is never named in canon; no taint/provenance labels on tool output or context; the "lethal trifecta" (private data + untrusted content + egress) survives the lease model intact.
4. **[HIGH] "Canon is authoritative" is already false in places.** Recency-based conflict rule, 178 code anchors citing a deleted package behind a blanket disclaimer, and a 5,006-line master guide whose slices 731–1034 exist nowhere else despite the guide claiming they were archived.
5. **[HIGH] Distributed-systems correctness gaps in the bounded-DAS core.** No timing/clock model; fencing stops at the Agentgres log while authority and external effectors go unfenced; no durable epoch/membership compare-and-swap.
6. **[HIGH] Attestation and enforcement-point integrity are pre-RATS.** Node claims are self-reported, `policy_declared` counts as measurement, no hardware-rooted node identity, no verifier role, no receipt binds the daemon's own identity/integrity.
7. **[HIGH] The economic engine has no demand origination and no verifiable billing primitives.** All three flywheel loops are supply-side; Work Credits are load-bearing across five surfaces with no ledger/quote/hold object model.
8. **[HIGH] Naming and category coinages are a standing credibility and legal exposure.** Web4, "intelligent blockchain," cTEE (a non-TEE), Foundry (Palantir/Azure collision with adapted-UI provenance), Type 1/2/3 hypervisor, "Standard DAS" (reads as Data Availability Sampling to the blockchain audience).
9. **[HIGH] Massive speculative surface frozen at field level before any implementation** — embodied/AIIP/cTEE/chain families carry full field-level state machines while built-core envelopes lack even schemas; this inverts the pack's own pull-based canon-to-code rule.
10. **[HIGH] The verification/dispute/adjudication layer lacks its game-theoretic mechanisms.** Challenges carry no bonds; sas.xyz escrow hangs on a "deterministic arbitration" defined nowhere; disputes implicitly presume honest parties.
11. **[MEDIUM] Work-spine execution semantics underspecified** — ~15 status enums with no transition grammar; a mutable-in-place control loop with no per-transition receipts (a replay hole against the pack's own physics).
12. **[MEDIUM] The regulatory/compliance perimeter is unowned** — wallet money-movement, decentralized.trade (MiCA/CFTC), EU AI Act conformity, GDPR erasure vs the immutable log.

### The naming program (digest — full table in Part II)

Seven pathologies dominate, the worst being **borrowed-term squatting** (names that hijack entrenched vocabulary the docs then spend paragraphs disclaiming) and **overload without reservation rules** ("agent" naming three different objects in one API; "kernel" carrying four senses; "sparse" carrying three). Highest-value renames, conflict-resolved: **cTEE → Sealed Custody Envelope**; **Type 1/2/3 → `substrate_posture: bare_metal_node_root | hosted_workstation | autonomy_plane`**; **Foundry → Capability Foundry**; **IOI L1 → IOI Settlement Layer**; **GoalKernel → GoalConductor**; **wallet.network → IOI Authority Plane** (canon name; consumer brand retained); **`ignition_evidence`/`inflection_evidence` → `meta_improvement_evidence`/`sustained_trend_shift_evidence`**; **"Standard DAS" → StandardAutonomousSystemProfile** (DAS acronym banned from contract-facing names). Part II also lists names critics attacked that should be **kept** (e.g., Agentgres — see facet review — and OutcomeRoom), plus a phased alias strategy so cross-links never break.

### Ten priority actions (sequenced by leverage)

1. **Make the contract layer machine-checkable** (R1): one resolvable schema artifact per envelope/receipt/event (JSON Schema 2020-12 or CUE), cross-field rules as CEL under the already-minted `invariant://` namespace, prose walls *generated* from the registry; extend `check-architecture-docs.mjs` to enum-parity, scheme-registry, and anchor-existence checks.
2. **Specify the cryptographic spine** (R2): one canonical serialization (RFC 8785 JCS or deterministic CBOR) with per-envelope domain separation; DSSE/COSE signatures **required** on consequential receipts and grants; transparency-log inclusion/consistency proofs; AuthorityGrant as a signed, attenuable, offline-verifiable capability token (UCAN/Biscuit-shaped) with a revocation-epoch beacon.
3. **Add prompt injection and IFC to the invariants** (R3): name the attack class; taint/provenance labels on tool outputs, connector payloads, and context; provenance-conditioned egress gates on leases; CaMeL-style planner/executor separation in the default harness profile.
4. **Stop the doc-drift engine** (R4): delete the recency conflict rule; lint the status headers; extract a machine-readable status registry; decompose the 5,006-line master guide — *first archiving slices 731–1034, which currently exist nowhere else*.
5. **State the missing distributed-systems physics** (R5): explicit clock/timing model; durable epoch/membership CAS with a named home; fencing extended through the authority plane and external effectors; lost-suffix doctrine after failover.
6. **Adopt RATS-shaped attestation** (R6): verifier/appraisal role, hardware-rooted node identity, nonce freshness, enforcement-point identity bound into every consequential receipt; explicit GPU confidential-computing posture.
7. **Complete the economic mechanism** (R7): a demand-origination loop; `WorkQuote → hold → final cost` object chain; a Work Credit ledger object model; bonded challenges so verification funding scales.
8. **Execute rename Tier A** (R8): the eight renames above, with Part II's alias strategy; add a naming grammar + collision-review gate so the estate stops minting disclaimer-dependent names.
9. **Downgrade speculative field-freezes** (R9): move embodied/AIIP/cTEE/chain field-level state machines to sketch status; per the pack's own `canon-to-code-delta` rule, surfaces resume only when pulled by implemented contracts.
10. **Give the trust layer its game theory** (R10): challenge bonds, defined arbitration (name the mechanism or delete the word "deterministic"), dispute-game semantics for value-bearing slash/revoke/accept, hostile-counterparty assumptions throughout.

## Part I — Program-Level Risk Synthesis

**Scope:** cross-facet synthesis of 29 verified facet reports over `docs/architecture`. Every red-flag / contradiction finding, plus critical/high findings of other kinds, was clustered into systemic program-level risks — the things most likely to keep this architecture from succeeding *as a whole*. Ranked by probability × damage, most dangerous first.

**One-line diagnosis:** The *doctrine* is unusually strong and often ahead of SOTA; the *mechanisms that would make the doctrine real, buildable-as-specified, and verifiable by outside parties* are largely unspecified prose. The gap between "what the canon promises" and "what an implementer or auditor can mechanically check" is the program's dominant risk surface.

---

### Overall Program Assessment

#### Where IOI is genuinely ahead of SOTA
Twelve facets grade **near-SOTA** and six **divergent-by-design**, and the lead is concentrated in the conceptual/governance layer, where the pack invents primitives no shipping system has. The strongest are: the **EvaluationExposureLedger** (append-only, inheritance-aware accounting of what adaptive search learned from sealed holdouts — `recursive-improvement`, `foundry-evals` both flag it as ahead of Inspect/Braintrust/OpenAI Evals and a real operationalization of Dwork reusable-holdout theory); **accountable-actor-vs-resolver separation** so responsibility survives executor swaps (`object-model-work`, ahead of A2A/LangGraph/AutoGen); **first-class negative/inconclusive/exploit results as creditable lineage** (`object-model-work`, `goal-space`, `events-receipts`); the **progressive assurance ladder with non-collapsing honesty** (evidence < verification < acceptance < adjudication < settlement — `invariants-safety` INV-9, `protocol-aiip`, ahead of A2A/MCP conflating "reported complete" with "accepted"); **consensus-as-deployment-profile with a deterministic transition function and intelligence strictly upstream** (`das-kernels`, solving the LLM-nondeterminism-in-a-replicated-state-machine problem most "AI blockchain" projects ignore); the **Agentgres determinism rule** (no wall clock / no ambient randomness in admission — `agentgres`, FoundationDB/TigerBeetle-class); **Simplex/RTA-faithful physical-action safety** (`invariants-safety`, `embodied`); the **Model Independence Test and bidirectional route-rights matrix** (`learning-boundary`, `router-connectors-storage`, beyond any enterprise data-governance or LLM-gateway product); and the **candidate-is-never-authority** discipline applied uniformly to quotes/routes/venues/cloud (`wallet-authority`, `sas-decentralized`). The **claim-boundary honesty** — explicit Non-Claims lists, "absence of a risk label is never safety," "a receipt binds only its declared boundary fact" — is rarer and better than nearly all commercial agent-platform docs.

#### Where it is at parity
The **divergent-by-design** facets sit at or slightly ahead of parity in concept but unproven in mechanism. The semantic plane's **bitemporal ProvenanceAssertion** (valid/transaction time, confidence, contradiction, supersession, dispute) is at XTDB/Datomic level (`ontology-data`, `object-model-core`). **AIIP's** effect-recovery classes and assurance non-equivalences exceed A2A/MCP interop writing, and its **settle-locally-by-default** economics correctly encode the IBC/relayer lesson (`protocol-aiip`). The **cTEE / Candidate-Lattice Private Decoding** idea is a genuine architectural contribution that respects real FHE/MPC cost physics (`privacy-ctee`). Marketplace **neutrality-as-auditable-data** (challengeable RoutingDecisionReceipts, disclosed seed fleet) goes beyond DMA-style legal obligations (`economics`, `worker-domain`). These are defensible bets — but each is "planned" or "speculative," so parity is a claim about design, not shipped behavior.

#### Where it is behind or exposed
Twelve facets grade **behind**, and they cluster precisely on the operational substrate SOTA systems actually compete on. **Cryptographic mechanism** is the sharpest deficit: receipts, grants, and attestations are behind SLSA/in-toto/Sigstore/CT/RATS on signing, transparency, and offline verification (`events-receipts`, `wallet-authority`, `assurance-liability`, `l1-chain`, `privacy-ctee`, `hypervisoros-substrate`). **Machine-readable schema/status** is behind Kubernetes/KEP/OpenTelemetry/Backstage practice (`meta-status-planning`, `object-model-core`, `daemon-doctrine-api`, `events-receipts`). **Distributed-systems timing and failover** lack the clock/lease/CAS physics of Chubby/Spanner/Raft/Kafka (`das-kernels`, `agentgres`). **Commercial billing, dispute, and settlement** are behind Metronome/Stripe/FOCUS, Upwork/Kleros/UMA, and CoW/UniswapX (`economics`, `sas-decentralized`, `l1-chain`, `assurance-liability`). **Node OS lifecycle** is behind Talos/Bottlerocket (`hypervisoros-substrate`), and **provider placement** is behind SkyPilot/Karpenter (`providers-environments`). The exposure is not that the doctrine is wrong — it is that the doctrine has written checks the unbuilt mechanism layer cannot yet cash, and several of those checks (verifiable proof, fail-closed authority, honest attestation) are the exact claims the product is sold on.

---

### Systemic Risks (ranked, most dangerous first)

#### R1 — The contract layer is prose, not schema: the master drift engine (CRITICAL)
**Failure mode.** The canon's load-bearing artifacts — ~200 envelopes, ~180 receipt types, ~500 API endpoints, ~374 URI schemes, ~430 vocabulary terms, dozens of enums, and the entire status/implementation tracker — exist only as hand-maintained YAML-in-Markdown prose with no machine-checkable schema, constraint language, or generation pipeline. The pack's own doc-gates lint prose literals but validate zero instances. Consequence: every implementation independently re-derives hundreds of conditional admission rules, and they *already* disagree — this is not a future risk, it is a measured present condition. Because "prevent split-brain API design" is the stated purpose of the object-model files, this defeats the canon's central promise.

**Evidence across facets.**
- `object-model-core` — "The canon's contract layer is prose-only: no machine-checkable schema or constraint language for ~200 envelopes" (`common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3335`); "111 of 374 registered URI schemes are invalid per RFC 3986 (underscores)" (`:342`); "The ID registry claims exhaustiveness but envelopes use unregistered schemes, and two schemes are registered twice" (`:586`).
- `events-receipts` — "The ~300-kind event list and ~180-type receipt registry as prose is a drift engine" (`events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:61`); "No canonical serialization spec — every sha256/root field commits to undefined bytes" (`:1861`); "Two conflicting RoutingDecisionReceipt schemas inside the file that claims schema supremacy" (`:1045` vs `:2418`).
- `daemon-doctrine-api` — "The API canon is a self-admitted hand-maintained reference wall whose real source of truth is the code" (`api.md:18`).
- `meta-status-planning` — "Status tables are hand-maintained prose; extract a machine-readable status registry" (`implementation-matrix.md:18`); "Implementation states are an open vocabulary no linter can validate" (`canon-to-code-delta.md:55`).
- `vocabulary-enums` — "vocabulary.md dual-owns semantics with envelope/component docs and lacks the excerpt discipline the enums file invented" (`canonical-enums.md:24`); duplicate parallel term entries prove flat-glossary capacity exceeded (`vocabulary.md:2061`).
- `shell-surfaces` — "The hand-maintained 1000-line YAML reference wall is already drifting" (`core-clients-surfaces.md:3056`).
- `agentgres` — consistency-level and other enums "drift within the same file" (`api-object-model.md:1880` vs `:546`).

**Leading indicator.** Count of contradiction findings where two files (or one file) disagree on an enum/field/schema for the same object; it is already large and grows each alignment pass. A concrete tripwire: the next duplicate-enum or dead-scheme discovered *inside a single owner file*.

**Cheapest credible mitigation.** One schema artifact per envelope/receipt/event as a resolvable `schema://` object (JSON Schema 2020-12 or CUE), cross-field rules as CEL referenced via the already-minted `invariant://` namespace, and **generate the prose walls from it**; extend `check-architecture-docs.mjs` from phrase-presence to (a) scheme-in-registry, (b) enum-member parity across files, (c) anchor-path existence. This breaks no pack physics — docs stay canon, the registry is their machine form — and it is the single highest-leverage fix because it converts a dozen other findings into mechanical CI failures.

---

#### R2 — The proof and authority plane is not cryptographically verifiable as specified (CRITICAL)
**Failure mode.** The entire product thesis — "verifiable bounded agency," exportable proof, receipts-for-everything, fail-closed authority — reduces to cryptographic verification that the canon never specifies. Signatures are *optional* on the base ReceiptEnvelope and every shown receipt; there is no signing-envelope format (DSSE/COSE), no signer-key identity/rotation/revocation lifecycle, no transparency-log inclusion/consistency proofs, no canonical serialization for the bytes every hash commits to, and no offline verification procedure. The AuthorityGrant — the security keystone — is the *thinnest* object in the object model, and wallet grants are field-level records rather than verifiable capability tokens. A buyer, auditor, or AIIP counterparty cannot verify an exported bundle; a deposed writer or provider-rooted node can forge or equivocate.

**Evidence across facets.**
- `events-receipts` — "Receipts are unverifiable by outside parties: signatures optional, no verification procedure, no signer-key lifecycle" (`events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:926`, **severity critical**); "No transparency-log rigor: receipt roots exist but inclusion/consistency proofs, checkpoints, and split-view defense are unspecified" (`:2044`); "No canonical serialization spec" (`:1861`).
- `object-model-core` — "AuthorityGrantEnvelope — the security keystone — is the thinnest object in the file: no signature, no delegation chain, no key binding, example literals in the schema" (`common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3491`).
- `wallet-authority` — "Grants are field-level records, not verifiable capability tokens: no attenuation, proof chains, or offline verification" (`api-authority-scopes.md:401`; RootGrant/SubGrant named, never specified); "'Panic/revocation must kill active grants' has no distribution mechanism or latency bound" (`doctrine.md:998`); "request_hash/policy_hash have no canonical serialization, so guardian signatures … are unimplementable across the Rust/TS boundary" (`doctrine.md:997`); "No workload identity or sender-constrained leases" (`api-authority-scopes.md:323`).
- `assurance-liability` — "CertificationClaim has no signature, envelope, or trust-root mechanism — relying parties cannot verify claims" (`ecosystem-assurance-certification-liability.md:242`); "No revocation distribution or transparency log for claims consumed across domains" (`:183`).
- `l1-chain` — "Challenge functions have no dispute-game semantics" (`ioi-l1-contract-interfaces.md:291`); "No evidence-availability binding" (`:439`).
- `protocol-aiip` — "Canonical serialization, signature coverage, and hash agility are undecided and missing from the workstream list" (`aiip.md:969`); "No identity resolution, key rotation, or revocation story for sovereign peers" (`:331`).
- `privacy-ctee` — "CustodyProof's 'no_plaintext_custody' verdict is unverifiable when the enforcing daemon runs on the adversary's node" (`private-workspace-ctee.md:704`); "Result envelope from remote nodes is unauthenticated" (`task-capsule-protocol.md:233`).

**Leading indicator.** Any exported DeliveryBundle / ComplianceAuditExportBundle / CertificationClaim that a second implementation (or an external auditor) cannot verify offline. The first cross-runtime hash-reproduction test between the Rust core and `@ioi/wallet-protocol` that fails is the canary.

**Cheapest credible mitigation.** Pin one canonical serialization (RFC 8785 JCS or deterministic CBOR/COSE) with a domain-separation tag per envelope type, owned in `common-objects-and-envelopes.md`; make signatures **required** on all consequential receipts and grants via a DSSE/COSE profile; specify AuthorityGrant as a signed, attenuable token (parent_grant_ref, holder_key, caveats, revocation_epoch) with a Biscuit/UCAN-style offline check within a bounded staleness window; add a signed monotonic RevocationEpochBeacon with per-risk-class max-staleness. This is additive today and closes a whole family of critical findings at once.

---

#### R3 — Prompt injection / information-flow control is absent from the safety spine (CRITICAL)
**Failure mode.** The number-one attack class for exactly this product category — autonomous agents that read untrusted content and take effectful actions — is never named anywhere in non-archive canon. Authority leases bound *blast radius* but not *in-scope misuse*: a worker legitimately leased to read private context plus any outbound-capable action can exfiltrate within its envelope when steered by injected content (the "lethal trifecta"). Tool outputs, context cells, connector payloads, and web/room-derived artifacts carry no origin/trust/taint label, so effect admission cannot condition on the provenance closure of the inputs that shaped a proposal. Approvals themselves are steerable (the human-facing summary can be authored by injected content).

**Evidence across facets.**
- `invariants-safety` — "Prompt injection — the top attack class for this exact product — is never named or analyzed in the safety spine" (grep-verified zero occurrences; nearest is schema validation at `security-privacy-policy-invariants.md:317`); "No provenance-conditioned egress control for in-scope exfiltration (the lethal trifecta survives the lease model)" (`verifiable-bounded-agency.md:163`); "Ordinary approvals bind request bytes but not what the human saw" (`security-privacy-policy-invariants.md:51`).
- `harness-memory` — "No prompt-injection/context-poisoning defense at the harness seam: observations carry no origin/trust field" (`default-harness-profile.md:987`).
- `router-connectors-storage` — "Tool outputs are never tainted: no information-flow control between untrusted content and subsequent effectful calls" (`connectors-tools/contracts.md:244`); "RuntimeToolContract has no network egress binding; prim:net.request is ambient network" (`:44`).
- `learning-boundary` / `ontology-data` support: taint doctrine exists at adjacent boundaries (INV-17 for participant input, MCP prompts untrusted) but never reaches the model-context / effect-admission path.

**Leading indicator.** First red-team demonstration of exfiltration or scope-widening via injected content within an already-granted lease; also, any tool contract shipping with `prim:net.request` and no egress allowlist.

**Cheapest credible mitigation.** Add a registry invariant (an "INV-35: context provenance bounds proposal trust"): every ContextCell, tool output, connector payload, and web/room artifact carries a provenance label (`trusted_principal | admitted | untrusted_external`); any effect at risk_class `external_message` or above whose derivation closure mixes private-context and untrusted-provenance labels fails closed to a step-up path, with the label closure bound into the effect receipt. Add a CaMeL/dual-LLM separation requirement to the default harness profile, per-tool egress allowlists to RuntimeToolContract, and a rendered-presentation commitment to ApprovalReceipt. Name OWASP LLM01 explicitly.

---

#### R4 — "Canon is authoritative" is already false: institutionalized doc drift and single-owner erosion (HIGH)
**Failure mode.** The pack's foundational meta-claim is that the canon is the single source of truth. That claim is already contradicted inside flagship files: a *recency-based* conflict-resolution rule that cannot settle owner-vs-owner disputes, 178 code anchors citing a deleted package (papered over by a blanket disclaimer while their CI hooks "rot green"), a 5,000-line master guide that falsely claims its slice ledger was archived while 246 slice paragraphs remain (some existing in no archive at all), and the same doctrine restated with drifting wording across three-to-seven files despite an explicit single-owner rule. Monolithic owner files (9,375 / 5,006 / 4,398 / 2,282 / 2,188 lines) carry one "Last alignment pass" date and one status line for families at wildly different maturity, guaranteeing invisible per-section drift. Each drift instance erodes the credibility that is the pack's central value proposition.

**Evidence across facets.**
- `meta-navigation` — "Recency-based conflict rule is a drift engine" (`source-of-truth-map.md:99`); "README (navigation-index class) owns 37 doctrine Non-Negotiables, with broken numbering" (`README.md:530`/`533` both "30.").
- `meta-status-planning` — "The flagship status artifact already rotted: 178 code anchors cite a deleted package, papered over by a blanket disclaimer" (`implementation-matrix.md:36`); "Staleness metadata is file-global" (`:10`).
- `meta-master-guide` — "The guide claims its slice ledger was archived out, but ~2,900 lines / 246 slice paragraphs remain in-file, and Slices 731-1034 exist ONLY in the guide" (`hypervisor-kernel-substrate-unification-master-guide.md:4997`); "a mechanical rename left 17 ungrammatical fragments" (`:812`).
- Single-owner erosion is a recurring structural finding: `object-model-work` (11-role enum duplicated 3×), `das-kernels` ("Single-writer promotion doctrine is restated in four places with diverging strength"), `recursive-improvement` ("S/J/A … duplicated between foundations and the app doc"), `agentgres` ("77-bullet 'What Agentgres Owns' list triple-enumerates ownership"), `worker-domain` ("worker-marketplace.md … restates the lifecycle, memory, and console canon"), `events-receipts` ("Delivery object soup"), `harness-memory`, `providers-environments`, `foundry-evals`, `economics` — all flag restated/monolithic ownership as drift engines.

**Leading indicator.** Any CI gate that passes while its referenced code anchor is deleted (the "rot green" pattern); any two owner docs carrying the same normative sentence with different wording after a "Last alignment pass."

**Cheapest credible mitigation.** Replace the recency rule with mechanical precedence (map-owner wins for its subject; `Supersedes` header wins within a subject; else ADR required). Add CI checks that fail on (a) any `Code anchor` path absent from the working tree outside `_archive/`, (b) `SESSION \d` / `Slice \d+` tokens in `_meta/*.md`, (c) duplicate receipt-type / enum-member declarations. Finish the master-guide extraction and promote its durable contracts (Step/Module ABI, conformance command contract) into component canon. Split the largest owner files by owner-altitude. Move status to the machine-readable registry from R1 so per-row `audited_at` and enum validity are lintable.

---

#### R5 — Distributed-systems correctness gaps in the "intelligent blockchain" core (HIGH)
**Failure mode.** The governed-autonomous-system chain is the substrate everything else settles on, and its safety-over-liveness claims rest on physics the canon never states. There is no timing/clock model anywhere leases, heartbeats, and observation-expiry are used (no skew bound, lease-duration rule, or partial-synchrony assumption). Fencing stops at the Agentgres write log — a deposed-but-live writer holding unexpired wallet capability leases can still fire consequential *external* effects that never traverse a replica handshake. There is no durable home for the epoch/membership compare-and-swap that must survive writer loss (a bootstrap circularity: the epoch lives in the failed writer's own domain). Lost-suffix / orphaned-receipt semantics on sub-quorum promotion are undefined, and policy/authority checks over stale projections can authorize effects the canonical state forbids.

**Evidence across facets.**
- `das-kernels` — "Fencing stops at the Agentgres log; the authority plane and external effects are never fenced" (`common-objects-and-envelopes.md:1539`); "No durable home for the epoch/membership compare-and-swap that must survive writer loss" (`:1497`); "'Prove the old writer cannot continue' is unachievable without a timing model the canon never states" (`governed-autonomous-systems.md:374`); "Lost-suffix semantics on promotion are undefined: receipts can outlive the chain that issued them" (`common-objects-and-envelopes.md:1573`); "No canonical read-consistency vocabulary; authority checks over stale projections are unaddressed" (`governed-autonomous-systems.md:365`); "The hard protocol content lives in dozens of unwritten policy:// refs — schema rigor masking a protocol vacuum" (`common-objects-and-envelopes.md:1710`).
- `agentgres` — "Batch-rooting doctrine contradicts per-operation state_root" (`doctrine.md:233` vs `api-object-model.md:532`); "No log compaction, segment retirement, or snapshot-cadence contract — rebuild cost is unbounded" (`postgres-bridge-and-readiness-contract.md:561`); "No erasure mechanics for personal data inside the immutable operation log" (`doctrine.md:527`).
- `events-receipts` — writer-epoch fencing exists in chain receipts but nothing binds it to external effect gates (consistent with the das-kernels finding).

**Leading indicator.** The first two-node deployment where a partitioned/deposed writer fires an external effect (spend, connector, actuation) after promotion; any `policy://` ref in the failover/ordering envelopes still unwritten when `preauthorized_policy` automation is first exercised.

**Cheapest credible mitigation.** Add an invariant making `writer_epoch` a fencing token verified at **every** consequential effect gate (Kleppmann's check-the-token-at-the-resource, extended to the authority plane); require FailoverProfile to name a durable epoch/membership CAS substrate (witness quorum, wallet.network, or external coordinator) and a `timing_assumptions` block (max skew, heartbeat interval, lease TTL, wait-out rule) before automated failover; define an OrphanedSuffix/Divergence record on sub-quorum promotion; add a read-consistency enum requiring consequential effect gating to read at `writer_linearizable`. Name the already-built Agentgres fenced primary/standby protocol as the default resolution of the unwritten `policy://` refs.

---

#### R6 — Attestation and enforcement-point integrity are pre-RATS and posture-specific (HIGH)
**Failure mode.** Every guarantee in the pack reduces to "the daemon enforced it," and receipts are minted by that same daemon — yet no registry invariant requires a consequential receipt to bind the daemon build hash, policy-engine version, or runtime posture, and assurance claims do not automatically downgrade when the enforcement point is unattested. The node-integrity machinery that does exist is pre-RATS: no nonce/freshness, no distinct verifier role, no hardware-rooted node identity (so "forge node measurements" is unmitigated), no re-attestation cadence for persistently-rented nodes, and no GPU confidential-computing lane — the exact venue where AI plaintext actually lives. On DePIN/rented hardware the whole custody/attribution/billing story is trust-me JSON.

**Evidence across facets.**
- `invariants-safety` — "No registry invariant requires enforcement-point integrity evidence in consequential receipts" (`verifiable-bounded-agency.md:489`).
- `hypervisoros-substrate` — "No verifier/appraisal architecture: attestation is node-self-reported and 'policy_declared' counts as measurement" (`hypervisoros.md:467`); "No hardware-rooted node identity, so measurement receipts prove nothing about which machine emitted them" (`:196`); "Measurement is boot-time-only, but the flagship use case is nodes that run for months" (`:727`).
- `privacy-ctee` — "Attestation has no freshness, verifier role, endorsements, or re-attestation — a pre-RATS design" (`runtime-nodes-tee-depin.md:250`); "GPU confidential computing (H100 CC-mode era) is absent as a defined custody lane" (`private-workspace-ctee.md:1256`); "Enterprise Secure Profile has no Non-claim block" (`runtime-nodes-tee-depin.md:149`); "CustodyProof … unverifiable when the enforcing daemon runs on the adversary's node" (`:704`).
- `events-receipts` — "TEE attestation envelope lacks freshness/report_data binding and omits the TEEs that matter for AI (TDX, SEV-SNP, GPU CC)" (`task-capsule-protocol.md:192`).
- `providers-environments` — "No typed workload-identity object" (`providers-and-environments.md:1084`); "Static sealed cloud keys where SOTA is short-lived federated credentials" (`byo-provider-plane.md:257`).

**Leading indicator.** First remote/DePIN run whose receipts qualify for payout or an assurance tier on `policy_declared` measurement; any "cTEE-backed" or "tee_enterprise" badge surfaced on a marketplace before a composite CPU+GPU attestation object exists.

**Cheapest credible mitigation.** Add a registry invariant ("the boundary proves itself"): consequential receipts bind daemon build hash, policy-engine version, and runtime posture (`attested_tee | measured_boot | trusted_operator`), with assurance auto-downgrading to trusted-operator when unattested. Adopt RFC 9334 roles (nonce-fresh Evidence, distinct Verifier producing AttestationResult, endorsement/TCB checks), a TPM EK/AK (or DICE/IDevID) node-identity ceremony, periodic re-attestation bound to the lease revocation epoch, and a ConfidentialGpuMount lane. Re-anchor CustodyProof as a guardian-side egress proof, not a node-side claim.

---

#### R7 — The economic engine has no demand origination and no verifiable billing primitives (HIGH)
**Failure mode.** The monetization doctrine is honest about *why nothing accrues automatically* but never says *where buyer demand originates*: all three value loops describe supply-side capture, and take-rates/escrow/L1 settlement all price a transaction flow nothing in the stack generates. Simultaneously the billing substrate is prose-only: Work Credits are load-bearing across five surfaces with no credit-ledger object (grants, holds, drawdown priority, refunds), no WorkQuote/hold binding estimate→reservation→final cost, no rate-card versioning, no normalized multi-provider cost schema, and no settlement-side fraud gate. Verification — the load-bearing trust mechanism — has no funding mechanism that scales with attempt volume. A sovereign L1's security budget is circular (sparse fees can't pay a validator set whose token value depends on those fees).

**Evidence across facets.**
- `economics` — "The flywheel has no demand-origination engine — all three loops describe supply-side value capture" (`economic-flywheel-and-pricing-boundaries.md:27`); "Work Credits are load-bearing across five surfaces but have no credit-ledger object model" (`:377`); "No WorkQuote/hold object binds estimate → reservation → final cost" (`:441`); "No rate-card/plan versioning governance" (`:430`); "Settlement-side fraud loop … has visibility but no gate" (`:556`).
- `goal-space` — "Verification is the load-bearing mechanism but no mechanism scales verifier funding with attempt volume" (`collaborative-outcome-pattern.md:558`, **red-flag high**).
- `l1-chain` — "Security-budget circularity of a sparse-commitment sovereign chain is never modeled" (`ioi-l1-mainnet.md:412`); "State the fee-denomination doctrine now; burn-and-mint is a structural misfit" (`:416`).
- `worker-domain` — "Marketplace economics are unlinked and unmapped to invocation modes" (`worker-marketplace.md:453`); "Composition-bound rebenchmarking is economically combinatorial and will strangle supply" (`:384`).
- `sas-decentralized` — "Same-lifecycle claim across settlement rails hides a buyer-protection cliff" (`service-marketplace.md:169`).

**Leading indicator.** First commercial cohort where receipt-storm (retry loops) drains a wallet at flat OCU rate with no relation to supplier cost; first Sparse Worker Category leaderboard that renders with only IOI seed-fleet supply on all sides.

**Cheapest credible mitigation.** Define canonical ledger objects (WorkCreditGrant/Hold/Debit/Adjustment with drawdown priority) plus a WorkQuote+Hold with an overrun policy, owned in a billing-mechanics doc; adopt FinOps FOCUS as the supplier-cost schema; add a VerificationFundingPolicy to CollaborationTerms and bonded-challenge payouts; name one atomic demand-origination wedge (a vertical outcome category with IOI-funded escrowed bounties under the same neutrality rules as the seed fleet). For L1, invert the default to "deploy on an existing settlement environment," with sovereign chain as a demand-gated ADR, and add a quantified security-budget threshold.

---

#### R8 — Naming and category coinages are a standing credibility and legal exposure (HIGH)
**Failure mode.** A striking number of canonical names pick fights the honest prose then has to lose, and they will be quoted verbatim by critics, procurement, and regulators — the surfaces that read names and function, not disclaimers. Several carry real legal exposure. Because the terms are being baked into wire keys, receipts, contract names, and SDK surfaces, the cost is cheap now and permanent after implementation freezes them.

**Evidence across facets.**
- `vocabulary-enums` — "Type 1/2/3 hypervisor taxonomy hijacks Popek–Goldberg and invents a 'Type 3'"; "cTEE puts 'TEE' in the name of a non-TEE"; "'Foundry' collides with Palantir Foundry and Azure AI Foundry — with adapted-UI provenance making it worse" (the enums file itself encodes `surface_creation_method: adapted`); "'Agentgres' reads as a Postgres derivative"; "Credibility-hostile coinages … Web4, StockfishStyleCodingSearch, IntelligentBlockchain, decentralized.*."
- `protocol-aiip` — "Claiming to define 'canonical Web4' is a credibility liability"; "'Blockchain-native underneath' contradicts AIIP's no-consensus assumption" (`web4-and-ioi-stack.md:120`); "The acronym AIIP does not decode to its own title expansion."
- `das-kernels` — "'Intelligent blockchain' for a PoA-1 hash-chained log invites the exact credibility attack the pack elsewhere avoids" (`domain-kernels.md:63`).
- `l1-chain` — "'IOI L1' as a name front-runs the very decision the bootstrap gate defers"; "'Standard DAS' collides with Data Availability Sampling for exactly this spec's audience."
- `privacy-ctee` — "'cTEE' trades on the TEE brand the doc spends pages disclaiming."
- `sas-decentralized` — "'decentralized.exchange' / 'decentralized.trade' name exactly what the doctrine disclaims" (MiCA/CFTC read names and function).
- `foundry-evals` — "'Foundry' collides with Palantir Foundry and Microsoft Azure AI Foundry"; "'EvaluationEpoch' collides with training epochs."
- `hypervisoros-substrate`, `providers-environments` — "'Type 1' / 'Type 3' overloads the canonical hypervisor taxonomy."

**Leading indicator.** First external critique or regulator inquiry that quotes "intelligent blockchain," "canonical Web4," "cTEE," or "decentralized.trade"; first public whitepaper/API surface that ships a contested name into a wire key.

**Cheapest credible mitigation.** Run one coordinated rename pass *before* wire keys and receipts freeze: substrate postures (`bare_metal / workstation / autonomy_plane`), `SealedComputeEnvelope` for cTEE, `AdmissionLedger`/keep "Agentgres" as internal codename, `RouteIntelligence/VenueIntelligence/ResourceIntelligence` as the canonical namespace with `decentralized.*` demoted to brand alias, "verifiability-native" not "blockchain-native," and a machine-authority-first framing with Web4 as one labeled positioning section. Add an external-collision register to `vocabulary.md` as a mandatory review gate for new coinages. Because most of these names live in `README` Non-Negotiables and `foundations/invariants.md` (INV-10/11/13/29), each rename must explicitly reword the cited invariant rather than drift from it.

---

#### R9 — Massive speculative surface is frozen at field level before any implementation (HIGH)
**Failure mode.** The canon authors exhaustive field-level state machines for families it *itself* marks planned or speculative (embodied's ~30 objects, AIIP's 23 packet classes, cTEE/CLPD, the five ordering profiles, the L1 contract set), while the actually-built core envelopes lack even a `schema_version`. This inverts effort away from the built core, guarantees v2 churn as the speculative shapes meet first implementation contact, imposes a maintenance obligation to keep 5+ coherent profiles aligned every edit, and — via all-or-nothing maturity bars — pushes adopters to treat "shim" bridges as the real product while canon frames them as subordinate.

**Evidence across facets.**
- `object-model-core` — "Versioning discipline is inverted: speculative families carry schema_version, the built core envelopes carry none" (`common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3940`); "Full field-level canon is frozen for families the file itself marks planned or speculative" (`:9`).
- `das-kernels` — "Five ordering profiles, embodied fleets, and federation are canonized while nearly everything is 'planned or speculative'" (`governed-autonomous-systems.md:9`).
- `embodied` — "Maturity bar demands a full native robotics platform before any claim" (`embodied-runtime.md:1934`); "One 2,188-line owner for ~25 subjects is a drift engine."
- `privacy-ctee` — "Split the 1872-line file: custody canon, unvalidated CLPD/CLE scheduling theory, and the quant worked example are three documents."
- `foundry-evals` — "Foundry canon is the union of ~8 commercial product categories."
- `protocol-aiip` — "Monolithic 23-packet vocabulary hardwired into one envelope enum inverts minimal-core-plus-profiles protocol design."
- `providers-environments` — "55 canonical objects with fully-fielded schemas under one 'partial' status is a drift engine."

**Leading indicator.** The first `ioi.<name>.v2` minted with no recorded v1→v2 delta (already true for `ioi.settlement.v2`); count of speculative sub-objects edited per alignment pass that no implemented contract pulls.

**Cheapest credible mitigation.** Apply the pack's own "surfaces resume only when pulled by an implemented contract" rule *inside* the object files: mandate `schema_version` on built/partial envelopes first; publish speculative families as **shape contracts** (identity scheme, key refs, invariants) with per-section status rather than exhaustive field-level state machines; mark the three non-single-writer ordering profiles `reserved: semantics non-normative`; restructure the all-or-nothing maturity bars into named claimable conformance levels (L0 bridge-governed … L3 native).

---

#### R10 — The verification / dispute / adjudication trust layer lacks its game-theoretic mechanisms (HIGH)
**Failure mode.** "Verified work" is the moat, and the assurance ladder is doctrinally sound, but the mechanisms that make verification and dispute *incentive-compatible* are missing across every layer that needs them. Challenge functions emit events and stop; VerifierChallenges carry no bond, window, or escalation; the flagship sas.xyz escrow loop hangs on a "deterministic arbitration path" defined nowhere in the corpus; disputes presume private evidence survives a hostile counterparty with no availability binding; insurance "covered" status has no owning fact; and IOI is structurally the liable certifier of last resort for its own top trust tier.

**Evidence across facets.**
- `l1-chain` — "Challenge functions have no dispute-game semantics — they emit and stop" (`ioi-l1-contract-interfaces.md:291`); "No evidence-availability binding" (`:439`); "Value-bearing slash/revoke/accept functions lack the CAS + adjudication + authority hardening" (`:270`).
- `goal-space` — "VerifierChallenge carries no challenger-side cost, leaving re-verification economics unpriced" (`collaborative-outcome-pattern.md:706`); "No verifier-assignment mechanism" (`:1150`).
- `sas-decentralized` — "Escrow release hangs on an arbitration mechanism that exists nowhere in the pack" (`service-marketplace.md:148`); "Worker Training escrow release keyed to benchmarks with no verifier independence or holdout secrecy" (`:145`).
- `assurance-liability` — "IOI is structurally the liable certifier of last resort for its own top trust tier" (`ecosystem-assurance-certification-liability.md:484`); "liability_status 'covered' has no owning fact" (`:371`); "No issuer accreditation or certifier-liability economics" (`:167`).
- `foundry-evals` — "No decontamination contract" and "exposure-spend function … has no reference semantics, making the ledger unfalsifiable" (`evaluations.md:183`).

**Leading indicator.** First real escrow dispute on the default `local_domain` rail with no adjudicator to call; first frivolous VerifierChallenge that forces expensive re-verification at zero cost to the challenger; first exit of a counterparty whose committed roots become unadjudicable.

**Cheapest credible mitigation.** Define one shared OptimisticChallenge primitive (challenger bond, respondent window, escalation, timeout default, bond distribution) referenced by every challenge function; create a `dispute-and-arbitration.md` owner naming adjudicator identity, deadlines, non-response defaults, appeals, and per-rail enforceability; add an EvidenceRetentionBinding (root-class → availability-witness/retention bond); separate profile authorship from claim issuance with an accredited, bonded issuer class; require sealed-holdout + non-provider evaluator for training-acceptance escrow release.

---

#### R11 — Work-spine execution semantics are underspecified; two conforming daemons will diverge (MEDIUM)
**Failure mode.** The goal/work spine — the thing that actually drives autonomous spend and delegation — ships ~15 bespoke status enums with no legal-transition grammar, a central control loop that is mutable-in-place with no revision/hash/per-transition receipts (a replay hole in the pack's own physics), unbounded mutable ref arrays with no compaction contract, no idempotency/dedup key for non-room submissions despite doctrine requiring idempotent retry, and no cancellation-propagation contract from GoalRun down through cells, invocations, and external handles. Two conforming implementations will legitimately disagree on transition legality, duplicate autonomous runs on retry, and orphan in-flight work on cancel.

**Evidence across facets.**
- `object-model-work` — "GoalGroundingLoopEnvelope is mutable-in-place with no revision, hash, or transition receipts — a replay hole in the pack's own physics" (`common-objects-and-envelopes.md:8733`); "Status-machine explosion with no transition grammar" (`:8685`); "GoalRun carries unbounded mutable ref arrays" (`:8673`); "No cancellation-propagation contract" (`:9116`); "GoalRun.active_loop_phase duplicates GoalGroundingLoop.phase — two writers of the same fact" (`:8653`).
- `daemon-doctrine-api` — "Doctrine mandates idempotent retry but no idempotency mechanism exists anywhere in the API" (`doctrine.md:538`); "Six overlapping unit-of-execution resources with parallel lifecycle verbs" (`api.md:1893`).
- `agentgres` — branch/merge objects exist but the underlying operation semantics (per-op vs per-batch root) are internally contradictory (feeds the same divergence).

**Leading indicator.** First cross-implementation (or SDK-vs-daemon) disagreement on whether a transition like `sleeping→delegated` is legal; first duplicate autonomous run created by a retried POST.

**Cheapest credible mitigation.** Add a lifecycle-grammar section (small closed state machines with legal-transition tables and who-may-transition per object); make the grounding loop append-only children or a declared projection over receipts; apply the ImprovementCampaign head/projection split to GoalRun's ref arrays; define one Stripe-style Idempotency-Key contract on every consequential POST; add a cancellation-propagation section mirroring the embodied drain/fence discipline.

---

#### R12 — The regulatory / compliance perimeter is unowned across finance, data, and physical safety (MEDIUM)
**Failure mode.** The product deliberately spans several heavily-regulated domains — value movement, event markets, training-data governance, physical action — with only policy-hook-level treatment and no named accountable entity or conformity route. wallet.network concentrates revenue on regulated value movement with no money-transmitter/VASP posture; decentralized.trade canonizes a MiCA/CFTC-adjacent lane with no regulatory posture for the engine itself; there is no EU AI Act conformity route or deadline-bound incident reporting, and no GDPR erasure mechanics inside the immutable operation log (crypto-shredding absent); physical action lacks a safety-integrity-level taxonomy, e-stop proof-test cadence, and human takeover-time budget. The "receipts-for-everything, ComplianceAuditExportBundle" positioning actively invites the scrutiny that finds these holes.

**Evidence across facets.**
- `wallet-authority` — "1379 lines of perps/prediction-market brokerage doctrine are fused to the enterprise authority layer — an adoption and regulatory-optics hazard" (`product-exchange-risk.md:646`).
- `economics` — "wallet.network revenue concentrates on regulated value movement with only policy-hook-level compliance doctrine and no regulated-entity posture" (`economic-flywheel-and-pricing-boundaries.md:807`).
- `sas-decentralized` — "trade.md canonizes a regulation-heavy lane with no regulatory posture for the engine itself" (`trade.md:13`).
- `assurance-liability` — "No EU AI Act conformity route or deadline-bound regulator incident reporting" (`ecosystem-assurance-certification-liability.md:700`).
- `agentgres` — "No erasure mechanics for personal data inside the immutable operation log" (`doctrine.md:527`); `router-connectors-storage` — crypto-shredding absent as deletion on immutable backends (`filecoin-cas.md:188`).
- `learning-boundary` — "No mapping from boundary artifacts to the regulatory deliverables that actually drive 2026 enterprise training-data governance" (`institutional-learning-boundary.md:267`).
- `invariants-safety` — "No safety-integrity-level taxonomy or e-stop proof-test cadence"; "HumanSupervisionPolicy has modes but no takeover-time budget."
- `l1-chain` — "On-chain payouts are not bound to the pack's own jurisdiction/eligibility policy machinery" (`ioi-l1-contract-interfaces.md:256`).

**Leading indicator.** First enterprise procurement or regulator that asks "who is the money transmitter / notified body / data controller of record?" and finds no named entity; first EU high-risk embodied or service deployment with no conformity assessment route.

**Cheapest credible mitigation.** Add a per-revenue-line regulatory-perimeter note (partner-of-record / licensed-sponsor / explicit deferral) to wallet.network and a regulatory-posture section gating any live trade venue adapter; add crypto-shredding as canon for personal data in the operation log; add EU AI Act conformity routes and Art. 73 incident-reporting to JurisdictionPolicyPack; type physical-action SIL/PL levels, e-stop proof-test cadence, and takeover-time budgets; add a "Regulatory Projection" mapping from rights/revocation/independence artifacts to AI Act summaries, GDPR erasure evidence, and DORA exit tests.

---

### Cross-cutting note: the shape of the whole
The twelve risks are not independent — they share two roots. **R1 (prose-not-schema)** is the enabling condition for R4, R9, R11, and half of the contradiction findings in every facet: without a machine form, drift, over-specification, and semantic divergence are the default. **R2 (unverifiable proof)** is the enabling condition for R6, R10, and R12: without cryptographic verification, attestation, dispute adjudication, and compliance evidence are all assertions rather than proofs. Fixing R1 and R2 first — a schema/serialization/signing substrate that the doctrine compiles down to — would convert a large fraction of the remaining findings from open design questions into mechanical CI failures, and would let the genuinely strong doctrine (the near-SOTA and divergent-by-design half of the pack) actually cash the checks it has written.

---


## Part II — Naming-Coherence Program

Synthesized from all 31 verified facet reports plus a full read of `_meta/vocabulary.md` (2,181 lines) and `foundations/canonical-enums.md` context. This program resolves per-facet rename suggestions into one internally consistent estate-wide plan: one concept, one name; no proposed name collides with another proposal or a kept name.

The naming system itself is near-SOTA in *mechanics* (three-layer language policy, presentation map, alias/retirement discipline, canonical-enums single ownership) and behind in *law*: there is no stated naming grammar, no machine-checkable registry, and no external-collision review — so ~430 terms accreted with predictable, recurring failure modes.

---

### 1. Systemic naming pathologies

#### P1 — Borrowed-term squatting (semantic hijacking of entrenched technical vocabulary)
The canon repeatedly names things after established industry terms whose meaning it then has to disclaim, guaranteeing misreads by exactly the audiences the pack courts.

- **Type 1/2/3 hypervisor**: `type3_autonomy_plane` has no referent in virtualization literature ("not-even-wrong to the infrastructure audience", vocabulary-enums); HypervisorOS also manages literal microVMs, so "hypervisor" is half-literal, half-metaphor in one family (hypervisoros-substrate, providers-environments).
- **cTEE**: puts "TEE" in the name of an explicitly-not-a-TEE software pattern; one lowercase `c` carries the entire "this is NOT that" load, and the enums file must keep `tee_enterprise` and `ctee_private_workspace` as sibling modes (vocabulary-enums, privacy-ctee).
- **Foundry**: collides with Palantir Foundry (whose UI this estate adapted — `surface_creation_method: adapted` is in the enums) and Azure AI Foundry, which occupies the identical model-catalog/tuning/eval semantics (vocabulary-enums, foundry-evals).
- **Standard DAS**: to every blockchain reader, DAS = Data Availability Sampling; the L1 spec is the one doc guaranteed to be read by that audience, and neither L1 file expands the acronym (l1-chain).
- **Intelligent blockchain** for a PoA-1 single-writer log is "the canonical strawman critics deride" (das-kernels); **Web4** is a contested, repeatedly burned label, and "IOI implements canonical Web4" pattern-matches to Web3-era category invention (protocol-aiip).
- **epoch** means three unrelated things: EvaluationEpoch (judgment freeze), training epoch (data pass), writer epoch (fencing token) — inside a platform that runs training loops and failover (foundry-evals, das-kernels).

#### P2 — Disclaimer-dependent names (the name needs a paragraph to survive)
When canon must disclaim a name inline, the name is wrong. This tax recurs across the estate:

- cTEE: "It is not a hardware TEE claim" is the second sentence of its own definition.
- wallet.network: "It is not merely a crypto wallet"; the presentation map must translate it to "SSO, permissions" to stop users misreading it (wallet-authority, vocabulary-enums).
- Default Harness Profile: ~25 lines of "not a meta-harness" denial spread across an It-is-not list, anti-patterns, and a non-negotiable (harness-memory).
- Work Credits: "not cash, a speculative token, a worker payout rail" re-disclaimed in four places (economics).
- decentralized.exchange/trade: "neither decentralized nor an exchange" — a centrally operated quote API owning no liquidity or execution (sas-decentralized).
- PhysicalMissionControlEnvelope carries an inline paragraph disclaiming its own "mission" (embodied).
- DigitalWorkerOntology apologizes for its name in its second sentence ("The name is historical") (worker-domain).
- Agentgres: docs must repeatedly clarify "operation-backed state, not mutable relational rows" — the clarification tax the `-gres` fusion imposes (agentgres, vocabulary-enums).

#### P3 — One word, many objects (overload without reservation rules)
No rule reserves contested words, so they accrete senses until every reader must disambiguate per sentence:

- **agent** — three objects in one API: `/v1/agents` (managed worker instances), `/v1/hypervisor/agents` (configured runtime actors), `/v1/agent-executions` (run lane) — plus Agent Harness Adapters and CLI aliases (daemon-doctrine-api); the `agent://` URI scheme is minted on an object canon insists is a ManagedWorkerInstance (worker-domain).
- **lease** — five objects of wildly different authority weight: CapabilityLease, session-access lease, IP lease, Akash provider lease, participant lease (providers-environments).
- **kernel** — four senses: GoalKernel (loop interpreter), IOIKernelL0 (chain substrate), "domain kernel", the literal Linux kernel (vocabulary-enums).
- **claim** — certification assertion vs liability demand in one file; `assurance.claim.opened` is ambiguous between them (assurance-liability).
- **settlement** — chain-level admission, node-level mediation, and economic finality, diagnosed and then kept by the same doc (das-kernels).
- **sparse** — niche category (SparseWorkerCategory), infrequent settlement, and the MoE sense the doc explicitly fights (learning-boundary, worker-domain).
- **domain** — bounded execution domain, Domain Ontology, Agentgres domain, domain kernel, sovereign domain; no disambiguation entry exists (protocol-aiip).
- **tool** — agent-callable capability (RuntimeToolContract, `tool://`) vs a class of UI panels (`tool_surface`, `tool_kind`) in the same owner file (shell-surfaces).
- **Goal Space** — subscription SKU vs per-outcome container; a fresh reader cannot tell whether an account has one or many (goal-space).
- **receipt_root** — per-receipt self-commitment, ledger accumulator root, and (as receipt_hash) the self-commitment again (events-receipts).
- **mission** — product surface, retired generic record, and physical envelopes (embodied) — partially resolved by the HypervisorMission retirement.

#### P4 — Same object, many names (synonym drift between owner files)
The single-owner vocabulary breeds its own synonyms:

- `AssuranceState` (vocabulary.md) vs `assurance_stage` (canonical-enums.md) — the two naming authorities disagree on the assurance enum's own name (vocabulary-enums).
- Doctrine emits RootGrant/SubGrant/AuthorityScopeLease/ApprovalToken; the API defines AuthorityGrant/CapabilityLease/AuthorityReview — four names that appear nowhere else in the pack vs the chain enforcement actually checks (wallet-authority).
- IOICli/IOITui/IOISdk/IOIAdk duplicate HypervisorCliHeadless/HypervisorTui/SDK/ADK ~1,100 lines apart with shared phrasing (vocabulary-enums).
- FQF / CSPS / Agentgres / Provenance / Work Ledger — five names for one truth plane; the taxonomy paper still names crate `fqf` while reality shipped `crates/agentgres` (agentgres).
- DeliveryEnvelope vs DeliveryBundle vs ServiceCompositionReceiptBundle — three overlapping deliverable containers with divergent fields (events-receipts).
- `LearningEvidenceEligibility` / `...Envelope` / `TrainingEvidenceEligibility` used uncoordinated across five docs (learning-boundary).
- `local_cached` vs `cached_projection` consistency levels inside one Agentgres file (agentgres); two run-to-idle state sets across the two model-router owners (router-connectors-storage).

#### P5 — Names that contradict their own semantics (field-level naming lies)
Schema field names are the API integrators actually read, and several encode the wrong contract:

- `context_topology_planning_required: best_effort` — a `*_required` field holding a non-required value (harness-memory).
- `agentgres_memory_mutation_allowed` implies direct writes in a propose-never-write-silently plane (harness-memory).
- `NodeMeasurementReceipt.privacy_claim` — a boot-measurement receipt carrying the exact conflation its own note forbids (privacy-ctee).
- CustodyProof verdict `no_plaintext_custody` claims more than a guardian-side stack can prove; the provable fact is `no_plaintext_release` (privacy-ctee).
- `EmbodiedRuntimeGuarantee` declares requirements, not guarantees — inviting the claims-without-evidence anti-pattern the pack polices (embodied).
- RunEnvelope's `state` vs 100+ sibling `status` fields (object-model-core); `runtimeTruthSource` camelCase 19× in a file that fail-closes on camelCase aliases (daemon-doctrine-api).
- `goal_execution_policy` on model-route selection collides with actual GoalRun execution policy (router-connectors-storage).
- Shipped `ctee_*`-named receipt statuses that prove only Rust-side ref binding, not TEE custody (meta-status-planning).
- Foundry scorecards carrying `verdict: pass | fail` — judgment vocabulary the boundary reserves for Evaluations (foundry-evals).
- `rejoin_and_state_merge_policy_ref` implying CRDT merge on a linear predecessor-commitment chain where the real semantics are re-admission (das-kernels).

#### P6 — No naming grammar, no registry, no collision review
The pack has a *consistent implicit* suffix system (Profile/Receipt/Lease/Projection/Envelope/Run) that is never stated as rules (vocabulary-enums). Consequences:

- Nine execution nouns (GoalRun, WorkRun, AutomationRun, TransformationRun, AgentExecution, HarnessInvocation, ModuleInvocation, ComputeSession, HypervisorSession) with no declared convention; AgentExecution breaks the implicit one.
- Casing law violated in the naming files themselves: `Standard`/`Private` backticked as enum values; `RiskCoverageState` uses Title-Case-with-spaces members (vocabulary-enums).
- 111 of 374 registered URI schemes contain underscores and are invalid per RFC 3986 — stock parsers in every language external AIIP parties use will reject them (object-model-core).
- Unexpandable acronyms in canon: AIIP does not decode to its own title ("Inter-Autonomous-System Protocol" yields IASP); `mow:` appears exactly once, undefined; `HHAI` is defined nowhere; OCU is never expanded (protocol-aiip, object-model-core, l1-chain, economics).
- `scope:*` is an unbounded flat string namespace with policy parameters leaking into scope names (`scope:wallet.transfer_under_limit`) (wallet-authority).
- No external-collision register despite hypervisor, TEE, kernel, wallet, Foundry, and DAS all colliding with entrenched industry meanings.

#### P7 — Credibility-hostile / FOOM-and-marketing vocabulary inside canonical enums and doctrine
Names critics will quote verbatim: `ignition_evidence` and `inflection_evidence` import takeoff-debate vocabulary into a deliberately sober claims ladder (recursive-improvement); `StockfishStyleCodingSearch` embeds a third-party OSS project name as canonical shorthand; "IOI implements canonical Web4"; "root moat" prose inside the alignment foundations doc (invariants-safety); `decentralized.*` names inviting decentralization-washing accusations (vocabulary-enums, sas-decentralized).
