New naming *laws* to land with the table (vocabulary.md "Naming Rules" section — from vocabulary-enums, providers-environments, learning-boundary):

- **Suffix law**: Profile = immutable declared posture; Receipt = immutable evidence of one boundary fact; Lease = bounded expiring grant; Projection = derived rebuildable view; Envelope = wire schema object; Run = durable admitted attempt; Invocation = one daemon-mediated call; Session = bounded interactive/allocation context; Contract = spec-level requirement set.
- **Casing law**: PascalCase objects; snake_case wire keys and enum members; hyphen-only URI schemes.
- **Reservation rules**: bare *lease* = CapabilityLease only (all others qualified compounds); *kernel* = L0/OS only; *claim* = liability/dispute only; *sparse* = settlement frequency only; *epoch* = fencing/training only; *settlement* = economic finality only; *tool* = callable capability only; *Agent* = configured-actor record family only, *Worker* = accountable actor; Envelope = schema object, bare name = the decision/record, `Training*` = the training_compatibility profile only.
- **Acronym policy**: must decode from its expansion; expanded at first use in every owner doc; registered in vocabulary.md (kills mow/HHAI/OCU-class orphans).
- **External-collision register**: every new coinage passes a collision review (hypervisor, TEE, kernel, wallet, Foundry, DAS, Web4 are the cautionary entries).
- **Enforcement**: vocab↔canonical-enums member/casing parity check, duplicate-term lint, scheme-grammar lint, and banned-alias list wired into `check:architecture-docs`.

---

### 3. Names critics attacked that should be KEPT

| Name | Attack | Why keep |
|---|---|---|
| **Agentgres** | "-gres reads as a Postgres fork"; presentation map hides it as Provenance (vocabulary-enums proposed AdmissionLedger/OperationLedger) | The agentgres facet's deeper review resolves this: the Postgres-compatible read bridge is the declared *survival strategy/builder wedge*, so the pun is an asset exactly where the name is used (builder/protocol contexts), and the product mapping (Provenance) already exists and is canonical. Fix instead: first-line "not a Postgres fork; Postgres-compatible read bridge only" clause at the term definition, a PostgreSQL trademark check, and retirement of the true offenders (FQF/CSPS). One concept, three *scoped* names (Agentgres protocol / Provenance product / work-ledger admin family) is the pack's own deliberate language-layer policy working as designed. |
| **GoalRun** | vocabulary-enums floated `GoalPursuit` ("canon constantly explains it with 'pursuit'") | Under the declared suffix law, `*Run` = durable admitted attempt record — which GoalRun is. It is also the pack's first code seam (WorkResult/OutcomeDelta → GoalRun spine) with rows in canon-to-code-delta and the held PR stack; renaming the spine noun now buys marginal clarity for maximal churn. The pursuit semantics live where they belong: GoalRunProfile = "reusable immutable pursuit definition" prose, and the loop rename (A5: GoalPursuitLoop) captures the pursuit word. |
| **AIIP** (the acronym) | "does not decode to its own title expansion" (protocol-aiip) | Keep the protocol brand; the defect is the expansion, not the acronym. Fix the title once ("Autonomous Institution Interoperation Protocol") — renaming a wire protocol identity pre-adoption costs external mindshare for zero semantic gain. |
| **Worker** (canonical protocol actor) | The Worker-only style rule is violated by the daemon's own Agent* names (vocabulary-enums) | Keep Worker as the accountable protocol/economic actor. Resolve the contradiction by *scoping* (A7), not by renaming the daemon plane to WorkerOperatingPlane: AgentRecord/AgentOperatingPlane name a genuinely different object (configured runtime actor) and product reality uses "agent". The register rule is rewritten as a scoped reservation, which is enforceable; the blanket rule never was. |
| **Fleet\*Mission\* physical contracts** (PhysicalMissionControlEnvelope, FleetMissionCoordinationRecord, FleetMissionAllocationLease) | "When canon must disclaim a name inline, rename it" (embodied proposed PhysicalControlEnvelope / FleetWork*) | README NN33 deliberately preserves typed physical mission contracts; "mission" is the industry-standard robotics/drone term (mission planning, mission control) — for once the borrowed term means exactly what the domain audience expects; and the colliding senses are being retired anyway (HypervisorMission retired; legacy Missions routes map to Work/Goals/Rooms). Delete the inline disclaimer once the HypervisorMission aliases are gone, rather than renaming the survivor. |
| **Hypervisor** (product name) | External-collision list names it; "half-literal, half-metaphor" | Too deep to move (product brand, daemon binary, whole doc estate), and *partially literal* — the daemon really does govern VMs/microVMs and a bare-metal node profile. The credibility exposure is concentrated in the borrowed Type-1/2/3 *axis*, which A2 removes; the product name itself stays, with an external-collision register entry. |
| **bounded DAS / BoundedDistributedAutonomousSystem** (foundations usage) | DAS collides with Data Availability Sampling (l1-chain) | The collision is audience-specific: it bites in L1/contract-facing docs (fixed by A10) and is tolerable in foundations where the term is always introduced with its expansion. Estate-wide retirement of "DAS" would touch nearly every foundations file for a collision only one audience experiences. Rule: expand at first use, never in contract/registry names. |
| **ManagedWorkerInstance** | worker-domain notes doctrine/product split ("call it an agent, canonical state remains a managed worker") | The doctrine name is correct; the defect was the `agent://` identifier (fixed in A7). Keeping the disclaimer-free canonical noun plus a product-facing display label is the language-layer policy working as intended. |
| **PlaintextFreeRuntimeMounting / PlaintextFreeModelMount** and the behavior-first family | (Adjacent to the cTEE attack) | Exemplary behavior-first names that say exactly what they do; they survive the cTEE→SCE rename untouched and should be cited in the Naming Rules section as the house style. |
| **OCU** (as internal unit) | economics flagged dual units | Keep OCU as the internal telemetry unit rather than collapsing units prematurely — but expand the acronym at first use and ban it from commercial UX; the ledger-grade unit is UsageCredit (B6) with an estimate→reconciled→invoiced grade axis. |
| **Provenance** (product surface name) | (implicitly questioned via Work Ledger oscillation) | The taxonomy convergence already picked Provenance; the fix is sweeping the "Work Ledger" stragglers (Tier C), not reopening the choice. |

---

### 4. Migration note: ordering and alias strategy

#### Guiding physics
1. **Wire freezes first, prose freezes last.** URI schemes, enum members, receipt field names, and route paths ossify at first implementation; prose can be swept any time. Therefore renames race the build sequence, not the doc calendar: everything touching the WorkResult/OutcomeDelta → GoalRun → OutcomeRoom seam (A5, A7 suffix law, B4, Tier C outcome_class fix) lands **before the first code PR of that seam**; everything touching planned-but-specified planes (A1, A9, A10, B5, B7, B14) lands before those planes are implemented.
2. **Invariant-bearing names never drift silently.** A2 (NN2), A1 (NN25), A3 (INV-13), A4/A6 (INV-10/11), B6 (NN18), B9 (NN35), B12 (INV-29), the embodied KEEP (NN33): each rename PR must reword the invariant/Non-Negotiable in the same commit, with the rationale stated — the pack's own citation rule makes these greppable, which is what makes the sweeps safe.
3. **The CI gates pin exact literals.** `check-architecture-docs.mjs` and `check-pre-next-leg-readiness.mjs` assert exact phrases across README, source-of-truth-map, vocabulary, and envelope schemas. **Every rename must update the checker literals in the same change**, or the gate turns red and (worse) a later editor "fixes" the doc back to the stale literal. Add the new-name literal, keep the old literal only in the alias table.
4. **Edit-first order per the pack's own rule:** owner doc → canonical-enums/vocabulary alias entries → digests (current-canon-defaults) → maps (source-of-truth-map) → README/start-here → checker literals. Never digest-first.

#### Alias strategy (uniform for every rename)
Every rename lands as a five-part unit, reusing machinery the pack already has:

1. **Owner edit**: new canonical name in the owning doc; definition unchanged unless the rename is semantic (then it is an explicit amendment).
2. **Alias entry**: old → new recorded in the appropriate existing table — `HypervisorSurfaceAlias` for surfaces, the deprecated-alias table in canonical-enums for enum members (the risk-class precedent: "mapped on sight"), the compatibility-adapter pattern in common-objects for wire keys/schemes (the `harness-profile://` and `TrainingEvidenceEligibility` precedents), typed fail-closed route aliases for routes.
3. **Read-side-only aliasing**: old names are accepted and normalized on read; **no new object, receipt, or schema is ever minted under an old name** after the rename lands. Durable identifiers already admitted (e.g. built cloud-plane receipts) are never rewritten — they resolve through the alias table forever.
4. **Sweep + lint**: grep-driven cross-link sweep, then add the old name to the banned-phrase list in the docs gate so it cannot re-accrete (the estate already runs banned-phrase audits; the mangled "SDK/later stable client protocol rows" incident shows why sed-without-lint fails).
5. **Retire the alias** only when the checker shows zero non-alias-table occurrences and (for wire names) no live consumer — alpha no-shim policy applies to code, but doc aliases are cheap and may persist in the alias tables indefinitely.

#### Phasing

**Phase 0 — land the law before the names (1 PR).**
Add the "Naming Rules" section to vocabulary.md (suffix law, casing law, reservation rules, acronym policy, external-collision register) and wire the four lints (vocab↔enums parity, duplicate-term, scheme grammar, banned aliases) into `check:architecture-docs`. Every subsequent rename PR then has a gate to conform to. Include the mechanical parity fixes that need no debate: B3 (AssuranceStage), RiskCoverageState promotion, ManagedExecutionMode casing, risk-class literal normalization.

**Phase 1 — wire-freeze-racing renames (before the first WorkResult/OutcomeDelta code PR and the admission planes).**
A5 (GoalConductor/GoalPursuitLoop), A7 (AgentRun / worker-instances / worker-instance:// + Agent/Worker scoping), A8 (SpecialistWorkerCategory), B2 (scheme grammar + alias table), B4 (receipt_body_hash), B10 (model_selection_mode), Tier C field renames (state→status, planning_required, mutation_allowed, rejoin_and_readmission, mow:, connector-mapping://, authority-client://), outcome_class exploit_found removal. These are cheap now and become deprecated-alias migrations the moment the seam ships.

**Phase 2 — credibility/public-brand renames (before whitepaper v-next and any public product copy).**
A1 (cTEE→SCE), A2 (Type-postures), A3 (Capability Foundry), A4 (IOI Settlement Layer), A9 (claim-class enums), A10 (StandardAutonomousSystemProfile), B1 (*Intelligence namespaces), B6 (UsageCredit), B12 (governed autonomous-system chain scoping), B13 (machine-authority category). Each is one coordinated pass touching its invariant; the ioi-ai public-copy plane (canonicalMessaging.js single-owner + banned-phrase audit) is the enforcement point on the public side — extend its banned list with the retired names as each pass lands.

**Phase 3 — coherence sweeps and dedup (any time, low risk).**
A6 authority-chain vocabulary convergence, B5 (CertificationAttestation), B7 (JudgmentContract), B8 (Connections — as an explicit taxonomy amendment), B11 (Reference Harness Profile), B14 (party_system_id), remaining Tier C (HHAI, UsageSettlement, INV-19, Foundry scorecard verdict, Work Ledger stragglers, phantom link labels, IOICli dedup, FQF/CSPS retirement, DigitalWorkerOntology→WorkerOntology, GoalSpaceSubscription).

#### Cross-link safety
The canon's cross-links survive because (a) file renames (A4, B13) leave a redirect-stub at the old path per the entry-point-shim convention — and the doc-classes register should gain the `redirect-stub` class meta-navigation proposed, so these stubs are governed; (b) every object rename keeps its old name greppable in exactly one place (the alias table), so inbound prose that was missed resolves through the table rather than dangling; (c) the link-integrity check already in CI catches path breaks, and the new label-equals-target rule (Tier C) catches the phantom-label class. Renames that change URI schemes never break refs in *admitted* data because normalization is read-side.

#### What NOT to do
- Do not rename GoalRun, Agentgres, Worker, AIIP, Hypervisor, the physical Mission contracts, or bounded-DAS-in-foundations (Section 3) — churn without commensurate value, several against explicit invariants.
- Do not batch multiple invariant-bearing renames into one PR; each NN/INV rewording needs its own reviewable diff.
- Do not run sed passes without the Phase-0 lints in place — the master guide's 17 mangled "SDK/later stable client protocol rows" fragments are the standing warning.
- Do not let any alias appear in newly minted receipts, schemas, or registry names; aliases are read-side compatibility only.

---


## Part III — Per-Facet Deep Review

Fifteen groups, thirty facets. Every facet: current shape, strengths, SOTA verdict with named anchors, gaps, then severity-ordered findings with `path:line` evidence and a concrete recommendation.


## Part III.1 — Meta layer: the doc estate itself

### Doc estate: navigation, entry points, ownership model, doc classes

**Files reviewed:** `README.md (581)` · `START_HERE.md (41)` · `_meta/start-here.md (510)` · `_meta/doc-classes.md (101)` · `_meta/source-of-truth-map.md (430)` · `_meta/canon-readability-audit.md (142)` · `_meta/wallet-protocol-sdk-packaging-plan.md (12)`

**Current shape.** The pack's front door is two-stage: a 41-line START_HERE.md shim at docs/architecture/ root redirects to _meta/start-here.md, a 510-line canonical orientation owning the five-minute mental model, product shape, effect boundary, a route-by-problem table, four role-based reading paths, and an anti-pattern catalog. README.md declares itself the "canonical navigation and source-of-authority index" but also carries the canonical stack, the defining decisions, 37 numbered Non-Negotiables (1-36 with a duplicated 30), and implementation discipline. _meta/source-of-truth-map.md implements edit-first single ownership: a ~78-row subject-to-owner table, a "Validation anchors" digest, conflict rules (docs/architecture beats plans/evidence; between two architecture files "prefer the newer aligned direction"), and per-subject edit rules. _meta/doc-classes.md names twelve document classes, mandates a two-axis status header (Doctrine status x Implementation status, plus audit date and optional code refs), and sets placement rules banning implementation-plan/evidence classes from docs/architecture with an _archive exception lane. _meta/canon-readability-audit.md is a self-audit workplan with per-doc gap rows, a standard owner-doc shape, a terminology watchlist, and an acceptance checklist; wallet-protocol-sdk-packaging-plan.md is a 12-line tombstone stub pointing at _archive. A CI script (npm run check:architecture-docs) mechanically enforces a Status: line near the top of every file, link integrity, a root markdown allowlist (README.md, START_HERE.md), generated-artifact placement, that internal-docs never declare canonical status, stale-terminology bans (legacy cap:* vocabulary), and — beyond what the estate's own meta docs describe — hundreds of exact content literals pinned across README, the ownership map, vocabulary, and owner-doc envelope schemas. Adjacent to this facet, foundations/invariants.md is a stable-ID invariant registry (INV-*) with its own explicit precedence rule ("this file wins") that other docs cite by ID.

**What it already gets right:**

- Edit-first single-owner subject mapping with explicit conflict rules is genuinely ahead of most industry doc estates — it is CODEOWNERS-for-prose, and the 'edit the owner first, digests never override owners' discipline directly attacks split-brain drift.
- The two-axis status header (Doctrine status vs Implementation status) cleanly separates 'is this authoritative' from 'is this built' — the honesty problem that sinks most aspirational architecture canons — and the rule 'never demote doctrine because implementation is future' (doc-classes.md:56) is crisp and correct.
- Real docs-as-code enforcement exists: check-architecture-docs.mjs validates status-block presence, link integrity, root-file placement, banned generated artifacts, blocks internal-docs from claiming canonical status, bans stale capability vocabulary, and pins exact envelope-field and enum-value literals across owner docs as doctrine regression checks — most orgs have none of this.
- The pack audits itself: canon-readability-audit.md tracks per-doc enterability gaps, a standard owner-doc shape, and a terminology watchlist; route-by-problem and role-based reading paths in start-here.md are deliberate reader-path design.
- Anti-pattern catalogs ('Most Common Boundary Mistakes', reject/use-instead pairs in start-here.md:442-496) are an unusually effective onboarding device that Diátaxis-style estates rarely include.

**SOTA verdict: `near-sota`** — compared against: Diátaxis framework (tutorial/how-to/reference/explanation separation); arc42 + C4 model (standard sections, visual zoom levels); ADR practice (Nygard) and IETF RFC obsoletes/updates chains; Rust RFC process (stable numbered normative artifacts); docs-as-code with Backstage TechDocs (machine-parseable front matter, generated indexes, CI linting); Google design-doc culture (review checkpoints, freshness ownership); Stripe/AWS docs IA (progressive disclosure, task-first entry)

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No inter-class authority precedence or RFC-style supersession chain: within-architecture conflict resolution is a recency-plus-alignment judgment ('prefer the newer aligned direction', with current-canon-defaults.md as the alignment referent) rather than mechanical precedence, and the existing Supersedes/Superseded-by header fields are not wired into it — IETF/ADR practice uses explicit obsoletes/updates links.
- Status headers are freeform prose, not schema-validated front matter; CI checks only that a legacy 'Status:' line exists, not the two-axis fields doc-classes mandates — Backstage TechDocs-style estates lint metadata against a schema.
- No Diátaxis tutorial/how-to lane: the estate is entirely reference+explanation; a new engineer gets no runnable first task.
- No rendered visual model at any zoom level (C4 context/container, arc42 building-block views); no image, mermaid, or PlantUML source exists anywhere under docs/architecture — diagrams exist only as ASCII text blocks.
- Hand-maintained duplicate indexes (ownership map cells restate owner docs' 'Owns' lists) where SOTA generates indexes from owner-doc metadata.
- README's Non-Negotiables lack stable requirement IDs and RFC 2119-style keyword discipline — the numbered list has already broken (two items numbered 30) — even though the estate already proves the pattern elsewhere: foundations/invariants.md assigns stable INV-* IDs that other docs cite.

**Findings (14, severity-ordered):**

1. **Recency-based conflict rule is a drift engine** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: _meta/source-of-truth-map.md:99 — 'If two architecture files disagree, prefer the newer aligned direction.'
   - Recommendation: Replace recency with mechanical precedence: (1) the map-designated owner doc wins for its subject, always; (2) if the disagreement is inside one subject, the file whose header carries an explicit 'Supersedes' pointer to the other wins; (3) anything else requires an ADR before either reading is treated as canon. The rule does name a referent for 'aligned' — the next sentence points at current-canon-defaults.md — but that digest explicitly 'does not override owner docs' (map:111), so it cannot settle an owner-vs-owner conflict without inverting the pack's own physics, and 'newer' rests on hand-maintained 'Last alignment pass' dates that are not comparable across files (README 2026-07-15 vs doc-classes 2026-06-23). The pack already proves the mechanical pattern works: foundations/invariants.md declares 'if a restatement elsewhere conflicts with this file, this file wins' for INV-* wording, and every canon header already carries Supersedes/Superseded-by fields — wire the general conflict rule to the same machinery.
2. **README (navigation-index class) owns 37 doctrine Non-Negotiables, with broken numbering** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: README.md:4 — 'Canonical owner: this file for architecture navigation'; README.md:530 and 533 — both numbered '30.' ('Product surfaces monetize…' / 'UI boards, chat…')
   - Recommendation: The Non-Negotiables, Canonical Stack, and 'Decisions That Define The Target' are doctrine living inside a file whose own header claims only navigation ownership — a class violation by the pack's own doc-classes taxonomy (canonical-index = 'Navigation and ownership index'), and the ownership map has no subject row assigning the Non-Negotiables an owner at all. The same doctrine themes are restated (not literally copied) in start-here.md and current-canon-defaults.md, so three files carry overlapping normative prose with no designated winner. Move Non-Negotiables to foundations/invariants.md (which already maintains stable cite-by-ID INV-* entries) or a dedicated owner with stable IDs (NN-01…NN-37) so other docs can cite them safely; the duplicate '30.' proves bare ordinals are already unciteable — and because markdown renderers renumber ordered lists, every rendered item after the duplicate silently disagrees with its source literal. Shrink README to purpose, stack diagram pointer, and the ownership tables — noting that check-architecture-docs.mjs pins several exact literals in README (taxonomy lines and phrases from Non-Negotiable 35), so the checker must be updated in the same change.
3. **Canonical front door is buried in _meta/ and the entry-point ownership is split** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: _meta/source-of-truth-map.md:110 — 'First-read architecture path | start-here.md, ../START_HERE.md'; START_HERE.md:3 — 'Status: entry-point shim.'
   - Recommendation: Invert the shim: move the canonical orientation content into top-level START_HERE.md (already on the CI root allowlist) and make _meta/start-here.md the redirect or delete it after updating inbound links. A _meta/ directory signals 'about the docs', not 'the front door' — new engineers who open the repo see a 41-line redirect as their entry experience. The map row does explain itself ('top-level shim avoids broken references'), and the shim's own header names exactly one canonical owner (_meta/start-here.md), so ownership is resolvable — but the map's Canonical Owner column still lists both files on its very first row, inconsistent with both the shim's header and the map's own single-owner discipline. Fix the row to name one owner either way.
4. **Master guides 'do not own doctrine' yet a _meta master guide is a canonical subject owner for durable contracts** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: _meta/doc-classes.md:26 — 'Internal master guides are private execution scaffolding and do not own doctrine.' vs _meta/source-of-truth-map.md:121 — master guide owns 'migration, Step/Module ABI, route-family owner map, Rust core extraction target'
   - Recommendation: Step/Module ABI and route-family ownership are durable contracts, not sequencing. doc-classes.md:78 does carve an exception ('remain in place as live migration owners'), and the guide's own header declares 'Doctrine status: canonical' with 'Primary owner candidate: architecture meta until promoted into component canon' — i.e., the promotion path is acknowledged but unrealized, while the map's own Purpose section separately demands that any durable decision a master guide produces 'must be distilled into the canonical owner below or into an accepted ADR'. Either define an explicit `migration-plan` class in doc-classes.md with bounded authority (sequencing and cleanup conditions only; never object contracts) and move ABI/route-family contract ownership into components/daemon-runtime owners, or promote the guide to canonical-doctrine honestly. The current shape leaves durable-contract ownership set to evaporate when the migration guide retires.
5. **Ownership-map cells are hand-maintained object registries duplicating owner docs** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: _meta/source-of-truth-map.md:150 — one Subject cell enumerates 'HypervisorApplicationSurfaceRegistration, HypervisorSurfaceReleaseRecord, HypervisorSurfaceInstallationBinding, HypervisorSystemInterfaceBinding…' (~70 object names)
   - Recommendation: Rows at lines 150 and 163 are 400+ word table cells listing every object an owner doc defines — a second copy of each owner's 'Owns' section that must be manually synced (guaranteed drift) and is undiffable and unreadable. Instead: each owner doc carries structured front matter (owns: [subject slugs, object names]); check-architecture-docs.mjs generates the subject→owner index and fails on duplicate ownership claims. The map file keeps only conflict rules and hand-written edit rules. Note the checker currently requires specific object names to appear in the map verbatim (e.g., HypervisorRouteAliasRegistration), so those assertions move to the generated index in the same change. This is standard Backstage-TechDocs/CODEOWNERS mechanics and directly serves the pack's own single-owner physics.
6. **North-star proof duplicated with divergent normative wording across the entry docs** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: README.md:447 — 'with portable permitted state. The proof must not require participants to share' vs _meta/start-here.md:436 — 'portable policy-filtered participant-state bundle. Passing the test cannot'; a third formulation at _meta/current-canon-defaults.md:104-111 — 'exiting with a portable `ParticipantStateBundle`… must not require one runtime, operational database, administrator, or continued IOI-host trust'
   - Recommendation: The single most important acceptance test in the architecture exists three times in three normative phrasings ('portable permitted state' vs 'portable policy-filtered participant-state bundle' vs the typed `ParticipantStateBundle`; 'continued trust/access' vs 'continued access' vs 'continued IOI-host trust'), and the test statement itself has no owner row in the source-of-truth map. The underlying exit contract is partially owned — map row 147 assigns `ParticipantStateBundle` and 'exit preserves permitted state without continued host dependence' to collaborative-outcome-pattern.md — and the defaults copy is a declared non-overriding digest, but neither README nor start-here says which paraphrase is binding. Give the north-star test one owner (governed-autonomous-systems.md, aiip.md, or collaborative-outcome-pattern.md fits the map's physics), a stable anchor, and have README, start-here, and the defaults digest link to it with at most a one-sentence paraphrase. As written, a builder cannot tell which exit-bundle formulation is the contract.
7. **README assigns 'authority order' to doc-classes.md, which defines none** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: README.md:360 — 'doc-classes.md | document classes and authority order' vs _meta/doc-classes.md:14 — 'This register names the document classes used to keep architecture doctrine…'
   - Recommendation: doc-classes.md contains class names, the status axis, and placement rules — no precedence among classes; actual conflict precedence lives in source-of-truth-map.md:94-104. Either add an explicit authority ladder to doc-classes.md (canonical-doctrine > canonical-reference > canonical-digest > canonical-index > everything else; conformance-contracts bind implementations, not doctrine) and have the map's conflict rule reference it, or correct README's ownership claim. Today a reader hunting for 'which class wins' is routed to a file that cannot answer.
8. **Doctrine digest has regrown inside the ownership map after the audit declared it split out** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: _meta/canon-readability-audit.md:32 — 'Split defaults into current-canon-defaults.md and kept source-of-truth-map.md focused on ownership' vs _meta/source-of-truth-map.md:34 — 'Validation anchors:'
   - Recommendation: The 'Validation anchors' section (source-of-truth-map.md:34-92, ~58 lines) restates cross-owner doctrine — GoalRun/OutcomeRoom boundaries, receipt semantics, INV-30/31 — which is exactly the digest content the audit says was extracted to current-canon-defaults.md, and the map's own conflict rule 2 says 'Keep this map focused on edit-first ownership.' One complication: check-architecture-docs.mjs literally requires several anchor phrases to exist in this map (e.g., 'IOI Authority Gateway is the daemon sidecar/compatibility profile', 'Smarter-agent runtime loop'), so the section is partly CI-load-bearing — move the anchors to current-canon-defaults.md or the relevant owners and repoint the checker's phrase requirements in the same change. Without a hard 'no doctrine prose in the map' rule, the map will keep re-accreting a competing digest.
9. **Status headers are unlintable prose; CI enforces only the legacy freeform field** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: _meta/doc-classes.md:35 — 'Every canon file carries two orthogonal status fields in its front matter'
   - Recommendation: The convention now stacks three status-ish fields (freeform 'Status:', 'Doctrine status:', 'Implementation status:') plus two hand-edited dates, and scripts/check-architecture-docs.mjs validates only that /^Status:/ exists in the first 12 lines (line 104) — the mandated two-axis fields are entirely unenforced, so they will rot. Convert headers to YAML front matter with a schema (doctrine_status enum, implementation_status enum, implementation_refs required when built/partial, supersedes/superseded_by as paths), extend the checker to validate it, retire the redundant freeform 'Status:' line, and derive alignment dates from git history rather than hand edits.
10. **Map link labels are phantom legacy filenames that don't exist on disk** — `medium` · Rename
   - Evidence: _meta/source-of-truth-map.md:114 — '[`model-router-byok-run-to-idle.md`](../components/model-router/doctrine.md)'
   - Recommendation: Dozens of map rows label links with retired basenames — `agentgres-state-substrate.md`, `wallet-network-authority-layer.md`, `ioi-cli-daemon-runtime.md`, `aiagent-xyz-worker-marketplace.md` — that exist nowhere under docs/architecture, not even in _archive/, while the targets are actually doctrine.md files. A new engineer who greps for the displayed filename finds nothing; README.md:400 already rules that 'the owner path, not the basename, carries the namespace'. The existing CI link check validates targets only, never label-target consistency. Rename all link labels to the real path form (`components/model-router/doctrine.md`) and add a checker rule: markdown link text ending in .md must equal the target basename or the repo-relative path.
11. **No tutorial lane and no rendered diagrams: onboarding is 1,100 lines of noun-dense prose** — `medium` · SOTA gap *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: _meta/canon-readability-audit.md:15 — 'new implementers can still encounter the system as scattered doctrine instead of a guided build map'
   - Recommendation: Against Diátaxis and Stripe/AWS IA, the estate has no tutorial/how-to content and no rendered visual model (diagrams exist only as ASCII text blocks; no image or diagram source exists anywhere under docs/architecture): README (581 lines) plus start-here (510 lines) introduce hundreds of proper nouns before a reader can do anything. Add (1) one 'first hour' tutorial keyed strictly to implementation-matrix built rows — start the daemon, admit one GoalRun, inspect its receipt and Agentgres head — which also stress-tests the built/partial honesty axis; note doc-classes placement rules would need a home for it (a new how-to class, or an explicit exception), since it fits none of the twelve current classes; (2) one C4 context + container diagram pair owned by README (rendered SVG committed, source in-tree). The audit's own top finding admits this gap; its fix list stops at prose reorganization.
12. **Readability audit is stale, self-graded, and its acceptance bar is unfalsifiable** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: _meta/canon-readability-audit.md:131 — 'A new reader can explain the core stack in under five minutes.'
   - Recommendation: The audit table covers 23 docs while the ownership map spans ~78 subject rows, with nothing for embodied-runtime, institutional-learning-boundary, governed-autonomous-systems, or core-clients-surfaces — the last being the map's single largest owner; its last alignment pass (2026-07-04) trails the map and README (2026-07-15); 'Current strength' is graded by the docs' own authors; and the acceptance checklist has no test procedure. Make it operational: a coverage rule (every canonical owner appears in the table or is explicitly out-of-scope, checker-enforced), and replace the checklist with timed fresh-reader tasks with pass/fail evidence (e.g., 'given start-here only, locate the owner of receipt schemas in under 3 minutes'). Otherwise the audit certifies readability nobody has measured.
13. **Tombstone stubs are an undeclared doc class and still cited as live reference** — `low` · Refactor
   - Evidence: _meta/wallet-protocol-sdk-packaging-plan.md:3 — 'Status: entry-point stub; the plan moved out of canon.' cited at _meta/source-of-truth-map.md:171 as low-level reference
   - Recommendation: The estate uses ad hoc statuses ('entry-point shim', 'entry-point stub') that appear nowhere in doc-classes.md's register, and the wallet stub — whose content is one paragraph saying 'moved to _archive' with Doctrine status: archived — is still listed as a Low-Level Reference for the wallet subject row. Add a `redirect-stub` class to doc-classes.md with rules (max N lines, must name the target, must never appear as an owner or reference in the map), then repoint map row 171 to the archive path or the wallet doctrine owner and delete stubs once inbound links are clean.
14. **Reader paths omit the security/authority reviewer and operator roles** — `low` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: _meta/start-here.md:392 — '### Runtime implementer' (paths exist only for runtime, product, semantic/interop, and economics implementers)
   - Recommendation: For a pack whose core physics is fail-closed authority, receipts, and custody, there is no ordered reading path for a security reviewer or a node operator — the two audiences most likely to be handed this pack cold. Add two paths: Security/authority reviewer (invariants.md → security-privacy-policy-invariants.md → wallet-network/doctrine.md → improvement-governance-gates.md → conformance contracts) and Operator (hypervisoros.md → providers-and-environments.md → runtime-nodes-tee-depin.md → events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md). Google-style design-doc culture and AWS Well-Architected both treat the reviewer path as first-class.

### Status/planning layer: canon defaults, horizons, implementation matrix, code delta

**Files reviewed:** `_meta/current-canon-defaults.md (1086)` · `_meta/execution-horizons.md (504)` · `_meta/implementation-matrix.md (494)` · `_meta/canon-to-code-delta.md (123)` · `_meta/refactor-baseline.md (777)` · `_meta/hypervisor-kernel-substrate-migration-matrix.md (589)`

**Current shape.** This layer is the pack's self-tracking machinery for the gap between target canon and shipped code. `current-canon-defaults.md` is a 1086-line hand-synchronized bullet digest of live cross-owner doctrine defaults, subordinate to owner docs named in the source-of-truth map. `execution-horizons.md` frames maturity bands (Horizon 0 contract convergence through Horizon 6 public economic commitments), a product-taxonomy migration gate, and a 14-step contract-first build sequence whose rule is "closure is the working proof," not PR numbers. `implementation-matrix.md` (~368 concept rows, ~570KB) maps each concept to canonical owner, current durable form, promote/keep guidance, code anchors, and conformance hooks — and also carries dozens of JS→Rust migration-history rows. `canon-to-code-delta.md` is the newer object-level delta: 37 table rows covering the canonical object families with an honest implementation state ("a precedent is never a partial instance"), the next daemon/Agentgres/wallet authority crossing, a named conformance proof, and the owning UI projection, plus a deferred application-UX backlog that resumes only when an implemented contract pulls it. `hypervisor-kernel-substrate-migration-matrix.md` tracks the JS-daemon retirement per route family with a macro authority-cut ledger, terminal blockers, and a do-not-recreate list; `refactor-baseline.md` is a self-declared temporary before/after census of the 2026-07-05 docs refactor. Enforcement is real but literal: `check-architecture-docs.mjs` and `check-pre-next-leg-readiness.mjs` assert required/banned phrases in these files and in code, and every canon file carries a Doctrine status / Implementation status / Last-audit header.

**What it already gets right:**

- The honesty discipline is genuinely rare and better than most industry roadmap practice: canon-to-code-delta defines `not started` so that a named adjacent substrate is explicitly NOT partial credit ("a precedent is never a partial instance"), and `partial` requires a persisted slice of the object itself. Vendor roadmaps and even many KEPs inflate; this refuses.
- The delta table's "Next authority crossing" column plus per-row conformance proofs (including named negative tests like "candidate cannot control evaluator" and "revocation releases claims") is a sharper definition-of-done than typical KEP graduation criteria — it names the exact boundary that turns a planned object into governed truth.
- Uniform per-file status axis (Doctrine status / Implementation status / Last implementation audit) across all 77 canon files, with CI coverage gates — comparable in spirit to OpenTelemetry's document status model and applied more uniformly than most specs.
- The Build Rule promotion ladder (event → receipt → Agentgres operation → projection → artifact ref → canonical object) is a crisp, reusable decision procedure that actively prevents object-class inflation.
- Contract-first pull sequencing ("surface work resumes only when pulled by an implemented contract"; "closure is the working proof") correctly inverts the demo-driven default; retiring the ranked surface queue in favor of contract pull is a real architectural decision, not roadmap theater.

**SOTA verdict: `behind`** — compared against: Kubernetes feature-gate tables (generated from Go source annotations) and the KEP process (machine-readable kep.yaml, per-stage graduation criteria, Production Readiness Review, tracking issues bound to milestones); OpenTelemetry specification status model and per-language spec-compliance matrices maintained as structured data rendered to docs; TCK/conformance-suite practice (Java TCK, CNCF conformance): runnable assertion registries with stable IDs, not prose hook names; Standards-body implementation reports (W3C/IETF) built from structured per-feature implementation submissions rather than hand-edited tables; Internal platform catalogs (Backstage-style) where component status/ownership is registry data with staleness policies

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No machine-readable source of truth: every status lives in freeform Markdown table cells; the CI gates (check-architecture-docs.mjs) verify phrase PRESENCE, not that a row's state matches code — a row can rot green (the layer's own `npm run check:artifact-availability-incident` hook passes today by asserting doc phrases while the row's code anchor is deleted).
- No per-row audit timestamp or commit binding: one file-global 'Last implementation audit' covers ~368 rows audited at different times, some citing code deleted weeks earlier; K8s/OTel bind status to releases/commits.
- No automated anchor verification: nothing fails CI when a 'Code anchor when known' path no longer exists (178 references to the deleted packages/runtime-daemon survive under a blanket disclaimer).
- No closed, enforced maturity enum at row level: rows use ad hoc states ('partial precursor', 'not started (adjacent HA substrate built)') that no linter can validate against doc-classes.md's built/partial/mixed/planned/speculative vocabulary (which is enforced only per-file).
- No generation/single-sourcing: defaults digest, implementation matrix, code delta, horizons gate, and migration matrix restate the same facts in 3–5 hand-synced copies, where SOTA renders all views from one registry.

**Findings (14, severity-ordered):**

1. **The flagship status artifact already rotted: 178 code anchors cite a deleted package, papered over by a blanket disclaimer** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: _meta/implementation-matrix.md:36 — "Rows whose 'Code anchor when known' cells cite `packages/runtime-daemon/**` … reference the RETIRED implementation"
   - Recommendation: This is the facet's core question answered in the negative: drift is institutionalized (though disclosed), not controlled. Rows like `ArtifactAvailabilityIncident` (line 421) still present `packages/runtime-daemon/src/runtime-artifact-availability-incident.mjs` as the live anchor, distinguishable only by a header notice ~385 lines away — and its conformance hook `npm run check:artifact-availability-incident` still runs green because it asserts only doc phrases, never code, so the row rots while its gate passes. Do the row-level cleanup the notice defers: move every `Runtime*Retired`/`Runtime*ProtocolClients` migration-history row to `_archive/change-ledgers/`, re-anchor surviving concept rows to `crates/**`, and add a CI check (extend check-architecture-docs.mjs, which already walks the tree and resolves links) that fails on any anchor path absent from the working tree outside `_archive/`.

   - Recommendation: The update protocol is manual double-entry with no verifier. Create `_meta/status-registry.yaml`: one record per canonical object with `owner_docs[]`, `state` (closed enum from doc-classes.md), `qualifier` (free text), `code_anchors[]`, `conformance_hooks[]` (typed IDs), `verified_against` (commit), `audited_at`. Render the delta table and the matrix's status columns from it at build time; extend check-architecture-docs.mjs from phrase-presence linting to (a) anchor-path existence, (b) enum validity, (c) staleness budget on `audited_at`. This is exactly the K8s feature-gate / OTel compliance-matrix move and breaks no pack physics — docs remain canon; the registry is their machine form.

Two concrete fixes: (a) give every `###` section an explicit `Owners:` line listing its source docs, enabling a link-coverage check; (b) for the highest-drift claims (defaults that gates depend on), move the sentence into the owner doc as a marked `<!-- digest -->` block and generate the digest section from those blocks, the same single-owner-plus-render pattern the ioi.ai public copy already uses (canonicalMessaging.js).
10. **Conformance hooks are untyped strings — runnable scripts, invariant IDs, and 'planned' prose are indistinguishable and unresolvable** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: _meta/implementation-matrix.md:405 — "Agentgres runtime object | canonical object for serious runs | runtime thread/run records | HP-1, HP-7"
   - Recommendation: The Conformance-hook column mixes npm scripts (`npm run check:wallet-protocol`), bare invariant tags (HP-1..HP-10, CEC — defined in `default-harness-profile.md` but not resolvable from the cell), conformance-tier names, and planned-test prose; canon-to-code-delta's proof column likewise mixes real verifier scripts with "planned:" items. Nothing verifies a hook resolves — and even a hook that resolves and passes may verify only doc phrases (see `check-artifact-availability-incident.mjs`, which asserts strings in four Markdown files). TCK/K8s practice gives every assertion a stable ID resolvable to a runnable job. Introduce typed hook syntax — `run:<npm-script>`, `inv:<INV-nn|HP-n>`, `conf:<docs/conformance path#anchor>`, `planned:<owner doc#anchor>` — plus a registry check that every `run:` script exists in package.json and every `inv:`/`conf:` target resolves.
11. **Staleness metadata is file-global, so one header date launders rows of wildly different ages** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: _meta/implementation-matrix.md:10 — "Last implementation audit: 2026-07-12 (anchor-staleness audit — see the notice below)"
   - Recommendation: A 2026-07-12 header date sits atop rows whose anchors died 2026-06-23 and rows freshly audited that day; canon-to-code-delta has the same single date over 37 rows; there is no maximum-staleness rule anywhere in the layer. Because the files' whole value proposition is trustworthy state, undated rows convert honest-when-written claims into silently stale ones. Add `audited: YYYY-MM-DD` per row (trivial in the registry form proposed above), and a CI staleness budget: rows with `state != not started` older than N days fail the docs gate until re-audited. K8s ties feature status to a release; this is the repo-native equivalent.
12. **Implementation states are an open vocabulary no linter can validate** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: _meta/canon-to-code-delta.md:55 — "partial precursor; not yet canonical room-result admission"
   - Recommendation: doc-classes.md defines built/partial/mixed/planned/speculative (enforced only at file level) and the matrix header defines an object-kind taxonomy (canonical object / operation / event / receipt / projection / profile / artifact ref), but actual row cells use ad hoc compounds — "partial precursor", "not started (adjacent HA substrate built)", "not started/rename planned", "target canonical; implementation transitional". Ad hoc states defeat any mechanical rollup (you cannot count what fraction of the canon is built) and blur the pack's most valuable property, state honesty. Enforce `state ∈ {not_started, precursor, partial, built, retired}` plus a free-text `qualifier`, and validate in check-architecture-docs.mjs. K8s tolerates exactly alpha/beta/GA for the same reason.
13. **Horizon 0 lists the embodied contract freeze, but the pull-ordered build sequence defers embodied contracts to step 11** — `medium` · Contradiction *(confidence: medium)*

   - Recommendation: H0's bullet list makes the embodied contract freeze (manifest, profiles, strata, stream contracts, adapters, supervisor, activation transaction, space-time lease) part of "make the architecture executable," yet the build sequence — "the ORDER work is pulled in" (line 408) — first pulls embodied contracts inside step 11, after two-node continuity. The doc partially defuses this: horizons "frame maturity" while the sequence owns ordering, and the H0 bullet itself disclaims implementation ("This contract work does not make the compiler … implemented"). But H0 completion remains ill-defined: either it includes ~30 embodied objects nothing pulls until step 11, or the bullet is dead text. Move the bullet out of H0 with an explicit "pulled by step 11" annotation, or add embodied contract-freeze as its own numbered step. Generally, annotate every H0 bullet with its owning sequence step so H0 stops being an unordered superset.

Per the pack's own `_archive/` convention (which this file itself references at line 773), move it to `_archive/change-ledgers/refactor-baseline-2026-07-05.md`, keeping only the ten-line After-snapshot summary if any live doc still links it. ### Kernel/substrate unification master guide (5k-line program doc)

**Files reviewed:** `_meta/hypervisor-kernel-substrate-unification-master-guide.md (5006)`

**Current shape.** The file is a 5,006-line "implementation master guide" in _meta that defines how IOI resolves the historical split brain between the product daemon (formerly Node/JS, deleted 2026-06-23) and the Rust/WASM kernel/workload substrate. It opens with a one-page doctrine and ownership tables (daemon owns admission/enforcement/execution; Workflow Compositor owns graph shape; HarnessProfiles resolve scoped steps; StepModuleRouter bridges to backends; Agentgres records admitted truth; wallet.network authorizes), then defines the canonical StepModuleInvocation/StepModuleResult ABI, receipt/state-root unification rules, compositor doctrine, and the Improvement Proposal Plane. Part VII lays out a twelve-phase migration program (Phases 0-11: inventory → ABI → bridge → shadowed tool → receipts → compositor → cTEE → packages → meta-improvement → Rust core extraction → JS facade retirement → full conformance) with a hypervisor-conformance command contract, an operating discipline (macro-cut bias, clean-cut commits, alpha no-shim policy), and a per-route-family owner/deletion table. Roughly 3,000 lines of Part I are a per-slice change ledger (~246 "Slice N" paragraphs covering Slices ~731-1440) narrating individual JS-facade retirements, even though a closing note claims that ledger was archived out of the file into _archive/change-ledgers/ (the archive slice ledger actually holds only Slices 1035-1440). The guide declares itself canonical doctrine while its header says it is only an "owner candidate until promoted into component canon," and the source-of-truth map assigns it sole ownership of the Step/Module ABI, route-family owner map, Rust-core target, and terminal conformance contract. Completion is defined as execution, not publication: the guide is "done" only when the split brain no longer exists and the full conformance suite passes.

**What it already gets right:**

- Ownership is specified with unusual crispness: the target Rust-core module layout carries explicit 'Owns / Must not own' columns per module (authority, step_router, receipt_binder, agentgres_admission...), which is sharper boundary discipline than most industry design docs achieve.
- The migration mechanics are textbook-plus strangler fig: projection→shadow→gated→live promotion per step, per-route-family deletion/demotion conditions, and a negative-conformance suite (direct JS mutation fails, accepted-receipt append outside the Rust core fails, plaintext cTEE mount fails) that functions as architectural fitness functions.
- Part VIII names its own failure modes honestly, including doc-level ones ('Treating this master guide as a planning artifact that must be followed by a separate implementation migration'), and the anti-rewrite doctrine ('Port by extraction, not by rewrite') is correct and rare discipline.
- The alpha compatibility policy (delete shims once the canonical path is verified; no legacy route aliases) prevents the shim-accretion failure that kills most strangler migrations.
- The Step/Module ABI itself is a genuinely strong unification object: one invocation envelope spanning daemon tools, WASM modules, workload jobs, model mounts, cTEE actions, verifiers, and AIIP exits, with custody, authority, and state-root fields bound in.

**SOTA verdict: `behind`** — compared against: Kubernetes Enhancement Proposal (KEP) process (status lifecycle in kep.yaml, design separated from tracking issue); Rust RFC process (immutable merged RFC + separate tracking issue); Fowler strangler-fig migration pattern; Google large-scale-change practice (LSC/Rosie: tool-derived tracking, mechanical commits, dashboards); Stripe API versioning / GitHub GraphQL migration playbooks (frozen versioned stages, changelog separated from reference); Nygard Architecture Decision Records; Progressive delivery practice (shadow/canary promotion); Fitness functions (Building Evolutionary Architectures)

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No spec/tracking separation: KEP and Rust-RFC practice keep the design immutable and track status elsewhere; here design doctrine, sprint lane, resume markers, and ~246 hand-written slice paragraphs mutate in one 5k-line file.
- Tracking is hand-narrated prose rather than tool-derived: Google LSC derives ledgers from commit metadata and dashboards; hand-narration here already produced a provably failed mechanical rename (17 mangled 'SDK/later stable client protocol rows' fragments) and a split slice history (Slices 731-1034 live only in the guide while the closing note claims Slice 1 → 1440 is archived verbatim).
- No phase lifecycle status: completed phases (ABI, bridge, first shadowed tool, receipts) are textually indistinguishable from open ones inside Part VII (only the file header and the migration matrix carry aggregate status); KEPs carry provisional/implementable/implemented markers.
- No superseding-in-place discipline: ADR practice appends small immutable records; this guide rewrites old slice paragraphs with 'later superseded by' prose, degrading the historical record's reliability.
- No doc fitness functions on the guide itself: cited file paths (packages/runtime-daemon/*, domains/aiagent-xyz), internal section references ('Final Doctrine'), and the archival claim are all machine-checkable and all currently false, despite the repo's own conformance-gate culture (the hypervisor-conformance:docs npm script exists but does not catch these).

**Findings (14, severity-ordered):**

The repo already has the gate infrastructure (scripts/conformance/hypervisor-conformance.mjs with a docs tier); canon file references are currently an unchecked link class, and this guide alone contains at least three families of dead references.

### Vocabulary and canonical enums: the naming system itself

**Files reviewed:** `_meta/vocabulary.md (2181)` · `foundations/canonical-enums.md (666)`

**Current shape.** The naming system lives in two owner files. `_meta/vocabulary.md` (2181 lines) first declares a three-layer language policy — Product, Admin/Builder, and Protocol/Architecture vocabularies — with a presentation map that translates protocol names into product copy (wallet.network → "SSO, permissions"; Agentgres → "Provenance"; OutcomeRoom → "Goal Space workstream"), then defines roughly 430 terms in one flat bullet list spanning builder kits (SDK/ADK/ODK), the Hypervisor runtime family (Daemon, OS, Type 1/2/3 substrate modes), the work spine (GoalRunProfile/GoalRun/GoalKernel/OutcomeRoom/ContextCell/HarnessInvocation/WorkResult), the wallet/authority family, the cTEE private-compute pattern, embodied robotics, improvement governance, ontology/data, memory, and audit labels. Entries characteristically state what a term is and then a litany of what it is not, to fence authority/truth scope. `foundations/canonical-enums.md` (666 lines) owns the member sets and spellings of cross-component enums — a twelve-member risk_class ladder (plus a physical_action peer class), privacy classes, capsule privacy modes projected from a nine-venue taxonomy, eleven orthogonal surface-classification axes, outcome/contribution/claim classes, model-route commercial rights, ordering/finality profiles, IOI Network enrollment, settlement modes, and assurance stages — with a deprecated-alias mapping table for risk classes, per-enum implementation grounding (built vs planned, honestly stated), and ownership pointers for enums owned elsewhere. Renames are governed through explicit alias and retirement entries (HypervisorMission retired to typed backers; Workbench → Developer Workspace; Work Ledger → Provenance), and other docs may show enums only as labeled excerpts linking back to the enums file.

**What it already gets right:**

- The three-layer language policy with an explicit protocol→product presentation map (vocabulary.md:20-43) is DDD published-language practice made operational; very few stacks formally separate protocol vocabulary from product copy and admin copy.
- canonical-enums.md is genuinely disciplined schema-registry practice: single owner for member sets, an excerpt-only rule for other docs, a deprecated-alias mapping table for risk classes, and per-enum honesty about code grounding ("this file does not pretend it has already happened", canonical-enums.md:71).
- Axis-purity policing is above industry norm: privacy_class explicitly refuses custody/venue overloads (canonical-enums.md:79-86), and admission/installation/enablement/availability/operational-state are kept as independent surface axes rather than one mushy status enum.
- There is a consistent implicit suffix grammar — Profile = immutable declared posture, Receipt = evidence, Lease = bounded expiring grant, Projection = derived view, Envelope = wire object — that makes most of the ~430 object names predictable once learned.
- Retirement discipline is real rename governance: HypervisorMission is retired with typed resolution to GoalRun/OutcomeRoom/AutomationRun (vocabulary.md:1084-1092), and deprecated risk-class aliases must be "mapped on sight" (canonical-enums.md:55-64).

**SOTA verdict: `near-sota`** — compared against: DDD ubiquitous language / published language (Evans); Kubernetes API naming conventions; Stripe API design guide; Confluent Schema Registry (versioned schemas, compatibility rules, subject strategies); SKOS / ISO 11179 controlled vocabularies and metadata registries; Google AIP naming standards

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No machine-readable registry: SKOS/ISO-11179 give every concept an identifier, labels, kind, and relations, and Confluent versions schemas with compatibility rules; here the glossary is unversioned prose with no lintable form, so guard tests cannot enforce it.
- No stated naming grammar: Kubernetes conventions and Google AIP lead with rules (casing, suffixes, acronym policy, coinage review); vocabulary.md is almost all instances — beyond the behavior-first-names sentence (vocabulary.md:12-13) it carries only scattered per-term reservations (KeyShard, Worker-vs-Agent, Recipe) and no systematic casing, suffix, or acronym law.
- No per-term status or kind metadata — the file header says 'Implementation status: mixed' globally, while SOTA registries carry status on every element.
- ~430 terms in one flat namespace far exceeds ubiquitous-language tractability; DDD would segment by bounded context, and the file already shows the failure mode (duplicate parallel entries for the same client lanes).
- Internal alias management is strong but there is no external-collision register: hypervisor, TEE, kernel, wallet, Foundry, and DAS all collide with entrenched industry meanings and no doc acknowledges or mitigates it.

**Findings (16, severity-ordered):**

1. **Type 1/2/3 hypervisor taxonomy hijacks Popek–Goldberg and invents a 'Type 3'** — `high` · Rename
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/_meta/vocabulary.md:88 — 'Values are `type1_hypervisoros`, `type2_desktop_workstation`, and `type3_autonomy_plane`.'
   - Recommendation: Rename the axis away from the VMM Type-1/Type-2 classification: `substrate_posture: bare_metal_os | workstation_daemon | autonomy_plane`. To be fair, the Type 1 and Type 2 members map coherently onto the literal VMM senses (bare-metal root vs hosted on a normal OS); the invented member is `type3_autonomy_plane` — 'Type 3 hypervisor' has no referent in virtualization literature and will read as not-even-wrong to exactly the infrastructure audience Hypervisor courts, especially since HypervisorOS also manages literal microVMs, making the word half-literal and half-metaphor in one family. Keep 'like a Type-1/Type-2 hypervisor' only as prose analogy, never as enum members. Note the taxonomy is already embedded in README Non-Negotiable #2 ('Hypervisor Type 1, Type 2, and Type 3 are deployment/control postures of one product') and foundations/web4-and-ioi-stack.md, so the rename must explicitly reword NN#2 and the stack doc — substance unchanged (one product, three postures) — rather than silently drift from them. canonical-enums.md has no such enum yet; do it before `HypervisorSubstrateMode` reaches a contract surface.
2. **cTEE puts 'TEE' in the name of a non-TEE, inviting hardware-attestation overclaims** — `high` · Rename
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/_meta/vocabulary.md:531 — 'Cryptographic Trusted Execution Envelope. A portable systems pattern for private agency on untrusted compute. It is not a hardware TEE claim'
   - Recommendation: Rename to drop the TEE substring entirely — e.g., `SealedComputeEnvelope` or `CryptographicCustodyEnvelope` — so buyers and auditors cannot read SGX/TDX/SEV attestation into a software custody pattern. Today one lowercase 'c' carries the entire 'this is NOT that' load (the definition's own disclaimer proves the name needs one), and the enums file must maintain both `tee_enterprise` and `ctee_private_workspace` as sibling venue modes (canonical-enums.md:104-105), maximizing confusion. Rename the privacy mode to `sealed_private_workspace`. Blast-radius correction: cTEE is wider-spread than one enum member plus a few entries — it threads through a dedicated component doc (daemon-runtime/private-workspace-ctee.md), README Non-Negotiable #25 ('not cTEE no-plaintext custody', whose wording the rename must explicitly update), and roughly twenty vocabulary entries (PrivateWorkspaceCtee, CustodyType/CustodyProof, the PlaintextFree* mounts) — but all of it is design-stage prose per the files' own implementation grounding, so the rename is still cheap now and expensive after AIIP peers adopt the wire key.
3. **'Foundry' collides with Palantir Foundry and Azure AI Foundry — with adapted-UI provenance making it worse** — `high` · Rename
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/_meta/vocabulary.md:1942 — 'HypervisorFoundry: the Hypervisor application surface for model catalog, model registry, model routes/mounts, tuning, training, evaluation'
   - Recommendation: Rename to a plain-noun owner application consistent with its siblings (Ontology, Data, Governance, Provenance, Evaluations): 'Models' or 'Training'. 'Foundry' is a common noun, so trademark exposure from the word alone is arguable — but Foundry is Palantir's flagship product mark, this estate's UI was adapted from that reference product (a lineage the enums file itself encodes as `surface_creation_method: adapted`, canonical-enums.md:289-291), and Azure AI Foundry now occupies the identical model-catalog/tuning/eval semantic; the adapted-UI lineage is what converts ordinary name reuse into genuine legal and credibility exposure. Blast-radius correction: beyond the owner-application registration, the vocabulary entry, and the HypervisorApp/README app lists, the name is baked into INV-13 ('Foundry builds; Hypervisor executes; Governance promotes', foundations/invariants.md), so the rename must explicitly reword that invariant (substance unchanged) rather than silently diverge from the invariant registry. Act before public product copy ships.
4. **vocabulary.md dual-owns semantics with envelope/component docs and lacks the excerpt discipline the enums file invented** — `high` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/canonical-enums.md:24 — 'Other docs show the enum only as a labeled excerpt with a link here; adding or renaming a member happens here first.'
   - Recommendation: Apply the same discipline to the glossary. Entries like GoalRun (vocabulary.md:298-306) and OutcomeRoom (413-420) restate full field inventories that common-objects-and-envelopes.md owns — two prose copies of one schema is a drift engine, and at 2181 lines the file is unreviewable. Shrink every entry to: name, one-sentence gloss, term_kind, owner-doc link. Split the single 'Runtime Terms' list into per-domain sections mirroring source-of-truth-map.md, and generate the markdown from a structured registry (YAML/JSON) so guard tests can lint naming, casing, duplicates, and enum parity mechanically.
5. **The two naming files disagree on the name of the assurance enum itself** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/_meta/vocabulary.md:1822 — 'AssuranceState: the ordered evidence/admission posture attached to a contribution or interop result' vs docs/architecture/foundations/canonical-enums.md:633 — 'Assurance Stages (`assurance_stage`)'
   - Recommendation: Pick one. The member sets are identical (attested…settled), so this is the same enum under two nouns, and it is not a casing artifact: vocabulary.md's PascalCase entries conventionally shadow the enums file's snake_case wire keys (ManagedExecutionMode/managed_execution_mode, EffectRecoveryClass/effect_recovery_class), so the expected term is AssuranceStage. README Non-Negotiable #21 also says 'Assurance stages remain explicit', leaving vocabulary.md:1822 as the lone State-spelling outlier. canonical-enums.md owns member sets and spellings, so retire `AssuranceState` in vocabulary.md to a pointer entry ('`assurance_stage` — see canonical-enums.md') and sweep canon for the State spelling. Then add a guard test to the architecture-docs gate asserting every enum mentioned in vocabulary.md matches its canonical wire key and member spelling in canonical-enums.md — this is precisely the drift class the enums file was created to kill (canonical-enums.md:20-24), now reproduced between the two naming authorities themselves. (Severity rescored from high: a one-noun drift with a mechanical fix; the meta-point rides in the guard test.)
6. **Duplicate parallel entries prove the flat glossary has exceeded self-consistency capacity** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/_meta/vocabulary.md:2061 — 'IOICli: the terminal/headless operator client over daemon/public runtime APIs' (vs HypervisorCliHeadless at line 925, defined near-identically)
   - Recommendation: Dedupe the parallel families: IOICli/IOITui/IOISdk/IOIAdk (lines 2061-2075) versus HypervisorCliHeadless/HypervisorTui/SDK/ADK (lines 47-52, 925-931) define the same client and kit lanes ~1100-2000 lines apart, down to shared phrasing ('it does not own execution semantics' appears in both CLI entries). Keep one canonical entry each and convert the other to an explicit alias line, following the file's own HypervisorSurfaceAlias pattern (vocabulary.md:1017-1037). A single-owner vocabulary that breeds its own synonyms is failing at its one job; a duplicate-detection lint over term names should join the docs gate. (Severity rescored from high: a real structural defect in the naming authority, but internal doc quality with a cheap mechanical fix and no external exposure.)
7. **'wallet.network' names the authority plane after the one thing the docs insist it is not — and it also literally trades** — `medium` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/_meta/vocabulary.md:30 — '| wallet.network | SSO, permissions, connected access, authority, recovery |'
   - Recommendation: When your own presentation map must translate a protocol name into 'SSO, permissions' to stop users misreading it, the name is wrong at the protocol layer too. Worse, the clean correction ('it's not a crypto wallet') is unavailable because WalletExchange/WalletTrade/TxIntent (vocabulary.md:749-765) make it a literal trading wallet as well. Keep wallet.network as the consumer asset/trade cockpit brand, but rename the reusable authority pipeline `WalletAuthorityCore` → `AuthorityCore` (with AuthorityClient, AuthorityMCP, AuthorityCLI) in protocol prose, so the single authority-crossing primitive (CapabilityLease's issuer) is not branded as custody software. Caveat: INV-10's quadrant is worded 'wallet.network owns authority; Agentgres owns operational truth' (foundations/invariants.md), so this rename must explicitly argue that invariant's rewording — the substance (one authority owner) is unchanged, but the change cannot be made silently. Blast radius: ~10 Wallet* protocol terms plus INV-10 wording, all doc-stage.
8. **'Agentgres' reads as a Postgres derivative; the docs then have to keep clarifying the truth model the name obscures** — `medium` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/_meta/vocabulary.md:2095 — 'AgentgresPostgresBridge: a Postgres-compatible read/query surface over named Agentgres projections.'
   - Recommendation: The '-gres' fusion tells first-time readers 'our Postgres fork for agents'. Correction to the original framing: the Agentgres docs do not deny the association — they half-own it, headlining 'Agentgres is a Postgres-compatible operational substrate' (postgres-bridge-and-readiness-contract.md:24) while repeatedly clarifying 'its source of truth is operation-backed state, not mutable relational rows' (agentgres/doctrine.md:63). That recurring clarification is the tax the name imposes: the object is an admitted-operation truth ledger where Postgres compatibility is one read-oriented bridge, and the presentation map already hides the name as 'Provenance' (vocabulary.md:31), conceding it doesn't communicate. Either rename the protocol object to what it is — `AdmissionLedger` or `OperationLedger` — or explicitly define Agentgres as an internal codename and check PostgreSQL trademark policy on '-gres'/'Postgres' marks before the name reaches public whitepaper/API surfaces. Blast radius is the largest of any rename here (crates, envelopes, dozens of docs), so if kept, at minimum add a first-line 'not a Postgres fork; Postgres-compatible read bridge only' clause at the term definition.
9. **'Kernel' carries four meanings; GoalKernel already has a better name the docs keep using** — `medium` · Rename
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/_meta/vocabulary.md:288 — 'GoalKernel: goal-shaped orchestration kernel used by ioi.ai and Hypervisor Sessions for one bounded goal or claimed subgoal.'
   - Recommendation: Kernel currently means: GoalKernel (loop interpreter, 288), IOIKernelL0/L0Substrate (chain substrate, 1620), 'domain kernel' (EdgeInTopology, 1640-1642), and the literal Linux kernel in HypervisorOSBootProfile (99-101). The docs themselves call the GoalKernel role 'the conductor' three times (vocabulary.md:1157 'goal/conductor state', 1182 'outcome-conductor', 1193 'the conductor may satisfy the VerifierPath'). Rename GoalKernel → GoalConductor, reserve 'kernel' exclusively for the L0 substrate sense, and change 'domain kernel' to 'domain L0 instance'. Blast radius: doc-wide prose but zero code, since GoalKernel is planned.
10. **Enum member spellings contradict between the two files, and one enum ignores casing law entirely** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/_meta/vocabulary.md:578 — 'Values are `Standard` and `Private`.' vs docs/architecture/foundations/canonical-enums.md:189 — 'standard | private'
   - Recommendation: Normalize every enum member cited in vocabulary.md to the canonical snake_case wire spelling from canonical-enums.md. One nuance: the vocabulary entry frames ManagedExecutionMode as 'the user-facing execution/privacy selector', so `Standard`/`Private` may have been intended as display copy — but they are presented as backticked enum Values, which is exactly the ambiguity the excerpt-only rule exists to kill; if display copy is meant, write 'rendered as Standard/Private' and cite the wire members. Separately, `RiskCoverageState` (vocabulary.md:735-738) uses Title-Case-with-spaces members ('Partially Covered', 'Conflicting Sources') — the only enum in the pack violating snake_case — and it crosses components (ApprovalInboxItem coverage states, CandidateEvidence, placement candidates), so promote it into canonical-enums.md with snake_case members and a deprecated-alias table. Add member-spelling parity to the enum guard test proposed above; today the naming files fail their own drift standard.
11. **Sixty repeated negation litanies should become one typed term_kind taxonomy that guard tests can enforce** — `medium` · Better way *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/_meta/vocabulary.md:59 — 'It is not a runtime, authority layer, semantic truth store, data warehouse, training-consent owner, marketplace, or settlement layer.'
   - Recommendation: Nearly every entry ends in an is-not litany because the flat glossary has no type system. Introduce a closed `term_kind` enum (~10 values: truth_owner, runtime_owner, definition_object, projection, receipt, lease, policy_object, product_lens, protocol, alias) stamped on every entry in the structured registry. Then the boundary rules are stated once per kind ('no projection owns truth', 'no definition_object executes or holds authority') and enforced mechanically, instead of hand-restated in prose dozens of times with inevitable omissions. This is the pack's own fail-closed physics applied to its vocabulary: kinds are checkable; prose negations are not. SKOS/ISO-11179 registries do exactly this with concept types and scope notes.
12. **No naming grammar: the pack has a consistent implicit suffix system but never states the rules** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/_meta/vocabulary.md:12 — 'The agent harness uses behavior-first names in runtime code and reserves compliance acronyms for hidden audit material.'
   - Recommendation: Kubernetes API conventions and Google AIP derive their value from rules, not lists; vocabulary.md is almost all instances. Correction: that sentence is not literally the file's only rule — it also carries scattered per-term reservations (KeyShard: 'shard is reserved for cryptographic or threshold authority', 711-713; the Worker-vs-Agent register rule, 1711; Recipe owner-qualification, 2031) — but there is no systematic grammar. Add a 'Naming Rules' section owning: suffix semantics (Profile = immutable posture, Receipt = evidence, Lease = expiring grant, Projection = derived view, Envelope = wire object, Run = admitted attempt), prefix rules (when Hypervisor*/Wallet*/Agentgres* apply), casing law (snake_case wire keys, PascalCase objects), acronym policy (expand at first definition — AIIP is expanded in its owner doc, foundations/aiip.md:1 'Inter-Autonomous-System Protocol', but never at its vocabulary.md definition, 1417), and a mandatory external-collision review for new coinages. This converts naming from taste into a gate, matching how canonical-enums.md already governs members.
13. **Credibility-hostile coinages sit inside canonical definitions: Web4, StockfishStyleCodingSearch, IntelligentBlockchain, decentralized.*** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/_meta/vocabulary.md:2102 — 'AgentgresInvariant: a Web4 validity rule for consequential action'
   - Recommendation: These names will be quoted verbatim by critics. Correction on Web4: it is not a stray undefined coinage — it is the canon's deliberate category bet with its own owner doc (foundations/web4-and-ioi-stack.md, 'Canonical owner: this file for the Web4 category definition') and pervasive use across the agentgres, daemon, and security-invariants docs. The defect at this facet is narrower: the naming owner itself uses 'Web4' inside AgentgresInvariant's definition (2102) with no vocabulary entry or pointer for the term — add a pointer entry to web4-and-ioi-stack.md rather than deleting it, and record the coinage in the proposed external-collision register since the category bet is itself a credibility exposure. The remaining points stand: `StockfishStyleCodingSearch` (1210) embeds a third-party open-source project name as canonical product shorthand — rename to `TreeSearchCodingProfile`. `IntelligentBlockchain` (1360-1368) applies 'blockchain' to a single-authority hash-chained log; the doc honestly declares PoA-1 validity, but the name picks a fight the honest prose then has to lose — prefer 'ordered admitted state machine' in protocol prose. And `decentralized.cloud/.exchange/.trade` (775-795) are each defined as 'a preferred first-party ... engine', inviting decentralization-washing accusations from the exact web3 audience the L1 addresses.
14. **Nine execution nouns (Run/Invocation/Execution/Session) with no declared convention; AgentExecution breaks the implicit one** — `medium` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/_meta/vocabulary.md:1725 — 'AgentExecution: one admitted execution of an AgentRecord inside a session, environment, work run, thread, or automation.'
   - Recommendation: GoalRun, WorkRun, AutomationRun, TransformationRun, AgentExecution, HarnessInvocation, ModuleInvocation, ComputeSession, HypervisorSession all mean 'one bounded doing of X' with no rule distinguishing them. Declare suffix law: `*Run` = durable admitted attempt record; `*Invocation` = one daemon-mediated call; `*Session` = bounded interactive/allocation context — then rename AgentExecution → AgentRun to comply. Secondarily, reconsider GoalRun itself: it is a durable multi-session pursuit container, and canon constantly explains it with the word 'pursuit' (README.md:146-147 — 'GoalRunProfile = reusable immutable pursuit definition', 'GoalRun = durable state of one admitted pursuit'; Non-Negotiable #12 calls GoalRunProfile 'reusable pursuit method'); `GoalPursuit` would carry the semantics the name lacks, and the rename is doc-wide but code-free while GoalRun remains planned.
15. **The Worker-not-Agent protocol style rule is violated by the daemon-plane objects' own names** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/_meta/vocabulary.md:1711 — 'New protocol prose should still use `Worker` when referring to the accountable execution actor.' vs :1715 — 'AgentOperatingPlane: the daemon-owned control plane for configured agents'
   - Recommendation: The rule says protocol prose uses Worker, yet the most protocol-central family — AgentOperatingPlane, AgentRecord, AgentExecution (1715-1729) — is named with the product-layer word, and the rule's own escape hatch offers two more names (IntelligentExecutionNode, GovernedAutonomousSystemChain) for the same acting thing. Resolve one way: either accept `Agent` as the canonical protocol noun for the configured-record family and retire the Worker-only style rule, or rename the daemon plane to WorkerOperatingPlane/WorkerRecord/WorkerRun. A register-dependent naming rule that canonical object names already break guarantees drift in every future doc.
16. **outcome_class mixes valence with content via exploit_found, breaking the file's own single-axis discipline** — `low` · Refactor *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/canonical-enums.md:403 — 'positive | negative | inconclusive | invalid | exploit_found | superseded'
   - Recommendation: Five members are outcome valences; `exploit_found` is a content category — a red-team attempt that finds an exploit is a positive outcome, so the enum forces a false choice. Remove `exploit_found` from `outcome_class` and represent exploit discovery through the Finding object's typing or contribution_kind, where `integrity_report` already exists (canonical-enums.md:462).
