One obligation to state explicitly: README Non-Negotiable #21 requires 'exploit' information be preserved among outcome information classes — the Finding/contribution_kind representation still satisfies that, so the migration note should say so rather than appear to drop mandated information. This matters now, not later: outcome_class is the canonical wire key on Attempt and WorkResult (canonical-enums.md:406-407), and per the pack's build sequence WorkResult is the first code seam. Fixing the axis before the first contract PR costs one line; after, it costs a deprecated-alias migration.


## Part III.2 — Foundations: the object model

### Common objects & envelopes — first half: core envelopes, identity, refs, policy objects

**Files reviewed:** `foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md`

**Current shape.** foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md (9,375 lines) is the single canonical owner for ~180 named shared "envelope" object types plus a ~374-scheme URI registry that every IOI component must understand. Each envelope is written as a YAML pseudo-schema (fields, pipe-separated enums, `scheme://...` ref placeholders) followed by prose admission rules: which fields are conditionally required, what fails closed, and which fields are "registry projections" excluded from the object's content hash. The first half covers the base ManifestEnvelope and package/genesis lifecycle; the bounded autonomous-system chain family (constitution, deployment, membership, failover, ordering/finality, oracle, lifecycle, enrollment); AIIP channel/packet and cross-party offer/acceptance/handoff/settlement envelopes; CollaborationTerms and multi-party collaboration; the authority family (scope request, grant, client, access point, step-up challenge) split into two tiers — `prim:*` runtime capabilities vs `scope:*` wallet.network authority; core runtime objects (Task, Run, RuntimeAssignment, RuntimeEvent, Receipt, Artifact, Delivery, Settlement, Contribution); and the ontology/learning family (ontologies, assertions, mappings, data recipes, learning-rights and learning-boundary profiles). Identity is scheme-per-concept URIs declared "exhaustive," with actor identity constrained to a ProtocolPrincipalRef union; released revisions are immutable and content-addressed with successor-revision evolution. Roughly 28 of the first half's ~65 envelope definitions carry `ioi.<name>.vN` schema_version keys — concentrated in the newer autonomous-system, AIIP, pairing, and collaboration families — while the built core runtime envelopes (Task, Run, Receipt, Artifact, RuntimeEvent, AuthorityGrant, Delivery) carry none. Per the file's own status header, large families specified here (autonomous-system chain, AIIP, pairing, embodied, improvement) are planned or speculative rather than built.

**What it already gets right:**

- Spec/status separation is real and consistently reasoned: released revision bodies are immutable and content-addressed while registry_status/lifecycle refs are explicitly excluded projections (e.g. WorkflowTemplateEnvelope lines 874-878, SkillEntry 961-971) — this is Kubernetes-grade discipline applied to a governance domain, with the exclusion set spelled out per object.
- OntologyAssertionEnvelope (4162-4185) is genuinely state-of-the-art: bitemporal (valid_time/transaction_time), provenance-bearing, with confidence, contradiction refs, applicability scope, supersession, and dispute state — stronger than most production knowledge-graph schemas and on par with XTDB/Datomic bitemporal practice plus W3C PROV concerns.
- The two-tier capability/authority split (prim:* runtime feasibility vs scope:* wallet-granted authority, lines 620-654) with the explicit rule that neither may collapse into a generic capability bag is cleaner than mainstream agent-framework practice (MCP, LangChain tool permissions) and directly supports the fail-closed physics.
- Fail-closed conditional-field contracts are written as testable rules, not vibes: TaskAcceptanceEnvelope's response-hash binding rules (2573-2593), ArtifactEnvelope's derived_export lineage requirements (3898-3907), and the pairing envelope's phase-qualified subobjects (2189-2203) each enumerate exactly what must be null/non-null per state.
- AIIPExternalProtocolBindingEnvelope's assurance_non_equivalences field and the rule that a remote completion 'never silently becomes an IOI verification, acceptance, authority grant' (2318-2321) is an anti-conflation discipline the A2A/MCP interop ecosystem largely lacks.

**SOTA verdict: `behind`** — compared against: Kubernetes resource model (GVK, spec/status/conditions, OpenAPI-published schemas, CEL validation rules on CRDs); protobuf + Buf/Confluent schema-registry governance (enforced compatibility modes, breaking-change CI); CloudEvents 1.0 (minimal envelope, specversion, reverse-DNS type discipline); W3C DID Core (one URI scheme + method registry instead of scheme-per-concept); UCAN / Biscuit / GNAP capability tokens (attenuation chains, audience binding, proof-of-possession); IPFS multiformats/multihash (self-describing digest agility); in-toto/DSSE attestations (mandatory signing envelope for proof objects); Stripe object model (single Money representation, versioned API with automated upcasters)

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No machine-readable schema layer at all: ~200 envelopes exist only as YAML prose; nothing corresponds to K8s OpenAPI schemas, protobuf IDL, or CDDL, and the hundreds of conditional admission rules have no CEL/constraint-language expression despite the canon minting schema:// and invariant:// namespaces.
- No enforced versioning/evolution story: only 28 of the first half's ~65 envelope definitions declare schema_version — and the built core envelopes are the ones without it; there is no additive-compatibility rule, deprecation window, or upcaster doctrine — schema registries and event-sourcing upcasters solved this a decade ago.
- Identifier grammar violates RFC 3986: 111 of 374 registry schemes contain underscores (work_run://, wallet_client://), which standard URI parsers reject; DID solved the same problem with one scheme plus a method registry.
- No digest agility for the commitment chain: signature blocks declare schemes (ed25519|ml-dsa) and ArtifactEnvelope pins sha256/CID, but release_root, manifest_root, content_hash, terms_body_root and the state-commitment chain are bare 'hash' fields with undeclared algorithm; only one envelope (OrderingAdmissionFinalityProfile) binds a hash_and_signature_suite_ref.
- Authority grants lack the attenuation-chain machinery (parent grant refs, holder proof-of-possession key binding, caveats, signatures) that UCAN/Biscuit/GNAP treat as table stakes.
- No canonical Money type: max_budget_usd literals, unit-less max_budget, and asset_kind/amount decimal_string coexist.

**Findings (16, severity-ordered):**

1. **The canon's contract layer is prose-only: no machine-checkable schema or constraint language for ~200 envelopes** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3335 — 'At least one of `goal_run_ref`, `task_ref`, `run_ref`, `work_claim_ref`, `harness_invocation_ref`, or `embodied_unit_ref` is required.'
   - Recommendation: Adopt one schema artifact per envelope: publish each `ioi.<name>.vN` as a resolvable `schema://` object (JSON Schema 2020-12 or CUE), and express cross-field/cross-object admission rules (RuntimeAssignment's ~10 paragraphs of system_id/membership/closure-hash matching, TaskAcceptance's conditional rules) as CEL-style validation expressions referenced via the already-minted `invariant://` namespace. Keep prose as commentary. Without this, every implementation independently re-derives hundreds of prose rules — the single biggest drift engine in the pack, and it contradicts the file's stated purpose of preventing split-brain API design. The pack's existing doc-gates lint the prose but validate no instances.
2. **111 of 374 registered URI schemes are invalid per RFC 3986 (underscores), breaking standard URI tooling and AIIP interop** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:342 — 'harness_invocation://... daemon-mediated invocation of a selected HarnessProfile or Agent Harness Adapter'
   - Recommendation: RFC 3986 scheme grammar permits only ALPHA/DIGIT/+/-/.; `work_run://`, `wallet_client://`, `goal_loop://`, `context_cell://` etc. will be rejected by stock URI parsers in every language external AIIP parties use. Either collapse to a DID-style single scheme (`ioi:<kind>:<id>` with a kind registry) or mandate hyphen-only schemes and add the underscore forms to the existing compatibility-alias mechanism. Note the file already canonicalized `harness-profile://` over `harness_profile:` (lines 597-603) — apply that same decision to the other 110 schemes before implementations freeze them.
3. **The ID registry claims exhaustiveness but envelopes use unregistered schemes, and two schemes are registered twice with different meanings** — `high` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:586 — 'The table above is exhaustive for URI schemes that this shared-object canon owns and promotes across components.' Yet `governance://` (611, 3090), `attestation://` (3319, 3516), `object://` (4167), `source://` (4173), `approval-policy://` (4247), `compatibility://` (4135), `query://` (4200) appear in envelope fields without registry entries; `package://` is registered twice with divergent definitions (208 vs 306), as is `commitment://` (243 vs 575).
   - Recommendation: Run a mechanical audit (grep all `://` tokens in envelope blocks against the registry) as a doc-gate check, register or replace the seven unregistered schemes (ProtocolPrincipalRef's `governance://` is the most serious since it defines who may act), and merge the duplicate `package://` and `commitment://` rows into single definitions. The registry's own escape hatch (586-591) covers component-local refs in component-local examples only — these usages sit inside this file's own canonical envelope definitions, so the exemption does not apply. An 'exhaustive' registry that its own file violates trains readers to treat the whole registry as advisory.
4. **Versioning discipline is inverted: speculative families carry schema_version, the built core envelopes carry none** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3940 — 'schema_version: ioi.settlement.v2' — while ReceiptEnvelope (3807), RunEnvelope (3680), TaskEnvelope (3637), ArtifactEnvelope (3843), AuthorityGrantEnvelope (3476), RuntimeEventEnvelope (3780), DeliveryEnvelope (3912), and ManifestEnvelope (659) declare no schema_version; 28 of the first half's ~65 envelope definitions do, concentrated in the planned autonomous-system/AIIP/pairing/collaboration families.
   - Recommendation: Mandate schema_version on every envelope (the ones already built/partial in the daemon are exactly the ones whose wire evolution will hurt first), and add one owner section — 'Envelope evolution' — defining additive-compatibility rules, deprecation windows, and the adapter/upcaster pattern the file already uses ad hoc four times (harness-profile aliases 597-603, local_microharness 2389-2391, autonomous_system_id 3124-3127, TrainingEvidenceEligibility 4778-4792). ioi.settlement.v2 exists with no recorded v1→v2 delta anywhere in the pack; that precedent needs rules before v2s multiply.
5. **AuthorityGrantEnvelope — the security keystone — is the thinnest object in the file: no signature, no delegation chain, no key binding, example literals in the schema** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3491 — 'constraints: max_budget_usd: 10, expires_at: 2026-05-01T00:00:00Z' — a ~24-line envelope (3476-3499) with no schema_version, signature, parent-grant ref, holder key binding, or typed constraint schema.
   - Recommendation: For a pack whose core physics is fail-closed authority, the grant object should be its most rigorous contract, not its least. The grant does bind a grantee (`subject_id`), and AuthorityClientEnvelope carries origin/key/attestation binding at the client layer — but the grant object itself is unsigned and unbound. Adopt the UCAN/Biscuit-style shape: `parent_grant_ref` for attenuation chains (the constitution already distinguishes delegable_scope_refs at 1314-1315 but grants cannot express delegation), `holder_key_ref` for proof-of-possession, `constraint_schema_ref: schema://...` replacing the open example map, and a mandatory signature block like ManifestEnvelope's (666-669). Also purge the literal past date and `max_budget_usd: 10` — canonical schemas must not embed example values as field definitions. No other owner doc specifies a fuller grant contract.
6. **Package representation is split-brain by construction: ManifestEnvelope profile OR AutonomousSystemManifestEnvelope 'or both'** — `high` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:743 — 'Implementation may represent the package as a strict `ManifestEnvelope` profile, as `AutonomousSystemManifestEnvelope`, or as both.' — while the file's Purpose (14-15) is 'to prevent split-brain API design'.
   - Recommendation: Pick one canonical wire form. AutonomousSystemManifestEnvelope (1027-1183) is the complete one; demote ManifestEnvelope's `manifest_type: autonomous_system` arm (661) to a registry/catalog projection of it, or delete the arm entirely. Two sanctioned representations of the release artifact means two commitment roots for the same release (ManifestEnvelope's `manifest_root` at 664 vs ASME's `release_root` at 1036), two admission paths, and every consumer writing a normalizer — exactly the failure mode the file exists to prevent. The same overload audit should cover the other '*Manifest*' envelopes (SkillManifest, ServiceModuleManifest, ODKManifest): ServiceModuleManifestEnvelope carries a `manifest_ref: ai://...` and ManifestEnvelope enumerates `service_module` as a manifest_type, but whether these are profiles of the base envelope or parallel schemas is never normatively stated.
7. **One 9,375-line file is the wrong owner artifact for a dozen unrelated envelope families** — `medium` · Refactor *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:4 — 'Canonical owner: this file for shared envelope and contract names, ID namespaces, principal refs, primitive capability tiers, authority grants…' (a ~120-word single-sentence ownership claim).
   - Recommendation: Split into foundations/envelopes/: identity-and-refs.md (ID registry + ProtocolPrincipalRef + tiers), authority.md, system-chain.md, aiip-and-settlement.md, work-spine.md (Task/Run/Assignment/Receipt/Artifact), ontology-and-learning.md, with embodied and Foundry families moving beside their doctrine owners; keep this file as the envelope index plus cross-cutting rules. The pack already splits ownership this way (canonical-enums.md owns member sets per line 2336; the events/receipts owner owns the receipt registry per 3833). One file means one 'Last alignment pass' date and one implementation-status line (7, 9) for families at wildly different maturity, guaranteeing stale metadata, and forces every reviewer and merge through a single hotspot.
8. **Full field-level canon is frozen for families the file itself marks planned or speculative, inverting effort from the built core** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:9 — 'Implementation status: mixed (core runtime envelopes/IDs built or partial in the daemon; … AIIP transport/bindings … embodied, cTEE, and prediction families planned or speculative)'
   - Recommendation: The pack deliberately works contract-first, and the file-level status header does disclose which families are speculative — but the disclosure is one line for a 9,375-line file, with no per-family status. Apply the pack's own status axis inside this file: speculative families (LocalAgentPairingSession with its 12-state lifecycle and failure_reason_code enum at 2175-2187, the failover/ordering-recovery machinery, CollaborationTerms' ~110-line bargain) should carry per-section status and be published as shape contracts — identity scheme, key refs, invariants — rather than exhaustively enumerated state machines. Field-level detail authored before any implementation contact will churn as v2 rewrites across dozens of types (the four existing compatibility adapters are early evidence), while the actually-built envelopes lack even schema_version.
9. **Doctrine constants encoded as wire fields (`: false`, `: true`, `: prohibited`) bloat envelopes and create parser ambiguity** — `medium` · Better way *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:2811 — 'each_required_party_accepts_expected_net_benefit: true' — likewise agent_may_commit_amendment: false (1333), self_report_creates_payout_right: false (2794), ordinary_work_credit_substitution: prohibited (2809), constitution_must_be_preserved: true (1823), the bootstrap_non_grants block (2159-2166).
   - Recommendation: A field that admits exactly one legal value is an invariant, not data: it forces every instance to serialize dead weight, and while the pack's general fail-closed posture (invariants.md INV-14) implies an admitter rejects the other value, no per-field rule says so (reject vs normalize is unstated). The self-carrying-non-grant rationale — a signed object that documents what it does NOT grant — is real, but it is served equally well by the hash-committed schema. Move each constant to foundations/invariants.md or a referenced `invariant://` object and delete the fields; where auditor visibility is the intent, state it normatively once in the envelope's schema doc — the pattern the Terminology Boundary Table (787-814) already uses well.
10. **TypedWorkSubjectBinding outlaws undiscriminated ambiguity, yet dozens of fields are bare 4-8-way scheme unions** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3209 — 'A discriminator may not rescue an intrinsically ambiguous identity namespace.' vs :2536-2538 — 'budget_and_funding_refs: - goal-budget://… | budget://… | escrow://… | procurement://… | order://…'
   - Recommendation: The file's best ref-discipline idea (kind+ref agreement, owner stays authoritative — 3186-3210) is applied to exactly one binding while consequential fields elsewhere (funding, acceptance refs, registry_lifecycle_ref at 859 unioning agentgres object vs package release) rely on implicit scheme-sniffing. Either add a normative rule to Common ID Conventions that scheme dispatch IS the canonical discriminator (and require unions to be scheme-disjoint), or convert consequential unions — funding, authority, acceptance, lifecycle owners — to typed bindings on the TypedWorkSubjectBinding pattern. Today each implementation invents its own dispatch.
11. **No digest agility for the commitment chain: content roots are bare 'hash' fields with undeclared algorithm, despite PQ signature schemes already in the enums** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:1693 — 'hash_and_signature_suite_ref: schema://...' — the only envelope that binds a hash suite, while release_root (1036), manifest_root (664), content_hash, and terms_body_root (2726) are untyped 'hash'.
   - Recommendation: Signature blocks already anticipate migration (ed25519 | secp256k1 | ml-dsa | hybrid at 667, 2498), and a few leaf fields are already agile or pinned (ArtifactEnvelope's sha256 + self-describing cid at 3845-3846, StepUpChallenge's sha256:-prefixed hashes) — but the commitment chain itself, the thing that must survive decades per the lifecycle/succession doctrine, has no algorithm binding. Adopt multihash-style self-describing digests, or generalize OrderingAdmissionFinalityProfile's hash_and_signature_suite_ref so every content-addressed family names its suite once. Also fold the hash-projection idea into it: only CollaborationTerms names its projection ('terms_body_hash_profile: ioi.collaboration-terms-body.v1', 2725) while every other envelope defines its hash-excluded fields in prose — make the named hash-profile the universal pattern.
12. **RunEnvelope embeds REST API paths and uses `state` where every sibling uses `status`** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3705 — 'event_stream: /v1/runs/{run_id}/events' (plus artifacts/receipts/trace/inspect/scorecard endpoints, 3706-3710) and :3700 'state: queued | assigned | …' — the only top-level `state:` lifecycle field in the first half against 100+ sibling `status:` fields.
   - Recommendation: Storing HTTP routes inside the canonical truth object couples every stored Run to one API surface and version — Kubernetes deliberately removed selfLink and derives URLs via discovery. Delete the six endpoint fields (they are derivable from run_id by any client that knows the API contract, which belongs in the daemon-runtime API owner doc), and rename `state` to `status` for consistency; the 15-value flat enum would also benefit from the conditions-style split DeliveryEnvelope already demonstrates (delivery/acceptance/adjudication/settlement statuses, 3929-3932).
13. **RuntimeEventEnvelope's kind is a ~95-item single-line union self-described as a non-exhaustive 'compatibility sample'** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3797 — 'The `kind` line above is a compatibility sample, not the exhaustive event registry.'
   - Recommendation: An enum whose owner disclaims exhaustiveness is not a contract — it is a maintenance liability that will drift from the true registry in events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md (already the declared owner, 3800-3802). Replace with `kind: string` plus a normative naming grammar (dotted namespace, CloudEvents reverse-DNS-style: `outcome_room.claim.leased`) and keep the ownership pointer; delete the ~95-item sample line and the `authority_client.*` wildcards. While there, align with CloudEvents 1.0 minimums the envelope lacks: schema_version (absent) and an explicit source field distinct from actor_id.
14. **Three incompatible money representations across the first half; no canonical Money type** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3954-3956 — 'asset_kind: none | fiat | stablecoin | native_asset | service_credit … amount: decimal_string' vs :3462/:3491 'max_budget_usd: 10' and :3659 'max_budget: optional' (unit-less).
   - Recommendation: Define one canonical Amount contract in this file — {amount: decimal_string, asset_kind, asset_identifier} exactly as SettlementEnvelope's rail block already has it — and require it in AuthorityGrant constraints, TaskEnvelope constraints, budgets, and quotas. Stripe-grade systems learned that ad-hoc currency-suffixed numeric fields (max_budget_usd) become un-migratable; for a protocol that meters spend as an authority ceiling, an ambiguous budget unit is a correctness hole, not a style issue.
15. **`mow:` is an unexpanded acronym appearing exactly once in the entire canon, gating routing eligibility** — `low` · Rename
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:704-708 — 'mow: sparse_worker_category: optional … routing_eligibility_status: draft | submitted | benchmarking | eligible | suspended | revoked'
   - Recommendation: Rename the block to something self-describing — `marketplace_and_routing:` or `worker_market_profile:` — and register the term in _meta/vocabulary.md. This block carries a real lifecycle (routing_eligibility_status) inside the base ManifestEnvelope, so an opaque three-letter key here propagates into every SDK and API surface; grep confirms no definition of 'mow' anywhere in this file or in _meta/vocabulary.md, meaning a fresh implementer cannot even guess the domain it governs.
16. **AuthorityClientEnvelope's identity scheme is wallet_client://, disagreeing with its own type name** — `low` · Rename
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3505 — 'authority_client_id: wallet_client://...' while the registry (:526) defines wallet_client:// as 'wallet.network CLI/MCP/mobile/web/embedded client session'.
   - Recommendation: Pick one name for the concept: either rename the envelope WalletClientEnvelope, or mint `authority-client://` (hyphenated, per the RFC 3986 fix) and make `wallet_client://` a compatibility alias via the existing normalization mechanism. Type-name/scheme mismatches are exactly the class of drift the harness-profile alias cleanup (597-603) was written to stop; this one also collides with `origin://... authority-client origin binding identity` (:527), which already uses the authority-client vocabulary.

### Common objects & envelopes — second half: goal/work/collab objects

**Files reviewed:** `foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md`

**Current shape.** This half of common-objects-and-envelopes.md defines the goal/work/collaboration spine as wire envelopes. GoalRunProfileEnvelope is an immutable, content-addressed, reusable pursuit specification; a GoalRunEnvelope binds exactly one admitted profile revision plus a resolved-component snapshot and resolution receipt, and carries intent, constraints, role topology pointers, context cells, leases, attempts, results, findings, verifier state, and a 19-phase active_loop_phase. GoalGroundingLoopEnvelope is the concrete Goal Kernel loop (receive_intent through continue_or_close) with escalation states and exit conditions. RoleTopologyEnvelope maps roles to accountable actors separately from resolvers/model routes/runtimes; ContextCell/ContextLease/ContextHandoff bound each role's working context, and TaskBrief → HarnessInvocation → HarnessAdapterEvent → WorkResult (with ImplementationResultPayload as the software profile) is the execution seam, with OutcomeDelta as the typed proposed state change. Above GoalRuns, OutcomeRoomEnvelope is a durable collaborative-pursuit "room" that is canonically a full bounded-DAS instance (own system_id, genesis, constitution, ordering/lifecycle profiles); rooms expose discovery/participation envelopes, TTL+heartbeat participant leases, a claimable WorkFrontier with WorkClaimLeases, Attempts, Findings, VerifierChallenges, resource/capability offers, portable ParticipantStateBundles, and a shared RoomAdmittedObjectBase compare-and-swap admission spine for every mutable room child. Orchestration constraint/policy/plan envelopes, NetworkGoalBudget, VerifierPath, RoutingDecision, and Benchmark close the loop between plan selection, external solicitation, verification, and settlement.

**What it already gets right:**

- Accountable-actor vs resolver separation is genuinely ahead of SOTA: RoleTopology/HarnessInvocation forbid a HarnessProfile, adapter, model route, or runtime from occupying accountable_actor_ref, so responsibility survives executor swaps — A2A, LangGraph, and AutoGen all conflate agent identity with executor.
- Negative results are first-class: AttemptEnvelope durably preserves negative | inconclusive | invalid | exploit_found work and FindingEnvelope carries uncertainty, valid-time, contradiction, and dispute state — mainstream orchestrators (Temporal, Argo, LangGraph) retain failures only as execution history or node status, not as first-class knowledge objects with contradiction, reproduction, and dispute semantics.
- RoomAdmittedObjectBase is one clean event-sourced CAS admission spine (expected revision + predecessor commitment + payload root + admission receipt) reused by every mutable room child — a single mechanism where most multi-agent systems grow N ad-hoc mutation paths.
- The pull-based WorkFrontier with duplication_policy (exclusive | allowed | encouraged | independent_replication_required) plus TTL/heartbeat/epoch work-claim leases is a blackboard/tuple-space coordination model that supports adversarial replication — richer than any DAG fan-out in Argo or GitHub Actions, and correctly fail-closed on lease expiry ('an expired heartbeat cannot satisfy active-capacity or work-claim admission').
- External protocol handles are handled correctly: an MCP task stays an opaque commitment bound to the invocation and 'never becomes the GoalRun identity or authoritative GoalRun status' — avoiding the identity-laundering mistake most interop layers make.

**SOTA verdict: `divergent-by-design`** — compared against: Temporal/Cadence durable execution (event history, retry policies, cancellation scopes, continue-as-new, signals/queries); Google A2A task lifecycle (small closed task state machine, typed artifacts, opaque remote tasks); LangGraph checkpointed graph state + human-in-the-loop interrupts; AutoGen/AG2 conversation-programming state models; Argo Workflows / GitHub Actions declarative DAG + retryStrategy; Erlang/OTP & Akka supervision trees (hierarchical failure propagation); BPMN 2.0 (cautionary lesson: 100+-element standards collapse under their own taxonomy); Linda/blackboard tuple-space coordination (the frontier model's true ancestor); CQRS/event-sourcing head-vs-projection discipline

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No transition grammar: Temporal and A2A publish small closed state machines with legal-transition tables; this half ships ~15 bespoke status enums (GoalRun alone has continuation_state × status × active_loop_phase) with only scattered entry preconditions (claim activation, GoalRun activation, room open/active) and no owner of which transitions are legal or who may perform them.
- No cancellation-propagation contract: Temporal cancellation scopes and OTP supervision define what happens to children when a parent dies; here GoalRun→cell→invocation→external-handle teardown is unspecified beyond a 'cancelled' status value and lease-exit claim release.
- Retry/backoff is only coarse-grained: FoundryRunPlan has retry_policy_ref and GoalRunProfile a recovery_policy_ref, but there is no declarative Attempt/HarnessInvocation-level retry (Temporal RetryPolicy, Argo retryStrategy), so transient infra failure routes through conductor cognition (repair_or_escalate).
- No idempotency/dedup key for non-room submissions (Temporal workflow-id reuse policy); RoomAdmittedObjectBase CAS covers room children only, and a retry after a lost admission response cannot distinguish 'mine admitted' from 'someone advanced'.
- No history-compaction contract for GoalRun's unbounded ref arrays (Temporal continue-as-new); ImprovementCampaign gets the head/projection split, GoalRun does not.
- HarnessAdapterEvent has wall-clock timestamps but no per-invocation monotonic sequence, unlike Temporal's ordered event history.
- No challenge-bonding/anti-spam economics on VerifierChallenge, which optimistic-verification systems consider mandatory for open challenge markets.

**Findings (16, severity-ordered):**

1. **GoalRun.active_loop_phase duplicates GoalGroundingLoop.phase — two writers of the same fact** — `high` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:8653 — 'active_loop_phase:' with the identical 19-member enum repeated at :8734 'phase:' in GoalGroundingLoopEnvelope, plus grounding_loop_ref at :8652
   - Recommendation: Delete active_loop_phase from GoalRunEnvelope and declare current phase a projection of the referenced GoalGroundingLoop (grounding_loop_ref already exists), or delete phase from the loop envelope and make GoalRun the sole owner. The pack's own projections-never-truth physics forbids two independently mutable copies of the same coordination fact; nothing in the text says which one wins when they disagree after a crash or partial admission. The file already has the exact fix pattern: ContextCell's role-binding fields are explicitly declared 'convenience projections, not independent selection truth' (:8940-8942) — active_loop_phase gets no such declaration.
2. **GoalGroundingLoopEnvelope is mutable-in-place with no revision, hash, or transition receipts — a replay hole in the pack's own physics** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:8733 — 'loop_iteration: integer' — the envelope carries a live phase/iteration/escalation_state with no revision_ref, content_hash, predecessor, or per-transition receipt fields
   - Recommendation: Every sibling coordination object (OrchestrationPlan :7681-7683, RoleTopology :8820-8821) is immutable-by-revision with decision receipts; the loop — the object that actually drives spending and delegation — is the one mutable-in-place record. Either make loop iterations append-only children (goal_loop://.../iteration/N, each bound to the admitting operation) or explicitly declare the envelope a rebuildable projection over receipts and name the receipt types that source it. As written, the central control loop is unreplayable and unauditable, in a doc that elsewhere insists a top-level hash without the resolved-component commitment 'is insufficient for replay'.
3. **Status-machine explosion with no transition grammar — a drift engine across implementations** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:8685 — 'open | waiting_on_user | waiting_on_frontier | sleeping | delegated |' — GoalRun alone carries continuation_state (10 values) plus status (6) plus active_loop_phase (19)
   - Recommendation: Temporal ships ~7 workflow states and A2A ~6 task states, each with documented legal transitions; this half ships ~15 bespoke enums (GoalRun, loop, claim, lease, attempt, finding, room, invocation...). The doc does state scattered entry preconditions — WorkClaimLease 'active' requirements (:8322-8331), GoalRun activation (:8695-8699), room open/active binding (:7908-7912) — but never a legal-transition table, who may perform a given transition, or what receipts each requires. Add a lifecycle-grammar section (or new owner doc) defining small closed state machines with transition tables per object, and collapse GoalRun's continuation_state into either status or a declared projection. Without this, two conforming daemons will disagree on legality of e.g. sleeping→delegated.
4. **GoalRun carries unbounded mutable ref arrays while ImprovementCampaign explicitly splits contract root from operation projections** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:8673 — 'attempt_refs:' (plus work_result/finding/receipt/topology arrays) vs :7312 — 'The child-run list, candidate/archive refs, active and historical Epoch refs,' ... 'are operation projections excluded from that contract root'
   - Recommendation: Apply the Campaign's own discipline (operation_head_sequence/operation_head_root/derived_state_projection_ref, :7301-7303) to GoalRun: a frozen admission contract (profile revision, resolution snapshot, initial topology) with all accumulating ref arrays declared rebuildable projections of admitted operations. This also solves history growth (Temporal's continue-as-new problem): a month-long GoalRun otherwise accretes thousands of refs in one envelope with no compaction contract. The OutcomeRoom is closer to done: its mutable children already advance through the RoomAdmittedObjectBase CAS spine with admitted_sequence and resulting_room_revision/state roots (:8176-8179), so the room envelope mainly needs one explicit sentence declaring its ref arrays projections of that admitted sequence. GoalRun has neither a head nor such a declaration — an internal inconsistency, since the pack invented the right pattern 1,300 lines earlier.
5. **Every durable OutcomeRoom is a full bounded-DAS instance (genesis, constitution, ordering profile) — a per-room fixed cost with no middle tier** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:7904 — 'Every durable OutcomeRoom is an instance of the reference bounded-DAS package,' with genesis, constitution, and active profiles required before open/active
   - Recommendation: The doc already mitigates part of this: hosted_admission lets 'one named governed domain' order and admit room updates (:7923-7924), and :7915-7919 explicitly gates room creation on persistent collective pursuit being worth the machinery. But the remaining tiering is binary — ephemeral application session or sovereign chain: even a hosted permissioned_team room for a persistent two-person workstream must bind its own genesis + constitution + ordering/finality + lifecycle-continuity profiles before it may enter open/active. Add a canonical hosted-lite room profile: the host domain's existing Agentgres domain supplies ordering/admission, the room inherits the host constitution by reference, and genesis is deferred until the room goes cross-org or federated (with a defined upgrade transaction). Reserve full DAS binding for federated_admission and open_challenge modes, where it earns its cost. This deliberately argues that the 'each durable room is the flagship reference DAS' canon decision should gain a tier, and should say so explicitly.
6. **The minimal path through the goal spine is undefined, so Non-Negotiable #5's promised collapse is unverifiable** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:8698 — 'The resolved-component snapshot, hash, and resolution receipt are required before the GoalRun becomes `active`.'
   - Recommendation: README Non-Negotiable #5 promises 'simple work collapses to a direct path.' Some collapse does exist: truly simple work can avoid a GoalRun entirely via a direct AutomationRun/WorkRun/attempt invocation with null goal refs (:9130-9134), and within a GoalRun the plan tuple, topology, loop, and cells are all nullable or optional. But the mandatory floor for any admitted GoalRun is still profile revision + resolution receipt + resolved-component snapshot + active-skill-set snapshot + explicit run constraints (:8695-8699), plus TaskBrief/HarnessInvocation/WorkResult to do anything — and the doc never defines the canonical degenerate composition for a one-prompt goal-shaped run. Define a canonical direct profile in this doc: single-cell runs where one fused admission receipt stands in for plan revision, topology revision, and cell/lease creation, with the full objects derivable as projections. Otherwise every implementation invents its own shortcut and the shortcuts drift.
7. **No cancellation-propagation contract from GoalRun down through cells, invocations, and external handles** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:9116 — 'completed | failed | cancelled | superseded' — cancelled exists as a status but nothing states who cancels or how revocation cascades
   - Recommendation: Temporal has cancellation scopes; Erlang/Akka supervision defines child fate on parent death. The doc covers fragments: participant-lease exit 'releases or reassigns live claims' (:8093-8094, exit_and_claim_release_refs), profile revocation may 'pause, quarantine, migrate, or allow bounded completion' (:8702-8705), and failed/cancelled invocations require a terminal receipt and failure evidence (:9141-9143). What is missing is the parent→child cascade: revoking a GoalRun or expiring a WorkClaimLease says nothing about in-flight HarnessInvocations, open ContextCells, or external_invocation_bindings (MCP tasks). Add a cancellation section to the spine: work-subject revocation obligates the daemon to drive dependent invocations to cancelled with terminal receipts, issue best-effort cancel on external handles with a reconciliation record (consistent with Non-Negotiable #22's reconcile-before-retry), and release/reassign claims — mirroring the drain/fence discipline the embodied activation transaction already has at :5923-5927.
8. **The 11-role enum and other member sets are duplicated verbatim across three envelopes instead of owned by canonical-enums.md** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:8907 — 'conductor | implementer | reviewer | verifier | operator |' — identical sets inline at :8027 (lease admitted_role), :8838 (topology role_kind), :8906 (cell role)
   - Recommendation: The pack already has the right mechanism: claim_class at :7410-7411 delegates its member set to canonical-enums.md. Do the same for the role set (3 copies; canonical-enums.md currently does not own it), the materialization set (OrchestrationPolicy :7639-7649 vs OrchestrationPlan :7688-7693), the result_profile set (WorkResult :8501-8504 vs TaskBrief :9056-9059), and the 19-phase loop enum (2 copies). Adding one role today requires 3+ synchronized edits in a 9,375-line file — a guaranteed drift engine as the doc evolves.
9. **Attempt.outcome_class and WorkResult.outcome_class are dual writers with a singular link and no disagreement rule** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:8384 — 'positive | negative | inconclusive | invalid | exploit_found | superseded' — identical enum on WorkResult at :8521; Attempt has singular work_result_ref at :8385
   - Recommendation: Since an Attempt binds at most one WorkResult, the duplicated classification is pure redundancy with undefined conflict semantics (attempt says negative, result says positive — which does contribution accounting read?). Either declare Attempt.outcome_class a projection of its WorkResult when work_result_ref is non-null (own value only for result-less attempts), or make Attempt hold plural result refs and define outcome_class as an aggregation rule. State it in the paragraph at :8598-8604 that already handles room-scoping rules for these same five objects.
10. **WorkFrontierItem's bare unitless scalars are in tension with the pack's own no-universal-scalar doctrine** — `medium` · Contradiction *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:8267 — 'expected_value: number | null' (plus uncertainty, priority) vs :6775 — 'No universal scalar `fitness` or mandatory Pareto/bandit algorithm is canonical.'
   - Recommendation: The improvement plane explicitly refuses a universal fitness scalar (a statement scoped to that plane, so this is tension rather than a strict contradiction), yet frontier prioritization — which feeds claim allocation, budget, and eventually contribution/settlement — gets three untyped, denomination-free numbers with no declared estimator. In a canon where budgets carry denomination (:7752-7753) and every judgment is a typed ref, either type these (value_estimate_ref naming estimator, basis, and denomination) or add one sentence scoping them as room-local advisory ordering hints excluded from contribution, settlement, and routing-prior admission. Otherwise 'expected_value: 7' becomes load-bearing economics.
11. **TaskBrief's generic output_contract embeds software-only flags immediately after disclaiming them** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:9025 — 'Software-specific fields are one output profile rather than the generic result contract.' vs :9063 — 'changed_files_required: boolean'
   - Recommendation: changed_files_required, diff_summary_required, and tests_required sit in the generic output_contract that every research, ontology, incident, service, physical-mission, review, and evaluation brief must carry — exactly the overloading the WorkResult redesign (:8474-8478) and README Non-Negotiable #4 just eliminated. Keep the profile-neutral booleans (work_result_required, outcome_delta_required, finding_refs_required, blocker_report_required, receipt_refs_required) and move the three software flags behind output_contract.profile_requirements_ref: schema://... keyed by result_profile, matching how ImplementationResultPayload is already the software profile of WorkResult.
12. **No declarative infrastructure-retry seam at the invocation/attempt level: transient failures route through conductor cognition** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:8739 — 'publish_result | verify_compare_or_challenge | repair_or_escalate |' — the loop's failure path; FoundryRunPlan gets 'retry_policy_ref: policy://...' at :5175 but HarnessInvocation/Attempt get none
   - Recommendation: Temporal activities and Argo steps carry declarative retry (backoff, max attempts, non-retryable error classes) so mechanical failures never consume orchestrator reasoning. GoalRunProfile does carry recovery_policy_ref/stop_policy_ref/escalation_policy_ref (:7539-7541), but their semantics are unspecified and nothing binds retry at the object that actually fails: a harness crash, runtime loss, or provider 429 must round-trip the Goal Kernel's repair_or_escalate phase — burning tokens and latency on failures a policy could absorb. Add optional retry_policy_ref to HarnessInvocationEnvelope scoped to infrastructure-class failure (adapter/runtime/transport), with each retry an appended invocation record sharing the TaskBrief, explicitly distinct from semantic repair which rightly stays with the conductor loop.
13. **ContextHandoff.handoff_kind fuses transport taxonomy with object taxonomy, creating a second path for admitted objects** — `medium` · Better way *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:9003 — 'attempt_result | finding | resource_request | capability_offer |' — 15 kinds, several 1:1 with first-class admitted envelopes
   - Recommendation: finding, verifier_challenge, capability_offer, and frontier_update already have their own envelopes with room-admission spines; carrying them additionally as cell-to-cell packets creates two channels for the same fact. FindingEnvelope's branch_local status (:8436) does give findings a pre-admission state, but the handoff layer never says the referenced object's own status governs usability. Collapse handoff_kind to transport-level kinds — task_brief, result, blocker, review_request, decision_request, continuation_summary — and require object-shaped payloads to be refs to proposed-or-admitted objects whose own status governs usability; the payload_ref union at :9006-9009 already points at those envelopes, so the kind enum is the redundant layer. The software-specific kinds (diff_summary, test_result) in this generic enum repeat the TaskBrief overloading finding.
14. **NetworkGoalBudget tracks spend as four mutable decimals instead of the append-only ledger pattern the same file uses for exposure** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:7753 — 'authorized_amount: decimal' with reserved/admitted_spend/remaining as sibling mutable fields; contrast EvaluationExposureLedger's 'ledger_head_sequence' / 'ledger_head_root' at :7391-7392
   - Recommendation: Running totals as in-envelope mutable fields invite lost-update races between concurrent reservations and contradict the local-first/receipts physics — this is exactly why EvaluationExposureLedgerEnvelope (:7381-7394) models spend as an append-only entry chain with derived projections. Restructure: keep authorized_amount and denomination in the frozen contract; make reserved/admitted/remaining derived projections of the allocation_and_reservation_refs and contribution_delivery_and_settlement_refs the envelope already lists (:7763-7766), with a head sequence/root for CAS. Same fix, same file, already proven.
15. **GoalGroundingLoopEnvelope misnames the full pursuit loop — grounding is 2 of its 19 phases** — `low` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:8721 — 'Low-level orientation and course-correction loop for bounded goal-shaped work.' — phases include execute_attempt, publish_result, reconcile, continue_or_close
   - Recommendation: Rename to GoalPursuitLoopEnvelope (or GoalKernelLoopEnvelope, matching the doctrine name 'the concrete loop behind the Goal Kernel' at :8722). 'Grounding' describes only gather_grounding/inspect_state; a reader scanning the object index will file this as a retrieval/context object and miss that it is the execution heartbeat of the entire spine — the ID scheme goal_loop:// already uses the right noun. Keep goal_loop:// unchanged; add the old envelope name to the compatibility-adapter pattern the file already uses for TrainingEvidenceEligibility (:4778).
16. **HarnessAdapterEvent ordering rests on wall-clock timestamps with no per-invocation monotonic sequence** — `low` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:9172 — 'timestamp: iso8601' — no sequence, predecessor, or ordering-contract field on the event envelope
   - Recommendation: These events are 'durable coordination evidence' (:9150) and replay inputs, but clock skew, sub-second bursts, and retried emission make iso8601 insufficient for total order — Temporal's event history is ID-sequenced for exactly this reason, and this file already has two in-file precedents: the room spine's admitted_sequence (:8176) and RuntimeEventEnvelope's 'cursor: integer' (:3793). Add event_sequence: nonnegative_integer, monotonic per harness_invocation_ref and assigned at daemon admission, plus an optional predecessor commitment for gap detection. Cheap now; retrofitting ordering onto shipped adapters later means every adapter renegotiates its event contract.


## Part III.3 — Foundations: invariants, security, and physical safety

### Invariants, security/privacy invariants, verifiable bounded agency, physical action safety

**Files reviewed:** `foundations/invariants.md (300)` · `foundations/security-privacy-policy-invariants.md (415)` · `foundations/verifiable-bounded-agency.md (571)` · `foundations/physical-action-safety.md (555)`

**Current shape.** The facet defines IOI's safety spine in four layers. `foundations/invariants.md` is a registry of 34 canonical one-sentence invariants (INV-1..34) covering no-ambient-authority, leases-not-secrets, receipt/assurance ladders, federation/participation boundaries, corrigibility-style constitution/succession/persistence limits, and physical-action authority; each invariant names owner docs and a citation rule forbids re-derived wording elsewhere. `security-privacy-policy-invariants.md` is the security-domain application set: 15 sections of numbered invariants (alignment-security, authority, runtime, state, continuity, artifacts, model/provider rights, training, improvement campaigns, data/ontology, collaborative pursuit, connectors, marketplace, institutional learning, mainnet) plus privacy and execution-privacy doctrines. `verifiable-bounded-agency.md` states the alignment thesis: IOI claims execution-boundary alignment, not cognitive alignment — probabilistic cognition may propose, but consequential effects cross only a daemon-mediated, policy-checked, authority-scoped, receipted boundary, with credential isolation via wallet.network, proposal-mediated upgrades, separation-of-powers principals, and an explicit Non-Claims list. `physical-action-safety.md` defines a Simplex/RTA-shaped embodied contract: a two-speed governance/local split whose fast local side contains three distinct assurance strata (on-unit autonomy, deterministic motion, independent runtime-assurance/safety), a `SafetyEnvelope` binding safe-set invariants, safety monitor, command switch, recovery controller, WCET response bounds and failure-independence requirements, ten hard local invariants (local veto survives network loss; restart is not resumption), segment-commitment receipts, incidents, and a long unnumbered conformance bullet list. Implementation statuses are candidly mixed-to-speculative (physical two-speed execution is unimplemented beyond package-install admission).

**What it already gets right:**

- Claim-boundary honesty is rare and real: verifiable-bounded-agency.md explicitly disclaims solving model-internal alignment, lists seven Non-Claims, and rejects 'UI approval dialogs alone' and 'logs alone' — better epistemic hygiene than nearly all commercial agent-platform docs.
- INV-9's progressive assurance ladder (evidence < verification < acceptance < adjudication < settlement) plus INV-14 honest-posture/fail-closed is a genuinely strong anti-overclaiming discipline; most industry receipt/audit systems conflate 'logged' with 'proven'.
- Physical-action safety is structurally faithful Simplex/RTA: independent monitor/switch/recovery with WCET bounds, failure-independence requirements, ODD binding, local veto that survives network/wallet/ledger loss, restart-is-not-resumption, and ambiguous-effect reconciliation — ahead of any mainstream agent stack, which simply doesn't address embodiment.
- Corrigibility is engaged directly, not hand-waved: INV-21 (constitution outranks improvement), INV-26 (succession preserves bounds), INV-28 (self-preservation externally bounded), and the 'cannot remove its own kill switch or dissolution path merely because an eval score improved' line (verifiable-bounded-agency.md:341) map cleanly onto safe-interruptibility/corrigibility research.
- INV-33 and the Bounded Improvement Campaign invariants separate Search/Judgment/Authority with sealed evidence, epoch-frozen evaluators, and no candidate/evaluator mutual activation — stronger than current evals-governance practice at most labs.

**SOTA verdict: `near-sota`** — compared against: NIST SP 800-207 zero trust (PDP/PEP separation); OWASP LLM Top 10 (LLM01 prompt injection, insecure output handling); CaMeL (Debenedetti et al./Google DeepMind) and dual-LLM quarantine patterns; Willison's 'lethal trifecta'; Object-capability security literature (seL4, Fuchsia capability model); Simplex/runtime assurance (Sha 2001), ASTM F3269 bounded-behavior RTA; UL 4600 safety case; IEC 61508 SIL; ISO 13849 PL; ISO 10218 + ISO/TS 15066 collaborative robots; SAE J3016 takeover/handover research; NIST AI RMF; EU AI Act high-risk obligations; Safe interruptibility (Orseau & Armstrong) and corrigibility research; SLSA/Sigstore-style build provenance and remote attestation

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- Prompt injection is never named anywhere in non-archive canon (grep-verified). Generic input taint exists — INV-17 taints participant input, and MCP prompts normalize to untrusted inputs (README Non-Negotiable 13; connectors-tools/contracts.md:244) — but there is no control/data-flow separation (CaMeL/dual-LLM) and no context-provenance labels feeding effect admission; the number-one attack class (OWASP LLM01) for exactly this product category is never analyzed.
- No provenance-conditioned exfiltration control for in-scope misuse: environment-level egress postures (no_egress/declassified_proxy, egress_policy_ref) and connector send/spend approval requirements exist, but nothing conditions an admitted outbound effect on the provenance closure of its payload — a lease permitting private-context reads plus any outbound channel reproduces the lethal trifecta inside an admitted envelope.
- Enforcement-point integrity machinery exists (HypervisorOSBootReceipt binds the daemon binary hash; RuntimeAttestationReceipt; TEE attestation policy-required for enterprise secure placement) but is posture-specific and speculative; no registry invariant requires consequential receipts to bind daemon/policy-engine identity, and assurance claims do not automatically downgrade when the enforcement point is unattested.
- No safety-integrity-level taxonomy (IEC 61508 SIL / ISO 13849 PL) per stop condition or monitor/switch/recovery component, and no mandated e-stop proof-test cadence; `EmbodiedDeploymentAssuranceCase` (embodied-runtime.md) covers hazards, timing/fault assumptions, fault-injection results, and standards, but physical-action-safety.md's `runtime_assurance_profile_ref` is not typed to it.
- No takeover-time budget or operator-readiness evidence for human_on_loop supervision (SAE J3016 handover lesson).
- No per-invariant enforcement-class mapping (mechanical gate vs conformance test vs audit vs normative definition), so enforceable properties and aspirational prose are indistinguishable in the registry.
- EU AI Act / regulatory high-risk mapping for physical_action is not bound from the SafetyEnvelope; JurisdictionPolicyPack and the ecosystem-assurance owners exist and the deployment assurance case references them, but nothing in physical-action-safety.md routes an envelope to a jurisdiction pack.

**Findings (14, severity-ordered):**

1. **Prompt injection — the top attack class for this exact product — is never named or analyzed in the safety spine** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: Grep over non-archive canon finds zero occurrences of 'prompt injection'. Nearest treatments: docs/architecture/foundations/security-privacy-policy-invariants.md:317 — 'Tool outputs validate against declared schemas.' — and docs/architecture/components/connectors-tools/contracts.md:244, where prompts are 'always untrusted/tainted until schema, policy, and provenance checks pass.'
   - Recommendation: Schema validation and generic input taint are not content trust. INV-17 covers participant→room admission and Non-Negotiable 13 normalizes MCP prompts to untrusted inputs, but nothing covers content→model-context steering, which is the actual SOTA threat. Add a registry invariant (INV-35, 'Context provenance bounds proposal trust'): every ContextCell, tool output, connector payload, and web/room-derived artifact carries a provenance label (trusted-principal | admitted | untrusted); effect admission MUST be able to condition on the provenance closure of inputs that shaped the proposal. Require default-harness-profile.md to support CaMeL-style separation: the planning context that selects effectful actions never ingests raw untrusted content, only typed/quarantined extractions. Name OWASP LLM01 explicitly in security-privacy-policy-invariants.md.
2. **No provenance-conditioned egress control for in-scope exfiltration (the lethal trifecta survives the lease model)** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/verifiable-bounded-agency.md:163 — 'this worker may call this connector only under this policy hash'
   - Recommendation: Scoped leases bound blast radius but not in-scope misuse: a worker legitimately leased to read private context plus any outbound-capable action (external_message, connector write, URL fetch with query params, commit message) can exfiltrate within its envelope when steered by injected content. Partial controls exist — Connector Invariant 2 requires approval for external send/spend/publish (security-privacy-policy-invariants.md:315), and environment connectivity profiles carry egress postures (no_egress, declassified_proxy, egress_policy_ref in providers-and-environments.md) — but these are envelope-level and approval-level, not payload-derivation-level, and approvals themselves are steerable (see the presentation-binding finding). Add an egress-provenance gate: any effect at risk_class external_message or above whose payload derivation closure includes both private-context labels and untrusted-provenance labels fails closed to a step-up path (block, human approval with provenance disclosure, or projection-only transform per the Privacy Doctrine at security-privacy-policy-invariants.md:407). Bind the label closure into the effect receipt so exfiltration attempts are audit-visible.
3. **Registry mixes mechanically enforceable gates with normative/legal definitions and never says which is which** — `high` · Better way
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/invariants.md:139 — 'INV-18 — Multiplicity is not independence.'
   - Recommendation: INV-24 (fencing before promotion) is a mechanical gate; INV-18 and INV-31 are definitional/adjudicative norms no daemon can fail-closed on (detecting one principal behind many keys is a Sybil-detection research problem, not a gate). Tag every INV with an enforcement class — daemon-gate | conformance-test (CIRC/CEC/CPAS id) | audit-detectable | normative-definition — plus the named mechanism or detection path. This single change converts the registry from mixed prose into an answerable question ('what enforces INV-16?') and directly resolves the enforceable-vs-aspirational ambiguity that is this facet's core weakness.
4. **No registry invariant requires enforcement-point integrity evidence in consequential receipts** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/verifiable-bounded-agency.md:489 — 'independently verifiable rather than dashboard-asserted;'
   - Recommendation: Every guarantee in this facet reduces to 'the daemon enforced it,' and receipts are minted by that same daemon. The canon does carry integrity machinery — `HypervisorOSBootReceipt` binds boot epoch, image hash, and daemon binary hash (events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:1357), `RuntimeAttestationReceipt`/`NodeMeasurementReceipt` exist, and TEE attestation is a policy requirement for enterprise secure placement (runtime-nodes-tee-depin.md:265) — but HypervisorOS is speculative, these objects are posture-specific, and no INV-* requires enforcement-point identity in ordinary consequential receipts on default Type-2/3 deployments. Add a registry invariant ('The boundary proves itself'): consequential receipts bind the daemon build hash, policy-engine version, and runtime posture (attested-TEE | measured-boot | trusted-operator); assurance claims automatically downgrade to trusted-operator tier when unattested — mirroring INV-14's honest-posture rule applied to IOI itself. Without this, the 'can the actor prove it operated only inside the delegated envelope' claim (verifiable-bounded-agency.md:457) remains partially dashboard-asserted outside attested postures.
5. **invariants.md header promises per-invariant enforcement locations; no entry provides one** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/invariants.md:5 — 'each invariant lists where it is enforced today' vs invariants.md:28 — 'Owner application: [`../components/wallet-network/doctrine.md`]'
   - Recommendation: Every INV entry lists only 'Owner application' doc links — doctrine owners, not enforcement sites. Either add an 'Enforced at:' line per invariant citing code paths (as physical-action-safety.md:9 does with runtime_worker_package_install_admission.rs) and conformance IDs, or correct the header to 'each invariant names its owner application.' As written, the file front-matter makes an implementation claim the body does not support — exactly the honest-posture failure INV-14 forbids.
6. **Several sections of security-privacy-policy-invariants.md carry canonical wording with no stable IDs or registry links** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/security-privacy-policy-invariants.md:18 — 'The canonical one-sentence wording of cross-cutting invariants is owned by the invariant registry' vs :314 — 'Connector refresh tokens remain in wallet.network.'
   - Recommendation: The file is the declared canonical owner for security-domain invariants with an explicit deference rule (registry wording wins on conflict), and several sections do cite registry IDs (Continuity cites INV-23/24/25/26/28/29; Collaborative-Pursuit cites INV-16/17/18/20/30; State cites INV-15/22; Marketplace item 8 cites INV-31; Mainnet cites INV-27). But the Connector, File/Artifact, Model/Provider, Runtime, and most Marketplace items carry no stable IDs, no registry citations, and no owner-doc pointers — wording other docs cannot cite without re-deriving, the drift disease invariants.md:13-18 was created to cure. Assign stable IDs (SEC-C1.., SEC-A1..) to every numbered item; for each, either cite the registry INV it applies or promote it to the registry; move purely subject-local items (e.g., Model/Provider item 8's rights matrix) into their owner docs (model-router). Add the enforcement-class tag from the companion finding while renumbering.
7. **INV-34 requires 'fresh authority' per physical action; the safety doc permits cached authority and 'fresh' is never defined** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/invariants.md:279 — 'Every physical action binds fresh authority' vs physical-action-safety.md:88-89 — 'A cached authority result may permit the run to proceed'
   - Recommendation: The safety doc already carries most of the reconciliation machinery: envelope expiry, `max_remote_revocation_latency_ms` (physical-action-safety.md:258), and the rule that remote revocation must propagate within the declared maximum latency (:113-115). But 'fresh' is undefined in the registry, and since registry wording wins on conflict (invariants.md:17-18), a literal reading of INV-34 forbids the cached path the owner doc requires (per-action fresh leases are impossible at motor rates). Define freshness in the SafetyEnvelope: cached authority is valid iff within envelope expiry AND its last revocation check is younger than max_remote_revocation_latency_ms. Amend INV-34 wording to 'unexpired, revocation-latency-bounded authority' so the registry and the owner doc state one rule.
8. **No safety-integrity-level taxonomy or e-stop proof-test cadence; runtime_assurance_profile_ref is not typed to the existing assurance-case object** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/physical-action-safety.md:241 — 'runtime_assurance_profile_ref: assurance_profile://...'
   - Recommendation: The Simplex-shaped structure is right, and more of the assurance object exists than this doc reveals: `EmbodiedDeploymentAssuranceCase` (embodied-runtime.md 'Deployment-Bound Assurance'; wire shape in common-objects-and-envelopes.md) binds the exact graph, binaries, hardware, ODD, hazards, timing/fault assumptions, monitor/recovery implementations, fault-injection results, applicable standards, assessment, residual risk, and amendment lineage, and references the ecosystem-assurance/certification owners. Remaining gaps against named SOTA (IEC 61508 SIL, ISO 13849 PL, UL 4600, ASTM F3269): (a) physical-action-safety.md never names that case object — `runtime_assurance_profile_ref` is an untyped `assurance_profile://` ref, so the load-bearing link from SafetyEnvelope to hazard/timing/independence evidence is not canonical; (b) no required integrity level per stop_condition or per monitor/switch/recovery component anywhere in canon; (c) `last_tested_at` on EmergencyStopAuthority (:310) records history but nothing mandates a proof-test interval. Type the ref to the assurance case, add integrity-level and test-cadence fields, and link JurisdictionPolicyPack from the envelope so EU AI Act high-risk mapping is reachable.
9. **Physical-action conformance is an unnumbered ~20-bullet wall with no conformance file, unlike CIRC/CEC** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/physical-action-safety.md:429 — 'A conforming implementation must ensure:'
   - Recommendation: verifiable-bounded-agency.md:196-201 shows the pack's own pattern: deterministic boundaries get ID'd conformance docs (CIRC intent-resolution, CEC effect-execution in docs/conformance/hypervisor-core/, which today contains only intent-resolution, effect-execution, harness-profile-adapter, and institutional-learning-boundary). Physical action — the highest-consequence class — gets untestable prose bullets instead. Create docs/conformance/hypervisor-core/physical-action-safety.md with CPAS-1..n checks (one per bullet, each stating the observable pass evidence: receipt field, denied admission, timing measurement), and reduce this doc's section to a pointer. This also gives the speculative implementation status (:9) a concrete acceptance target instead of a vibe.
10. **Ordinary approvals bind request bytes but not what the human saw; only the high-risk guardian path has display binding** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/security-privacy-policy-invariants.md:51 — 'No policy-required approval without exact request hash, policy hash, scope, expiry, and revocation epoch.'
   - Recommendation: Exact-request-hash approval is necessary but insufficient: the realistic failure is a compromised worker composing a genuinely-approved bad request whose human-facing summary (title, description, rendered diff) was authored by injected content. The canon partially covers this: wallet-network/doctrine.md:391-404 requires the out-of-band guardian surface to display and sign the exact subject, action, resources/destination, budget, expiry, risk class, policy hash, and request hash — a WYSIWYS-shaped control. But that path is 'should be available' for high-risk actions only; the approval invariant at :51 requires no presentation binding for ordinary in-product approvals, hashes displayed to a human convey no semantics, and nothing tracks the provenance of free-text summary/description fields. Extend the mechanism: the ApprovalReceipt additionally binds a rendered-presentation commitment (template id + input hash) and the provenance labels of every displayed field; untrusted-provenance content may never supply the approval summary; funds/physical_action approvals require the guardian-style independent display channel as mandatory rather than available. Non-Claim 7 ('UI approval dialogs alone are sufficient authority', verifiable-bounded-agency.md:554) already gestures here — turn it into a mechanism.
11. **HumanSupervisionPolicy has modes but no takeover-time budget or operator-readiness evidence** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/physical-action-safety.md:318 — 'autonomous | monitored | human_on_loop | human_in_loop |'
   - Recommendation: SAE J3016 L3 experience is unambiguous: on-loop supervision without a bounded, tested takeover path is a known killer failure mode — the mode enum alone reproduces it. Hard local invariant 6 (loss of heartbeat/freshness invokes degrade-or-safe-state) and the OperatorHandoffReceipt cover system-detected degradation, but nothing bounds how fast a human supervisor must respond or evidences that they can. Add to HumanSupervisionPolicy: max_takeover_response_ms (envelope-verified against recovery_entry_deadline_ms), the declared behavior when the supervisor misses the deadline (auto minimum-risk maneuver, never continuation), supervisor concurrent-session limits, and an operator-readiness evidence ref (training/currency, analogous to last_tested_at for e-stop). The escalation_policy_ref (:323) is not a substitute because it carries no timing contract.
12. **Positioning copy ('root moat', 'IOI is selling', Category) embedded in a canonical foundations doc** — `medium` · Refactor *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/verifiable-bounded-agency.md:562 — 'The root moat is verifiable bounded agency under sovereign control.'
   - Recommendation: Sections 'Category', 'Product Promise', and 'Canonical Framing' (:504-566) are marketing framing, not architecture, and the repo already has a single-owner public-copy plane (canonicalMessaging.js per the public-copy program). Move those three sections to the messaging owner and leave a pointer. Two concrete harms: (a) drift — two owners for positioning language; (b) credibility — the doc's genuinely careful Claim Boundary and Non-Claims sections read weaker when followed by moat/selling prose, and skeptical security reviewers (this doc's target audience) will discount the whole file accordingly. While relocating, reconsider the 'zero-trust operating fabric' framing line (:561): the architecture maps reasonably onto NIST 800-207 (wallet/policy as PDP, daemon as PEP), but in a blockchain-adjacent product the phrase invites 'trustless' conflation that the Execution Privacy Doctrine's host-trust concession (security-privacy-policy-invariants.md:411) does not support.
13. **The invariant registry — the pack's constitution — has no amendment gate for itself** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/invariants.md:290 — 'When a doc needs one of these invariants, it writes one line — the ID'
   - Recommendation: INV-21 declares 'Constitution outranks improvement' for governed systems, yet the file owning all 34 constitutional wordings can be reworded by any ordinary doc edit; the Citation Rule governs citers, not the registry, and the 'Last alignment pass' header is a timestamp, not a gate. Add an Amendment section: INV wording changes require (a) a logged alignment-pass entry with rationale, (b) a reverse-citation sweep of every doc citing that ID (greppable because citations are ID-based), and (c) semantic-change vs clarification classification — semantic changes bump an invariant revision (INV-9.2) so downstream docs can pin. Without this, the registry is a drift engine with extra steps: single-owner wording that silently mutates under every citing doc.
14. **INV-19 'Complexity collapses when boundaries do' reads as a failure mode, not a design rule** — `low` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/invariants.md:147 — 'INV-19 — Complexity collapses when boundaries do.'
   - Recommendation: Intended meaning: when trust/coordination boundaries are absent, the machinery must collapse away (single-user local work stays simple). Natural reading: when boundaries fail/collapse, complexity collapses — a security incident description. Since these are one-sentence canonical wordings meant to be cited by ID across the pack, ambiguity multiplies. Rename to 'INV-19 — Machinery only at real boundaries' or 'Progressive machinery', keeping the existing body text which is already clear.


## Part III.4 — Foundations: bounded DAS and domain kernels

### Governed autonomous systems (bounded DAS) and domain kernels

**Files reviewed:** `foundations/governed-autonomous-systems.md (1228)` · `foundations/domain-kernels.md (520)`

**Current shape.** The two files define a "governed autonomous system" (bounded DAS): a constitution-bound autonomous institution whose intelligence may propose, route, and improve work, but whose consequential transitions become canonical only through declared ordering, policy, authority, evidence, and lifecycle paths, with admission strictly deterministic and intelligence strictly upstream. Its substrate is an "intelligent blockchain": a cryptographically continuous admission log binding monotonic sequence, expected-predecessor commitment, operation commitment, admission proof, state root, and receipt root per admitted operation — where ordering/finality (single_authority, replicated_single_authority, threshold_authority, bft_consensus, external_chain_finality) is a declared deployment profile, not the defining property; a PoA-1 single-node system still qualifies. domain-kernels.md casts the IOI kernel as an L0 substrate that instantiates many sovereign domains (aiagent.xyz, sas.xyz, ioi.ai, enterprise), each owning its own Agentgres operation log in an edge-in, fractal topology where IOI L1 is optional sparse settlement for explicitly enrolled systems. One system keeps one system_id across governed node membership: deployment profiles declare desired roles (admission writer, hot standby, replicas, verifiers, witnesses, consensus members) while observed membership envelopes with epochs, leases, catch-up receipts, and heartbeat-expiring readiness prove what is actually live. Failover doctrine requires a new writer epoch, catch-up/state-root proof, and prior-writer fencing before a promoted standby admits effects, and ambiguous partitions fail closed. Three coordination planes — continuity replication, same-system distributed work (leased claims, watermarks, embodied fleets), and AIIP cross-system federation — are profiles over the one substrate, and self-improvement is proposal-mediated rather than direct self-modification.

**What it already gets right:**

- Consensus-as-deployment-profile with a mandated deterministic transition function and intelligence strictly upstream is genuinely coherent — it mirrors the ABCI/Cosmos-SDK separation of app state machine from ordering, and it solves the LLM-nondeterminism-in-a-replicated-state-machine problem that virtually every other 'AI blockchain' project ignores.
- Durability and replication honesty is rare and correct: acks never claim unproven durability classes, quorum_replicated cannot be claimed by same-host replicas, and INV-23 forbids node count or replication from upgrading authority-distribution or finality claims — the exact opposite of industry marketing norms.
- Desired-vs-observed topology discipline (AutonomousSystemDeploymentProfileEnvelope vs AutonomousSystemNodeMembershipEnvelope with readiness evidence that expires) matches Kubernetes spec/status doctrine and explicitly kills the 'config says three replicas so we are HA' fallacy (gas:370-375).
- Fence-before-promotion (INV-24), fail-closed ambiguous partitions, and the two-gate distributed conformance milestone (continuity gate, then useful-work gate, before any AIIP federation claim, gas:412-420) put the CP safety-over-liveness choice on the record and sequence claims behind proofs.
- The Agentgres replication design (epoch fencing frames in the log, handshake carries epoch, ahead-replica refuses overwrite, deposed primary NACKed mid-stream) is a correctly shaped split-brain refusal mechanism at the storage boundary, and part of it is already built — with the admission core additionally bound to a strict determinism rule (no wall clock, no ambient randomness; nondeterminism only as recorded operation inputs) that keeps replay and deterministic simulation honest.

**SOTA verdict: `near-sota`** — compared against: Raft/Paxos (leader election, log matching, joint-consensus membership change, safety proofs); Kafka KRaft leader epochs / fencing checked by every follower and fetcher; Chubby/ZooKeeper lease-based fencing + Kleppmann's fencing-token-verified-at-the-resource rule; FoundationDB coordinators (durable external home for epoch/configuration state) and deterministic simulation testing; Tendermint/CometBFT + ABCI (pluggable consensus precedent; BFT-time for deterministic timestamps) and IBC (timeout/acknowledgement semantics for cross-chain interop); Temporal durable execution (exactly-once state transitions, at-least-once activities with idempotency keys); Kubernetes spec/status reconciliation and leader-election patterns; Spanner TrueTime / bounded-staleness reads; PACELC; Certificate Transparency / Trillian / QLDB verifiable single-writer logs; Virtual synchrony (Birman) view-membership semantics; CRDT/Bayou disconnected-merge literature

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No timing/clock model anywhere leases, heartbeats, and observation expiry are used: no clock-skew bound, lease-duration rule, or partial-synchrony assumption — Chubby-style 'new leader waits out old lease' is never stated. The built log-level handshake fencing needs no clock, and gas mandates operator-controlled failover absent a safe automatic protocol, but gas:374 still demands proving the old writer cannot continue, and heartbeat-expiring readiness has no declared skew bound.
