- No designated durable substrate for the writer-epoch/membership compare-and-swap that must survive writer loss (FDB coordinators, ZooKeeper, Raft joint consensus all name one); the epoch lives in envelopes whose canonical store is the failed writer's own domain.
- Fencing is enforced at the Agentgres replication handshake and chain receipts carry the admitting writer_epoch, but nothing binds writer_epoch into wallet.network capability leases or external effect gates, so a deposed-but-live writer can still fire consequential external effects — the exact hole Kleppmann's fencing-token rule closes.
- Lost-suffix semantics on promotion below quorum durability are undefined: acks are durability-class-honest, so nothing lies about what was durable, but clients holding receipts for acknowledged-then-lost operations have no defined divergence/compensation object (Kafka unclean-election truncation and Raft's commit rule make this explicit).
- Substantial protocol content is deferred to unwritten policy:// refs (failure detection, promotion authorization, conflict, liveness, fault model). The built Agentgres fenced primary/standby protocol partially fills this for the replicated_single_authority data plane, but SOTA ships protocols with state machines, proofs, or simulation conformance, and the deterministic-simulation harness remains a named gate, not a shipped battery.
- No deterministic time rule in the foundations canon for admission under threshold/BFT profiles: Agentgres's admission core is already forbidden from reading a wall clock (time only as recorded operation inputs), but who assigns the binding commitment timestamp and its validity bounds is unstated (CometBFT solves this with proposer-attached BFT-time).
- disconnected_autonomy partition mode with a rejoin 'state merge' policy is irreconcilable with a linear expected-predecessor commitment chain unless merge means re-admission of proposals; the embodied-runtime rejoin contract already models re-admission, but the generic field name and the general chain doctrine leave CRDT/Bayou-class semantics neither adopted nor explicitly rejected.
- AIIP defines timeout-to-reconciliation, idempotency/causation, and separately-authorized compensation at the sovereign boundary, but the Hypervisor Node settlement section never binds those contracts to co-hosted chain interop and states no atomicity posture (IBC and saga literature define these).

**Findings (14, severity-ordered):**

1. **Fencing stops at the Agentgres log; the authority plane and external effects are never fenced** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:1539 — '`hot_standby` cannot admit writes until a governed epoch promotion fences the previous writer.'
   - Recommendation: Fencing is defined only against write admission (concretely at the Agentgres replication handshake per agentgres/doctrine.md:279-285), and chain receipts do already bind the admitting writer_epoch (events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:2462, 2505) — but a deposed writer daemon still holding unexpired wallet.network capability leases can keep firing consequential external effects (spend, connectors, actuation) that never traverse a replica handshake, and identity-access-and-metering.md carries no epoch or fencing binding at all. Add an invariant: writer_epoch is a fencing token verified at every consequential effect gate — capability-lease validation for external effects is epoch-scoped, and promotion requires expiry/revocation receipts for the prior writer's effect-bearing leases (the Chubby wait-out rule). This is Kleppmann's check-the-token-at-the-resource rule extended to IOI's authority plane; the receipt half of the fix already exists for chain-admitted transitions.
2. **No durable home for the epoch/membership compare-and-swap that must survive writer loss** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:1497 — 'membership_epoch: nonnegative_integer'
   - Recommendation: Bootstrap circularity: membership records, writer epochs, and promotion decisions are Agentgres-admitted objects, but the canonical Agentgres writer is exactly the node that just failed. Who durably CASes epoch N -> N+1 such that two concurrent promotions cannot both succeed? Raft solves this with joint consensus; FoundationDB with an external coordinator quorum; Kubernetes with etcd. Partial mitigations exist — `ambiguous_partition_response` is hardcoded fail_closed, promotion binds `required_authority_refs` (a wallet.network/governance decision that serializes manual promotions in practice), and OrderingFinalityRecoveryEnvelope's predecessor fields are explicitly 'compare-and-swap inputs' — but that envelope excludes single-writer promotion (coe:1656-1659), and under `preauthorized_policy` automation nothing named arbitrates two concurrent epoch-N+1 mints. Require FailoverProfile to designate the epoch/membership CAS substrate — a witness quorum with compare-and-swap semantics, wallet.network as lease/epoch authority, or an external coordination service — and state that a fencing epoch minted without a durable CAS store is not a fence.
3. **'Prove the old writer cannot continue' is unachievable without a timing model the canon never states** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/governed-autonomous-systems.md:374 — 'must carry a new fencing epoch and prove the old writer cannot continue before it admits effects'
   - Recommendation: In an asynchronous network you cannot prove a remote process has stopped; you can only make its continued actions inadmissible (epoch checks at every effect target) or time-bound them (leases under a declared clock-skew bound). The built Agentgres layer already implements the achievable version — epoch verified at every replica handshake, deposed primary NACKed mid-stream (agentgres/doctrine.md:279-285) — and gas:374-375 mandates operator-controlled failover when no safe automatic protocol exists, which blunts the immediate risk. But the canon uses leases, heartbeats, and observation_expires_at everywhere while stating no max clock skew, lease-duration rule, or partial-synchrony assumption (the only skew constant in the pack is embodied-runtime's max_skew_ms) — the load-bearing physics of Chubby, Spanner, and every lease-based system. Reword gas:374 to 'render the prior writer's effects inadmissible,' and add a required timing_assumptions block (max skew, heartbeat interval, lease TTL, wait-out rule) to AutonomousSystemFailoverProfileEnvelope before preauthorized_policy automation is exercised. Without it, INV-24's automated branch is an aspiration, not a protocol.
4. **Lost-suffix semantics on promotion are undefined: receipts can outlive the chain that issued them** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:1573 — 'minimum_durability: buffered | device_flush | replicated_same_host | quorum_replicated'
   - Recommendation: single_writer_promotion legally proceeds at minimum_durability below quorum_replicated, so operations acked to clients (with receipts) at device_flush can be absent from the promoted standby. Acks are durability-class-honest (every admission ack carries its durability class per agentgres/doctrine.md), so the loss is within the declared contract and no receipt lied — but the new chain still forks from acked history, and while cryptographic continuity makes the divergence detectable, no object defines it. Add an OrphanedSuffixRecord/DivergenceRecord: promotion must publish the fencing point (last verified sequence + state root); receipts beyond it become provable orphan claims routed into a mandated reconciliation/compensation path (the ambiguous-effect reconciliation machinery in README NN-22 is the natural mount point); and profiles permitting acks above the promotion durability class must declare RPO greater than zero in continuity_targets. This mirrors Kafka's unclean-election truncation being surfaced rather than silent, and Raft's committed-entry rule.
5. **gas's universal room-genesis rule and domain-kernels' no-per-room-blockchain default are reconcilable, but the texts pull apart and the boundary is undefined** — `medium` · Contradiction *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/domain-kernels.md:489 — 'A GoalRun, attempt, participant, or room does not receive its own blockchain by default' vs docs/architecture/foundations/governed-autonomous-systems.md:552 — 'open or active only after its system_id, genesis, constitution, active release/profile refs, and cryptographically continuous admitted room state are bound'
   - Recommendation: This is not a strict contradiction: dk:310-315 supplies the reconciliation model — a hosted OutcomeRoom keeps its own logical room system_id and constitution while the host domain operates its ordering/admission state, so a genesis-bound room need not have its own blockchain, and 'cryptographically continuous admitted room state' can be satisfied by host-chain admission. But the surface texts pull apart: gas:551-552 reads as a universal precondition on any open/active room; gas:141's genesis-tax exemption list (Session, Project, AutomationSpec, stand-alone GoalRun, headless WorkRun) conspicuously omits rooms; and neither file defines where a 'durable OutcomeRoom' (genesis required) ends and a 'temporary collaboration aggregate' (no system contract) begins — an adoption-relevant ceremony boundary left implicit. Fix: state in gas that hosted admission per dk NN-7/NN-8 satisfies the room-continuity requirement without a per-room chain, define the threshold at which a collaboration must claim the bounded-institution lifecycle, and re-run the alignment pass. Do not resolve by folding durable rooms under the host system_id — dk:312 ('host identity does not replace logical room identity') deliberately forbids that.
6. **The hard protocol content lives in dozens of unwritten policy:// refs — schema rigor masking a protocol vacuum** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:1710 — 'fault_model_ref: policy://...' (with liveness, failure-detection, catch-up, conflict, and promotion rules likewise deferred)
   - Recommendation: Failure detection, promotion authorization, conflict resolution, liveness, fault model, and partition behavior are policy pointers in the foundations envelopes — a drift engine where each implementation invents incompatible semantics behind identical schemas. The gap is narrower than it first appears: agentgres/doctrine.md:278-301 already documents a built fenced primary/standby protocol (epoch fencing frames, handshake catch-up by offset streaming, ahead-replica refusal, mid-stream NACK of a deposed primary, receipted promotion at epoch+1) and gates the consensus tier behind a named deterministic-simulation harness — a real partial reference resolution for replicated_single_authority's data plane, and something dk:443's dual-writer conformance test can actually test against. What remains missing: name that built protocol from the foundations envelopes as the default resolution of those refs for single_authority and replicated_single_authority, and ship the unwritten pieces — failure detection, promotion authorization, liveness, timing assumptions — as one reference protocol document each with message/receipt sequences, state diagrams, and conformance test vectors, in the spirit of the Raft paper or FoundationDB's simulation battery. Schemas describe protocols; they are not protocols.
7. **disconnected_autonomy plus 'rejoin and state merge' is irreconcilable with a linear expected-predecessor chain** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3281 — 'fail_closed | read_only | bounded_local_execution | disconnected_autonomy' with 'rejoin_and_state_merge_policy_ref: policy://...' (line 3283)
   - Recommendation: A partitioned member operating in disconnected_autonomy accumulates state that cannot be 'merged' into a chain whose every transition binds an expected predecessor commitment — merge of divergent histories is CRDT/Bayou territory the ordering profiles do not support. The embodied plane already models the correct answer: embodied-runtime.md requires a disconnected cell to operate only on its last admitted membership/watermark/leases (never minting or extending leases from elapsed wall time) and requires rejoining units to report last accepted epoch, watermarks, effect identifiers, and ambiguous effects for reconciliation before any new allocation — re-admission, not merge. Generalize that: state that disconnected members produce only branch-local proposals, attempts, and evidence (never admitted chain state); rejoin is re-admission through the single admission path with duplicate-effect controls; rename rejoin_and_state_merge_policy_ref to rejoin_and_readmission_policy_ref to kill the merge implication; and say explicitly in gas 'Multi-Node Deployment' that CRDT-style state merge is out of scope.
8. **No canonical read-consistency vocabulary; authority checks over stale projections are unaddressed** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/governed-autonomous-systems.md:365 — 'replication/durability class, consistency and read-watermark rules'
   - Recommendation: Watermark machinery exists — RuntimeAssignmentEnvelope's state_and_partition block binds required_state_watermark_ref, minimum_operation_offset, and state-freshness policy, and the Postgres bridge stamps SELECTs with a freshness watermark — but consistency levels are only ever a policy:// ref (coe:1461), canonical-enums.md has no read-consistency enum, and nothing states which read class policy/authority evaluation must use. A policy check evaluated against a stale projection can authorize an effect the canonical state (e.g., a fresh revocation) forbids — the exact hazard bounded-staleness systems like Spanner and FoundationDB read-versions make explicit. Add a read-consistency enum to canonical-enums.md (writer_linearizable | watermark_bounded | projection_stale_ok), require consequential effect gating to read at writer_linearizable (or bind the evaluated watermark into the admission receipt so a stale-read authorization is detectable post hoc), and reference it from consistency_and_read_watermark_policy_ref.
9. **Deterministic admission has no canonical time rule, which breaks threshold/BFT replication of freshness-dependent policy** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:1683 — 'deterministic_transition_function_ref: artifact://... | cid://...'
   - Recommendation: Admission is declared deterministic, but admission includes oracle-freshness windows, lease expiry, and heartbeat evaluation — all wall-clock-dependent. Under threshold_authority or bft_consensus, replicas evaluating 'is this evidence fresh?' against local clocks will diverge and consensus stalls or forks. Agentgres's component doctrine already sets the mechanism direction (agentgres/doctrine.md:225-227: no wall clock inside admission logic; nondeterminism, including time, enters only as recorded operation inputs), but the foundations canon never lifts this into the ordering profile, and nothing states who assigns the binding timestamp under threshold/BFT or its validity bounds. CometBFT solves this with proposer-attached BFT-time. Add the canon rule to the cryptographic_continuity block of OrderingAdmissionFinalityProfileEnvelope: the operation/batch commitment carries a sequencer/proposer-assigned timestamp; the deterministic transition function is forbidden from reading a local clock; all freshness, expiry, and window checks evaluate against commitment time.
10. **'Intelligent blockchain' for a PoA-1 hash-chained log invites the exact credibility attack the pack elsewhere avoids** — `medium` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/domain-kernels.md:63 — 'A single-authority/PoA-1 system is still an intelligent blockchain when those boundaries are real'
   - Recommendation: To the distributed-systems community, a single-writer hash-chained admission log is a verifiable log (Certificate Transparency, Trillian, QLDB lineage) — and 'blockchain with one writer' is the canonical strawman critics deride (QLDB itself died partly of this positioning, a lesson agentgres/doctrine.md itself records with ''immutable ledger' is never the headline'). The pack partially defends against this already: gas:77-79 instructs leading externally with 'bounded distributed autonomous systems' and using 'intelligent blockchain' only as the technical classification. But the classification itself (dk:63, INV-29) still names a PoA-1 log a blockchain, and whitepaper-lineage copy will inherit it. Keep the already-dominant canonical term 'governed autonomous-system chain' / 'governed commitment chain' as the classification for all profiles, and reserve 'intelligent blockchain' strictly for profiles with distributed ordering (threshold/BFT/external finality). Update gas:19-28, gas:77-79, dk:44-65, and INV-29's phrasing — this is an explicit proposal to change that invariant's naming, not a silent violation of it.
11. **Single-writer promotion doctrine is restated in four places with diverging strength** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/domain-kernels.md:443 — 'legitimate only with a declared promotion rule, catch-up/root proof, new writer epoch, prior-writer fencing, recovery objectives, and a conformance test'
   - Recommendation: The promotion contract lives in gas:373-375, dk:441-448, INV-24, and AutonomousSystemFailoverProfileEnvelope — and they already differ: dk demands 'a conformance test that excludes dual writers,' which the failover envelope does not carry as a field (the ordering profile has only generic conformance_receipt_refs); gas demands proving the old writer 'cannot continue,' which INV-24 softens to fencing. This restatement drift violates the pack's own Citation Rule (invariants.md: 'Docs must not re-derive canonical wording'). Make the failover envelope in common-objects-and-envelopes.md the sole normative statement, add a dual_writer_exclusion_conformance_ref field to it, and reduce gas and dk to one-sentence citations of INV-24 plus a link. Four owners of one protocol is a measured drift engine in a pack whose own house rule is single ownership.
12. **Five ordering profiles, embodied fleets, and federation are canonized while nearly everything is 'planned or speculative'** — `low` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/governed-autonomous-systems.md:9 — 'autonomous-system chains, federated admission, and local operational-finality domains planned or speculative'
   - Recommendation: Canon carries envelope surface (recovery classes, proof schemas, view/round rules) for threshold_authority, bft_consensus, and external_chain_finality before a single two-node single-writer deployment exists, and every future edit must keep five profiles coherent. The pack does have discipline here already: every file carries doctrine-status/implementation-status headers, and _meta/canon-to-code-delta.md tracks object-level canon-to-code deltas with a surfaces-resume-only-when-pulled rule. What's missing is the enum-level marker: canonical-enums.md and the envelopes carry no 'reserved: enum stable, semantics non-normative' label on the three non-single-writer profiles, so their sub-objects accrete normative weight the file headers don't scope. Mark those three profiles reserved in canonical-enums.md, gas, and dk, and freeze their envelope sub-objects behind that label until the continuity and useful-work gates (gas:412-420) pass. This preserves the profile architecture while stopping speculative surfaces from accreting maintenance obligations.
13. **Hypervisor Node 'settles local interop' between chains without binding the pack's own timeout/reconciliation contracts** — `low` · SOTA gap *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/governed-autonomous-systems.md:315 — 'The Hypervisor Node coordinates many governed autonomous-system chains. It settles local interop between them'
   - Recommendation: Task offers, handoffs, and cross-system module invocations between co-hosted chains are each admitted to a different chain — a cross-log coordination problem. The semantics largely exist elsewhere: aiip.md mandates sequence/idempotency/causation fields, 'a timeout after dispatch enters the declared reconciliation path,' compensation as a separately authorized receipted action, and a mandatory effect-recovery class per external effect; README NN-22 types effect recovery. But the 'Hypervisor Node Settlement' section (gas:313-327) never binds those contracts, states no atomicity posture, and leaves one case unowned: interop between co-hosted chains of one principal, which is not clearly 'independently governed' and hence arguably not AIIP under INV-32. Add two sentences to that section: node-level interop is compensations-only (no multi-chain atomic commit, matching gas:409-410's refusal of global physical atomicity), and offer/handoff records between co-hosted chains carry the same timeout and acknowledgement receipt obligations as AIIP packets regardless of whether the sovereignty boundary is crossed.
14. **'Settlement' names three unrelated things across one section** — `low` · Rename
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/foundations/governed-autonomous-systems.md:297 — 'Settlement means different things at different layers.'
   - Recommendation: The doc diagnoses its own overload and then keeps it: chain-level admission of module invocations and memory writes is 'settlement' (gas:299-311), node-level mediation is 'settlement' (gas:313-327), and economic finality is 'settlement' (gas:329-343). In both finance and distributed systems, settlement connotes final value transfer; calling a workflow transition 'settled' primes exactly the wrong finality reading the Non-Negotiables then have to un-teach (gas:1182-1185). Rename the first to 'Local Admission and Operational Finality' and the second to 'Inter-System Mediation,' reserving 'settlement' for the economic layer. Pure heading/vocabulary change in gas plus a vocabulary.md entry; no object renames required.


## Part III.5 — Foundations: institutional learning and governed improvement

### Institutional learning boundary, worker training lifecycle, mixture of workers

**Files reviewed:** `foundations/institutional-learning-boundary.md (416)` · `foundations/worker-training-lifecycle.md (567)` · `foundations/mixture-of-workers.md (363)`

**Current shape.** This facet defines how an institution's intelligence compounds without leaking. institutional-learning-boundary.md defines the Enterprise Learning Boundary: InstitutionalLearningBoundaryProfile, a versioned compiled policy overlay (explicitly not a new runtime or authority plane) that intersects source rights, data-use policy, model-route rights, custody, retention, export, and revocation into one fail-closed decision context — unknown or conflicting rights deny training/distillation/export by default, rights travel through derivative lineage, revocation propagates via an Agentgres impact graph with honest non-claims about machine unlearning, and every profile must define an executable Model Independence Test (swap Model A for Model B, restore only institution-controlled state, measure the eval delta). worker-training-lifecycle.md defines Worker Training as a supply-creation lifecycle deliberately broader than fine-tuning — prompt optimization, retrieval curation, verifier tuning, adapters, fine-tuning, distillation — operated through Foundry as a ten-stage capture→specify→generate→curate→distill→train→evaluate→bind→publish→monitor pipeline whose output is a signed WorkerManifest with training lineage, evaluation receipts, and policy envelope; training never grants execution authority. mixture-of-workers.md defines MoW as labor routing over Worker Compositions (manifest + model route + tools + policy + verifier obligations + settlement identity), explicitly distinct from MoE token routing, with Auto/Pinned/Compare execution policies, Sparse Worker Categories as benchmarked niche labor markets, receipted routing decisions with candidate-set commitments, disclosed first-party seed supply, and ContributionReceipt-based attribution and sparse settlement. All three files honestly declare implementation status as planned or partial.

**What it already gets right:**

- The directional two-question rights model — provider-use rights and customer-use rights evaluated independently per route (ILB:175-193, WTL:196-202) — is more precise than typical 2026 enterprise DPA/ZDR checklists, correctly capturing that a provider no-training promise does not grant the customer distillation rights and vice versa. The Model-Route Commercial Rights enums (canonical-enums.md:486-524) back this with closed value families, not prose.
- Honest non-claims are ahead of industry marketing: revocation receipts do not prove forgetting (ILB:267-271), residual public exposure stays visible instead of being erased from the ledger narrative, receipts cannot observe hidden provider training (WTL:216-218), and differential privacy 'cannot create missing rights' (ILB:289-291) — all consistent with 2026 machine-unlearning research consensus.
- The Model Independence Test plus the golden conformance demo (canary seeding, blocked-before-egress proof, packet-capture assertion, Model B continuity — ILB:319-331, 368-391, backed by a real runner interface in conformance/hypervisor-core/institutional-learning-boundary.md) is an executable, falsifiable proof no mainstream data-governance platform (Collibra, Purview) offers.
- MoW genuinely answers the 'rebrand of model routing' critique in substance: the Worker Composition as tested/routed unit, the four-kinds-of-plurality table (MoW:70-77) refusing to count ten IOI-owned workers as federation, disclosed first-party seed supply with anti-self-dealing rules (MoW:225-238), and routing receipts with candidate-set and affiliation commitments (MoW:260-271) exceed anything OpenRouter/NotDiamond-class routers or agent marketplaces publish.
- The rights/eligibility machinery is schema-real, not vaporware prose: LearningSourceRightsClaimEnvelope has a closed permitted_uses enum and derivative_disposition lattice, and LearningEvidenceEligibilityEnvelope has intended_use, learning_use_posture, and contamination_posture enums (common-objects-and-envelopes.md:4363-4460, 4689-4785), giving fail-closed intersection actual computable substrate on those dimensions.

**SOTA verdict: `near-sota`** — compared against: Microsoft Purview / Collibra / OneTrust (enterprise data classification and governance — coarse sensitivity labels, no machine-readable training-use rights); OpenAI/Anthropic/Azure enterprise terms: zero-data-retention and no-training-by-default DPAs (the real 2026 mechanism enterprises rely on); W3C ODRL rights-expression language and its known policy-composition/conflict-semantics problems; EU AI Act Art. 53 GPAI training-content summaries (in force Aug 2025), DSM Directive Art. 4 TDM opt-outs, GDPR Art. 17 erasure, DORA ICT exit-strategy testing; RLHF/DPO/verifier-reward (RLVR) post-training pipelines and LoRA/PEFT adapter ecosystems; Model registries and experiment tracking (MLflow, W&B), model cards, datasheets for datasets, MLCommons Croissant dataset metadata; LLM routing SOTA: RouteLLM, FrugalGPT-style verified cascades, NotDiamond, OpenRouter; MoE routing literature (Switch, Mixtral); Agent interop and marketplaces: Google A2A Agent Cards, MCP, GPT Store / Salesforce AgentExchange; Benchmark-integrity lessons: SWE-bench contamination, LMArena live eval, Kaggle private leaderboards; Federated learning (Flower/FedML) and DP-SGD as cross-tenant learning controls; C2PA and the Data Provenance Initiative for content/dataset provenance

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- The facet's foundations docs omit preference-optimization/RL post-training: neither worker-training-lifecycle.md's supported training profiles nor its stage 6 methods name preference datasets, reward models, DPO, or verifier-reward RL. The component owner components/hypervisor/foundry.md does canonize 'preference optimization' and 'environment RL' as staged training with RL/verifier trainer backends, so the gap is a foundations-to-component alignment hole rather than a pack-wide absence
- No bridge from what enterprises actually have (Purview/Collibra classification labels, contract repositories) to LearningSourceRightsClaimEnvelopes — no rights-onboarding or bulk-claim doctrine, so the fail-closed default is deny-everything on day one
- No regulatory artifact mapping: the pack builds parallel machinery but never maps rights claims to EU AI Act training-content summaries, revocation receipts to GDPR erasure evidence, or the Model Independence Test to DORA exit testing — the compliance deliverables that drive 2026 enterprise training-data governance budgets
- No benchmark contamination, private-holdout, or rotation discipline bound to Sparse Worker Category routing eligibility, though eligibility is earned through benchmark performance; the marketplace doc defines benchmark staleness and rebenchmark-on-composition-change, and Evaluations owns sealed-holdout/rotation machinery, but neither is wired into category eligibility
- No standard dataset-metadata binding (Croissant/datasheets) even though model cards are named in the Artifact Packaging Graph
- Federated learning is never named as an aggregate-learning control in the cross-tenant section, though it is the SOTA mechanism for exactly that problem

**Findings (14, severity-ordered):**

1. **The fail-closed rights model has no rights-onboarding path: it presumes a rights-claim supply chain no enterprise has** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: foundations/institutional-learning-boundary.md:166 — 'unknown, expired, conflicting, or unsupported source rights -> no training, distillation, cross-tenant learning, publication, or export'
   - Recommendation: The schema is implementable, and LearningSourceRightsClaimEnvelope's subject_refs already admits connector://-scoped subjects with source_class buckets, so scoped bulk claims are schema-expressible — but no doctrine says so, and 2026 enterprises hold Purview/Collibra-style classification labels and contract PDFs, not per-source training-use rights; with deny-by-default, day one is deny-everything. Add a 'Rights Onboarding' section to institutional-learning-boundary.md owned by Ontology/Data governance: (1) scoped bulk claims — one LearningSourceRightsClaimEnvelope over a connector scope + source_class ('all employee-authored docs under IP-assignment policy X'); (2) classification-mapping DataRecipes translating existing sensitivity labels into claim templates with human attestation receipts; (3) a named Governance adjudication path out of the `disputed` status (the envelope defines the status, and the Product Projection only says Ontology/Data 'show' disputed material — no doc defines who resolves it); (4) an explicit statement of expected claim granularity.
2. **'Most-restrictive deterministic intersection' is undefined over ref-valued policy dimensions, collapsing to total denial on real heterogeneous corpora** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: foundations/institutional-learning-boundary.md:104-106 — 'Effective policy is the most-restrictive deterministic intersection. A conflict, missing required contract, or indeterminate right denies the disputed use.'
   - Recommendation: The quoted rule governs profile inheritance, and per-source intersection is extended by ILB:186-188 ('outputs inherit the intersection of every contributing source and route') and WTL:56-58 ('most restrictive intersection of every input'). Intersection is computable over the closed permitted_uses set, but LearningSourceRightsClaimEnvelope also carries retention_policy_ref, deletion_or_forget_policy_ref, and jurisdiction_and_region_refs — opaque refs with no meet or comparison operator. WTL:417-419 partially anticipates this ('Curation computes rather than unions constraints: every accepted row keeps its source-rights, eligibility, retention, export, revocation, and derivative lineage refs'), which suggests an apply-all-obligations conjunction semantics, but no doc states it; as written, two non-identical retention policies are 'indeterminate' and deny, so any corpus mixing sources with different retention policies (i.e., every real corpus) risks failing closed. ODRL's decade of unresolved conflict-composition semantics is the cautionary anchor. Define canonical comparable forms per dimension in canonical-enums.md: retention as (max_duration, region_set) with min/intersection; jurisdiction as region-set intersection; declare the ordering of derivative_disposition. Require claims to normalize to these at admission; reserve conflict→deny for genuinely non-normalizable custom policies.
3. **Worker Training's canonical definition annexes change classes the same file routes around Foundry via the improvement plane** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: foundations/worker-training-lifecycle.md:18 — 'Worker Training is broader than model fine-tuning. A worker can be improved by:' vs :170-174 — 'Only some proposals need Foundry. A small tool-call refinement, workspace skill, Agent Wiki memory, route preference, verifier rule, or workflow patch may be admitted directly through governance.'
   - Recommendation: The canonical definition (lines 18-37) claims prompt optimization, route-policy training, verifier tuning, and workflow refinement as Worker Training, but lines 170-174 say those exact classes may bypass Foundry through the Improvement Proposal Plane, and only model training/distillation/dataset/benchmark/packaging 'should route through Foundry'. The file is partly aware of the tension: its own three-tier taxonomy (lines 228-247, 'Do not collapse all learning into model training') reserves 'model/worker training' for Foundry-governed work while distinguishing instance memory and package improvement. But the Canonical Definition at the top still labels the whole umbrella 'Worker Training' while the file owns 'training-vs-mutation doctrine' — so 'Worker Training' both is and is not the Foundry lifecycle within one owner file. Narrow the canonical definition to Foundry-governed supply creation (dataset, distillation, training, benchmark, packaging, manifest binding) and demote the lightweight methods to cross-references into the improvement plane, or explicitly rename the broad umbrella 'worker improvement' and reserve 'Training' for Foundry-governed builds — the three-tier taxonomy at 228-247 already supplies the vocabulary.
4. **A 25-item product-primitive catalog lives inside a foundations doctrine file, overlapping the Foundry component owner** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: foundations/worker-training-lifecycle.md:294 — 'Hypervisor Foundry should expose these product primitives:'
   - Recommendation: Lines 294-362 enumerate Training Orchestrator, Training Copilot, Batch Planner, Gate Console, Model Bake-Off, Cost/Quality Simulator, Worker Card Builder, Human Review Queue, etc. — UI/product surface inventory, not lifecycle doctrine, and outside this file's declared owner claim (line 4: lifecycle, training-vs-mutation doctrine, receipts, profile semantics, lineage semantics). components/hypervisor/foundry.md is the component owner and already carries its own Surface Shape IA (foundry.md:173-215) that overlaps this catalog under divergent names — Dataset Workbench vs Dataset Factory, Trial And Checkpoint Console vs Trials/Checkpoints, Teacher Session Log vs Teacher Sessions — so the two-owner vocabulary drift the pack's single-owner discipline exists to prevent is already visible. Move the product-primitive catalog (and the 'Hypervisor Foundry Product Doctrine' promise ladder at 257-293) to components/hypervisor/foundry.md and reconcile the two vocabularies there, leaving worker-training-lifecycle.md with the canonical stages, training-vs-mutation doctrine, rights semantics, and receipt/lineage contracts it declares itself owner of.
5. **Sparse Worker Category routing eligibility is benchmark-earned with no contamination, private-holdout, or rotation discipline** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: foundations/mixture-of-workers.md:212-214 — 'Routing eligibility is earned through benchmark performance, receipt completeness, policy compatibility, price, runtime posture, and reputation.'
   - Recommendation: Benchmark-gated market access is a direct Goodhart target: sellers will train on or overfit to category suites (SWE-bench contamination and LMArena gaming are the named lessons). The category definition list (lines 198-209) has benchmark suite and rubric but no integrity fields. Adjacent machinery exists but is not bound to categories: domains/aiagent/worker-marketplace.md defines benchmark staleness, a disputed benchmark status, and rebenchmark-on-composition-change (:387, :960, :981), and components/hypervisor/evaluations.md owns sealed-holdout exposure accounting with rotation/declassification policy (:174-198) — but evaluations.md explicitly does not own marketplace truth (:111), and nothing requires contamination or private-split discipline for eligibility-bearing category scores. Add holdout_rotation_policy, a private-split requirement for eligibility-bearing scores, contamination_posture (reusing the existing enum in LearningEvidenceEligibilityEnvelope), and challenge-triggered re-benchmarking to the SparseWorkerCategory contract; state that public-suite scores alone cannot maintain routing eligibility. The pack already declares sealed holdouts ineligible as training evidence — mirror that discipline on the marketplace side.
6. **Contribution valuation doctrine promises marginal-information attribution nobody can compute or defend in disputes** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: foundations/mixture-of-workers.md:303-304 — 'Credit follows accepted marginal information and derivation, not only the winning run.'
   - Recommendation: Marginal-information credit across planner/executor/verifier/replication graphs is Shapley-value attribution — intractable, unstable, and adversarially gameable. The file does gate economic effects behind a declared assurance path (attribution → evidence → 'verification under a named rule/version' → acceptance → challenge/adjudication, MoW:291-299) and labels the payout formula as reference components ('may include'), but quality_delta_bonus and royalty_share (lines 328-335) still appear with no named measurement rule, and 'credit follows accepted marginal information' is stated as doctrine. When settlement is real money, an undefined valuation rule becomes a dispute engine. Canonize a v1 mechanism: declared per-category fee schedules (invocation_fee + metered_compute + accepted-work bonus at fixed role rates), and explicitly classify quality-delta and marginal-information credit as a later assurance class requiring a named, versioned attribution rule before it may affect payout or reputation. State the limitation the way the file already states benchmark non-claims.
7. **The MoW routing runtime has no named component owner — the exact gap that would let it be built as a thin model-router shim** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: foundations/mixture-of-workers.md:242-243 — 'A MoW routing decision is protocol-visible. A router must be able to explain and receipt why a worker was selected.'
   - Recommendation: The doctrine boundary is stated (components/model-router/doctrine.md:37: 'Mixture of Workers is labor routing'), the RoutingDecisionEnvelope/RoutingDecisionReceipt schema has an owner (components/daemon-runtime/events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:1038-1045, whose router_ref admits worker/runtime/system/domain actors), and domains/marketplace-neutrality.md owns the neutrality obligations — but no component doc owns the labor-routing runtime itself: who runs candidate discovery, who evaluates the eligibility/affiliation/semantic/policy/budget checks, who holds the routing policy hash. The passive 'a router must' remains ownerless in a pack built on single owners, and that vacuum invites implementing MoW as an alias over the existing model router, which would confirm the 'rebrand' critique the file works hard to refute. Add a labor-router owner (a section of components/model-router/doctrine.md or a new components doc) naming which plane runs candidate discovery, who holds the routing policy hash, and how it consumes model-router eligibility without absorbing it.
8. **No mapping from boundary artifacts to the regulatory deliverables that actually drive 2026 enterprise training-data governance** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: foundations/institutional-learning-boundary.md:267-268 — 'A revocation or impact record does not prove that a trained model has forgotten the source.'
   - Recommendation: Enterprises govern training data in 2026 primarily to satisfy EU AI Act Art. 53 training-content summaries, GDPR Art. 17 erasure, DSM Art. 4 TDM opt-outs, and DORA exit-strategy testing — none appear anywhere in this facet (or the wider architecture pack). The pack has the raw material: rights claims + dataset lineage can emit AI Act summaries; revocation-impact receipts + residual-exposure records are exactly GDPR erasure-response evidence; the Model Independence Test is a DORA provider-exit test. Add a 'Regulatory Projection' subsection to institutional-learning-boundary.md mapping each artifact to the obligation it can evidence, and add TDM opt-out as a rights_basis/source_class input. Without this mapping, buyers default to Purview/OneTrust checklists that name the regulations.
9. **Model Independence Test is a one-shot genesis obligation; swap-readiness decays without a drill cadence** — `medium` · Better way *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: foundations/institutional-learning-boundary.md:319 — 'Every enterprise profile must define a **Model Independence Test**:'
   - Recommendation: Provider entanglement accretes continuously (new provider-specific tool behaviors, prompt idioms, hidden thread state), so a test defined once and run in the golden demo goes stale exactly like an untested disaster-recovery plan. The plumbing half-exists: InstitutionalLearningBoundaryProfileEnvelope already carries model_independence_report_ref (common-objects-and-envelopes.md:4838), and foundry.md's Model-Swap Continuity (:361) re-runs the comparison when a candidate route is promoted — but nothing re-tests the incumbent between promotions. Make it a recurring receipted drill: add model_independence_drill_interval and last_drill_receipt_ref next to the existing report ref; require re-execution at promotion of any worker claiming portability and after N route-contract revisions; surface drill staleness in the route selector next to 'where learning goes' (lines 355-357). Framing it as the DORA-style provider exit drill also gives it a procurement-recognizable name.
10. **'Changed terms cannot silently override the profile' has no detection mechanism — route-contract attestation freshness is missing** — `medium` · Better way *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: foundations/institutional-learning-boundary.md:191-192 — 'A provider's account-level opt-in, broad service default, or changed terms cannot silently override the effective profile.'
   - Recommendation: The enforcement half already exists and is normative in three places: components/model-router/doctrine.md:294-297 ('A provider/model term, commercial authorization, endpoint posture, or data policy change invalidates the cached eligibility decision. The route is killed or quarantined until a reviewed contract version is admitted'), ModelRouteRightsContract carries valid_from/valid_until plus provider_terms_version_ref, and conformance ILB-05 requires a changed or expired contract to invalidate cached eligibility. What no doc defines is the detection loop: who observes that live provider terms diverged from the bound terms:// ref, and valid_until is nullable with no maximum attestation age — an unnoticed change never fires the invalidation rule, so the invariant fires only on self-reported change or expiry events. Mirror the custody discipline the same file already has ('proof freshness', line 345): add attested_at + max_attestation_age to the route contract; stale attestation degrades to deny for protected-material uses; assign the model-router owner a terms-change detection duty (terms-hash monitoring or periodic re-attestation receipts). This is how mature vendor-management programs treat DPAs — as expiring evidence, not permanent facts.
11. **The training-lifecycle owner omits the preference-optimization/RL post-training class that foundry.md already canonizes** — `low` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: foundations/worker-training-lifecycle.md:429 — 'The worker is improved through one or more methods: prompt optimization, retrieval indexing, context graph revision, workflow graph refinement, verifier tuning, route-policy training, adapter training'
   - Recommendation: Neither Training Profile Flexibility (lines 74-85) nor stage 6 names preference datasets, reward models, DPO, or verifier-reward RL — the dominant 2026 post-training paradigm. This is not a pack-wide absence: components/hypervisor/foundry.md already canonizes 'preference optimization ... and environment RL' as staged training, 'reward/verifier functions ... rejection sampling' as feedback, and 'Hugging Face/TRL-style trainers ... RL/verifier frameworks' as trainer backends (foundry.md ~733-757). But worker-training-lifecycle.md is the declared canonical owner of training-profile semantics, and a reader of the foundations facet alone would conclude the lifecycle is 2023-era SFT/distillation canon — ironically, the pack's verifier workers, rubric judgments, and Human Review Queue accept/reject decisions are the natural preference-pair substrate. Add 'preference-optimized / verifier-reward post-trained workers' to the supported profiles and stage 6, add PreferenceDataset and reward-model artifact classes to the Artifact Packaging Graph (line 340), cross-reference foundry.md's staged-training stack, and state that RL runs bind to TrainingPipelineRun like any persistent training.
12. **WTL's 'worker continuity report' and ILB's Model Independence Test lack cross-references at the foundations level** — `low` · Refactor
   - Evidence: foundations/worker-training-lifecycle.md:253-255 — 'Foundry may compare an incumbent and candidate route against a frozen system snapshot and eval suite, but the resulting continuity report is evidence' vs institutional-learning-boundary.md:319
   - Recommendation: worker-training-lifecycle.md defines a Foundry continuity comparison (frozen snapshot + eval suite + delta report) and institutional-learning-boundary.md mandates the Model Independence Test (same shape: disable A, mount B, restore institution-controlled state, publish delta) — the two foundations files never reference each other. The drift risk is smaller than it first looks: components/hypervisor/foundry.md's Model-Swap Continuity section (:361-381) already unifies the semantics, explicitly calling the Foundry/Evaluations comparison 'a model-independence test' and binding the report to the frozen snapshot, incumbent and candidate route contracts, and observed deltas. The residual fix is cheap cross-referencing: make WTL:249-255 cite the Model Independence Test (and foundry.md's Model-Swap Continuity) as the canonical mechanism, with ILB — which declares 'model-independence doctrine' in its owner line — the single owner of test semantics and claim limits, and the continuity report its per-worker instantiation.
13. **'Sparse' carries three colliding meanings across this facet** — `low` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: foundations/mixture-of-workers.md:181-182 — 'IOI supports **Sparse Worker Categories**: narrow labor markets with explicit evaluation profiles.' and :129 — 'local accounting and sparse settlement only where required'
   - Recommendation: Within one file, 'sparse' means niche/narrow (Sparse Worker Categories), infrequent (sparse settlement to L1 at :129, :337, :363), and — because the whole doc is framed against Mixture of Experts — inevitably evokes MoE sparsity (few experts active per token), the one meaning it must not have here. A pack that maintains a vocabulary file and a dedicated MoE-vs-MoW disambiguation section should not overload its own disambiguating term. Rename to 'Specialist Worker Categories' (or 'Narrow Labor Categories') in mixture-of-workers.md, the `SparseWorkerCategory` vocabulary entry (_meta/vocabulary.md:1855), and worker-training-lifecycle.md's references; keep 'sparse' exclusively for settlement frequency.
14. **Three surface forms for one eligibility object across the facet: bare decision, Envelope, and Training profile alias** — `low` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: foundations/institutional-learning-boundary.md:235 — '`LearningEvidenceEligibility` is the general decision for admitting a Finding,' vs worker-training-lifecycle.md:52 — '`LearningEvidenceEligibilityEnvelope` decisions, and source/teacher/model-route'
   - Recommendation: ILB and bounded-recursive-improvement.md use bare `LearningEvidenceEligibility`; WTL, security-privacy-policy-invariants.md, and common-objects use the Envelope; the conformance contract (docs/conformance/hypervisor-core/institutional-learning-boundary.md:242) says '`TrainingEvidenceEligibility` decisions'. The fork risk is smaller than raw counting suggests: the compatibility relationship is normatively defined in the schema owner's adapter note (common-objects-and-envelopes.md:4771-4786 — same eligibility_id, `eligibility_profile: training_compatibility`, legacy wire-key normalization) and restated in ILB:235-241, agentgres api-object-model:357, and bounded-recursive-improvement:315. The residual drift is stylistic but real: common-objects calls the training wire key 'legacy' while bounded-recursive-improvement calls the profile 'valid', and bare-vs-Envelope usage is uncoordinated across doctrine files. Add a one-line naming convention to vocabulary.md (Envelope = the schema object; bare name = the decision; Training* = the training_compatibility profile only) and sweep stragglers so implementations do not fork the schema.

### Bounded recursive improvement + Improvement app + improvement governance gates

**Files reviewed:** `foundations/bounded-recursive-improvement.md (481)` · `components/hypervisor/improvement.md (425)` · `components/daemon-runtime/improvement-governance-gates.md (247)`

**Current shape.** The facet defines a proposal-mediated protocol for self-improvement: a bounded system may search for successors to declared mutable capability — including successors to its own improvement method — but never controls the evidence, resource meter, authority, or recovery path by which a successor becomes canonical. Two paths exist: a direct UpgradeProposal for one-shot changes, and an optional ImprovementCampaign for adaptive multi-epoch work, admitted under an immutable owner-qualified ImprovementGovernanceProfile and driven by a non-executable ImprovementAgenda. Every campaign separates Search (propose/attempt), Judgment (frozen evaluation), and Authority (admission/activation/recovery) as logical trust functions whose required independence scales with assurance class. An EvaluationEpoch freezes the utility regime — suites, holdout custody, statistical policy, false-promotion budgets — and every sealed access spends an append-only EvaluationExposureLedger whose spend inherits down descendant campaigns and never resets on identity change. Higher-order improvement is path-relative retargeting synchronized only through immutable ImprovementOrderCutoffReceipts moving evidence one adjacent order per cutoff, and claims form a five-class evidence ladder (bounded_optimization through inflection_evidence) that never self-promotes. The Hypervisor Improvement app is the product cockpit rendering agendas, campaigns, candidate archives, order graphs, and proposal handoff, owning no execution, evaluation, or release authority. The daemon-runtime gates doc specifies apply-time enforcement: an implemented direct gate (simulation-freshness fingerprints, approval/release controls for high-impact changes, deterministic 409 reason codes, canary/cohort rollouts with principal-derived contexts) plus a planned campaign-grade extension binding epochs, exposure, learning eligibility, and cutoff receipts.

**What it already gets right:**

- The EvaluationExposureLedger operationalizes reusable-holdout / adaptive-data-analysis discipline (Dwork et al., Blum-Hardt Ladder) more rigorously than typical deployed eval-gating practice: append-only spend, candidate-family binding, ancestor inheritance, and the explicit rule that renaming a candidate, starting a child campaign, or relabeling an order never restores spent exposure (evaluations.md:193-195; anti-pattern 'new order label = restored holdout or statistical budget') closes the identity-laundering loophole that defeats most frozen-holdout regimes.
- The five-class improvement-claim ladder plus the Non-Claims section (foundations:426-437) is unusually honest epistemics — it explicitly refuses to let receipts, benchmark wins, or schema representability masquerade as demonstrated recursive improvement, and forbids product copy stronger than still-valid evidence.
- Search/Judgment/Authority as proportional logical trust functions rather than mandatory runtime planes (foundations:124-130) avoids the classic governance-architecture failure of forcing full ceremony on low-risk work while still requiring independent actors/credentials/failure-domains at high assurance.
- The search doctrine is quality-diversity-literature-aware without over-prescription: mandatory retention of negative knowledge, stepping stones, and exploit findings; Pareto/QD archives permitted; no universal scalar fitness field and no mandated algorithm — matching AlphaEvolve/MAP-Elites-era understanding that archives, not winners, carry the value.
- The gates doc honestly partitions built from planned ('Rules 5–7 ... must not be described as built', gates:83-85), which is rare discipline in target-canon packs and prevents conformance-claim inflation.

**SOTA verdict: `near-sota`** — compared against: Anthropic RSP / OpenAI Preparedness / DeepMind Frontier Safety Framework capability-threshold gating; METR-style autonomy and dangerous-capability evaluations; Dwork et al. reusable holdout + Blum-Hardt Ladder (adaptive data analysis); Argo Rollouts analysis-template-driven canary with automated abort; AlphaEvolve / MAP-Elites quality-diversity program search; UL 4600 / Goal Structuring Notation safety cases; anytime-valid sequential testing, e-values, alpha-investing risk-wealth accounting; eval-gated deployment (frozen benchmark + regression gates in CI/CD)

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No suite-level, cross-campaign exposure aggregation: exposure is scoped to candidate family + ancestor chain, so unrelated campaigns (or tenants) each get full budgets against the same sealed holdout — the population-level leakage that Kaggle private-leaderboard rotation and reusable-holdout research treat as the primary threat.
- No dangerous-capability / capability-threshold lane: nothing escalates assurance class or forces Governance re-admission when an improved improver crosses a declared capability threshold, which RSP/FSF/METR-style regimes make the central gate for exactly this kind of system.
- No structured assurance-case artifact for non-embodied high-assurance activations: the pack's EmbodiedDeploymentAssuranceCase covers embodied deployments only, and the claim ladder is capability evidence, not a safety argument for constitutional/financial/security targets.
- Canary rollback is a manual endpoint; no Argo-Rollouts-style RolloutAnalysisPolicy binding guardrail metrics to automated fail-closed abort.
- Eval-set construction provenance is disclosure-only: independence disclosures and affiliation refs are frozen into the epoch and challenges exist post-hoc, but nothing fails closed at freeze when suite authorship intersects Search actors or when training-corpus contamination is unscreened.
- The one BUILT gate is fail-open by default (no simulation → apply allowed for most proposal kinds), which is behind ordinary eval-gated-deployment practice where unknown impact blocks by default.

**Findings (15, severity-ordered):**

1. **The implemented direct gate is fail-open: skipping simulation skips governance entirely** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/improvement-governance-gates.md:58-59 — 'Other kinds without a simulation keep existing behavior (`no_simulation` posture, apply allowed)'; :20-22 — 'A learned improvement whose saved what-if simulation is high-impact cannot become behavior without an APPROVED `ApprovalRequest`'
   - Recommendation: The high-impact gate only fires if a simulation was voluntarily saved; a lazy or adversarial Search ring simply omits the simulation and applies ungated — `launch_policy_suggestion` is the only proposal kind required to carry one (rule 1). Unknown impact is treated as low impact, against README Non-Negotiable 10 and INV-14's 'honest posture, fail closed' (unproven readiness blocks with a named reason). Partial mitigations exist — improvement apply is a sensitive endpoint requiring authentication outside `local_development` (gates:236-238), and planned rule 5 fail-closes participant-derived proposals — but neither substitutes for an impact-evidence requirement, and no rule or planned reason code ever closes the default for internally originated direct proposals. Invert the default: every proposal kind requires either a fresh simulation or an explicit per-kind waiver declared in the owner's ImprovementGovernanceProfile and receipted at apply; make bare `no_simulation` a blocking 409 (`simulation_required`) outside local_development posture.
2. **Simulation-freshness fingerprint omits the target base root — TOCTOU on the incumbent** — `high` · Better way
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/improvement-governance-gates.md:61 — '`proposal_fingerprint = sha256(proposal_kind, target_ref, suggested, evidence_refs)`'; foundations/bounded-recursive-improvement.md:176-177 — 'Promotion against a stale target base fails optimistic-concurrency admission.'
   - Recommendation: A simulation computed against base B1 stays 'fresh' forever after the target drifts to B2, because the fingerprint covers only proposal content — freshness is explicitly clock-free content identity (gates:63-64) and impact semantics exclude ambient drift (gates:158-160). A high-impact approval can then apply against a world never simulated. The gates doc claims ownership of 'freshness and exact-base rules' (:10) yet the direct path has none; only the planned campaign extension binds an exact predecessor root (`target_base_stale`, :125). Fix: include target_base_root (incumbent revision hash) in proposal_fingerprint, or add a distinct `target_base_stale` 409 to the direct gate, mirroring the campaign rule.
3. **No suite-level exposure aggregation across unrelated campaigns** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: foundations/bounded-recursive-improvement.md:261-263 — 'Each access or result spends append-only `EvaluationExposureLedger` entries against its candidate family and ancestor posture'; foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:7402-7403 — 'child Campaigns inherit effective ancestor spend and cannot reset it'
   - Recommendation: Exposure is scoped to the candidate-family + ancestor chain (the ledger envelope binds one `evaluation_epoch_ref` plus ancestor ledger refs), so two unrelated campaigns (or two tenants) evaluating against the same sealed holdout each carry full, independent budgets — the population-level leakage that reusable-holdout research (Dwork et al.) and Kaggle private-leaderboard practice treat as the dominant threat. Cross-campaign eval overfitting is the realistic failure mode for a shared Evaluations surface. Add a suite-scoped `HoldoutSuiteExposureLedger` owned by the holdout custodian, referenced by every epoch sealing that suite, with a population exposure budget whose exhaustion forces rotation/declassification per the existing rotation policy (evaluations.md:181-182).
4. **No dangerous-capability threshold lane or assurance-escalation trigger** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/evaluations.md:158 — '| adversarial | probes reward hacking, policy evasion, collusion, leakage, and unsafe edge cases |'; foundations/bounded-recursive-improvement.md:371 — 'A separately defined recursive-seat portfolio supports improved ability to improve an improver.'
   - Recommendation: The evaluation-lane table and campaign ceilings gate on performance, robustness, exposure, and budgets, and ImprovementGovernanceProfile carries stop policies and structural ceilings (max target order, nesting depth, unattended generations) — but every trigger is structural or budgetary, none is capability-measured. A system explicitly designed to represent ignition-class evidence has no rule that crossing a declared capability threshold (METR-style autonomy, replication, cyber-offense evals; RSP/FSF capability levels) halts work and forces re-admission under a higher assurance class; foundations:459-462 escalates assurance only by structural properties (higher orders, multi-node, embodied). Add `capability_threshold_refs` to ImprovementGovernanceProfile and EvaluationEpoch; make a threshold crossing a mandatory stop trigger plus Governance re-admission event, and add a `capability_threshold_crossed` code to the planned failure family.
5. **Canonical high-impact classification is a magnitude-blind magic constant** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/improvement-governance-gates.md:160-161 — '`high_impact = changed >= 3 || blockers_introduced > 0 || privacy_loosened`'; :81 — 'Authority widening is never an improvement side effect.'
   - Recommendation: Canonizing `changed >= 3` means two catastrophic changes that introduce no blockers are 'low impact' and apply without controls (rule 4, :70). Impact is a projection-count, not a risk class; and although rule 7 categorically forbids authority widening, that rule is planned and unenforced, and `authority_widened` is absent from the built formula while `privacy_loosened` is present — so the one classifier that actually fires today cannot see an authority delta. Replace the hardcoded formula with a versioned `impact-policy://` object frozen per ImprovementGovernanceProfile (matching that envelope's existing policy-ref pattern) — inputs include per-target risk class, authority/scope deltas, and magnitude, with the current formula demoted to the default policy revision. Canon should own the gate shape, not the constant.
6. **Governance-mass asymmetry is a drift engine: the strong path is expensive, the weak path is unenforced** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: foundations/bounded-recursive-improvement.md:151-153 — 'total resource, wall-clock, statistical-risk, evaluation-exposure, nesting, target-order, and authority ceilings, with disjoint reservations'; components/daemon-runtime/improvement-governance-gates.md:70 — 'Low-impact fresh simulations apply without controls.'
   - Recommendation: Campaign admission front-loads freezing evaluator, task-distribution, benchmark, model-route, tool, environment, dependency, rights, custody, and policy roots (foundations:149-156) plus a large EvaluationEpochEnvelope, while the direct path (as built) needs nothing. The doctrine already mandates the boundary — INV-33 ('Adaptive or multi-epoch search binds a finite ImprovementCampaign') and gates:25-30 ('Adaptive repeated search ... additionally binds the ImprovementCampaign') — and sealed-holdout access stays custody-gated by Evaluations regardless of path. But nothing detects or enforces the rule: no reason code, even in the planned family, fires when adaptive improvement is decomposed into serial 'one-shot' direct proposals, so multi-trial search launders past statistical-risk and false-promotion accounting on the honor system. Add an anti-decomposition rule to the gate contract: N direct proposals against the same target family within a declared window trigger `campaign_required` (a deterministic 409), with the threshold owned by ImprovementGovernanceProfile — turning INV-33's requirement into an enforced gate without forcing a campaign around a genuine one-shot change.
7. **Split the gates doc: rollout/cohort/auth-posture mechanics are not improvement-gate doctrine** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/improvement-governance-gates.md:172 — '## Canary release + rollback (learned-policy rollouts)'; :217-218 — 'raw member refs remain honored for backward compatibility'
   - Recommendation: Lines ~172-247 are a second document in implementation-changelog voice: HTTP endpoints, sha256 bucketing, deprecation notes, deployment auth postures. This is ReleaseControl/rollout mechanics that any governed release (not just improvement) needs, welded onto gate doctrine. Extract to `components/daemon-runtime/release-controls-and-rollouts.md` as the canonical rollout owner; keep improvement-governance-gates.md focused on gate rules, reason codes, the epistemic ladder, and the campaign-grade extension, referencing the rollout doc. This also fixes the voice mismatch between canonical-doctrine prose and release-note prose in one file.
8. **S/J/A, two-path diagrams, sync semantics, and placement tables are duplicated between foundations and the app doc** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: foundations/bounded-recursive-improvement.md:114-115 — 'Every Campaign separates three logical trust functions under its admitted governance owner'; components/hypervisor/improvement.md:259 — 'Search, Judgment, and Authority are logical trust rings under one system constitution'
   - Recommendation: Both files restate Search/Judgment/Authority, the two-path flow, cross-order synchronization, and near-identical surface-placement tables — and they are already diverging ('trust functions' vs 'trust rings', owner scoping differs). Both canonical-owner headers even claim cross-order synchronization. Three docs is the right factoring in principle (doctrine / product cockpit / daemon gate contract), but only if each layer owns distinct content. Cut improvement.md's restatements to one-paragraph pointers at foundations and keep only product-specific material (IA, rendering rules, claims language, cockpit boundaries); the pack's single-owner rule already demands this.
9. **Improvement.md scopes S/J/A to 'one system constitution' while both docs admit constitution-less campaigns** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/improvement.md:30 — 'A Campaign may be admitted under a user, project, organization, or System owner.' vs :259-260 — 'logical trust rings under one system constitution, not new runtime planes'
   - Recommendation: Foundations:114-116 gets it right: separation holds 'under its admitted governance owner and, when System-scoped, that System's constitution.' Improvement.md's S/J/A section instead binds the rings to 'one system constitution,' contradicting its own admission section (:30-37) which allows user/project/org campaigns with no constitution. Fix improvement.md:259-260 to the foundations wording (or, per the refactor finding, delete the restatement and link). Left as-is, an implementer could conclude non-System campaigns are exempt from S/J/A separation.
10. **Canary lacks automated metric-driven analysis and abort** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/improvement-governance-gates.md:192 — '**Rollback** (`POST …/rollout/rollback`): the overlay stops selecting the variant anywhere'
   - Recommendation: Rollback and promote are manual endpoints; nothing binds live guardrail telemetry to the rollout. Argo Rollouts-class systems attach an AnalysisTemplate to every canary — declared metrics, evaluation windows, auto-abort on threshold breach. Foundations' lifecycle says 'monitoring and rollback' (:170) but defines no automation contract, so the monitored-production rung of the epistemic ladder depends on a human watching. Add a `RolloutAnalysisPolicy` object bound to the ReleaseControl: declared guardrail metrics, windows, and an automated fail-closed abort that mints the existing rollback receipts. Keep promotion human/Governance-gated; automate only the abort direction.
11. **No structured assurance-case artifact for non-embodied high-assurance activation** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: foundations/bounded-recursive-improvement.md:358 — 'ImprovementEvidenceClaim is an immutable evidence artifact, never authority.'
   - Recommendation: For constitutional and financial targets the docs require 'protected amendment path' plus gate lists (foundations:335-343), but there is no UL 4600 / GSN-style safety-case object linking identified hazards to mitigations to evidence with explicit residual-risk acceptance by an accountable owner. The pack already has exactly this shape for embodied deployments — `EmbodiedDeploymentAssuranceCase` (common-objects:6119) carries `hazard_and_safety_requirement_refs`, mitigation implementation refs, `residual_risk_ref`, and verification evidence, and foundations:339-343 routes physical improvements through its assurance-amendment path — but nothing analogous exists for constitutional, financial, or security targets. ImprovementEvidenceClaim argues capability, not safety; gate checklists are not an argument structure. Generalize the existing shape: a typed `AssuranceCase` envelope (hazard set, mitigation claims, evidence refs, residual-risk acceptance, challengeable like evaluators) that ImprovementGovernanceProfile can require per assurance class and that UpgradeDecision must bind for declared high-assurance targets.
12. **Eval-set provenance is disclosure-only; suite/Search independence and contamination never fail closed at freeze** — `medium` · Better way *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: foundations/bounded-recursive-improvement.md:251-253 — 'holdout custody, independence disclosures, and inherited resource, false-promotion, and exposure budgets'; components/hypervisor/evaluations.md:218 — 'A challenge may concern leakage, contamination, calibration, scorer defects'
   - Recommendation: The epoch freezes 'independence disclosures' and carries `evaluator_version_and_affiliation_refs` / `holdout_custodian_refs`; challenges cover affiliation and collusion post-hoc; and foundations:127-130 requires proportional actor independence at high assurance — but nothing blocks admission when the sealed suite was authored by the same principals/models doing Search, or when suite/training-corpus overlap was never screened. The Foundry/Evaluations split (Foundry builds eval assets and candidates; Evaluations freezes and judges) means builder-side overlap is structurally possible with no canon rule blocking it at freeze. Require a `suite_provenance_ref` at epoch freeze (author principals, generation method, model taint, overlap-screening evidence) with a fail-closed admission rule: provenance intersecting the campaign's Search actors, or absent screening at the declared assurance class, yields `evaluation_epoch_invalid`.
13. **Claim classes 'ignition_evidence' and 'inflection_evidence' import FOOM-discourse vocabulary into a deliberately sober enum** — `low` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: foundations/bounded-recursive-improvement.md:371 — '`ignition_evidence` | A separately defined recursive-seat portfolio supports improved ability to improve an improver.'
   - Recommendation: The ladder's whole point is claims narrower than evidence (improvement.md:351-353 bans unqualified 'RSI' language), yet its top two enum values are the takeoff-debate's most loaded words — inviting exactly the misquotation the docs forbid ('IOI has ignition evidence'). The definitions are heavily hedged in-canon ('not proof of runaway or indefinite improvement') and the names are already entrenched in canonical-enums.md and ADR 0018, so this is a coordinated multi-file rename, not a local edit. If accepted, rename to mechanism-descriptive terms: `meta_improvement_evidence` (improved ability to improve an improver) and `sustained_trend_shift_evidence` (durable change in improvement dynamics), with definitions unchanged, updating schema enums, the claims table, improvement.md's claims-language section, and ADR 0018 together. Names in canonical enums are product copy.
14. **Seven-coordinate rendering sits in the cockpit's owned scope with no first-slice conformance tiering** — `low` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/improvement.md:103-106 — 'the distinct rendering of target improvement order, pursuit-method order, target generation, candidate generation, active nested depth, transfer tier, and claim class'; foundations/bounded-recursive-improvement.md:459-460 — 'first useful proof should be a single-System, target-order-0 software or pursuit-method Campaign'
   - Recommendation: The seven-coordinate system is sound doctrine, and mitigations exist: the integration is labeled planned, improvement.md's Conformance Checks do not currently mandate seven-coordinate rendering, and the pack's delta convention defers surface work until pulled by implemented contracts. But the Owns list still places 'distinct rendering' of all seven in the cockpit's owned scope with no tiering, while the sanctioned first proof is order-0 where five coordinates are degenerate — inviting either stalled scope or dead chrome that drifts. Tier the conformance explicitly: order-0 slice requires rendering target generation, candidate generation, and claim class only; the remaining coordinates become required at the assurance escalation that first makes them non-degenerate (higher orders, nesting, transfer claims). State the tiering in improvement.md's Conformance Checks.
15. **Gates doc oscillates between 'Work Ledger' and the converged 'Provenance' terminology** — `low` · Rename
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/improvement-governance-gates.md:167-170 — 'the Work Ledger record family (protocol terminology — the Provenance application renders these views)' vs :222 — 'Rollout receipts and Work Ledger `policy_rollout` entries'
   - Recommendation: The doc annotates the split once (:167-170) but then uses bare 'Work Ledger' three more times (:197, :222, :246) with no qualifier, in a pack whose taxonomy convergence renamed the product surface to Provenance. Normalize: use 'work-ledger record family (rendered by Provenance)' on first use and 'work-ledger entries' thereafter, or adopt 'provenance index entries' throughout; also refresh this file's alignment stamp (2026-07-11) to match the 2026-07-15 pass its two sibling docs carry, since it predates their converged vocabulary.


## Part III.6 — Foundations: the semantic plane

### Domain ontologies and data recipes

**Files reviewed:** `foundations/domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md (624)`

**Current shape.** The facet defines IOI's "locally canonical semantic world plane": every sovereign domain declares its own namespaced, versioned DomainOntology (with immutable OntologyVersions and OntologyOverlays), typed CanonicalObjectModels, and bitemporal ProvenanceAssertions that carry source, confidence, contradiction, supersession, and dispute state — Agentgres admission records that a domain asserted something without making it universally true. Cross-domain meaning transfer is opt-in and explicit: OntologyCrosswalks declare loss/ambiguity/compatibility between versions, every application emits a challengeable, receipted SemanticMappingDecision, AIIP negotiates semantic profiles per handoff, and the facet's Non-Negotiable 19 (tagged INV-30) forbids federation unless expected value exceeds mapping loss, disclosure, verification, and coordination costs — there is deliberately no global ontology or shared graph. Executable meaning is separated from power: an OntologyActionContract binds typed IO, pre/postconditions, risk class, idempotency, effect-recovery class, compensation, verification, and physical-safety profiles, but semantics never grant authority — the daemon still admits, and wallet.network supplies delegated authority. On the data side, immutable content-addressed DataRecipes and ConnectorMappings (whose content hashes commit exact semantic-component snapshots) turn raw sources into ontology-bound objects, DistilledOntologyDatasets, EvaluationDatasets, and OntologyProjections, with TransformationReceipts and most-restrictive-intersection rights composition gating training, export, and distillation. The Ontology Development Kit compiles these contracts into generated surfaces, domain apps, evals, and worker plans without owning truth, authority, or execution. Progressive adoption is a non-negotiable: minimal object/action contracts around consequential boundaries first, governed evolution from observed work afterward.

**What it already gets right:**

- OntologyActionContract is genuinely ahead of Palantir Foundry Action Types: it separates semantic action definition from execution authority (daemon admission + wallet authority remain independent), and its effect-recovery taxonomy (replayable/checkpointable/compensatable/reconciliation_required/non_retryable), ambiguous-effect reconciliation, compensation profiles, and mandatory physical-safety binding have no equivalent in Foundry, ServiceNow, or Salesforce action frameworks.
- ProvenanceAssertion is properly bitemporal (valid_time + transaction_time), with confidence, contradicting-assertion refs, applicability scope, supersession, and dispute as first-class schema fields — at XTDB/Datomic level and beyond typical production property-graph deployments; the operational-truth-vs-semantic-belief distinction ('admission records the assertion without making it universally true') is epistemically correct and rare in industry.
- Content-addressed DataRecipe and ConnectorMapping revisions whose content hashes commit an exact semantic-component snapshot (every referenced ontology version, mapping, object model, schema, policy view) — 'never follows current heads' — is stronger reproducibility discipline than dbt manifests or Confluent schema-registry practice, where transitive semantic dependencies drift silently.
- Progressive adoption as Non-Negotiable 16 ('no ontology program may require exhaustive enterprise modeling before useful work begins') directly counters the classic enterprise-ontology death spiral, and the NN19/INV-30 cost-gated federation posture is a coherent, defensible third way between Palantir centralization and the failed global-semantic-web vision.
- Honest epistemics about limits: unlearning claims require declared method+verifier and are never implied by deletion (NN21), receipts prove only declared facts, oracle evidence never makes external propositions true (INV-25) — this credibility discipline exceeds vendor norms.
