**SOTA verdict: `divergent-by-design`** — compared against: Palantir Foundry Ontology (object types, link types, Action Types, OSDK, object-set indexing/materialization); W3C RDF/OWL/SHACL/SKOS and SSSOM (Simple Standard for Sharing Ontological Mappings, with per-mapping justification and confidence); Bitemporal stores: Datomic, XTDB; Confluent Schema Registry compatibility modes and data contracts; dbt Semantic Layer / Cube / LookML metric definitions; Entity resolution: Senzing, Splink, Zingg (match confidence, survivorship, clerical review); Data observability / drift detection: Great Expectations, dbt tests, Monte Carlo-class monitors; ISO 25964 thesaurus crosswalks and SKOS mapping relations; GraphRAG / LLM-KG hybrid retrieval and grounding

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No quantified mapping confidence or calibration anywhere in the crosswalk plane — SSSOM ships per-mapping confidence and justification; IOI's compatibility_result is a 5-value enum with no numbers, no per-field quality, no calibration corpus.
- No drift-detection mechanism for active crosswalks/ConnectorMappings — the Ontology app's 'schema health' and Data app freshness/quality views are declared product surfaces, but no canonical monitoring object, sampling contract, threshold, or demotion trigger exists; immutable pinning prevents silent head-following but nothing watches a pinned mapping decay against a drifting provider schema or value distribution (data-observability SOTA).
- No instance-level entity resolution plane: crosswalks map types/schemas, dedupe is an intra-recipe step; nothing canonicalizes 'your Customer#123 is my Account#987' with match evidence, survivorship, and review queues (Senzing/Splink/Foundry object resolution).
- No standards interchange: bespoke meta-model with no OWL/SHACL/SKOS/SSSOM import-export contract, so FIBO/SNOMED/schema.org-holding institutions must hand-recast everything.
- No metrics/semantic-layer contract (measures, dimensions, grain) on OntologyProjection — dbt Semantic Layer/LookML/Foundry metrics territory.
- No NL-to-ontology grounding/retrieval index contract for agents at runtime (LLM-KG hybrid retrieval), despite workers being the primary consumers.
- No crosswalk composition semantics or hub/interchange-profile answer to O(N^2) pairwise mappings across many sovereign domains.

**Findings (15, severity-ordered):**

1. **Crosswalks carry no quantified confidence, per-field quality, or calibration — the NN19 (INV-30-tagged) value-exceeds-loss gate has no measurable inputs** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:4204 — 'compatibility_result: exact | compatible | lossy | requires_adapter | incompatible'
   - Recommendation: Add a mapping_confidence block to OntologyMappingEnvelope: per-field-mapping confidence, mapping_justification (manual/rule/LLM-proposed/learned), calibration corpus ref (a golden-pair EvaluationDataset), and measured round-trip loss. SemanticMappingDecision should record realized confidence at application time. SSSOM (the cross-ontology mapping standard) ships per-mapping confidence and justification; ProvenanceAssertion already has confidence_or_uncertainty (common-objects-and-envelopes.md:4174), so mappings are the anomaly. Note the OntologyCrosswalk concept already declares loss, ambiguity, scope, and verifier requirements qualitatively (domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md:123) — the primitive slots exist; what is missing is quantification and calibration. Without numbers, Non-Negotiable 19's 'value exceeds mapping loss' comparison (line 609) has no measurable loss side to receipt or challenge; INV-30 proper (invariants.md:230) frames federation as each party's governed acceptance and permits withholding raw private valuation, so the fix should quantify the loss/confidence side rather than mandate a disclosed value computation.
2. **No instance-level entity resolution plane — federation collides with 'same real-world entity' on day one** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: foundations/domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md:132 — 'Execution record for extraction, normalization, redaction, dedupe, validation, and mapping.'
   - Recommendation: Crosswalks map types/schemas; dedupe and link are intra-recipe steps; CanonicalObjectModel id_strategy is per-domain. The raw material partially exists — OntologyMappingEnvelope's application_target_refs already admits object://... targets (common-objects-and-envelopes.md:4199-4201), and a ProvenanceAssertion can carry an instance-equivalence claim with confidence and dispute state — but no contract canonicalizes that two domains' instances denote one real-world entity with match evidence, survivorship, and review workflow: the first problem any federated OutcomeRoom or cross-domain projection hits. Add an EntityResolutionDecision profile of OntologyMapping (instance-level): candidate pair refs, match features/evidence, confidence, method ref (deterministic/probabilistic/LLM), survivorship policy, clerical-review state, challengeable and receipted like SemanticMappingDecision. Identity claims stay ProvenanceAssertions, preserving the 'admitted, not universally true' doctrine (line 285). Senzing/Splink/Foundry object resolution all treat this as first-class; IOI currently has no dedicated object for it.
3. **Object-head truth vs assertion-graph belief reconciliation is undefined — projections read both with no merge rule** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: foundations/domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md:238 — 'projections are derived from ontology relationships, assertions, object models, crosswalks, recipes, and policy-bound views'
   - Recommendation: The canon holds two representations of instance state: canonical object heads (lifecycle states, constraints) and ProvenanceAssertions about properties/relationships (lines 168-171), and TransformationRuns emit both objects and assertions. Lifecycle step 11 does require projections to expose 'mapping posture, and unresolved disagreement' (lines 240-241) — which names the surface-the-dispute option — but the doc never says which properties are head-backed vs assertion-backed, or what a projection serves when an admitted assertion contradicts an object head. Define the reconciliation contract on CanonicalObjectModel: per-property truth class (head_backed | assertion_backed), and a declared, versioned merge policy for contested assertions in each OntologyProjection (e.g., latest-admitted-wins / confidence-weighted / surface-the-dispute). Without this, every implementation invents its own semantics and NN14 ('dispute must survive projection', line 594) is unenforceable.
4. **Crosswalk test suites are an untyped ref slot, not a mechanism — 'silent field equivalence is forbidden' has no enforcing artifact** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:4209 — '- test://... | verifier-challenge://... | evidence://...'
   - Recommendation: Define a crosswalk-conformance profile of EvaluationDataset (or a new CrosswalkConformanceSuite object): golden source-to-target pairs, round-trip loss assertions, target-invariant preservation checks (mapped objects must satisfy the target ontology's invariant_refs), and adversarial/ambiguity cases. Require a passing suite revision before OntologyMapping status may move validated-to-active, and require SemanticMappingDecision to bind the exact suite revision it passed. Lifecycle step 13 (domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md:251-253) does already require 'Compatibility tests, replay, affected-view analysis, policy review, and mapping challenges' before promotion of evolution proposals, so the doctrine gestures at this — but no typed, versioned test artifact exists and nothing requires a SemanticMappingDecision to bind the suite it passed. domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md:195 forbids silent field equivalence while the envelope offers only an optional test:// ref; the prohibition needs a required, versioned artifact in the promotion gate to be enforceable.
5. **No drift detection for active mappings: immutable pinning without health monitoring means pinned garbage, silently** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: foundations/domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md:251 — 'Observed gaps and candidate schemas become versioned proposals.'
   - Recommendation: Lifecycle step 13 governs proposed evolution, and the product layer names 'schema health' (Ontology app, line 341) and 'freshness, lineage, quality, and access posture' (Data app, line 328) as views, with NN8 forcing projections to expose freshness and source watermarks — but these are surfaces, not a mechanism. Nothing watches an active OntologyCrosswalk or ConnectorMapping decay while pinned: provider schema drift, value-distribution shift, rising unmapped-field or constraint-violation rates. Add a MappingHealthProbe/SemanticDriftMonitor object: scheduled sampled re-validation of live traffic against the mapping's conformance suite, with declared thresholds; a breach emits a verifier challenge and can demote status active-to-challenged (the status machine at common-objects-and-envelopes.md:4214 already supports this). This is standard data-observability practice (Great Expectations, Monte Carlo-class monitors) and is the missing half of the immutability discipline.
6. **NN19's federation cost-benefit gate has no measurement object — every federation decision becomes unauditable judgment** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: foundations/domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md:609 — 'expected operational value exceeds mapping loss, disclosure, verification, coordination, and downstream-action risk'
   - Recommendation: The facet's Non-Negotiable 19 (which tags INV-30) makes a quantitative comparison load-bearing but names no object that computes, records, or receipts it. Define a SemanticExchangeAssessment artifact required before an OntologyCrosswalk may be used cross-domain: measured loss (from the conformance suite of the test-suite finding), disclosure class of the policy-bound views exposed, and verification obligations and their cost; bind it to the SemanticMappingDecision receipt. One constraint the design must respect: INV-30 proper (invariants.md:230) explicitly allows a party to withhold its raw private valuation, so the assessment must record the measurable cost components and the acceptance decision without compelling disclosure of the accepting party's value basis — the value side can remain a private input referenced by the governed acceptance. This converts the invariant from vibes into a challengeable record, consistent with the pack's own receipts-for-everything physics.
7. **Revocation impact graph through distillation mandates per-derivative lineage without declaring tracking granularity — an unstated hard problem** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: foundations/domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md:486 — 'Revoking source or training eligibility must propagate through an impact graph covering derived views, distilled/evaluation datasets, caches, models, workers, packages, releases, and exports.'
   - Recommendation: Preserving source commitments through distillation (NN6, line 569) at per-example granularity multiplies storage and is often impossible for teacher-generated rows; at dataset granularity, one revoked source quarantines everything. DistilledOntologyDatasetEnvelope (common-objects-and-envelopes.md:4622) already carries source_commitments hashes and an impact_graph_ref, so the graph is real — but the granularity those commitments record and propagate at is never declared, so implementations will silently pick incompatible answers. Add a lineage_granularity field (per_example | per_shard | per_dataset) to DistilledOntologyDataset, let InstitutionalLearningBoundaryProfile declare the coarsest permitted class per source class, and state explicitly that revocation propagates at recorded granularity — which may force whole-dataset quarantine. This makes NN5/NN6 buildable and prices the tradeoff honestly.
8. **No crosswalk composition semantics or hub profile — pairwise-only mapping is O(N^2) across sovereign domains** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: foundations/domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md:191 — 'Cross-domain handoffs declare input/output ontology and action-schema profiles. Explicit crosswalks or semantic adapters resolve compatible versions'
   - Recommendation: AIIP negotiation is strictly pairwise; nothing says whether crosswalks compose (A→B then B→C), how loss/ambiguity compound, or whether a compose chain requires its own conformance suite. Define: (a) a compatibility_result composition table (lossy∘exact=lossy, requires_adapter absorbs, etc.) with a compounded-loss ceiling above which composition is forbidden; (b) an optional interchange-profile pattern — a shared hub ontology that is itself just another namespaced, versioned DomainOntology a domain may crosswalk to once. This preserves local-first doctrine (NN13) while giving N-domain ecosystems the hub-and-spoke economics that ISO 25964 practice and schema.org/FIBO ecosystems rely on.
9. **No W3C standards bridge: bespoke meta-model with no OWL/SHACL/SKOS/SSSOM interchange contract is an adoption killer for semantics-rich institutions** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: foundations/domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md:120 — 'Defines namespaced domain entities, relationships, events, actions, states, roles, invariants, and compatibility policy.'
   - Recommendation: Healthcare (SNOMED/FHIR), finance (FIBO), and science (OBO) institutions already hold OWL/SKOS assets; the canon offers no way in or out, and compatibility_result is expressively poorer than SKOS's directional exactMatch/closeMatch/broadMatch/narrowMatch (lossy conflates 'target coarser' with 'target incomplete'). Add an ontology_interchange_profile ref on DomainOntologyEnvelope (declared OWL2/SHACL/SKOS serialization as an artifact, treated as an import/export ConnectorMapping — never parallel truth), define the SKOS/SSSOM correspondence for compatibility_result, and add directionality to the enum. AIIP already plans A2A/MCP binding profiles (execution-horizons.md:477); the semantic-web binding is the missing sibling.
10. **OntologyProjection has no metrics/semantic-layer contract — aggregation semantics will drift per consumer** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: foundations/domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md:574 — 'OntologyProjections are serving views over Agentgres truth. They must expose freshness, recipe version, policy, and source watermark.'
   - Recommendation: The Data application promises freshness/lineage/quality views (line 328) and the estimating example implies aggregations ('project estimates by project, material, room', line 440), but nothing owns measure definitions: expression over object models, dimensions, grain, time semantics, policy-bound-view binding. This is exactly what dbt Semantic Layer/Cube/LookML and Foundry metrics exist to prevent — every dashboard and worker re-deriving 'total estimate value' differently. Add a MetricDefinition object (or metric profile of OntologyProjection) bound to CanonicalObjectModel fields and a PolicyBoundDataView; projections declare which metrics they serve at which grain.
11. **No NL-to-ontology grounding/retrieval contract — the path workers use every turn is unowned** — `medium` · SOTA gap *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: foundations/domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md:60 — '→ DistilledOntologyDataset / EvaluationDataset / WorkerTraining / OntologyProjection'
   - Recommendation: The consumer flow ends at training data and query projections; nothing specifies how a running worker resolves natural-language intent to the right CanonicalObjectModel, OntologyActionContract, or assertion subgraph (GraphRAG/LLM-KG hybrid retrieval — the dominant 2024-2026 pattern). Agentgres doctrine does assign semantic memory and retrieval surfaces to Agent Wiki / ioi-memory (components/agentgres/doctrine.md:1209) and already treats embeddings/indexes as never-canonical projections, so the projection-never-truth posture exists — but that is memory retrieval, not an ontology-scoped grounding contract. Add an OntologyGroundingIndex as a projection profile: embedding/index artifacts derived from ontology terms, object models, action contracts, and admitted assertions under a PolicyBoundDataView, versioned and watermarked like any OntologyProjection. Harness NL-action resolution and MoW routing then bind an exact index revision, making grounding replayable instead of ad hoc per harness.
12. **One 624-line foundations file owns ~13 subjects across four subsystems — a drift risk despite the owner header** — `low` · Refactor *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: foundations/domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md:4 — 'this file for locally canonical and optionally federated Domain Ontologies, semantic assertions and mappings, executable ontology actions, Data Recipes, canonical object models'
   - Recommendation: The same owner doc governs semantic contracts, the data pipeline plane, training-data distillation, ODK surface generation, and product-catalog placement (lines 307-315 name which app catalog entries exist). These evolve at different rates with different reviewers. To be fair, the pack already mitigates ownership ambiguity: the explicit 'Canonical owner' header, the source-of-truth-map, and monolithic single-owner facet files are the pack's established convention (common-objects-and-envelopes.md is far larger). The residual concern is review-rate mismatch, not ownership confusion; at minimum, product placement belongs in the hypervisor component docs with a pointer. If splitting: keep the semantic plane (ontology/version/overlay/assertion/crosswalk/mapping-decision/action-contract) here; move DataRecipe/ConnectorMapping/TransformationRun/distillation to a data-plane owner doc; move ODK manifest + OntologySurfaceDescriptor ownership to a components-level ODK doc. Update source-of-truth-map.md accordingly and preserve single-owner discipline.
13. **'Receipted' SemanticMappingDecision vs nullable decision receipt in the owning envelope** — `low` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: foundations/domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md:151 — 'mapping_record_profile: semantic_mapping_decision, target refs, challenge refs, and a decision receipt.' vs foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:4212 — 'mapping_decision_receipt_ref: receipt://... | null'
   - Recommendation: The facet file (also line 124: 'Challengeable, receipted application') makes the receipt constitutive of a SemanticMappingDecision, and the envelope's own profile table agrees in prose (common-objects-and-envelopes.md:4113 — the decision profile carries 'a decision receipt') — but the shared YAML allows null with no profile-conditional constraint stated. Since the envelope is shared with the ontology_crosswalk profile (where null is legitimate), add an explicit machine-checkable profile constraint in common-objects-and-envelopes.md: mapping_decision_receipt_ref must be non-null when mapping_record_profile = semantic_mapping_decision and status is beyond proposed. Small fix, but this is exactly the class of ambiguity the envelope-unification move was supposed to prevent.
14. **ConnectorMapping squats the generic mapping:// URI scheme while ontology mappings live at ontology-mapping://** — `low` · Rename
   - Evidence: foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:4335 — 'connector_mapping_id: mapping://...'
   - Recommendation: The envelope family literally named OntologyMapping uses ontology-mapping://, while the unrelated ConnectorMapping family owns bare mapping:// (also referenced as 'mapping://...' in DataRecipeEnvelope's connector_mapping_refs at line 4296 — and OntologyMappingEnvelope's own mapping_profile_ref accepts 'artifact://... | mapping://...' at line 4197, so the conflation is already live inside the semantic envelope itself). Every reader and every ref-validating implementation will conflate them. Rename the ConnectorMapping scheme to connector-mapping:// (keeping mapping:// as a documented legacy alias during migration, like the existing recipe:// alias precedent at line 4328), and reserve bare mapping:// as invalid. Cheap now; expensive after the first admitted objects exist.
15. **DistilledOntologyDataset reads as 'a distilled ontology', not 'distilled ontology-bound data'** — `low` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: foundations/domain-ontologies-and-data-recipes.md:35 — 'Distilled Ontology Dataset = compact, high-signal training/evaluation data derived from ontology-bound source truth.'
   - Recommendation: The object distills data that is bound to an ontology; the name parses as a compressed ontology (a real, different concept — ontology summarization exists). The file's own gloss at line 35 disambiguates for careful readers, but the bare identifier travels without the gloss. Rename to OntologyBoundDistilledDataset or DistilledDomainDataset across the sites that actually use the term: this file (including the Agentgres boundary list at line 462), common-objects-and-envelopes.md, components/agentgres/api-object-model.md, foundations/worker-training-lifecycle.md, components/hypervisor/foundry.md, and the _meta maps (current-canon-defaults.md, source-of-truth-map.md, refactor-baseline.md); vocabulary.md currently has no entry for the term. The pack's own naming style elsewhere ('distilled ontology-bound data', lines 47-48) already prefers the unambiguous form.


## Part III.7 — Daemon runtime

### Daemon doctrine + API surface (admission, scheduling, mediation)

**Files reviewed:** `components/daemon-runtime/doctrine.md (706)` · `components/daemon-runtime/api.md (3450) — read fully in chunks`

**Current shape.** doctrine.md defines the Hypervisor Daemon as the single "universal execution endpoint and hypervisor/control plane" for governed autonomous work: every client (Hypervisor App/Web, CLI/headless, TUI, SDK, ADK, IDE-sidecar "Authority Gateway" adapters, external agent harnesses) is a projection or proposal source that must not own execution semantics, while Agentgres records admitted truth and wallet.network supplies authority for consequential effects. Sensitive actions require a persisted policy decision, scoped authority lease, exact request hash, expiry, and receipts, and the daemon fails closed. api.md then hand-enumerates on the order of 500 HTTP endpoint listings under /v1 (544 method lines, including a handful of duplicates): runtime identity/health, client read-model projections (home cockpit, sessions, agents, models, privacy posture, provider placement), a proposal → approved-operation → execution-plan → dispatch admission pipeline bound by content hashes, plus workers, runs, work-queues/items/runs, an agent operating plane, threads/turns, sessions/environments, tools/models/connectors, thread-scoped MCP normalization, memory, subagents, identity/SSO/SCIM/secrets/metering, autonomous-system control, embodied-runtime control, OutcomeRoom/GoalRun and improvement-campaign routes. Events stream via SSE/NDJSON/WebSocket with monotonic cursors and exactly-once terminal events; all errors share one six-code redacted JSON shape. The file openly declares itself a hand-maintained "reference wall" whose live-endpoint source of truth is actually the Rust route registry, with many families explicitly target-only.

**What it already gets right:**

- The proposal→admission→dispatch pipeline (proposal_content_hash, expected revisions, atomic re-validation at admission, execution plans that stay awaiting_executor) is a genuinely stronger mutation discipline than most control planes ship; it generalizes Kubernetes admission plus Stripe-style request binding into one fail-closed pattern (api.md:1452-1677).
- Desired-vs-observed state separation is applied consistently at the doctrine level: WorkRun binds desired_phase and current_phase, autonomous-system topology distinguishes desired profiles from observed membership, and projections are explicitly read_model_only — real declarative-intent hygiene (api.md:1826-1827, 2860-2863).
- The event-stream contract states precise replay semantics most SSE APIs never define: monotonic cursors, exactly-once terminal events, no duplicate terminal on reconnect, and an ordering rule that tool execution can never precede its permitting policy decision (api.md:2405-2411).
- Per-family implementation-status candor (live vs target-only, 'not present in the currently audited daemon registry') is rare and valuable in a target-canon pack; readers are seldom misled about what exists (api.md:2815-2818, 2996-2997).
- Short-lived, audience-bound, revocation-epoch-bound access/log tokens derived from a canonical lease object (HypervisorSessionAccessLease) is ahead of typical dev-tool daemons that mint durable bearer credentials (api.md:2311-2326).

**SOTA verdict: `behind`** — compared against: Kubernetes API machinery (list/watch, resourceVersion, conditions, admission webhooks, OpenAPI discovery endpoint); Google AIP / gRPC API governance (AIP-132 list, AIP-158 pagination, AIP-134 field-mask update, AIP-193 errors); Stripe idempotency-key protocol and dated API versioning; RFC 9457 Problem Details / google.rpc.Status typed error details; Temporal (single WorkflowExecution abstraction with one lifecycle verb set and long-poll semantics); SCIM 2.0 / OIDC (which the daemon embeds wholesale)

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No machine-checkable IDL: the 3450-line prose wall is hand-maintained while the Rust route registry is admitted to be the real source of truth — Kubernetes, Stripe, and every gRPC shop generate docs from schema and CI-gate drift
- No idempotency-key protocol for creates/actions despite doctrine requiring idempotent retry (Stripe-class gap)
- Six-code error enum with no details field cannot express the doc's own failure taxonomy (RFC 9457 / google.rpc.Status gap)
- No uniform pagination or filter grammar on collections; only two client projections plus room discovery define cursors (AIP-158 gap)
- No collection-scoped watch; clients must fan out per-run/per-thread streams to build cockpits (K8s list+watch gap)
- No deprecation/version-negotiation policy; /v1 path, per-object schema_version, and query-param schema selection coexist (K8s API-versioning / Stripe dated-version gap)
- Imperative verb-tunnel setters instead of declarative partial update with field masks (AIP-134 gap)

**Findings (16, severity-ordered):**

1. **The API canon is a self-admitted hand-maintained reference wall whose real source of truth is the code** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/api.md:18 — 'This file enumerates the public daemon API surface by hand.'
   - Recommendation: Execute the file's own 'Generator TODO' (api.md:23-24) as a hard gate, not a wish: make an OpenAPI (or proto) document per route family the canonical artifact, generate the endpoint listing in api.md from it, and CI-diff it against the daemon route registry. Keep only contract semantics (admission rules, invariants, hard rules) hand-written. Every review round on this branch has hand-edited this wall; at roughly 500 endpoint listings (544 method lines) across 20+ families it is a guaranteed drift engine, and drift here damages the pack's core credibility claim that canon is authoritative.
2. **Doctrine mandates idempotent retry but no idempotency mechanism exists anywhere in the API** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/doctrine.md:538 — 'retrying the same payload must be idempotency-aware so it does not create duplicate autonomous runs'
   - Recommendation: Define one Stripe-style Idempotency-Key contract in api.md and require it on every consequential POST (/v1/runs, /v1/work-items, /v1/agent-sessions, /v1/hypervisor/goal-runs, proposal and approved-operation routes): key scoped to principal, bound to a request content hash, replayed responses returned verbatim within a retention window, 409 on same-key/different-payload. The only idempotency_key in 3450 lines is on llm-usage-events (api.md:2043). Without this, the WorkRun duplicate-run hazard doctrine names is unhandled at the exact boundary that creates autonomous spend.
3. **Six-code error enum with no details slot cannot carry the document's own failure taxonomy** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/api.md:3364 — '"code": "policy_denied | not_found | invalid_request | unavailable | conflict | internal"'
   - Recommendation: Adopt RFC 9457 problem+json or google.rpc.Status with typed detail objects, preserving the existing redaction discipline (redaction_status stays; details carry reason codes and refs, not payloads). The doc itself needs to express: typed-unavailable/target-only (api.md:1379-1382), requires_approval with approval_ref, stale proposal_content_hash, missing scope refs, revoked binding, executor-not-mounted, fenced writer epoch. Today all of these collapse into six strings plus a boolean retryable, so fail-closed clients cannot distinguish 'add scope and retry' from 'endpoint not implemented' from 'hash mismatch — refetch proposal'. Reuse the disabled_reason_codes pattern (api.md:1242) as the reason-code registry for errors.
4. **Loopback-stays-open default with no stated CSRF/Origin/DNS-rebinding defense on a consequential local daemon** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/api.md:2725-2726 — '`auto` mode authenticates when the deployment is exposed (loopback stays open), with a one-time lockout bootstrap.'
   - Recommendation: Any webpage can fire cross-origin POSTs at http://127.0.0.1 (CORS blocks reads, not effects), and DNS rebinding defeats host checks — the classic local-daemon CVE class (Docker API, older Ollama, JupyterLab). The owner doc identity-access-and-metering.md defines exposure only as 'bound non-loopback, or reached via a forwarded host'; neither heuristic covers a browser-origin request arriving directly at an open loopback listener, and neither doc names Origin checks, CSRF tokens, or DNS-rebinding defense. This daemon's loopback surface includes POST /v1/runs, tool calls, and session exec. Require, even on loopback: an Origin/Host allowlist, a non-simple custom header or locally-provisioned token (the /v1/hypervisor/api-tokens plumbing already exists), and document it in api.md plus identity-access-and-metering.md. Fail-closed authority for consequential effects mitigates but does not cover read projections, proposals, and non-wallet-gated routes.
5. **The 'execution endpoint' daemon absorbs a full IAM/SaaS plane: SCIM server, SSO/OIDC, domain verification, custom domains, invites, metering** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/api.md:2775 — 'Invites, domain verification (DNS-TXT over DoH), and custom domain:'
   - Recommendation: The pack already separates this conceptually: api.md:2717-2727 names it 'the daemon-local deployment-governance plane' that 'does NOT authorize consequential crossings', with its own canon owner (identity-access-and-metering.md) and INV-3 (AuthFactor ≠ AuthorityGrant) behind it. What canon does not separate is the process, API surface, versioning, and threat model: the SCIM 2.0 provisioning server, OIDC flows, DNS-over-HTTPS verifier, invites, api-tokens, and budget/metering (api.md:2732-2811) all live inside the admission daemon binary whose doctrine says 'The daemon executes work' (doctrine.md:344). Declare the deployment-governance plane a separable component that may front the daemon (even if co-deployed in local single-binary mode), so its attack surface and enterprise-IT release cadence are quarantined from the runtime kernel — mirroring how wallet.network already externalizes authority.
6. **The daemon compiles product navigation, favorites, and UI back-stack semantics — a BFF/presentation layer inside the admission kernel** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/api.md:1157 — 'compiles the one request-scoped, policy-filtered navigation, Applications, command-palette, and contextual-launch projection'
   - Recommendation: The doc has an explicit rationale for server-side compilation — 'No client may join these records or infer launchability locally' (api.md:1165-1166) and the response deliberately avoids 'a second hand-maintained card catalog' (api.md:1211-1213) — so a naive move of presentation to clients would fight stated doctrine. The unaddressed cost stands, though: grouping (recommended/recent/favorites, api.md:1192-1194), display copy (summary, primary_user_job, api.md:1260-1261), and route-alias UI context like open_application_identity_and_back_stack (api.md:1022) mean every navigation tweak, card-copy edit, or back-stack behavior change is a daemon API change — accretion the doctrine's own anti-pattern list warns against in reverse. Keep policy-derived eligibility (launchable, disabled_reason_codes, effective_enablement_state) daemon-owned, and name the product-surface compiler a separate Core projection service in canon (its own schema_version cadence, its own owner doc) so presentation churn is quarantined from the admission kernel even if it ships in the same process today.
7. **Six overlapping unit-of-execution resources with parallel lifecycle verbs and no required canonical mapping on facade responses** — `medium` · Refactor *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/api.md:1893 — 'It is not a second runtime beside `/v1/runs`, `/v1/work-*`, or `/v1/threads`.'
   - Recommendation: The API exposes runs, work-runs, agent-executions, threads/turns, jobs, and goal-runs, each with its own status/events/input/stop-or-cancel subresources (api.md:1743-1791, 1899-1913, 2679-2683, 3001-3008). The /v1/work projection partially addresses identity: every row returns exactly one canonical subject_kind/subject_ref over GoalRun/OutcomeRoom/AutomationRun/Session/WorkQueue/WorkItem/WorkRun (api.md:2370-2374). But agent-executions and jobs are not in that subject enumeration, and nothing requires a facade resource's own response to carry its canonical ref — agent execution status only 'should include' refs (api.md:1964-1967) and jobs never name their work_run. Temporal succeeds with one WorkflowExecution identity. Mandate that every facade resource returns the canonical execution identity (the agent-sessions response at api.md:1947-1961 already models this well), fold agent-executions and jobs into the /v1/work subject enumeration or map them onto existing subjects, and publish one shared lifecycle verb set so SDK authors have a single target instead of six.
8. **'Agent' names at least three different objects across the same API** — `medium` · Rename
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/api.md:1727-1728 — 'In the daemon API, `agents` are product-facing managed worker instances.'
   - Recommendation: /v1/agents = managed worker instances (api.md:1727-1741); GET /v1/hypervisor/agents = 'configured runtime actors over Hypervisor Core' with harness/model/memory bindings (api.md:365-366); /v1/agent-executions and /v1/agent-sessions = the operating-plane run lane; plus Agent Harness Adapters and the CLI's 'ioi agent' aliases (doctrine.md:228-229). Pick one: keep 'Worker Instance' for the installed-package binding (rename /v1/agents to /v1/worker-instances), reserve 'Agent' for the configured-actor record, and rename agent-executions to executions under the canonical run identity. The vocabulary doc's single-owner discipline exists precisely for this; the API is the surface where the collision will bite SDK users hardest.
9. **Approval decisions are writable through four different route families** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/api.md:2422 — 'POST /v1/threads/{thread_id}/approvals/{approval_id}/decision'
   - Recommendation: Approve/deny exists on /v1/approvals/{id} (api.md:2418-2420), thread-scoped decision (2422), /v1/runs/{id}/approve|deny (1755-1756), and /v1/action-requests/{id}/approve|deny (2450-2451). For the object that carries exact-scope approval tokens and request hashes (doctrine.md:604-611), multiple decision surfaces invite divergent validation paths and audit ambiguity about which route minted the token. Make Approval a single resource with one decision endpoint binding request_hash + policy_hash; make run-, thread-, and action-request-scoped routes read-only views (GET) that link to the canonical approval ref.
10. **Thread configuration tunneled through seven imperative verb-POST setters instead of declarative partial update** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/api.md:2130-2134 — 'POST /v1/threads/{thread_id}/mode … /model … /reasoning … /speed … /control'
   - Recommendation: mode/model/reasoning/speed/control/context-budget/compaction-policy are field mutations, not actions. Replace with PATCH /v1/threads/{thread_id} carrying a typed spec plus field mask and expected_revision (the optimistic-concurrency pattern the pack already uses for AutomationSpecs), emitting one thread-config-changed event and receipt per admitted patch. Keep verb POSTs only for true actions with runtime side effects: fork, resume, compact, interrupt, steer. This halves the endpoint count, gives clients atomic multi-field updates (change model+reasoning together), and aligns with AIP-134 and Kubernetes spec-update semantics without weakening admission — the PATCH still crosses the same policy gate.
11. **No uniform list/pagination/filter contract; only two projections plus room discovery define cursors** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/api.md:883-885 — 'Clients may request `page_cursor` / `page_size` values, but those cursors are projection inputs only.'
   - Recommendation: GET /v1/work-items, /v1/work-runs, /v1/sessions, /v1/agents, /v1/threads, /v1/approvals, /v1/jobs, /v1/hypervisor/goal-runs and every other collection (api.md:1763-1795, 2416, 2679-2680) have no pagination, ordering, or filtering contract; only receipt-evidence (856-859) and model-infrastructure (543-545) define page cursors, and room discovery defines an opaque cursor (3195-3197). Adopt one AIP-158-style contract in the shared-contract preamble: opaque page_token, page_size cap, next_page_token, plus a minimal filter grammar (subject_kind, project_ref, phase, since). Without it every daemon route family will invent its own, and the reference wall will faithfully record the divergence.
12. **No collection-scoped watch: cockpit clients must poll projections or fan out per-resource streams** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/api.md:2402 — 'GET /v1/runs/{run_id}/events?format=sse|ndjson|websocket&after={cursor}&mode=replay|tail|replay-and-tail'
   - Recommendation: Streams exist only per run, per thread, per work-run, per agent-execution, per harness-invocation. The Home cockpit, Work projection (/v1/work), and Missions views must therefore poll or open N streams. Add a scoped collection watch — GET /v1/work/events?scope=project://…&after={cursor} — reusing the already-excellent monotonic-cursor and exactly-once-terminal rules (api.md:2405-2411), emitting typed subject-change envelopes (subject_kind, subject_ref, phase, revision). This is Kubernetes list+watch applied to the pack's own read-projection doctrine: the watch stays a projection, never truth, so no invariant changes.
13. **Versioning is fragmented across path v1, per-object schema_version, query-param schema selection, and silent field aliasing — with no deprecation policy** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/api.md:943 — 'GET /v1/hypervisor/core-taxonomy?schema=ioi.runtime.hypervisor_core_taxonomy.v2'
   - Recommendation: Four mechanisms coexist: the /v1 path, ioi.*.v1/v2 schema_version strings, a query-param schema selector (943), and adapters that 'may still accept the historical nested `recipe`' names (1446-1449) or deprecated template_ref aliases (1576-1582). Pick one canonical mechanism — per-object schema_version with server-side conversion (Kubernetes-style) is closest to what exists — then write a deprecation contract in api.md: how long aliases live, how target-only v2 requests fail (which error code), and how clients discover supported schema versions per route (extend GET /v1/runtime/manifest). Drop query-param schema negotiation; it fragments caches and audit trails.
14. **Dispatch fails closed on camelCase aliases while the canon's own payloads carry camelCase `runtimeTruthSource` 19 times** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/api.md:1672-1673 — 'Dispatch fails closed when no executor is mounted, the client supplies camelCase aliases,'
   - Recommendation: The same file that rejects camelCase aliases as a fail-closed condition stamps `runtimeTruthSource` (api.md:115 and 18 more occurrences — 19 verified) into nearly every canonical response and into client request bodies, including the very execution_plan a dispatch client must echo back (api.md:1640; also 1421, 1436). Read charitably, the fail-closed rule targets camelCase aliases of canonical snake_case fields and `runtimeTruthSource` is the canonical (if inconsistently styled) name — but the doc never says so, and a strict casing-keyed validator or client linter generated from these examples cannot distinguish the sanctioned field from banned aliases. Either rename it to runtime_truth_source across api.md and the emitting route implementations, or add an explicit naming-convention rule to the file header naming it the single grandfathered exception — exactly the kind of internal inconsistency a generated IDL would have caught.
15. **doctrine's 'minimum API surface' includes GET /v1/deliveries/{id}, which api.md never defines** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/doctrine.md:393 — 'GET  /v1/deliveries/{id}'
   - Recommendation: api.md — the declared canonical owner of 'public daemon/runtime API endpoints' (api.md:4-6) — contains no deliveries route family at all; DeliveryBundle appears only as an envelope name (doctrine.md:479) and a desired_delivery enum plus delivery refs (api.md:1817, 1802). Either add a delivery-bundle read family to api.md's Artifact and Receipt section (GET /v1/deliveries/{id}, GET /v1/runs/{id}/deliveries), pointing at events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md for object semantics, or remove the endpoint from doctrine's minimum surface. A 'minimum' list containing an endpoint absent from the full reference is precisely the drift the reference-wall notice predicts.
16. **Proposal requests make clients haul server-owned truth refs (latest_receipt_refs, state roots) into request bodies** — `medium` · Better way *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/api.md:276 — '"latest_receipt_refs": ["receipt://..."]' (project-operations request body)
   - Recommendation: Project-operation (api.md:268-277), automation-run-proposal (452-471), and approved-operation (1489-1497) requests require callers to supply receipt refs, agentgres operation refs, and state_root_refs that the daemon and Agentgres already own. The doc says the daemon verifies rather than trusts them, but a dozen copy-through fields per request is an SDK-bug and confused-deputy factory, and stale copies force spurious fail-closed rejections. The file already models the right pattern once: autonomous-system create says 'The daemon derives the sequence-zero … commitments from canonical admitted inputs and may compare caller-supplied expected values; callers never author commitment truth' (api.md:2844-2847). Generalize exactly that: slim requests to the actual client commitments — proposal_ref + content_hash + expected state root (optimistic concurrency) + requested overrides — with the daemon resolving current receipts and operation refs itself and returning them in the response. Same fail-closed binding, far smaller trusted-input surface.

### Events, receipts, delivery bundles + task capsule protocol

**Files reviewed:** `components/daemon-runtime/events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md (2870) — read fully in chunks` · `components/daemon-runtime/task-capsule-protocol.md (245)`

**Current shape.** The events/receipts doc is the canonical registry for the runtime observability and proof plane: a flat list of ~300 required event kinds, one JSON event shape, and an exhaustive registry of ~180 receipt types that all extend a shared ReceiptEnvelope owned by foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md. Its core doctrine is an "assurance ladder" (receipt/attestation → evidence → verification → acceptance → adjudication → settlement, INV-9): a receipt binds exactly one declared boundary fact and proves nothing more, and projections must never collapse the ladder into a single verified boolean. Specialized receipt families cover model invocation with invoice-reconciled usage, learning-boundary egress, GoalRun/Automation resolution freezing, routing and orchestration decisions, contributions, private-workspace cTEE execution, embodied/physical action, OutcomeRoom collective work (each room-child receipt carrying a hash-chained revision/sequence/state-root "proof spine"), autonomous-system state-transition commitments with writer epochs and fencing, compliance audit exports, and delivery bundles that package artifacts, evidence, receipt roots, and Agentgres state commitments for marketplace acceptance and settlement. Trace bundles are declared inspection projections over events/receipts, distinct from a Proof/Settlement Explorer. The task-capsule doc defines runtime assignment, ComputeSession, a least-privilege TaskCapsule (visible context, hidden context classes, allowed/forbidden actions, TTL, watermark), execution privacy modes (mutual-blind, HypervisorOS bare-metal, TEE enterprise, plus design-stage cTEE/embodied), a TEE attestation envelope gating wallet.network secret release, and a result envelope returning proposed outputs that only daemon/verifier/Agentgres may settle into truth. Signing is present but optional throughout; Merkle roots and predecessor commitments appear as fields, with proof formats, key lifecycle, and third-party verification procedures left unspecified.

**What it already gets right:**

- The assurance ladder is genuinely ahead of industry practice: every receipt schema states which boundary fact it binds and explicitly enumerates what it does NOT prove (e.g., learning-egress 'admitted' proves policy allowed the crossing, not provider deletion or non-training; 'blocked_before_egress' reaches verified assurance only with enforcement evidence binding the request commitment and absence of a network write — Non-Negotiables 16-17). Most provenance systems, including SLSA/in-toto deployments, collapse this into one 'verified' bit.
- Anti-sock-puppet discipline is rare and correct: routing and collaboration receipts carry candidate-affiliation commitments and the rule that 'several models, workers, nodes, clouds, or provider keys controlled by one operator remain one party' (events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:1961), directly countering first-party seed fleets masquerading as independent verification — ahead of any named agent-marketplace design.
- Negative, inconclusive, exploit-found, and superseded attempts are first-class durable, creditable lineage (AttemptAdmissionReceipt, ContributionReceipt kinds, Non-Negotiable 11), which no mainstream work-attribution system models.
- Replication honesty in the state-transition commitment family is strong: writer epochs, catch-up/root verification, old-writer fencing, fail-closed ambiguous partitions (Non-Negotiables 13-14) encode lessons most event-sourced/ledger systems learn post-incident.
- Pragmatic payload/truth separation: heavy bytes stay in storage backends, Agentgres holds refs/roots, and high-frequency embodied control aggregates into PhysicalActionSegmentCommitmentReceipt with immediate exception receipts — receipt discipline that actually scales down to 100Hz control loops.

**SOTA verdict: `behind`** — compared against: Certificate Transparency (RFC 6962) and Sigstore Rekor: append-only Merkle logs with inclusion/consistency proofs, signed checkpoints, and witness/gossip ecosystems against split-view attacks; in-toto attestations + DSSE envelopes + SLSA provenance: mandatory signed statements with defined predicate types and offline verification workflows; COSE (RFC 9052) / JWS signing and RFC 8785 JCS (or deterministic CBOR) canonicalization for reproducible cross-implementation hashing; IETF RATS architecture (RFC 9334) + EAT: attestation freshness (nonce/report_data binding), verifier roles, and appraisal policies; NVIDIA Hopper/Blackwell GPU confidential computing for AI plaintext; CloudEvents 1.0 + transactional outbox + event-sourcing delivery semantics (at-least-once with idempotency keys); OpenTelemetry traces/spans as the interop standard the SessionTraceBundle parallels; WORM/object-lock retention (S3 Object Lock) — partially covered via retention_lock_refs; AWS QLDB as the pack's own cited cautionary anchor for ledgers without ecosystems

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- Signatures are 'optional' on the base envelope and every shown receipt; no signing envelope format (DSSE/COSE), signer-identity classes, key discovery, or rotation/revocation semantics for receipt-signing keys (rotation exists only for authority clients)
- No transparency-log machinery despite receipt_root/state-root fields everywhere: no inclusion or consistency proof format, no checkpoint object, no witness/countersigning profile, no split-view/equivocation defense for hosted rooms; AIIP names 'inclusion proof' without defining one; L1 anchoring is optional and sparse
- No canonical serialization spec for what bytes any sha256/root commits to — only the collaboration-terms family names a canonicalization profile (ioi.collaboration-terms-body.v1); every other hash is non-reproducible across implementations
- No trusted timestamping: receipt timestamps are self-asserted by the emitting daemon, so a compromised signer key can forge backdated history undetectably
- TEE attestation envelope lacks nonce/report_data binding to the assignment, omits TDX/SEV-SNP and GPU confidential computing — the venue where AI plaintext actually lives
- No delivery semantics for the runtime event stream owned here: at-least-once vs exactly-once, idempotency, outbox ordering relative to Agentgres admission, kind versioning, CloudEvents mapping all unspecified (the Agentgres postgres-bridge contract declares at-least-once subscription delivery with consumer idempotency, but that covers Agentgres subscriptions, not this event plane)
- No OpenTelemetry mapping for the trace-bundle plane
- Registry is prose, not machine-readable schemas, despite receipt_profile_ref: schema://... existing in the base envelope

**Findings (16, severity-ordered):**

1. **Receipts are unverifiable by outside parties: signatures optional, no verification procedure, no signer-key lifecycle** — `critical` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:926 — 'signature: optional' (same on ReceiptEnvelope, foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3829)
   - Recommendation: The facet's whole purpose is exportable proof (delivery bundles 'carry the evidence required for marketplace acceptance'), yet nothing specifies how a buyer, auditor, or AIIP counterparty verifies a receipt offline. Signing intent exists at the edges — AIIP packets are declared 'signed, sequenced, idempotent' and CollaborationTermsAcceptance carries acceptance_signature_ref — but no signing-envelope format, signer-identity classes, key discovery, or rotation/revocation semantics are specified anywhere for receipts. Add a Receipt Verification section to this file: (1) signature REQUIRED for all consequential receipt classes, using a DSSE or COSE_Sign1 profile over the canonical body; (2) signer-identity classes (daemon node key, room-host key, participant-lease key) enrolled via NodeMembershipAdmissionReceipt/wallet.network with key-rotation receipts; (3) an offline verify algorithm (resolve key → check signature → check inclusion). Without this, every exported bundle is an unverifiable assertion — signed-JSON-in-a-database at best.
2. **No transparency-log rigor: receipt roots exist but inclusion/consistency proofs, checkpoints, and split-view defense are unspecified** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:2044 — '"resulting_receipt_root": "sha256:..."'; foundations/aiip.md:463 — 'inclusion proof for later evidence, verification, acceptance, or dispute'
   - Recommendation: The pack has the ingredients — hash-chained per-system commitments (sequence + expected_predecessor_commitment_ref), Agentgres plans incremental content-addressed Merkleization (agentgres/doctrine.md:236), optional L1 anchoring, and the L1 docs name a bonded availability-witness role — but none of it yields inclusion/consistency proof formats, checkpoint objects, or equivocation defense, so a hosted room host or daemon can show different histories to different audiences: the exact attack CT (RFC 6962) and Sigstore Rekor exist to stop. Specify: a Merkle accumulator over admitted receipts per system_id; a signed checkpoint object (origin, tree size, root); inclusion + consistency proof formats; and a witness profile where AIIP counterparties, auditors, and optionally IOI L1 countersign checkpoints. Room participants MUST gossip checkpoints; divergence is a VerifierChallenge trigger. Anchoring becomes one witness class, not the only one.
3. **No canonical serialization spec — every sha256/root field commits to undefined bytes** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:1861 — '`accepted_scope_root` is the canonical hash of the referenced `CollaborationTermsEnvelope.scope` object'
   - Recommendation: Only the collaboration-terms family (terms_body_root/accepted_scope_root under ioi.collaboration-terms-body.v1) names its canonicalization profile. For every other hash in the ~180 receipt types — policy_hash, input_hash, operation_or_batch_commitment, receipt_root, candidate_set_commitment — there is no rule for key ordering, encoding, or null handling, so two conforming implementations produce different hashes and no third party can recompute any commitment. Adopt one canonical-hashing spec (RFC 8785 JCS or deterministic CBOR) with a domain-separation tag per envelope type, owned in foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md, and require every *_hash/*_root field to name its profile the way accepted_scope_root already does.
4. **TEE attestation envelope lacks freshness/report_data binding and omits the TEEs that matter for AI (TDX, SEV-SNP, GPU CC)** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/task-capsule-protocol.md:192 — '"provider": "eigen | sgx | sev | nitro | other"'
   - Recommendation: The envelope (task-capsule-protocol.md:188-200) has measurement, manifest hashes, and valid_until but no nonce and no binding to the assignment or capsule — a node can replay one quote across many capsules within its validity window before wallet.network releases sealed keys. (The cTEE plane defines a provider_bound_nonce for its own attestation flow, private-workspace-ctee.md:1184, but the capsule Secret Release Rule verifies only measurement/hashes/expiry/identity — nothing binds a quote to one assignment.) Require quote report_data = H(assignment_id ‖ task_capsule_ref ‖ daemon-issued challenge); align verifier_profile with RATS (RFC 9334) verifier/appraisal roles. Update the provider enum: add tdx, sev_snp, nvidia_cc_gpu; SGX is effectively legacy for new server workloads. And state explicitly that tee_enterprise for model execution requires a CPU+GPU attestation chain — otherwise 'sensitive data and worker IP may exist remotely only inside verified confidential compute' (line 84) is false for anything doing inference, since plaintext lives in GPU VRAM.
5. **Result envelope from remote nodes is unauthenticated — no node signature, attestation optional** — `high` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/task-capsule-protocol.md:233 — '"attestation_ref": "optional"'
   - Recommendation: The result envelope (task-capsule-protocol.md:219-235) carries only IDs and refs; nothing cryptographically binds the returned artifacts, receipts, and logs to the capsule or the executing node. The doc's proposal-not-truth posture (Non-Negotiable 1: verifier/Agentgres settle) means a forged result cannot directly become truth, but on adversarial DePIN venues attribution, billing, and dispute evidence are still trust-me JSON that any middlebox or substituted node can forge, and Non-Negotiable 4 ('every remote run must emit execution receipts and artifact commitments') has no teeth because those commitments are themselves unauthenticated. Require a node signature (DSSE) over (capsule_id, output artifact hashes, receipt refs, event-stream root) using the node membership key enrolled at admission; make attestation_ref mandatory for tee_enterprise and hypervisoros_bare_metal; daemon rejects unsigned results from all non-local venues.
6. **Two conflicting RoutingDecisionReceipt schemas inside the file that claims schema supremacy** — `high` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:1045 — 'RoutingDecisionReceipt:' vs :2418 — '"receipt_type": "routing_decision"'
   - Recommendation: The canonical YAML schema (1044-1096: routing_decision_ref, routing_decision_hash, reason_codes list, affiliation commitments, attempted/actual route refs) and the legacy JSON block in Worker Training Receipts (2415-2433: routing_decision_id, singular reason_code, selected_domain_or_worker bare string, no affiliation fields) disagree on identity, fields, and cardinality. This file declares 'when an excerpt elsewhere disagrees with a schema defined here, this file wins' (lines 70-72) — it violates its own rule internally. Delete the 2415-2433 block or reduce it to a link to the canonical schema, and add a duplicate-receipt-type lint to the architecture-docs gate.
7. **Delivery object soup: DeliveryBundle, DeliveryEnvelope, and ServiceCompositionReceiptBundle overlap with divergent shapes** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:2701 — '## DeliveryBundle' vs foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3909 — '## DeliveryEnvelope'
   - Recommendation: DeliveryEnvelope (common-objects) and DeliveryBundle (this file) model the same deliverable with divergent, partially overlapping field sets — both carry output_artifacts/evidence_bundle/quality_summary/policy_summary, then fork into local_receipt_root/remote_receipt_root/delivery_update_refs vs receipt_bundle_ref/state_commitment — with no stated mapping; ServiceCompositionReceiptBundle (2629) adds a third overlapping evidence container that must disclaim 'it is not a replacement for DeliveryBundle' (2634). Make DeliveryEnvelope the single base in common-objects; redefine DeliveryBundle here as its exported materialization profile with an explicit field-by-field mapping (state_commitment ↔ local_receipt_root); fold ServiceCompositionReceiptBundle into an evidence-profile section over DeliveryEnvelope refs. Add a parity assert between the two files to the docs gates.
8. **Token-and-chain literals leak into generic schemas, contradicting the declared settlement-mode spectrum** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:2745 — '"l1_contract": "0x..."' vs :2249 — '"settlement_mode": "local_domain | bilateral | invoice | external_escrow | external_chain | ioi_l1"'
   - Recommendation: The generic DeliveryBundle hardcodes settlement as an L1 contract object, and RuntimeAssignment prices work as '"quote": "5 IOI"' with a 0x escrow_ref (task-capsule-protocol.md:109-112) — while this same file insists flat charges are 'a development projection, not invoice-grade Work Credit truth' (681) and the pack's anti-pattern list bans 'L1 settlement for every nested service step by default' (2698). The fix is mechanical because the typed shape already exists: SettlementEnvelope (common-objects) carries settlement_mode, settlement_profile_ref, and a rail object with asset_kind/amount. Replace DeliveryBundle.settlement with {settlement_mode, settlement_profile_ref, settlement_ref} matching the compliance-export shape; replace the assignment payment with a typed money object (amount, unit_ref, rail/profile ref). These literals are credibility damage: they read as chain-first design the doctrine explicitly rejects.
9. **Mutable lifecycle status enums inside receipts contradict the receipts-are-immutable doctrine** — `medium` · Contradiction *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:1957 — '"status": "created | party_joined | party_removed | view_granted | view_revoked | proof_bundle_generated | archived"' vs :1868 — 'Acceptance receipts are immutable. Withdrawal, revocation, or supersession'
   - Recommendation: MultiPartyCollaborationReceipt's status enum plausibly names the recorded transition (it mirrors the collaboration.* event kinds), but the doc never states one-receipt-per-transition semantics, and other families genuinely read as current state: ArtifactAvailabilityReceipt carries 'open | quarantined | escalated' (1332) and the OutcomeRoom receipt 'proposed | admitted | challenged | superseded | rejected | revoked' (2046) — fields that either mutate (violating append-only) or go stale the moment they're minted. CollaborationTermsAcceptance receipts get the correct treatment: immutable, superseded via predecessor_acceptance_ref. Generalize that discipline: one receipt per transition, status = the transition outcome at emission time only, and add predecessor_receipt_ref/supersedes_receipt_ref to the ReceiptEnvelope base so every family gets append-only supersession instead of ad-hoc per-family fields. State the per-transition rule explicitly in the registry rules at the top of this file.
10. **'receipt_root' means three different things: per-receipt content hash, ledger accumulator root, and (as receipt_hash) the same content hash again** — `medium` · Rename
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:925 — 'receipt_root: hash' (per-receipt field) vs :2044 — '"resulting_receipt_root": "sha256:..."' (ledger root); :1172 — 'receipt_hash: sha256:...'
   - Recommendation: GoalRunProfileResolution/AutomationRunResolution/OrchestrationDecision receipts each carry a field named receipt_root that is evidently a self-commitment over the receipt body; state-transition and room receipts use resulting_receipt_root to mean the accumulated root of the whole receipt ledger; ContributionReceipt calls its self-commitment receipt_hash. A verifier implementing 'check the receipt against the root' will confuse these. Rename the per-receipt self-commitment to receipt_body_hash everywhere (defined once on ReceiptEnvelope with its canonicalization profile), and reserve receipt_root/resulting_receipt_root exclusively for the accumulator root that inclusion proofs verify against.
11. **Event plane has no delivery contract: no at-least-once/idempotency semantics, no outbox rule, no versioning, no CloudEvents mapping** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:464 — '"cursor": 42,'
   - Recommendation: This is the 'canonical low-level reference' for runtime events, yet the event shape (456-471) leaves cursor scope undefined (per run? per stream? per node?), has no schema-version field, and the file never states delivery semantics. The Agentgres postgres-bridge contract does declare 'subscription delivery may be at-least-once' with consumer idempotency via operation ids and cursors (postgres-bridge-and-readiness-contract.md:487-488), but that covers Agentgres subscriptions, not the runtime event stream owned here. Every serious event system specifies these (CloudEvents 1.0 attributes; transactional-outbox ordering; consumer dedupe on id). Add an Event Delivery Contract section: cursor is per-run monotonic; delivery is at-least-once with consumer idempotency on event_id; events describing admitted facts are emitted only after the corresponding Agentgres admission (outbox rule) so streams can never lead truth; each kind carries a schema_ref + version; define a CloudEvents binding for external consumers.
12. **The ~300-kind event list and ~180-type receipt registry as prose is a drift engine** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:61 — 'file owns the exhaustive receipt-type registry, cross-component specialized'
   - Recommendation: The flat lists (125-452 event kinds; 476-658 receipt types) span built, partial, and planned subsystems from MCP gateways to spacetime reservations, with no per-kind schema, status, or owner. Every new plane appends here by hand while its owner doc evolves independently — exactly the drift the pack's excerpt-supremacy rule exists to prevent, and the duplicate RoutingDecisionReceipt shows it already failing. Since ReceiptEnvelope already carries receipt_profile_ref: schema://..., make the registry machine-readable: one schema file per receipt profile and event kind (with owner doc, implementation status), generate these lists from it, and add a CI parity gate. Keep this file as semantics owner, not list custodian.
13. **Privacy-mode/venue enums drift within the 245-line capsule doc itself** — `medium` · Contradiction *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/task-capsule-protocol.md:96 — '"privacy_mode": "depin_mutual_blind | hypervisoros_bare_metal | tee_enterprise"' vs :37-44 — eight-mode excerpt including 'local … ctee_private_workspace, embodied_local'
   - Recommendation: The doc names canonical-enums.md as the mode owner and excerpts eight modes (already flagging ctee_private_workspace/embodied_local as design-stage at lines 47-49), then RuntimeAssignment.node_requirements allows only three. daemon_profile (line 93) and ComputeSession.venue (line 122) are venue/profile taxonomies rather than privacy-mode lists, but they are near-identical-looking seven-value enums sitting beside the mode fields, so a reader cannot tell whether local/hosted/customer_vpc capsules are unassignable by design or by staleness, or which axis each enum names. Either replace all inline enums with explicit refs to canonical-enums.md, or state the intended restriction ('assignment privacy_mode names only remote-hardening modes; local/hosted/customer_vpc derive from daemon_profile') and note the two design-stage modes' gating status at the assignment level. Then add these literals to the docs parity gates so the lists cannot diverge silently.
14. **SessionTraceBundle reinvents distributed tracing as a 40-array God object with no OpenTelemetry mapping** — `medium` · SOTA gap *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:2767 — '"span_waterfall": [],'
   - Recommendation: The bundle (2758-2811) embeds forty-plus untyped arrays — spans, graph edges, receipts of every family — duplicating what OTel traces plus the receipt ledger already structure. The file's prose (82-94) does declare the intent (grouping by turn/run segment/workflow node, waterfall-style spans, detail views, redacted export), but none of the JSON fields (span_waterfall, graph_edges, proof_links) are defined anywhere. Define an OpenTelemetry mapping (event → span with receipt_ref/policy_hash attributes, run_id → trace correlation) so existing OTel tooling can render Run Replay, and reduce SessionTraceBundle to a manifest: typed stream/query refs plus the redaction_manifest and verification_result, rather than embedded copies. This preserves the doctrine that trace bundles are projections while making the projection consumable by the tools operators already have.
15. **'Receipts must be reconstructable from trace bundles' inverts the projection physics stated 2,700 lines earlier** — `low` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md:2837 — 'Receipts must be reconstructable from trace bundles.' vs :82 — 'Trace bundles are the inspection projection over events, logs, receipts,'
   - Recommendation: If receipts (truth objects) are recoverable from trace bundles (declared projections), the projection becomes load-bearing for the trust plane — contradicting the pack-wide projections-never-truth rule and Non-Negotiable 8 in this same list. The defensible intent is exportability: an audit bundle must embed receipt bodies and roots sufficient for offline verification without live Agentgres access. Reword Non-Negotiable 2 to say exactly that ('trace bundles must carry receipt refs, bodies, and inclusion material sufficient for independent verification'), and state that the recovery source for receipts is the Agentgres receipt log, never a projection.
16. **Capsule anti-theft is a hedge, not a mechanism: 'should be non-transferable' with a two-field plaintext watermark** — `low` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/task-capsule-protocol.md:243 — 'Stolen capsules should be non-transferable: expiring, watermarked, authority-grant-bound, primitive-capability-constrained'
   - Recommendation: A Non-Negotiable that says 'should' isn't one, and the watermark is just {execution_id, node_id} in cleartext (172-175) — trivially strippable. The Secret Release Rule already gates sealed task keys behind attestation for Enterprise Secure, and mutual-blind's stated defense is that visible_context is minimized to the point of worthlessness — but nothing binds the capsule payload itself to the assigned node in the modes between those poles. Make it MUST with mechanism: encrypt capsule payloads to the attested node key (or seal via wallet.network release, extending the existing Secret Release Rule), so possession without the assigned node's key yields nothing; derive watermarks per-capsule from a keyed function and bind them to the DeterrenceDetectionReceipt profile this pack already defines (events file 1698-1717), so leaked-capsule detection has an attribution path.

### Default harness profile + portable memory vault

**Files reviewed:** `components/daemon-runtime/default-harness-profile.md (1481)` · `components/daemon-runtime/portable-memory-vault.md (112)`

**Current shape.** default-harness-profile.md (1481 lines) defines HarnessProfile — a daemon-executed or daemon-mediated resolver for one scoped step — plus AgentHarnessAdapterEnvelope, the versioned bridge to external harnesses (codex_cli, claude_code, openhands, ...), and the built-in Default Harness Profile as IOI's reference scaffold and fallback, explicitly not a meta-harness or the only admissible harness. A daemon-mediated HarnessInvocation carries a TaskBrief and context leases into the selected resolver; vendor-native prompt/command rendering stays adapter-private, and the durable contract is normalized HarnessAdapterEvents plus a canonical WorkResult/OutcomeDelta and terminal receipt. The doc specifies the bounded cognitive loop (ContextCell → ModelPass → ActionProposal → authority gate → execution → NormalizedObservation → model re-entry), context-topology planning and repair, a typed subagent/background-agent visibility contract, risk-scaled verification, Agentgres admission rules, a 22-step lifecycle, 13 conformance profiles (HP-1..13), and 29 non-negotiables. It insists persistent skills/memory belong to the workspace, not the selected harness or model. portable-memory-vault.md (112 lines) operationalizes that: harnesses receive scoped MemoryProjections, never the raw store, and propose durable changes through a receipted memory-mutation-proposal review lane (add/supersede/archive, one-shot approve/reject). It defines a human-readable Markdown vault export format (ioi.hypervisor.memory-vault.v1: space/entries/skills/affinities + JSON manifest sidecar) with fail-closed credential scrubbing and idempotent, conflict-explicit import, positioned as the memory slice of the InstitutionalIntelligenceExportBundle; OutcomeRoom content stays tainted until a proposal binds lineage, license, and admission receipts.

**What it already gets right:**

- The HarnessInvocation seam is at genuinely the right altitude: TaskBrief in, normalized typed events and WorkResult/OutcomeDelta out, vendor rendering adapter-private. This is harness-agnostic in a way Claude Agent SDK, LangGraph, and OpenHands integrations are not — it can host all of them as adapters without adopting any one loop as canon.
- The memory-ownership inversion (workspace/domain owns durable memory; harness- and invocation-local memory is cache; harnesses get policy-filtered MemoryProjections and only propose mutations) directly attacks the vendor lock-in that provider threads and per-harness brain files create. 'A provider thread ... must never be the only durable copy' is ahead of 2026 industry practice, where memory portability across harnesses is essentially nonexistent.
- The subagent/background-agent contract (RoomParticipantLease, WorkClaimLease, heartbeat/wake conditions, spend, blockers, quarantine controls; 'a token stream or opaque process count is not an adequate background-agent contract') is stronger than the opaque process trees most SOTA harnesses still ship.
- Honest scope labeling: the doc explicitly states the live implementation is narrower (one conductor, two implementers, parallel_implement_reconcile only) and the vault doc names its unbuilt fields as planned — rare candor that keeps the canon auditable.
- Vault import hygiene is well designed: fail-closed credential scrub with whole-bundle rejection, idempotent conflict-explicit import, and imported records passing the same validation gates as live creation.

**SOTA verdict: `near-sota`** — compared against: Claude Code / Claude Agent SDK (subagents, skills, auto-compaction, memory files, permission gating); OpenHands (action/observation event stream, condensers, sandboxed runtime); SWE-agent ACI (agent-computer interface as designed contract); LangGraph (graph-shaped orchestration; contrast for the compositor/harness split); MemGPT/Letta (self-editing memory, sleep-time consolidation); Zep/Graphiti (bi-temporal knowledge-graph memory, contradiction/supersession edges); MCP (external tool/task interop; explicitly handled as opaque handles in HarnessInvocationEnvelope); CaMeL / dual-LLM prompt-injection-resistant tool mediation; Firecracker/gVisor microVM/sandbox isolation for agent execution; LongMemEval-class memory retrieval benchmarks

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No memory consolidation/summarization/decay process anywhere in the pack — Letta sleep-time consolidation and Zep episode→entity summarization have no counterpart; retention/archive/expiry policy refs exist as envelope fields but nothing executes them, so the vault preserves bytes while nothing keeps memory useful at scale.
- No retrieval-quality evaluation contract for MemoryProjections despite the pack owning a whole Evaluations app; LongMemEval-style probes are standard practice.
- No prompt-injection/context-poisoning defense at the harness-loop level: INV-17 taints participant input and README non-negotiable 13 marks MCP prompts untrusted, but NormalizedObservation and ContextCell inputs carry no origin/trust/taint field and only whitepaper.tex §9.3 addresses semantic integrity, while CaMeL-style mediation is now table stakes.
- No declared containment/effect-mediation model for external adapters: OpenHands and Claude Code run in sandboxes with explicit permission gates; the AgentHarnessAdapterEnvelope has no isolation or effect-mediation field, leaving HP-2 conformance undefined for local_process vendor harnesses.
- No compaction contract (pre/post hashes, preserved-ref verification) — compaction is load-bearing and measured in every strong 2026 harness; here it is an aspirational boolean with lineage work only named as a Phase 2 bullet.
- No skill-acquisition loop from learned procedure memory to versioned SkillManifest, which Claude Skills / Voyager-lineage systems close.
- Memory admission throughput: one-shot per-proposal review versus Letta-class autonomous schema-constrained memory writes, with no declared admission policy that could scale it.

**Findings (16, severity-ordered):**

1. **HP-2 (proposal-gate-execute) conformance is undefined for external adapters because no effect-mediation/containment mode is declared** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/default-harness-profile.md:1291 — 'Consequential actions are proposed, policy/authority gated, then executed.'
   - Recommendation: A claude_code/codex_cli adapter over transport_kind local_process (:273-276) executes shell/file effects inside the vendor loop. The envelope's required_runtime_tool_contract_revision_refs (:282) could proxy some tool calls through daemon contracts, and HP-1 plus the daemon-boundary doctrine assert the daemon owns effect truth — but no field declares which containment/mediation mode actually applies to a given adapter, so HP-2 conformance is undefined rather than enforced for vendor loops. Add to AgentHarnessAdapterEnvelope: effect_mediation: daemon_inline_tools | isolated_workspace_egress | provider_trust, plus isolation_contract_refs (Firecracker/gVisor/microVM environment-recipe refs). For isolated_workspace_egress, vendor effects are contained in a disposable workspace and admitted only at the WorkResult boundary — exactly what the live parallel_implement_reconcile implementation already does (:148-151); canonize that pattern and key HP-2 conformance per mode.
2. **Vault export/import — the highest-exfiltration-value memory surface — names no authority scope, no sensitivity gate, and no receipt at its own endpoints** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/portable-memory-vault.md:89 — 'GET /v1/hypervisor/intelligence/spaces/:id/export → { vault: { format, manifest, files } }'
   - Recommendation: Individual mutation approvals mint receipts (:110), and the owning institutional-learning-boundary.md does define LearningEgressReceipt and a governed InstitutionalIntelligenceExportBundleEnvelope with authority/integrity/destination/receipt bindings — but the vault's own built endpoints (implementation status: export/import built in ioi_intelligence_routes.rs) are documented as bare routes with no binding to any of that machinery, and the memory slice is explicitly exportable standalone while records preserve sensitivity verbatim (:75-78). Bind both endpoints to wallet.network scopes (scope:memory.export / scope:memory.import) with a sensitivity ceiling (records above ceiling excluded or step-up required), and mint receipt://hypervisor/memory-vault/export|import receipts binding space_ref, manifest hash, record counts, and requesting principal — or state explicitly that these routes are only reachable through the boundary-profile egress path.
