As written the smallest mutations are receipted while the largest boundary crossing is not.
3. **Vault doctrine mandates per-record learning-boundary/source-rights bindings the v1 format has no field to carry** — `high` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/portable-memory-vault.md:50 — 'Every exported memory record must bind the applicable InstitutionalLearningBoundaryProfile revision or an explicit reason why no'
   - Recommendation: The format section (:60-86) preserves tags, source refs, confidence, sensitivity, compatibility refs, expiry — but no learning_boundary_profile_ref, no_profile_reason, source-rights, retention, export, or revocation-impact refs; manifest.json (:69-71) carries only schema_version/space_ref/exported_at/counts/sidecars/scrubbed. The header does mark license/export and assurance gates as planned, but that acknowledgment is scoped to the mutation-proposal lane, not the export format. Add per-record frontmatter keys for those refs and mirror aggregate hashes in the manifest, with fail-closed import (memory_vault_boundary_binding_missing) exactly like the credential gate. Otherwise either bump the format version or explicitly mark the doctrine sentence as unimplementable in v1 — right now the same 112-line file mandates what its own format cannot express.
4. **No consolidation, decay execution, or retrieval-quality evaluation anywhere in the memory plane** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/portable-memory-vault.md:10 — 'Canonical owner: this file for the vault serialization format and the memory-mutation proposal review lane.'
   - Recommendation: The 112-line owner covers serialization and admission only; grep confirms no consolidation or memory-eval coverage pack-wide. Retention/archive/expiry policy refs do exist as MemoryProfileEnvelope fields and expiry is preserved in the vault, but nothing names an executor. Add (here or a new memory-lifecycle owner): (1) MemoryConsolidationRun — a scheduled, receipted run proposing merge/supersede/archive mutations through the existing lane (Letta/MemGPT sleep-time consolidation); (2) decay execution — who archives entries past expiry/retention refs; (3) retrieval_eval_profile_ref on MemoryProfileEnvelope binding MemoryProjection quality to the Evaluations app with LongMemEval-style probes. Without these, 'portable memory truth' degrades into an append-only pile that retrieves worse as it grows — the exact failure Zep/Letta exist to prevent.
5. **No prompt-injection/context-poisoning defense at the harness seam: observations carry no origin/trust field** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/default-harness-profile.md:987 — 'Models should re-enter on normalized reality, not raw noise.'
   - Recommendation: Authority gating stops unauthorized effects but not poisoned context steering proposals within already-granted scopes. The pack does have taint doctrine at adjacent boundaries — INV-17 keeps participant input untrusted until admitted, README non-negotiable 13 normalizes MCP prompts to untrusted inputs, and the vault taints room content (:26-29) — but none of it reaches the loop contract: NormalizedObservation (:966-984) has confidence/uncertainty and no origin trust, and whitepaper §9.3 (semantic integrity) has no component-canon home. Add origin_trust: principal | governed_tool | untrusted_external plus taint_refs to NormalizedObservation and ContextCell input refs, and a gate rule: turns dominated by untrusted-origin inputs cannot propose scope-widening, memory mutations, or external_message/funds/credential risk classes without step-up (CaMeL/dual-LLM pattern). Reuse the existing INV-17 taint vocabulary in the loop contract.
6. **Mutation-proposal lane admits only add|supersede|archive while the owning ContextMutationEnvelope canon defines seven operations including forget** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/portable-memory-vault.md:102 — '(operation: add | supersede | archive, canon mutation_type + source_authority enums,'
   - Recommendation: common-objects-and-envelopes.md:7048 defines 'add | supersede | contradict | deprecate | activate | archive | forget'. forget is load-bearing: the vault's own retention/revocation-impact doctrine (:50-53) and MemoryProfileEnvelope's delete_or_forget_policy_ref have no executable path without it, and contradict is the operation the negative-results doctrine (:32-37) needs. The header's 'implemented initial contract' status explains a built subset, but the doc never declares the remaining canon operations deferred. Either extend the lane's enum to the full canon set, or add an explicit deferred-operations list with the invariant that no model-swap-continuity or revocation claim is asserted while forget/contradict remain unimplemented.
7. **One-shot per-proposal review with no declared admission policy is an adoption killer at agent-fleet scale** — `medium` · Better way *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/portable-memory-vault.md:113 — 'Review is one-shot; credential material is refused at propose time.'
   - Recommendation: The doc never says the reviewer must be human, but it defines no policy-driven alternative either — every proposal terminates in a per-proposal approve/reject call. Thousands of runs proposing preferences/route notes will bury that queue, so operators will either stop persisting memory or rubber-stamp — both defeat the design. Add a MemoryAdmissionPolicy keyed on mutation_type × sensitivity × source_authority: low tiers (owner's own runs writing preference/route/tool_affordance entries) auto-approve with context_mutation receipts and post-hoc sampled review; doctrine, org-visible, and connector-derived entries keep one-shot review. Letta-class systems let agents write memory autonomously within schema constraints; IOI keeps fail-closed physics because the policy — not a queue — remains the gate, and every auto-approval is still receipted.
8. **Import conflicts are reported-and-skipped with no resolution path, stranding newer knowledge** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/portable-memory-vault.md:93 — 'a differing record with the same id is reported in conflicts and skipped — never duplicated, never silently overwritten.'
   - Recommendation: Skip-only conflict handling terminates in a transient API response field the caller may discard — for a portability format whose whole point is cross-install continuity, divergent revisions need a durable resolution lane. Route each conflict into the existing memory-mutation-proposal lane as an auto-generated supersede proposal (incoming record as suggested, existing as target_ref, contradiction posture recorded); resolution then inherits review, receipts, and evidence retention. Zep/Graphiti resolve contradictions with temporal supersession edges; IOI already owns all the required objects — wire them together instead of dropping the conflict on the floor.
9. **default-harness-profile.md restates privacy-lattice, L1-settlement, and physical-safety doctrine owned by other files — a 1481-line drift engine** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/default-harness-profile.md:1259 — 'Candidate-Lattice Private Decoding lets rented GPUs generate candidates while private heads select'
   - Recommendation: Lines 1244-1280 restate CLPD/FHE/MPC/ORAM/provider-trust MUSTs owned by private-workspace-ctee.md (which also owns the External Model API Boundary), and :1198-1216 restates L1 triggers. The physical-action stanza (:933-941) is closer to compliant — it names physical-action-safety.md as canonical owner inline — but still carries normative daemon MUST-block behavior. The header already says this file 'does not redefine their state or ownership' (:6-8), and invariants.md's citation rule exists precisely to replace re-derived normative wording with one-line ID citations. Replace each restated stanza with a one-line pointer to its owner and keep this file scoped to HarnessProfile/AgentHarnessAdapter/DefaultHarnessProfile semantics, lifecycle, and HP-1..13; target under ~900 lines.
10. **Portable Memory Vault and MemoryArchiveEnvelope are two unreconciled portability mechanisms with no stated precedence** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/portable-memory-vault.md:39 — 'The Portable Memory Vault is the memory slice of the InstitutionalIntelligenceExportBundle owned by'
   - Recommendation: common-objects-and-envelopes.md:6987 defines MemoryArchiveEnvelope (encrypted restorable archive with restore-authority gating); the vault doc never mentions it. An integrator cannot tell which mechanism satisfies the model-swap continuity claim (:53-56). Add one custody paragraph: MemoryArchive is the encrypted custody form and the continuity-claim carrier; the vault is the human-readable interchange projection; vault import is re-admission (fresh receipts, current gates), never restore; restore flows only through archive + restore_authority. Cross-link both docs so neither can drift into claiming the other's role.
11. **Compaction has no contract — 'preserve provenance' is an unverifiable boolean while SOTA treats compaction as measured machinery** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/default-harness-profile.md:1172 — 'Compaction is allowed inside context cells, but compaction drift is telemetry that the topology may be wrong.'
   - Recommendation: compaction_strategy (:786-791) is five true booleans with no mechanism to verify them; Phase 2 (:1374) names 'compaction lineage and provenance preservation' as planned work but defines no shape for it. Every strong 2026 harness (Claude Code auto-compaction, OpenHands condensers, LangGraph summarization nodes) exercises compaction constantly and observably. Define a CompactionEvent: pre/post context hash, preserved-ref checklist verified against the strategy booleans, dropped-span artifact ref — emitted as a HarnessAdapterEvent kind and receipted for serious runs. This makes 'compaction preserves provenance' (non-negotiable 13, :1442) conformance-checkable and gives the repartition_policy triggers (:792-800) real telemetry instead of an unmeasurable drift claim.
12. **No promotion path from learned procedure memory to versioned SkillManifest — the skill-acquisition loop is open** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/default-harness-profile.md:639 — 'SkillManifest / SkillEntry / ActiveSkillSetSnapshot | immutable definition / successor-versioned owner binding / exact run resolution'
   - Recommendation: The pack has both endpoints — procedure-kind memory entries (ContextMutationEnvelope mutation_type procedure; vault entries/) and the SkillManifest registry lifecycle (draft|evaluable|released, :204) — but no edge between them; the vault only gestures that promotion into other object types uses 'their own proposal/evaluation/governance path' (:35-37) without defining the memory→skill lane. Claude Skills and Voyager-lineage systems close this loop: agents author reusable, versioned skills from repeated successful procedures. Define the lane: a consolidation or run proposal nominates a procedure entry cluster → drafts a SkillManifest → normal registry evaluation/release gates. Keep non-negotiable 28 (:1467-1470): promotion grants no authority and never rebinds active runs; it only creates a draft definition.
13. **Hand-editable 'Obsidian-class' vault with a split record (md + sidecar) has no per-record integrity binding** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/portable-memory-vault.md:60 — 'An Obsidian-class, human-readable bundle for a MemorySpace and its records:'
   - Recommendation: The format invites hand-editing, yet one record's truth is split between entries/<id>.md and the manifest's structured_payloads sidecar (:83) with only bundle-level counts — edited or desynced records import as authentic provenance-bearing memory (source refs, confidence, admission receipts preserved verbatim, :75-82), and the import gates (:94-95) validate enums, not provenance authenticity. Add per-record content_hash entries in manifest.json binding each file plus its sidecar slice; on mismatch, import demotes provenance (source_authority: user, confidence reset, receipt refs dropped) and routes the record through the mutation-proposal lane instead of direct admission. This keeps human editability — a real advantage over Letta's opaque stores — without laundering edits into receipted institutional truth.
14. **'Default Harness Profile' invites exactly the privileged-meta-harness reading the doc spends ~25 lines denying** — `low` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/default-harness-profile.md:29 — 'Harness Profile is IOI's reference scaffold and fallback profile, not a meta-harness and not the only admissible harness.'
   - Recommendation: Rename to Reference Harness Profile (harness-profile://reference-loop), keeping fallback_profile: true for selection semantics. 'Default' is why the file needs the It-is-not list (:43-58), anti-pattern bullets (:1223-1226), and non-negotiable 3 (:1424-1425) all repeating the same denial — and it sits awkwardly beside HP-8 marketplace neutrality (:1312-1314), since external harness vendors may read 'default' as first-party privilege regardless of the disclaimers. Note the profile record already encodes reference_profile/scaffold_profile/fallback_profile booleans (:213-215), so the semantics exist; the cost is real churn across the file name, profile_id (harness-profile://default-ioi), and pack-wide references — a naming-hygiene call, not a doctrine defect.
15. **Field names contradict their own semantics: a *_required field holding best_effort; 'mutation_allowed' implying direct writes** — `low` · Rename
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/default-harness-profile.md:225 — 'context_topology_planning_required: best_effort'
   - Recommendation: Rename to context_topology_planning: required | best_effort | off. In the same pass, rename ContextCell memory_policy field agentgres_memory_mutation_allowed (:843) to memory_mutation_proposal_allowed — as written it implies the cell may write durable memory directly, contradicting the propose-never-write-silently doctrine (portable-memory-vault.md:19-21) that governs every harness. Schema field names are the API integrators actually read; these two encode the wrong contract.
16. **adapter_family bakes today's vendor TUIs (hermes, deepseek_tui) into a canonical enum that will rot** — `low` · Rename
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/daemon-runtime/default-harness-profile.md:273 — 'codex_cli | claude_code | openhands | hermes | deepseek_tui |'
   - Recommendation: Vendor product names churn yearly; a canonical closed enum guarantees stale canon and awkward custom-bucket overloading. Keep transport_kind structural (as it already is, :275-276) and make adapter_family an open, registry-validated string — the envelope already has registry_lifecycle_ref/registry_status (:285-286) to govern legitimacy. If a closed enum is wanted, enumerate structural kinds only (local_cli, embedded_sdk, remote_agent_api, protocol_native) and move vendor identity to a free-form vendor_ref field.


## Part III.8 — Truth and data plane: Agentgres

### Agentgres: doctrine, object model, artifact refs, projections, postgres bridge

**Files reviewed:** `components/agentgres/doctrine.md (1284)` · `components/agentgres/api-object-model.md (2065) — read fully in chunks` · `components/agentgres/artifact-ref-plane.md (533)` · `components/agentgres/projection-system-reference.md (352)` · `components/agentgres/postgres-bridge-and-readiness-contract.md (642)`

**Current shape.** Agentgres is IOI's per-domain canonical operational state substrate: every consequential change enters as a proposed operation/patch, passes schema/policy/authority/constraint validation, and only admitted operations — plus object heads, batch-computed merkle state roots, receipts, and archive refs — constitute truth; mutable rows and all derived views are explicitly never truth. The substrate contract is five verbs (append, validate, advance-head, root, project) hosted by pluggable engines; a Rust engine v0 exists (append-only multiplexed log, single-writer-per-domain, deterministic admission core, fenced primary/standby replication with honest durability classes), while most of the ~150-class object catalog is planned. Reads are served through first-class projections (relational, search, vector, OLAP, stream, ledger engine adapters) carrying watermarks, freshness SLOs, checkpoints, and rebuild plans; a Postgres wire bridge (still planned — the bridge file marks its own roadmap claims) specifies SQL reads over named projections and compiles admitted SQL writes into governed operations with receipts, with arbitrary write compatibility a stated non-goal. Large payload bytes live in storage backends (S3, Filecoin/CAS, local disk); the artifact-ref plane keeps meaning, lifecycle, policy, authority, and restore validity inside Agentgres, with sealed encrypted content-addressed archives as the portable state format and ArtifactAvailabilityIncident making backend failure an admitted, repairable fact. Planned execution-branch objects (AgentExecutionTrace/Branch/StagedEffect/BranchCheckpoint/BranchMergePlan) version autonomous work the way Git versions code, with three merge classes (exclusive_owner, declared_commutative, adjudicated) and mandatory authority re-validation at merge time. A companion taxonomy paper names the generic category CSPS and calls the architecture FQF, while product surfaces call the same plane Provenance.

**What it already gets right:**

- The determinism rule (no wall clock, no ambient randomness in admission; nondeterminism only as recorded inputs) plus single-writer ownership partitioning is genuine FoundationDB/TigerBeetle-class discipline, and the docs correctly state it cannot be retrofitted — this is the strongest single design decision in the facet (doctrine.md:226-231).
- Unusual honesty discipline for a canon pack: durability classes with hard honesty caps (same-host peers can never yield quorum_replicated), a named measured blocker (raw fdatasync floor ~5.4ms), roadmap-vs-baseline recovery claims explicitly split (postgres-bridge:527-539), and the QLDB failure mode named as the thing to avoid.
- The projection plane is ahead of common CQRS practice: adapters, checkpoints with source operation ranges, freshness SLOs, health, and rebuild plans are typed admitted objects (api-object-model.md:1912-2002) rather than operational folklore, with DBSP-class incremental view maintenance named as the target realization.
- The artifact-ref plane cleanly separates byte availability from meaning/validity, and ArtifactAvailabilityIncident + repair receipts make storage-backend failure a first-class admitted fact — most content-addressed designs silently trust the store.
- SQL-writes-as-intents preserves exactly one admission pipeline while keeping a real adoption wedge; the refusal semantics (named SQL error, never silent drop or fake success) are better specified than most write-proxy designs.

**SOTA verdict: `near-sota`** — compared against: Datomic (immutable facts, single-writer transactor, peer reads, time-travel); XTDB v2 (bitemporal valid-time vs system-time SQL); EventStoreDB/Kurrent (streams, catch-up + persistent subscriptions, scavenging, maxAge retention); Kafka + Debezium outbox/CDC (durable external consumer cursors); Materialize/Feldera — DBSP incremental view maintenance; TigerBeetle + FoundationDB (deterministic simulation testing, single-writer state machines); Dolt/Prolly trees and Jellyfish Merkle Trees (structural sharing, diffable roots); Git expected-head compare-and-set ref semantics; IPLD/content addressing; ElectricSQL/Automerge local-first sync; crypto-shredding practice for GDPR in event-sourced systems; AWS QLDB (cautionary anchor the docs themselves cite)

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- Per-event schema evolution: no upcaster/lazy-transformation contract (Axon upcasters, Greg Young versioning); only heavyweight SchemaMigration objects with ambiguous backfill semantics
- Log compaction/scavenging/retention with bounded rebuild: EventStoreDB scavenge, Kafka compaction, Datomic's replay-free index architecture all have answers; Agentgres names `vacuum`, `CommitLogSegment`, and `DomainSequenceCheckpoint` with zero semantics
- GDPR erasure mechanics for personal data inside the immutable operation log (crypto-shredding per-subject keys); only revocation-not-erasure doctrine plus an unspecified erasure-policy ref in the shared envelope canon exist
- Bitemporality: time-travel is planned, transaction-time only; no valid-time axis on the operation envelope
- Durable external-consumer cursor / CDC contract: subscriptions are at-least-once with idempotency pushed wholly onto consumers
- Consensus-backed automated failover (deliberately deferred with named gates — acknowledged divergence, not an oversight)

**Findings (15, severity-ordered):**

1. **No erasure mechanics for personal data inside the immutable operation log** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/agentgres/doctrine.md:527 — 'Revocation is transitive operational state, not historical erasure.'
   - Recommendation: Adopt crypto-shredding as canon: operation payloads carrying personal data (inline `payload`, api-object-model.md:522) are encrypted under per-subject envelope keys held via wallet.network; a `SubjectErasure` operation destroys the key and mints a receipt, leaving hashes, roots, and log bytes intact. This is byte-compatible with replicate-then-ack byte-equality replication (doctrine.md:265) and root chains. The artifact plane's redacted/tombstoned states cover out-of-log bytes only, and the only erasure hook anywhere is an unspecified `data_export_retention_and_erasure_policy_ref` in the shared envelope canon (foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:1832). Without in-log mechanics, the general app-data profile (users, messages — postgres-bridge:614-624) is not lawfully deployable for EU personal data, and ComplianceAuditExportBundle marketing invites exactly the scrutiny that finds this hole. The fix is still additive today, but the window closes once families with plaintext inline payloads are cut over to the engine.
2. **Batch-rooting doctrine contradicts per-operation state_root in the operation shape and ack contract** — `high` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/agentgres/doctrine.md:233 — 'State roots are computed per admitted batch, never per operation' vs api-object-model.md:532 — '"state_root": "sha256:..."' in Operation Shape
   - Recommendation: The doctrine rejects root-per-op as write amplification, yet the canonical operation wire shape carries a per-operation `state_root`, and the durability ack list requires 'resulting state root or state-root delta commitment' per acknowledged operation (postgres-bridge:179). An implementer following api-object-model.md builds the explicitly rejected design. Make `state_root` nullable on operations, add `batch_root_ref` plus inclusion index (or define per-op roots as logical: batch root + position), and align the bridge durability list to name batch roots. ModuleInvocation/StateTransitionCommitmentRecord per-invocation roots (api:468-469) need the same clarification.
3. **No log compaction, segment retirement, or snapshot-cadence contract — rebuild cost is unbounded** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/agentgres/postgres-bridge-and-readiness-contract.md:561 — 'ioi agentgres vacuum'
   - Recommendation: `vacuum` appears once with zero semantics, and the ProjectionRebuildPlan example rebuilds from `domain_seq:0` (api-object-model.md:1995). The hooks already have names — `CommitLogSegment` and `DomainSequenceCheckpoint` sit in the object catalog (api:325-326) with no shape or semantics anywhere, a `checkpoint_restore_plus_delta` rebuild mode exists (api:1998), and the layered-storage section (doctrine.md:847-865) plus the 'operation-log segment archive' profile gesture at warm/cold tiers — but nothing binds them into a contract. Define DomainSequenceCheckpoint semantics (state root + object-head map + segment watermark, admitted with receipt) and a segment-retirement rule: segments older than an admitted checkpoint may seal to the artifact plane with a root-continuity proof; the replay contract becomes verified-checkpoint + tail. EventStoreDB scavenging, Kafka compaction, and Datomic's replay-free index design all bound this.
4. **Schema evolution lacks an event-upcasting contract; backfill is ambiguous against log immutability** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/agentgres/postgres-bridge-and-readiness-contract.md:291 — 'Schema evolution is operation-backed.'
   - Recommendation: SchemaMigration binds a `backfill_plan_ref` (api-object-model.md:609) but never says whether backfill rewrites admitted operations (violating immutability and root chains) or only projections — the lifecycle's `BackfillPlanned -> ProjectionRebuilt` step (bridge:295-301) hints at projection-side backfill, but no doc states that admitted operations are never rewritten, and 'upcast' appears nowhere in the pack. Adopt the event-sourcing SOTA answer (Axon upcasters, Greg Young's event versioning): admitted operations are never rewritten; deterministic upcast functions are registered as admitted objects keyed by (operation_type, from_version, to_version); replay and rebuild apply upcaster chains; backfill only ever touches projections/derived heads. State explicitly whether replay across schema versions reproduces identical roots via versioned reduction, or roots are re-anchored at an admitted migration commitment — determinism (doctrine.md:226) depends on this answer.
5. **~150 flat canonical object classes couple the substrate to every product wave — a drift engine** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/agentgres/api-object-model.md:331 — 'Ontology profile registration is exact and does not introduce parallel storage schemas'
   - Recommendation: The catalog (api:173-329) hard-codes domain families into substrate canon: 12+ training classes, the improvement spine, room objects. Every product wave therefore mutates the substrate's canonical class list — the opposite of Datomic/EventStoreDB, whose kernels know only facts/streams and push domain schema to registries. The pack has partial mitigations: field schemas for the chain and room families already live in foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md (api:443-446, 1419-1421), and envelope-profile registration (OntologyVersion→DomainOntology with a profile discriminator) exists — but it is applied to only ~13 classes, and the flat class list itself keeps growing in this file each wave. Generalize the mechanism the docs already invented: keep ~30 kernel classes (operations, heads, schema, constraints, projections, artifacts, receipts, branches, archives), and register domain families as schema packs (SchemaDefinition + profile discriminators) owned by their component docs.
6. **Bridge file owns 'durability expectations' but predates and omits the durability-class/replicate-then-ack contract** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/agentgres/postgres-bridge-and-readiness-contract.md:4 — 'durability expectations, schema/migration lifecycle, replication profile' vs doctrine.md:258 — 'Every admission ack carries a durability class'
   - Recommendation: The bridge file (last aligned 2026-05-14, line 7) still defines ack as local durable record only (bridge:159-187) and its replication progression ignores the fenced writer epochs and replicate-then-ack policy the doctrine (aligned 2026-07-15) now specifies as built. Two files claim the same contract at different revisions — exactly the drift the pack's single-owner rule exists to prevent. Either move durability/replication ownership into doctrine.md (which de facto owns it now) and narrow this file to bridge posture and readiness, or update bridge:159-187 and the deployment progression to reference durability classes, ack policy, and writer epochs.
7. **projection-system-reference.md is a positioning whitepaper, not the projection-system reference its name promises** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/agentgres/projection-system-reference.md:24 — 'This paper proposes a new system taxonomy: the **Canonical State and Projection System** (`CSPS`).'
   - Recommendation: The actual projection reference material — ProjectionEngineAdapter/Checkpoint/Health/FreshnessSLO/RebuildPlan objects, engine families, rebuild and invalidation rules — lives in api-object-model.md:1912-2002 and postgres-bridge:406-471, not here. The file's header does self-label as 'taxonomy reference' and subordinates itself to doctrine.md on any disagreement (lines 3-4), so this is a filename-vs-content mismatch rather than an ownership violation — but the file still contains category advocacy ('It deserves its own name', :352) and a stale claim that FQF is an 'eventual implementation' (:40) though engine v0 shipped as `crates/agentgres`. Move it to _archive/specs/ as positioning history (matching the pack's _archive convention it already follows for the v2 spec), and replace it with a real projection-system reference that consolidates the adapter/checkpoint/SLO/rebuild contract currently split across the other two files.
8. **Dev-diary build logs (Session 2/3, dev-box benchmark numbers) embedded in canonical doctrine** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/agentgres/doctrine.md:186 — 'Session 3 CUT the first family over (user decision: no downstream users,'
   - Recommendation: doctrine.md:160-207 carries session-by-session narration, box-specific numbers (Core Ultra 9 275HX, 8.6k adm/s, ~5.4ms fdatasync floor), env-var flags, and verifier scores (30/30) inside a file whose header claims 'canonical architecture authority'. These numbers change every engine session, guaranteeing staleness and undercutting the contract voice. Keep the durable rules in doctrine (five-verb contract, determinism, batch rooting, durability classes, HA posture, consensus gates); move measured baselines, session history, and env flags to _meta/implementation-matrix.md rows and the archived change ledgers, leaving one link.
9. **No valid-time axis: time-travel is transaction-time only, and only planned** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/agentgres/doctrine.md:335 — 'Time-travel reads (`AS OF` over the operation log, XTDB/Datomic-class) are a planned projection capability'
   - Recommendation: XTDB's core lesson is that audit/dispute surfaces need two time axes: what the system recorded when (transaction time) versus what was true of the world when (valid time). The object model gestures at this only per-class — Finding binds 'uncertainty / time / provenance' (api:1510), ContextMutation has validity_window, and a few shared envelope objects carry `valid_time`/`effective_at` fields (foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:4170, 8419) — but the operation envelope (api:507-534) carries no time field at all and AS OF is single-axis. Either add optional `effective_at` to the operation envelope with two-axis AS OF semantics defined before bridge Phase 3, or explicitly declare transaction-time-only as divergent-by-design with valid-time delegated to per-class fields. Currently the docs do neither.
10. **No durable external-consumer contract: subscriptions lack an admitted consumer cursor or CDC semantics** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/agentgres/postgres-bridge-and-readiness-contract.md:487 — 'Subscription delivery may be at-least-once. Consumers must use operation ids,'
   - Recommendation: EventStoreDB persistent subscriptions and Debezium/outbox CDC give downstream systems durable, resumable, group-scoped cursors; here idempotency is pushed wholly onto consumers with no substrate object. The catalog has ProjectionEngineCheckpoint for internal engines and federated rooms keep a 'last admitted federation watermark' (api:1528), but ordinary external consumers (exports, sinks, org data warehouses) get nothing. Add a SubscriptionCursor/ConsumerLease object — subscription id, policy-filtered scope, acknowledged domain-sequence watermark, lease/heartbeat — admitted like other state, so AIIP federation, audit exports, and third-party sinks share one resumption contract.
11. **Artifact lifecycle has no byte-reclamation path: no reachability rule, retirement operation, or deletion receipt** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/agentgres/artifact-ref-plane.md:160 — 'proposed | active | verified | redacted | archived | revoked | missing | invalid'
   - Recommendation: retention_policy is an opaque string ref (artifact-ref-plane.md:136), and the events list has ArtifactRedacted/Revoked but no governed deletion of payload bytes. Consequence: storage grows monotonically, or operators delete out-of-band and the system learns only reactively via ArtifactAvailabilityIncident (whose failure_kind even anticipates `retention_expired`). Add an ArtifactRetirement operation: policy-checked, reachability-proven (no live delivery/dispute/claim/retention-lock refs — the Git-GC/IPFS-pinning analogy), minting a deletion receipt and a terminal `retired` state that replay treats as expected-absent rather than incident-worthy. This pairs with the log-compaction finding: both need a single 'what may be forgotten, with proof' doctrine.
12. **Postgres compatibility roadmap understates the pg_catalog/introspection tar pit** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/agentgres/postgres-bridge-and-readiness-contract.md:577 — '3. Drizzle and Prisma read models.'
   - Recommendation: Compatibility targets 3-7 (ORMs, PostgREST, GraphQL, Supabase-style) all require system-catalog emulation — pg_catalog, information_schema, type OIDs, prepared-statement metadata — the part of wire compatibility that consumed years at CockroachDB and YugabyteDB and is orthogonal to Agentgres's actual value. The file marks phases and non-goals but neither pg_catalog nor information_schema appears anywhere in the pack. Cut canon to Phase 1 (psql/BI reads over projections) plus an explicit thin-catalog contract enumerating exactly which introspection views are served; demote ORM/BaaS targets to an exploratory appendix with the named risk. This protects the bridge-as-survival-strategy wedge from becoming the schedule sink.
13. **The 77-bullet 'What Agentgres Owns' list triple-enumerates ownership already carved elsewhere** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/agentgres/doctrine.md:364 — 'Agentgres owns per-domain operational truth:'
   - Recommendation: doctrine.md:364-450 (77 bullets) restates the object catalog (api:173-329) and per-plane owns-lists — DeliveryBundle alone is carved in doctrine, the api catalog, and artifact-ref-plane.md:61, and doctrine itself repeats ownership twice more in its 'Agentgres Owns' code block (doctrine:782-806) and Native Objects list (doctrine:1041-1084) — and it demonstrably grows by appendage each alignment pass (improvement spine, rooms, learning boundary all bolted on). Multiple synchronized lists are a standing contradiction generator. Replace the enumeration with the ownership rules that already exist ('What Agentgres Does Not Own' boundaries plus the admission-boundary test at artifact-ref-plane.md:392-407), and point at api-object-model.md as the single class enumeration.
14. **FQF naming stack is stale and contradicts the shipped crate; the Agentgres/Provenance mapping already exists in doctrine** — `low` · Rename
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/agentgres/projection-system-reference.md:304 — '**implementation/crate:** `fqf`' vs doctrine.md:200 — 'engine crate is `crates/agentgres`.'
   - Recommendation: Most of the naming problem is already solved in canon: doctrine's Product Presentation section (doctrine.md:339-360) states the canonical mapping — Agentgres for builder/protocol contexts, Provenance for product surfaces, Work Ledger surviving only in admin/protocol language and route compatibility — and the taxonomy paper opens with a naming reconciliation mapping FQF to Agentgres (projection-system-reference.md:18-22) and subordinates itself to doctrine on disagreement. What remains stale is the FQF stack itself: the paper still calls FQF an 'eventual implementation' (:40) and names the implementation crate `fqf` (:304) though reality shipped `crates/agentgres`. On the pun: 'Agentgres' is an asset at the builder wedge (the Postgres bridge is declared survival strategy, doctrine.md:321-324) and a mild liability as protocol canon (-gres reads as 'Postgres fork'); keep it. The fix is pruning: retire FQF entirely and archive the CSPS naming stack (which the whitepaper-relocation finding also accomplishes).
15. **Consistency-level enum drifts within the same file: local_cached vs cached_projection** — `low` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/agentgres/api-object-model.md:1880 — '"consistency": "local_cached | projection_consistent | state_root_consistent | linearized_domain"' vs api-object-model.md:546 — 'cached_projection'
   - Recommendation: The Relation Query API example uses `local_cached` and omits snapshot_consistent/serializable_domain, while the named-levels list (api:545-552) and ProjectionFreshnessSLO.consistency_floor (api:1981) use `cached_projection`. Normalize the query example to the six named levels. On ownership: the bridge file's header already claims 'consistency names' (postgres-bridge:4), while foundations/canonical-enums.md currently owns only durability classes — either keep the bridge as the enum's single owner or move it to canonical-enums.md alongside durability, but pick exactly one and have both agentgres files reference it.


## Part III.9 — Authority plane: wallet.network

### wallet.network: authority doctrine, scopes API, product/exchange risk

**Files reviewed:** `components/wallet-network/doctrine.md (1018)` · `components/wallet-network/api-authority-scopes.md (1065)` · `components/wallet-network/product-exchange-risk.md (1379)`

**Current shape.** wallet.network is canon's "Web4 authority layer": the single control plane that converts human/org intent into machine-executable power for autonomous agents, owning identity, auth factors, guardian surfaces, key shards, brokered provider credentials (OAuth/BYOK), scope:* authority grants, capability leases, approval tokens, payments, exchange/trade authority, decryption leases, revocation epochs, and audit lineage — while the Hypervisor daemon executes work, Agentgres records operational truth, and IOI L1 optionally settles. doctrine.md defines the taxonomy (AuthFactor ≠ AuthorityGrant; guardian surfaces that must render and sign the exact request hash; a four-level frictionless-to-fortress ladder; runtime privacy profiles for cTEE/enterprise) and the doctrine that agents get authority leases, never raw secrets. api-authority-scopes.md gives REST-shaped APIs and JSON sketches for scope requests, grants, capability leases, secret brokerage, archive restore, approvals/AuthorityReview, client sessions with anomaly/blast-radius handling, and typed revocation; every consequential object binds request_hash, policy_hash, expiry, and a revocation_epoch, and wallet.network itself evaluates policy at issuance. product-exchange-risk.md defines the user-facing cockpit grammar (intent → simulation → risk labels → policy → approval mode → execution → receipt), risk-coverage states where absence of a label is never safety, ExchangeIntent/TradeIntent/PredictionIntent objects, protection actions, the approval inbox, and the rule that route/venue/resource candidates are evidence, never authority. Delegation is described as issuer-side records (RootGrant/SubGrant named in doctrine.md but never specified anywhere; a field-level AuthorityGrantEnvelope schema exists in foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md); no verifiable token format, attenuation algebra, offline verification, policy language, or revocation-propagation contract is defined.

**What it already gets right:**

- The AuthFactor / GuardianSurface / KeyShard / ProviderCredentialBinding / AuthorityGrant object separation, with 'authentication is not authority' enforced as a non-negotiable, is stricter than mainstream enterprise IAM practice, and the guardian WYSIWYS requirement (render exact subject/action/budget/expiry and sign the request hash) correctly transplants hardware-wallet clear-signing discipline into agent authorization.
- The approval-mode taxonomy (one_shot_review, session_envelope, batch_review, silent_within_policy with mandatory receipts, after_the_fact_receipt, step_up_review) is a genuinely better consent model for agent-scale action volume than OAuth's one-shot consent screen, and the doc correctly makes the mode policy-derived rather than app-chosen.
- 'Absence of a risk label is never safety' plus explicit coverage states (Unknown/Unassessed/Stale/Conflicting Sources) with source, confidence, as_of, and evidence_refs on every label is ahead of production wallet/exchange risk UIs, which show nothing when unassessed.
- Candidates-never-authority is applied uniformly to quotes, routes, venues, and cloud resource candidates (CandidateEvidence envelope), a clean untrusted-input discipline most agent frameworks lack.
- Revocation as a typed, receipted action with monotonic epochs, plus client-session quarantine and blast-radius reporting, is conceptually richer than OAuth token-revocation practice.

**SOTA verdict: `behind`** — compared against: Biscuit v2 (offline attenuation blocks, datalog caveats); UCAN 1.0 (delegation proof chains, offline verification); Macaroons (caveat-based attenuation); GNAP RFC 9635 (async grant negotiation, interaction modes); OAuth 2.1 + RAR RFC 9396 (structured authorization_details over scope strings); Cedar (typed, formally analyzable policy language); OPA/Rego; SPIFFE/SPIRE workload identity; DPoP RFC 9449 sender-constrained tokens; W3C Bitstring Status List / CRLite-class revocation distribution; HashiCorp Vault dynamic secrets; Fireblocks-class MPC-CMP custody + policy engine; WebAuthn/passkeys + FIDO transaction confirmation

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No verifiable capability-token format: grants have field schemas (API sketch, foundations AuthorityGrantEnvelope) and a one-line hybrid-signature aspiration, but no signed envelope spec, no attenuation algebra, no delegation proof chain, no offline verification — the core mechanisms of Biscuit/UCAN/Macaroons are absent while RootGrant/SubGrant are named but never specified
- No revocation propagation contract: epochs and a GET /v1/revocation-epoch poll endpoint exist, but nothing says how verifiers learn the current epoch, with what staleness bound or kill latency (status-list SOTA absent)
- No policy language or evaluation semantics behind the ubiquitous policy_hash; nothing comparable to Cedar's analyzable policies or even OPA/Rego is named
- No workload identity: subject_id/runtime refs are unauthenticated URI strings with no SPIFFE-style attestation seam outside the Enterprise Secure Profile
- Leases appear bearer; no DPoP-style proof-of-possession on use
- No canonical serialization (JCS/deterministic CBOR) for the request/policy hashes every guardian signs
- Level-4 'MPC or threshold key shards' names custody without any signing-protocol or shard-lifecycle spec at Fireblocks rigor

**Findings (14, severity-ordered):**

1. **Grants are field-level records, not verifiable capability tokens: no attenuation, proof chains, or offline verification** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/wallet-network/api-authority-scopes.md:401 — '"grant_id": "grant_123"'; docs/architecture/components/wallet-network/doctrine.md:744-745 — 'RootGrant / SubGrant' (named in the emitted-artifact list, never specified anywhere in the pack per grep); foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3473 gives an AuthorityGrantEnvelope field schema with no signature, parent ref, or verification story
   - Recommendation: Specify AuthorityGrant as a signed envelope: issuer key, subject/audience key, parent_grant_ref, caveats, expiry, revocation_epoch — doctrine.md:927 already promises 'hybrid signatures for policy envelopes, grants, and approval records' but never defines the envelope or how a verifier checks it. Define the attenuation rule formally (CapabilityLease ⊆ SubGrant ⊆ RootGrant on scopes, resources, budget, expiry) and make it machine-checkable a la Biscuit attenuation blocks or UCAN proof chains, so the daemon can verify a lease offline within a bounded staleness window instead of requiring a wallet.network round-trip — or state explicitly that verification is online-only and accept the availability consequences. Give RootGrant/SubGrant schemas in api-authority-scopes.md or delete the names. Revocation latency (finding 2) and availability (finding on degraded mode) both hinge on which of these two designs the canon picks, and it stays fail-closed via epoch-staleness bounds.
2. **'Panic/revocation must kill active grants' has no distribution mechanism or latency bound, making Non-Negotiable 5 unimplementable as written** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/wallet-network/doctrine.md:998 — 'Panic/revocation must kill active grants.'; api-authority-scopes.md:496 — 'After revocation, future capability use must fail unless a later policy review'
   - Recommendation: The API exposes 'GET /v1/revocation-epoch' (api-authority-scopes.md:1031) and the emergency-stop section names blast-radius invalidation of clients, gateway profiles, leases, WorkRuns, and challenges — but nothing binds how fast or how reliably enforcement points learn a new epoch. Define the propagation contract: a signed, monotonic RevocationEpochBeacon per issuer; per-risk-class max_staleness_seconds that verifiers must enforce fail-closed (e.g., funds/secret_export require live check, read tolerates 60s); push channels to daemon/Guardian enforcement points plus the existing poll fallback; a stated worst-case kill SLO for emergency-stop; and a bitstring status list for revoking individual leases between epoch bumps. Without this, 'revocation_epoch: u64' stamped on every object is decoration — the industry-standard failure mode of OAuth revocation the canon is trying to escape.
3. **The policy-engine seam is a hash of an undefined object: no policy language, schema, evaluation semantics, or analysis story** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/wallet-network/doctrine.md:484 — '→ wallet.network evaluates policy'; doctrine.md:743 — 'PolicyEnvelope' (listed as emitted artifact, never given a schema anywhere in the pack per grep; only the unrelated OrchestrationPolicyEnvelope is specified)
   - Recommendation: Define PolicyEnvelope: a typed policy document with declared evaluation-engine profile, over which policy_hash is computed. Name a canonical profile — Cedar is the strongest fit: its principal/action/resource/condition model maps directly onto the Subject/Capability/Object/Policy grammar in product-exchange-risk.md:227-243, and its SMT-backed analysis lets the approval inbox's 'policy diff' for policy_widening be verified, not just displayed. Keep the seam pluggable (OPA/Rego acceptable for infra checks). The pack already fixes the evaluator split at the invariant level (README Non-Negotiable 10: authority providers authorize; the daemon admits, enforces, and fails closed), so the remaining canon work is the policy object, its language profile, and disagreement semantics — not who evaluates.
4. **No workload identity or sender-constrained leases: subjects are unauthenticated URI strings and leases appear bearer** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/wallet-network/api-authority-scopes.md:323-324 — '"subject_id": "agent://runtime-auditor", "requesting_runtime": "runtime://node_abc"'
   - Recommendation: Nothing specifies how runtime://node_abc proves it is node_abc at grant issuance, or prevents a leaked lease_id from being replayed by any process. Client sessions carry public_key_ref/attestation_ref (api-authority-scopes.md:697-698), and the Enterprise Secure Profile binds authority release to code identity via TEE attestation (doctrine.md:879-883) — but that binding is profile-specific, and the base AuthorityGrant and CapabilityLease objects have no holder key or proof-of-possession anywhere. Add a workload-identity seam: SPIFFE-style attestation resolving subject_id to a verifiable key document; require holder_key on every CapabilityLease; require DPoP-style proof-of-possession signatures on each lease use, bound into the per-use receipt.
5. **request_hash/policy_hash have no canonical serialization, so guardian signatures over 'exact request hash' are unimplementable across the Rust/TS boundary** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/wallet-network/doctrine.md:997 — 'Sensitive actions bind to exact request hash, policy hash, scope, expiry, and revocation epoch.'; api-authority-scopes.md:278 — '"must_display": {'
   - Recommendation: Every security property in these files reduces to signing sha256 of an unspecified byte encoding of a JSON-ish object produced by two runtimes (Rust anchors, @ioi/wallet-protocol TS). The repo already maintains cross-runtime receipt fixtures and conformance hooks (doctrine.md:649-654), so the seam exists — but no encoding is pinned. Pin a canonical encoding — JCS (RFC 8785) or deterministic CBOR/COSE — in @ioi/wallet-protocol, with cross-runtime hash-reproduction fixtures added to that conformance suite. Additionally define a typed intent-display schema per risk class (fields, units, truncation and homoglyph rules) and have guardians sign the canonical display encoding, not just an opaque hash — the Ledger clear-signing lesson: users approve what renders, so rendering must be part of the signed contract.
6. **scope:* is an unbounded flat string namespace with no grammar, registry, or versioning — and policy parameters are leaking into scope names** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/wallet-network/doctrine.md:504 — 'scope:wallet.transfer_under_limit'; api-authority-scopes.md:358 — 'scope:mow.route'
   - Recommendation: 'transfer_under_limit' embeds a policy parameter in an identifier — the exact anti-pattern OAuth RAR (RFC 9396) was created to fix; the limit belongs in grant constraints. foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md registers 'scope:*' as a URI scheme and INV-4 fixes the prim:*/scope:* two-tier split, but nothing governs the names themselves. Write a scope grammar spec: `scope:<namespace>.<action>` with declared namespace owners (provider connectors mint gmail.*, canon mints autonomous_system.*), an explicit no-implication rule (doctrine.md:544 already states it informally — formalize it), versioning, and a registry file in foundations/ (canonical-enums.md is precedent for exactly this consolidation move). Move all parameterization to a RAR-style structured authorization_details object on AuthorityScopeRequest/AuthorityGrant. Otherwise every connector team mints colliding strings and policy can't be written against a stable vocabulary.
7. **Brokered execution never names its enforcement point, leaving the preferred secret-release mode in tension with the boundary statement** — `medium` · Contradiction *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/wallet-network/doctrine.md:160 — 'wallet.network does not execute work, store app-domain operational truth'; api-authority-scopes.md:449-450 — 'the agent receives a scoped, expiring right to ask Wallet or a provider to use a capability'; api-authority-scopes.md:511 — 'The preferred mode is brokered execution.'
   - Recommendation: If Wallet performs the gmail.send with the brokered token (the secret-brokerage request even carries the app payload via input_ref), Wallet executes work, sees application payloads, and becomes a data-plane chokepoint — against its own boundary statement. The daemon-side alternative is already sanctioned in principle: INV-2 names 'daemon-sealed vaults' as legitimate custody, and doctrine.md:766 lets the daemon receive 'operation-scoped secret execution' without becoming a key custodian — but neither the brokered_execution mode nor the sealed_to_tee mode says which component actually holds the provider connection. Resolve by naming the enforcement point: an authority-injecting egress proxy inside the daemon's Guardian profile. wallet.network mints per-request, short-TTL, operation-scoped credentials (Vault-dynamic-secrets style) sealed to that proxy; the proxy injects them into the outbound call and never exposes them to worker code. Update the brokered_execution mode in api-authority-scopes.md:529 to say which component holds the provider connection.
8. **1379 lines of perps/prediction-market brokerage doctrine are fused to the enterprise authority layer — an adoption and regulatory-optics hazard** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/wallet-network/product-exchange-risk.md:646-647 — 'no restricted politics, elections, sports, employer-related, or insider-risk event markets unless policy explicitly allows them'
   - Recommendation: An enterprise buyer evaluating the authority plane for repo/deploy/data authority must read leverage caps, liquidation estimates, and event-market compliance carve-outs in the same canonical file — that is brokerage compliance programming, not authority doctrine, and it drags securities/CFTC-shaped regulatory surface onto the IAM story (the file itself concedes 'Finance remains a major high-risk vertical, but it is not the product boundary'). Note the fix cannot be moving TradeIntent/PredictionIntent into domains/decentralized/trade.md as such: this file explicitly states decentralized.trade does not own user authority or final approval, so the authority-binding objects must stay wallet-owned. Instead, split within the wallet-network component: keep the generic pieces (intent grammar, CandidateEvidence, coverage states, approval modes, approval inbox, protection actions, WalletReceipt) in product-exchange-risk.md, and quarantine TradeIntent/PredictionIntent/PositionReceipt plus venue/market/resolution/compliance detail into a separate wallet-network advanced-trade-authority file marked as an optional high-risk vertical module, cross-linked to domains/decentralized/trade.md for the market-intelligence side.
9. **The bespoke scope-request/approval REST flow re-derives GNAP without its negotiation state machine** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/wallet-network/api-authority-scopes.md:306 — 'POST /v1/authority/scope-requests'; api-authority-scopes.md:653-661 — 'one_shot_review / session_envelope / batch_review / silent_within_policy...'
   - Recommendation: The API implies an async negotiation (request → pending → step-up interaction → grant/deny) but never states the state machine, continuation semantics, polling vs push, or how a client resumes after guardian approval. GNAP (RFC 9635) specifies exactly this: grant requests, interaction start modes (redirect/app/user-code — mapping cleanly onto QR/push/CLI challenges), continuation URIs, and grant modification. Either adopt GNAP as the wire profile for /v1/authority/scope-requests (buying interop and prior art credibility), or add an explicit AuthorityScopeRequest lifecycle section (states, transitions, who may transition, receipts per transition). The approval-mode taxonomy would remain — it is stronger than GNAP's.
10. **Risk-class literals in the API file are not members of the canonical ladder the doctrine declares immutable** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/wallet-network/api-authority-scopes.md:233 — '"risk_ceiling": "read | draft | low_local_write"' and :715 — '"risk_ceiling": "funds | deploy | secret_export | policy_widening"'; vs doctrine.md:560 — 'read → draft → local_write → write_reversible → external_message → commerce'; the same drift also sits in foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3537-3539 (AuthorityClientEnvelope risk_ceiling includes 'low_local_write' and 'deploy')
   - Recommendation: doctrine.md:554-557 says the ladder is owned by foundations/canonical-enums.md and 'this section applies it, it does not redefine it' — yet the API file uses 'low_local_write' (canon: local_write) and 'deploy' (not a ladder member; also at line 242) as risk-class values, and the shared-object canon's AuthorityClientEnvelope repeats both, so the drift has already propagated into foundations. Normalize all risk_ceiling and step_up_required_for literals to canonical members (deploy presumably maps to write_reversible or system_destructive depending on target, or must be added to the ladder in canonical-enums.md first), fix the AuthorityClientEnvelope in common-objects-and-envelopes.md in the same pass, and add a docs gate that greps risk-class literals in wallet-network and foundations files against canonical-enums.md — the pack already runs literal-checking gates elsewhere.
11. **Two vocabularies for one authority chain: doctrine's emitted artifacts don't match the API's objects** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/wallet-network/doctrine.md:743-750 — 'PolicyEnvelope RootGrant SubGrant AuthorityScopeLease ApprovalToken RevocationEvent...'; api-authority-scopes.md:577,581 — 'Approval grant: … "approval_id": "approval_123"'
   - Recommendation: Doctrine emits RootGrant/SubGrant/AuthorityScopeLease/ApprovalToken; the API defines AuthorityGrant/CapabilityLease/'approval grant'/AuthorityReview, and never mentions RootGrant, SubGrant, or ApprovalToken (grep confirms those four names appear nowhere else in the pack). foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md's AuthorityGrantEnvelope already aligns field-for-field with the API's AuthorityGrant, so the doctrine list is the odd one out. A reader cannot tell whether AuthorityScopeLease and CapabilityLease are the same object or which object an enforcement point actually checks. Converge on one canonical chain — PolicyEnvelope → AuthorityGrant (root|sub via parent_grant_ref) → CapabilityLease → per-use receipt — define ApprovalToken as the single-use output of AuthorityReview step-up (or retire the name), and add a vocabulary table to api-authority-scopes.md mapping every doctrine artifact name to its API/foundations schema.
12. **The authority plane is a global availability chokepoint with no degraded-mode semantics** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/wallet-network/doctrine.md:296 — 'It may be always-on locally, hosted, mobile-assisted, enterprise-managed, or wakeable depending on profile.'
   - Recommendation: Every consequential action gates through online wallet.network policy evaluation, but the Availability Profiles section only enumerates deployment shapes — it never says what happens to in-flight capability leases when the authority plane is unreachable. The pack's fail-closed posture already exists at the invariant level (INV-14 honest-posture/fail-closed; README Non-Negotiable 10), so halting autonomous work is the implied default — but that is only survivable if paired with finding 1: leases verifiable offline at the daemon within per-risk-class staleness ceilings, with new issuance and step-up failing closed. Add a 'Degraded Authority Mode' section defining exactly which verifications are cacheable, for how long, per risk class, and what receipts record degraded-mode use.
13. **'wallet.network' as the canonical layer name collides with its own taxonomy and is disclaimed by its own doctrine** — `low` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/wallet-network/doctrine.md:24 — 'It is not merely a crypto wallet.'; doctrine.md:134 — 'The wallet.network name belongs in advanced, high-trust, or protocol contexts'
   - Recommendation: The Product Presentation section already engineers around the user-facing problem (hide the name behind 'SSO / permissions / connected access'), so the residual issue is canon-internal: 'wallet.network' the authority layer, 'Web3 wallet' the AuthFactor kind, and 'Wallet' the product console coexist in the same files, and for enterprise authority buyers the name reads crypto-first. If a rename is judged worth the churn, name the layer 'IOI Authority Plane' (matching INV-10's quadrant language) in canon and protocol artifacts, keeping wallet.network as one consumer-facing brand/deployment of that plane; at minimum, add a naming note distinguishing the three 'wallet' senses so canon readers stop colliding them. Pure doc-layer naming; no invariant changes.
14. **Client-session anomaly_state conflates concurrent detection signals with lifecycle status in a single enum** — `low` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/wallet-network/api-authority-scopes.md:718 — '"anomaly_state": "clean | watch | origin_mismatch | expired_use | scope_excess | suspicious_frequency | policy_denied | leaked | compromised"'
   - Recommendation: A session can simultaneously exhibit origin_mismatch and suspicious_frequency; an enum forces losing one. The same object also carries a separate lifecycle status (line 722), so anomaly_state is doing detection-verdict duty it can't structurally do. Replace with anomaly_events: a list of typed events {kind, observed_at, evidence_refs, receipt_ref} — consistent with the pack's own evidence-refs discipline used on risk labels — and keep status as the pure lifecycle machine (active → suspended/quarantined → rotated/revoked). Quarantine decisions then cite the triggering anomaly events in their receipts, which the blast-radius endpoint can aggregate. Apply the same fix to the mirrored AuthorityClientEnvelope in foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:3544-3546.


## Part III.10 — Substrate, privacy, and embodiment

### Private workspace cTEE + runtime nodes TEE/DePIN

**Files reviewed:** `components/daemon-runtime/private-workspace-ctee.md (1872)` · `components/daemon-runtime/runtime-nodes-tee-depin.md (291)`

**Current shape.** The pack defines "Private Workspace backed by cTEE" — an explicitly software-based custody discipline (not a hardware TEE) for persistent rented GPU nodes whose provider has root. Protected state (PII, strategy source, broker keys, live portfolio, final action authority) must never exist as node plaintext by default; the node receives only typed mounts of public trunks, redacted projections, encrypted refs, commitments, and capability handles via "Plaintext-Free Runtime Mounting," admitted at a daemon mount-admission boundary. Private agency uses Candidate-Lattice Private Decoding (CLPD): the rented GPU generates candidates at normal kernel speed while a guardian (authenticated browser, wallet.network, CLI signer, or threshold path) privately selects, declassifies, or signs; Counterfactual Lattice Execution commits the lattice before selection feedback, with leakage tracked in bits against explicit budgets. Every transition is receipt-bound (CustodyProof, ModelMountReceipt, PrivateInferenceReceipt, LeakageReceipt, DeclassificationReceipt), Agentgres records truth, wallet.network gates external effects, AutonomyLease bounds offline work, and AlphaSeal seals the quant-trading reference workload's private strategy head. The companion runtime-nodes doc taxonomizes nine execution venues (local, hosted, provider, DePIN, HypervisorOS bare-metal, cTEE, TEE-attested, customer VPC, embodied) and three placement privacy profiles: Mutual Blind, Private Workspace cTEE (no hardware TEE required), and Enterprise Secure (TEE attestation required). Both docs carry explicit non-claims — consumer GPUs are not TEEs, boot measurement is not privacy, metadata leaks — and the entire cTEE design is marked speculative with no implementation.

**What it already gets right:**

- The Non-Claims section and root-adversary threat model (private-workspace-ctee.md:167-211) are far more honest than typical DePIN/'encrypted compute' marketing: RAM snapshots, container non-protection, metadata leakage, and 'boot measurement is not a privacy root' are all stated plainly.
- Quantified leakage accounting — log2(K) per-selection bounds, choose(K,m) for top-m, cumulative budget fields in LeakageReceipt — exceeds what any shipping privacy product exposes; most vendors have no leakage model at all.
- Clean claim separation: privacy vs correctness vs authority (:184-195), and data custody (ExecutionPrivacyPosture) vs model-weight custody (lanes table :559-577) vs institutional learning rights are kept as independent decisions, preventing standard category errors.
- The Private Agency Transform / CLPD idea — public overgeneration on cheap untrusted GPUs plus small private selection — is a genuine architectural contribution that respects real FHE/MPC cost physics instead of hand-waving 'encrypted inference.'
- Provider-trust honesty: contractual no-training/retention terms are explicitly 'still provider trust' (:449-451), and posture labels are receipt/audit evidence rather than user-facing modes, which is the correct fail-closed shape for privacy claims.

**SOTA verdict: `divergent-by-design`** — compared against: NVIDIA Hopper/Blackwell Confidential Computing (H100 CC-mode, nvtrust/NRAS GPU attestation); Intel TDX / AMD SEV-SNP, including CipherLeaks-class ciphertext side channels and controlled-channel attacks; AWS Nitro Enclaves with KMS attestation-bound key release; RFC 9334 RATS attestation architecture (Evidence/Verifier/Attestation Result, freshness, endorsements); Confidential Containers (KBS attestation-gated key release) and Constellation cluster attestation; Apple Private Cloud Compute (reproducible builds, binary transparency log, researcher verifiability); Azure/GCP Confidential GPU VMs (CPU TEE + H100 CC composite attestation); Practical MPC/FHE (CKKS linear scoring realism vs transformer-scale infeasibility); DePIN compute trust models (Akash, io.net proof-of-work-done auditing); Model-extraction/distillation attack literature (query-oracle cloning of scoring functions)

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No GPU TEE custody lane or composite CPU+GPU attestation object, despite the flagship workload being private inference on rented GPUs — the exact problem H100 CC-mode ships an answer to; confidential hardware appears only as a brief overlay note (:1256) plus scattered enum hooks (:501, :567, :1599) with no mechanics
- No RATS-shaped attestation: no nonce/freshness, no distinct Verifier role (wallet.network verifies its own relying-party inputs), no endorsements/TCB revocation, no re-attestation cadence for persistent nodes
- No side-channel or physical-adversary non-claims for the hardware TEE lane — honesty is asymmetric: harsh on consumer 3090s, silent on SEV-SNP/TDX limits in the Enterprise Secure profile
- No PCC-style reproducible-build/binary-transparency anchoring for measured daemon images; the first-party hosted lane is covered only implicitly (threat model's 'host operator' :167, cTEE venue 'rented or hosted') and is never explicitly held to the same custody proofs as DePIN nodes
- No attestation-gated key-release architecture (Nitro/KMS, CoCo KBS pattern): the docs place keys in wallet.network/guardian custody but never define KEK/DEK structure or how release is evidenced
- No named mechanism for the 'correctness' claim (redundant execution, challenge games, verifiable inference) that DePIN settlement is said to depend on

**Findings (15, severity-ordered):**

1. **CustodyProof's 'no_plaintext_custody' verdict is unverifiable when the enforcing daemon runs on the adversary's node** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/private-workspace-ctee.md:704 — 'the accepted run carries checkable evidence that protected classes did not enter rented-node plaintext custody'
   - Recommendation: The threat model grants provider root process inspection (:167-180), and the same node 'may run the Hypervisor Daemon' (:115) — so the component enforcing Plaintext-Free Runtime Mounting can sit inside the adversary's TCB, where root can tamper with it or forge its receipts. The architecture already contains the sound anchor: keys live in wallet.network/guardian custody (Required Stack :257-259) and conformance check 5 requires a non-node authority path for key release — but the doc never states that a node-resident daemon is OUTSIDE the custody TCB, and the conformance claim at :703-707 presents node-side evidence as 'checkable.' Re-anchor CustodyProof as a guardian-side egress proof: the checkable statement is that the guardian/wallet.network side never released keys or plaintext toward node_ref (a signed send-log bound to the Agentgres-admitted mount graph). Rename verifier_result no_plaintext_custody → no_plaintext_release; label node-emitted receipts integrity/dispute evidence, never custody proof. As written, 'custody-proven' exceeds what a software stack on provider-rooted hardware can prove, violating the pack's own INV-9 (a receipt proves only the fact it binds) and INV-14 (honest posture).
2. **Offline autonomy silently relocates provider trust onto an unnamed guardian operator** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/private-workspace-ctee.md:304 — 'the architecture needs a small trusted or threshold authority path outside the rented GPU node'
   - Recommendation: AutonomyLease allows evaluate_alpha_seal while the user is offline (:1477), but a browser guardian is gone at 3am — so the private head must be evaluated by wallet.network, a 'cheap policy/key service' (:309-310), or a threshold committee. That party then custodies strategy plaintext or shares: provider trust relocated to the guardian operator, not removed. The doc already offers quorum options (AutonomyLease guardian_policy 1-of-1 | 2-of-3, :1493) and names HSM/enterprise-key-service/threshold guardian implementations (:290-294), and the 'no-provider-trust' phrase at :57 is scoped to model routing — but the Threat Model (:165-176) names only the node provider as adversary, and nothing requires disclosing guardian-operator trust when the offline evaluator is an IOI-managed service. Add GuardianCustodyProfile to AlphaSeal (user_device_only | user_hsm | threshold_committee | managed_guardian_service); extend the Threat Model to name the guardian operator as a second adversary; require guardian-trust disclosure (parallel to provider_trust) whenever the offline evaluator is a managed service, and forbid the Private label there unless threshold/HSM custody is proven.
3. **GPU confidential computing (H100 CC-mode era) is absent as a defined custody lane** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/private-workspace-ctee.md:1256 — 'TEE or confidential GPU support may upgrade where plaintext is allowed to exist'
   - Recommendation: By mid-2026, NVIDIA Hopper/Blackwell CC-mode with nvtrust/NRAS attestation and Azure/GCP confidential GPU VMs are the shipping industry answer to private inference on rented GPUs — the exact problem the CLPD machinery works around. The pack has hooks (tee_or_customer_cloud_mount lane :567, confidential_hardware execution strategy :1599, confidential_compute_attestation trust basis :501, TEE Runtime venue and TEE Flow in runtime-nodes) but no mechanics anywhere: no composite attestation object binding a CPU TEE report (TDX/SEV-SNP) to GPU attestation with RIM reference values, and no routing rule naming when to use it. Add a ConfidentialGpuMount lane under tee_or_customer_cloud_mount with that composite attestation object plus pre-TEE-I/O bounce-buffer throughput caveats, and route the 'full private transformer inference' rung (:400-402) to this lane as the named escalation when CandidateCoverageProfile redundancy mass is low, instead of leaving only transformer-infeasible FHE/MPC and unspecified 'trusted compute.'
4. **Attestation has no freshness, verifier role, endorsements, or re-attestation — a pre-RATS design** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: runtime-nodes-tee-depin.md:250 — 'wallet.network verifies measurement and policy'
   - Recommendation: NodeMeasurementReceipt (:1651-1662) carries no nonce, validity window, TCB status, endorsement source, or revocation check, and the six-line TEE Flow makes the authority plane its own attestation verifier. Adopt RFC 9334 roles: AttestationEvidence (nonce-fresh, per boot), a distinct AttestationVerifier producing an AttestationResultReceipt (validity_window, tcb_status, endorsement refs from Intel PCS / AMD KDS / NVIDIA RIM services), with wallet.network as relying party consuming results. For persistent nodes — the product's core value proposition — require periodic re-attestation bound to the AutonomyLease revocation_epoch, and define fail-closed key-release behavior on TCB downgrade, live migration, or stale evidence. Without freshness, a week-old boot measurement proves nothing about tonight's run.
5. **Hardware TEE is Private-qualifying 'no-provider-trust' in one section and a provider_trust_basis in another** — `high` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/private-workspace-ctee.md:60 — 'hardware TEE' (listed under 'Private = Standard plus no-provider-trust model routing')
   - Recommendation: The posture enum private_native | redacted_api | provider_trust | unsafe (:485) has no slot for plaintext-inside-attested-boundary, so an attested H100-CC run lands in provider_trust via basis confidential_compute_attestation (:501) — sharing a label with 'pinky-promise no-training contracts' — while :57-64 simultaneously lists hardware TEE as a no-provider-trust Private route, and runtime-nodes' Enterprise Secure profile requires TEE for the strictest claims. The same route cannot be both. Add posture value attested_confidential between private_native and provider_trust, and a ctee_claim value tee_attested_boundary; define which attestation qualities (verifier result, TCB currency, accepted physical-adversary class) allow a TEE route to satisfy Private mode. Otherwise receipts and product claims disagree about the flagship enterprise lane.
6. **Cumulative selection observations enable selector distillation; per-round bit bounds don't keep the alpha over months** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/private-workspace-ctee.md:1849 — 'Rent the node. Keep the alpha.'
   - Recommendation: The node observes candidate→disposal pairs round after round, plus public market fills as side information; log2(K) bits/round (:1708-1709) across thousands of rounds is a labeled training set for cloning the private head — a model-extraction attack the docs never name, and rotating keys doesn't help because the strategy itself is being distilled. The doc already concedes that 'selection leakage can accumulate across loop depth' for online CLPD (:1725-1727) and offers CLE as mitigation, and LeakageReceipt has cumulative_budget fields — but there is no exhaustion doctrine anywhere. Add: a hard cumulative-selection-bits cap per strategy epoch; mandatory strategy-epoch or node rotation on exhaustion; disposal-invisible CLE scheduling for high-value heads; public-fill observability charged as side information; and selector-extraction named in the Threat Model. Otherwise the headline promise degrades quietly with tenancy length on the flagship persistent workload.
7. **Enterprise Secure Profile has no Non-claim block: TEE side channels and physical adversaries get none of the honesty applied to consumer GPUs** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: runtime-nodes-tee-depin.md:149 — 'TEE-verified nodes required.'
   - Recommendation: Mutual Blind and the cTEE profile each carry paired Claim/Non-claim blocks; Enterprise Secure — the profile designated for 'regulated data' and 'proprietary strategy' — has neither. Add a Non-claim block with 2026 realities: SEV-SNP/TDX do not stop ciphertext side channels (CipherLeaks-class), controlled-channel/page-fault or interrupt-timing attacks from a malicious hypervisor, or physical interposer adversaries — precisely the adversary class on DePIN-hosted TEE hardware. Require TEE-route ExecutionPrivacyPosture records to name the accepted residual side-channel/physical-risk class. The pack's differentiator is symmetric honesty; today it is harsh on rented 3090s and silent on the hardware lane it recommends for the most sensitive data.
8. **'cTEE' trades on the TEE brand the doc spends pages disclaiming** — `medium` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/private-workspace-ctee.md:31 — 'cTEE = Cryptographic Trusted Execution Envelope'
   - Recommendation: Security reviewers parse 'TEE' as hardware trusted execution, forcing perpetual disclaimers ('does not turn a consumer GPU into trusted hardware' :91; 'No hardware TEE required' runtime-nodes:114) and inviting TEE-washing accusations from the confidential-computing community — credibility damage to a pack whose brand is honesty, and a live risk once marketplaces surface 'cTEE-backed' badges. The 'Envelope' vs 'Environment' distinction and the pervasive non-claims mitigate this inside the doc, but will not survive compression onto marketing surfaces. Rename the generic pattern to an acronym without 'TEE': Cryptographic Custody Envelope (CCE) or Sealed Custody Envelope (SCE); keep 'Private Workspace' as the product name; reserve 'TEE' strictly for attested hardware venues. The deprecated-wording migration mechanism already exists in this file (:126-134), so the rename is cheap now and expensive after launch.
9. **Key topology is unstated: specify guardian-side KMS envelope encryption with receipted per-object DEK release** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/private-workspace-ctee.md:1047 — 'decryption_key' (forbidden mount entry)
   - Recommendation: The docs forbid decryption_key mounts and do place keys in wallet.network/guardian custody (Required Stack :257-259; conformance check 5; pack INV-2 'leases, not secrets'), but never define the positive key architecture: KEK/DEK structure, derivation, and what evidence gates release. Specify standard envelope encryption: workspace KEKs only in wallet.network/guardian KMS or user HSM; per-object DEKs; DEK release exclusively through the DeclassificationGate emitting a KeyReleaseReceipt naming object, scope, TTL, and the authority/attestation evidence consumed. Also state which daemon instance executes PrivateWorkspaceMountAdmission (:519-521) — guardian-side, not node-side — so admission cannot be forged by node root. This is table stakes in AWS Nitro+KMS, CoCo KBS, and Constellation; without it the mount discipline is unenforceable as written and each implementer will invent divergent key handling.
10. **'Online branch-selection leakage before declassification is zero' ignores guardian-response timing and next-round seeding** — `medium` · Red flag
