   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/private-workspace-ctee.md:1730 — 'online branch-selection leakage before declassification is zero'
   - Recommendation: Zero holds only if (a) guardian response latency is padded — fast-deny vs slow-evaluate is a timing channel, and timing_bucket_bits_bound (:1745) is never bound to guardian responses — and (b) round n+1 expansion is independent of round n's selection; an agent loop seeding the next lattice from the chosen branch reveals the choice to the node. The path-privacy result (:800-809, counterfactual complete-trace sampling) already implies selection-independent expansion is the intended fix, but the leakage section leaves 'zero' quotable without those conditions. Qualify the claim: 'zero selection-content leakage within a committed round, under padded guardian responses and counterfactual next-round seeding,' and add a cross-round seeding rule to LeakageReceipt accounting (charge log2(K) whenever seeding is selection-dependent).
11. **No binary transparency for daemon images; the IOI-hosted lane is only implicitly held to its own custody bar (Apple PCC gap)** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/private-workspace-ctee.md:1654 — 'daemon_image_hash: sha256:...'
   - Recommendation: Apple PCC set the 2026 bar for managed private AI compute: reproducible builds, a public binary-transparency log, no-privileged-access architecture, researcher verifiability. Here daemon_image_hash floats unanchored — nothing binds it to an auditable build. The threat model does name the 'host operator' (:167) and the cTEE venue covers 'rented or hosted' nodes, so first-party hosting is implicitly in scope, but no explicit statement holds Hosted IOI Runtime (runtime-nodes venue 2) to the same CustodyProof and Plaintext-Free Runtime Mounting evidence as DePIN nodes. Add a reproducible-build plus Sigstore/Rekor-style transparency-log requirement for any image consumed by NodeMeasurementReceipt, and an explicit invariant that first-party hosting is not exempt. Otherwise Standard mode's 'IOI-managed execution' claim (:51) reduces to provider trust in IOI itself — the exact posture the pack disclaims for everyone else.
12. **Split the 1872-line file: custody canon, unvalidated CLPD/CLE scheduling theory, and the quant worked example are three documents** — `medium` · Refactor *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/private-workspace-ctee.md:11 — 'Implementation status: speculative (cTEE/CLPD design; no cTEE implementation)'
   - Recommendation: The coverage-frontier math (:757-810, e.g. m >= ceil(ln(1-rho)/ln(1-r))) is trivially true coupon-collector algebra, but its load-bearing applicability claim — that useful agent tasks are candidate-selection-reducible with constant redundancy mass — has zero benchmark evidence, yet sits under 'Status: canonical architecture authority.' The dual-status header (doctrine canonical / implementation speculative) and conformance check 11's benchmark-evidence obligation partially flag this, but the doctrine label still blesses the applicability claim. Split into: (a) custody-discipline canon (CustodyType, mounts, admission, declassification, receipts) — genuinely doctrinal; (b) a CLPD/CLE scheduling annex explicitly marked research-stage, carrying check 11's benchmark obligations; (c) AlphaSeal/quant reference workload as a worked example. This protects the falsifiable custody core from being discredited alongside any scheduling-theory failure and shrinks the drift surface of one of the component's largest doctrine files (1872 lines; only api.md and events-receipts-delivery-bundles.md are larger).
13. **An authenticated browser as default cryptographic second party is weak custody that disappears exactly when AutonomyLease starts** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/private-workspace-ctee.md:927 — 'authenticated browser'
   - Recommendation: A browser tab holds key material in script-reachable memory (XSS/extension exposure), has no uniform secure-element access for general decryption, cannot durably anchor MPC shares, and closes precisely when offline autonomy needs a live second party — the availability hole behind the guardian-custody finding. The doc's 'authority surface' default already includes stronger options (passkey-backed wallet agent, mobile approval, CLI signer, enterprise HSM :290-294, :926-932), and fallback_order already terminates in deny_or_escalate (:974-979) — but the browser session is listed first as a valid cryptographic party and nothing checks second-party availability against the lease window. Demote browser sessions to display/approval surfaces only; require the default second party (default_second_party: authority_surface, :968) to be a passkey/secure-element-anchored device signer, wallet.network key lease, or enterprise HSM; and add an availability clause to CryptographicOperatorPolicy: the second party must cover the AutonomyLease window at grant time, or the lease is denied — not discovered mid-lease.
14. **The 'correctness' leg (anti-fake-work) has no owning mechanism anywhere in the facet** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/private-workspace-ctee.md:189 — 'root cannot get paid for fake work without receipts, verification, redundancy, challenge, or dispute paths'
   - Recommendation: The three-claim split names correctness, and runtime-nodes hand-waves 'trusted verifier/settlement path accepts or rejects' (:212), but no document owns remote-execution verification: no redundant-execution quorum, spot-check challenge protocol, or verifiable-inference scheme — capabilities DePIN competitors already operationalize (io.net proof-of-work-done audits; Truebit-style challenge games; TOPLOC-style inference verification research). Pack invariant INV-9 correctly separates evidence/verification/acceptance/settlement states, but no mechanism doc fills the verification state for remote nodes. Either name an owner doc with a mechanism menu keyed to job class (deterministic kernels → sampled recompute; inference → activation/logit commitments; stochastic search → replication quorum) or mark correctness explicitly as future work in both files, so DePIN settlement and marketplace-payout claims (runtime-nodes invariant 6) do not outrun the pack.
15. **NodeMeasurementReceipt.privacy_claim invites the exact conflation its own note forbids** — `low` · Rename
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/private-workspace-ctee.md:1658 — 'none | blind_only | tee_attested | ctee_profile'
   - Recommendation: A measurement receipt carrying a privacy_claim field — plus a measurement_scope value 'privacy' (:1660) — is precisely the boot-measurement-as-privacy conflation the object's own note (:1661, 'Measurement is not a consumer-GPU plaintext privacy guarantee') and conformance check 9 prohibit; downstream verifiers will inevitably key privacy decisions off the field name. Rename privacy_claim → declared_execution_profile and drop 'privacy' from measurement_scope; keep privacy semantics exclusively in ExecutionPrivacyPosture and CustodyProof so no consumer of a NodeMeasurementReceipt can mistake integrity evidence for custody evidence.

### HypervisorOS Type 1 bare-metal substrate

**Files reviewed:** `components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md (818)`

**Current shape.** HypervisorOS is defined as the bare-metal ("Type 1") deployment profile of Hypervisor: a minimal, measured OS image whose sole purpose is to make the Hypervisor Daemon the root control plane of a physical or virtual node, so every agent, model server, tool, workspace mount, VM/container/microVM/WASM workload, and network egress is admitted, policed, and receipted by the daemon rather than running beside it. The doc specifies a required stack (measured-boot layer, minimal locked-package image, daemon as PID 1 or root service, worker substrate, cTEE runtime profile, Agentgres, wallet.network) and three deployment profiles: hosted/Type-2, bare-metal/Type-1, and an optional future firmware profile. It gives YAML shapes for HypervisorOSNode, HypervisorOSBootProfile (image/kernel/initrd/daemon/package/driver hashes, secure-boot/TPM posture, a two-flag update policy), HypervisorOSBootReceipt (typed measurement_method and privacy_claim tiers), and NodeEnforcementProfile (seccomp/eBPF/LSM hooks, executable allow/deny, default-deny egress, datawall detectors). It explicitly disclaims confidentiality from boot measurement — cTEE owns privacy, wallet.network owns authority, Agentgres owns admitted truth — and lists an adversary model plus 13 conformance checks centered on "no autonomous workload bypasses the daemon." Cluster membership is governed separately: a measured node is only a membership candidate, and boot integrity never grants system authority (INV-22..24). Implementation status is declared speculative; no HypervisorOS build exists.

**What it already gets right:**

- The Non-Claims section (hypervisoros.md:158-179) is unusually honest for this product category: it explicitly refuses to launder measured boot into confidential-compute claims for consumer GPUs, which is exactly the overclaim most DePIN/agent-node projects make.
- Boot integrity is cleanly separated from system authority (hypervisoros.md:329-347): a measured node is only a membership candidate, and 'Boot integrity never grants system authority' — this attestation-is-not-authorization discipline matches best practice and is stronger than many real attestation deployments.
- There is an explicit adversary model (hypervisoros.md:186-201) naming concrete attacks (forge node measurements, replay old leases, return fake work for payment) — most OS-profile docs have no threat model at all.
- Scope discipline is real: the firmware profile is explicitly optional (326-327), a minimal Linux/KVM base is permitted for v1 (121-122), and 'requiring a custom hardware hypervisor from day one' is a named anti-pattern (755).
- The receipt schema types its own honesty: measurement_method and privacy_claim enums (465-470) force every boot receipt to declare its assurance tier instead of implying uniform trust — a ladder few systems encode at the schema level.

**SOTA verdict: `behind`** — compared against: Talos Linux (machined as PID-1 API-driven init, A/B image upgrades with automatic rollback, Omni fleet desired-state management, system extensions for GPU drivers); AWS Bottlerocket (immutable variants, TUF-signed updates, update operator with staged waves); Flatcar Container Linux / Fedora CoreOS (A/B + OSTree transactional update rails, Nebraska/Zincati update orchestration); systemd UKI + measured boot stack (systemd-pcrlock, systemd-sysext, boot counting/auto-fallback); TPM 2.0 EK/AK remote attestation, IETF RATS architecture (RFC 9334), Keylime (runtime IMA attestation); SLSA / in-toto / Sigstore supply-chain provenance; Kairos (factory for building measured immutable derivatives of existing distros); Balena / Rancher Fleet (edge fleet desired-state and drift reconciliation); Firecracker / Cloud Hypervisor (microVM worker substrate)

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No transactional update mechanism: no A/B slots or OSTree deployments, no health-gated automatic rollback, no staged channels/waves — the entire update story is two booleans (450-452)
- No verifier/appraisal architecture for boot receipts: no nonce freshness, no TPM event-log binding to boot-profile reference values (the linked runtime-nodes doc sketches 'wallet.network verifies measurement and policy' for the TEE flow, but HypervisorOSBootReceipt itself remains node-self-reported with no appraisal contract)
- No hardware-rooted device identity (TPM EK/AK enrollment, DICE/IDevID) — receipt 'signature' has unspecified key provenance, so 'forge node measurements' is unmitigated
- No fleet desired-state/drift story: no object declares a target boot profile per cohort, detects drifted nodes, or reconciles them (Talos Omni / Fleet / Balena territory)
- Boot-time-only measurement: no post-boot runtime attestation (IMA/Keylime) for nodes explicitly intended to run persistently for weeks
- No driver/extension layering doctrine (systemd-sysext / Talos extensions) for the GPU driver stack that the boot profile hashes but cannot practically freeze
- Anti-rollback is a boolean with no mechanism (TPM monotonic counters, verified-boot rollback index)

**Findings (15, severity-ordered):**

1. **The facet's core competency — image update and rollback — is missing entirely** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md:450 — 'update_policy: signed_updates_required: true, rollback_protection: true'
   - Recommendation: Every SOTA anchor (Talos, Bottlerocket, Flatcar, CoreOS) treats transactional update-with-rollback as THE defining problem of an immutable node OS; this canon owns 'minimal node image doctrine' (:78) yet reduces it to two booleans. Add an HypervisorOSUpdateProfile (channel, target boot_profile_ref, A/B-slot or OSTree-deployment semantics, health gates = daemon readiness + conformance checks, boot-counting auto-fallback) and an HypervisorOSUpdateReceipt admitted to Agentgres; extend the event list (:689) with hypervisoros.update.staged/activated/rolled_back. Make anti-rollback concrete: bind boot_epoch (:459) to a TPM monotonic counter so an old-but-signed image cannot be replayed.
2. **No verifier/appraisal architecture: attestation is node-self-reported and 'policy_declared' counts as measurement** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md:467 — 'provider_attestation | policy_declared'
   - Recommendation: SOTA remote attestation (IETF RATS RFC 9334, Keylime) separates Attester evidence from a Verifier's appraisal against reference values. The linked runtime-nodes-tee-depin.md does sketch partial verifier notions — 'wallet.network verifies measurement and policy' in the TEE attestation flow (:250) and a 'trusted verifier/settlement path accepts or rejects' for work results (:212) — but nothing appraises HypervisorOSBootReceipt against HypervisorOSBootProfile reference hashes, there is no verifier-issued nonce for freshness, and no TPM event log accompanies the quote. Define NodeAttestationEvidence (quote + event log + verifier-issued nonce) and a Verifier role producing a NodeAttestationResult that settlement qualification and provider reputation (:580) consume — never the raw self-report. Keep policy_declared as a legal tier, but conformance must forbid it from qualifying for payout/reputation, or 'forge node measurements' (:196) is trivially cheap.
3. **No hardware-rooted node identity, so measurement receipts prove nothing about which machine emitted them** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md:196 — 'forge node measurements'
   - Recommendation: HypervisorOSBootReceipt ends in 'signature: ...' (:471) with unspecified key provenance, and HypervisorOSNode binds only owner_ref: wallet://. Any software anywhere can mint a receipt claiming a node_id. Adopt the standard enrollment ceremony: TPM EK certificate → AK creation → AK-certified node identity key, recorded as a NodeIdentityReceipt at provisioning (DICE/IDevID for non-TPM hardware); require all node receipts to be signed by the AK-bound key and require the Verifier (previous finding) to check the chain. Without this, the threat model's forgery adversary defeats the whole measurement doctrine, and the defense list's 'node measurements' (:215) is circular.
4. **Measurement is boot-time-only, but the flagship use case is nodes that run for months** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md:727 — '7. Node boot/runtime state emits measurement receipts.'
   - Recommendation: Conformance check 7 promises 'boot/runtime state' receipts, yet the only defined mechanism is the boot receipt; 'persistent rented GPU nodes' (:307) drift after boot via module loads, kexec, or daemon-binary replacement. Add a runtime-integrity contract: IMA/Keylime-style periodic re-attestation (or at minimum daemon-binary and kernel-lockdown self-measurement) producing NodeMeasurementReceipt on a declared cadence. The doc already has the perfect hook: PersistentHypervisorOSNode's freshness block (:660-666) — add node_measurement_fresh_within to lease_check_required_before so a stale attestation fails model_mount/declassification/capability_exit closed, consistent with pack physics.
5. **No fleet desired-state or drift-reconciliation story for a doc that owns boot profiles and conformance** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md:82 — 'boot profile manifests'
   - Recommendation: Talos Omni, Bottlerocket's update operator, Rancher Fleet, and Balena all center on: declare a target version per cohort, detect nodes that drifted, reconcile or quarantine. Here each HypervisorOSNode carries a boot_profile_ref but nothing declares the fleet target, compares node-reported boot receipts against it, or drives convergence. Add a NodeFleetProfile (cohort selector, target boot_profile_ref, rollout wave policy, max-drift window) plus hypervisoros.node.drifted feeding the existing hypervisoros.node.quarantined event (:693). If fleet orchestration belongs to Environments/Operations in the estate, say so explicitly here and define only the drift-evidence contract — right now no doc owns it.
6. **Default-deny egress doctrine contradicts the enforcement profile's 'allow' default** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md:607 — 'HypervisorOS must default to denied egress for autonomous workloads.' vs :497 — 'default: deny | policy_declared | allow'
   - Recommendation: The doctrine says egress MUST default to deny, but NodeEnforcementProfile's schema legalizes default: allow with no scoping. Either constrain the enum — autonomous-workload scopes get deny only, with allow reserved for an explicitly named node-management traffic class (Agentgres sync, wallet.network, receipt sinks) — or add a conformance clause to check 9 (:729) declaring any profile with default: allow for autonomous workloads non-conformant. As written, a conformant-looking profile object can violate the doctrine sentence.
7. **A custom OS is not justified for v1 — canon should mandate profiling an existing immutable OS** — `medium` · Better way *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md:122 — 'The first implementation may use a minimal Linux/KVM/microVM base.'
   - Recommendation: The doc already leans this way — it permits a minimal Linux/KVM base (:121-122) and names 'requiring a custom hardware hypervisor from day one' as an anti-pattern (:755) — but it stops at permission where it should mandate a realization strategy. The differentiators claimed (daemon-rooted admission, receipts, cTEE routing) require none of the OS-maintenance treadmill: kernel CVE stream, secure-boot signing infra, driver packaging. State explicitly that HypervisorOS v1 is a conformance profile realized as a derived image of an existing immutable OS — Talos with the daemon as a system extension, a Bottlerocket variant, or a Kairos-built derivative — and that 'minimal node image doctrine' means pinning an upstream release train plus the daemon layer, not maintaining a distro. Reserve a from-scratch image for a later profile if conformance genuinely cannot be met. This keeps the daemon-root invariant while inheriting SOTA update/measured-boot machinery for free.
8. **'policy_declared' as an enum value conflates a setting's value with its assurance provenance** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md:447 — 'enabled: true | false | policy_declared'
   - Recommendation: The same pattern recurs at :490 (executable mode) and :497 (egress default). A tri-state mixing {what is true} with {how we know} makes downstream logic mushy: a consumer cannot ask 'is secure boot on?' separately from 'is that measured or self-asserted?'. Split every such field into value (bool/enum) plus assurance: measured | attested | declared. This makes settlement qualification and provider reputation (:578-580) computable over assurance tiers, and it composes with the Verifier finding: appraisal upgrades declared→attested rather than mutating the value. Notably, HypervisorOSBootReceipt already gets this right with separate measurement_method/privacy_claim tiers (:465-469) — the fix extends the receipt's own discipline to the profile schemas.
9. **The GPU driver stack versus a measured, locked-package image is an unstated hard problem** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md:445 — 'model_hint: rtx_3090 | rtx_4090 | h100 | other'
   - Recommendation: NVIDIA kernel modules plus CUDA userspace churn monthly, need signing under Secure Boot, and must match kernel ABI — the exact problem that forced Talos system extensions and Bottlerocket variants. Here a single driver_manifest_hash (:442) freezes drivers into the measured image, meaning every driver update re-measures the node and needs the (currently missing) update pipeline plus reference-value redistribution. Adopt extension layering: drivers as separately versioned, separately hashed extension images (sysext-style) declared as a list in HypervisorOSBootProfile, measured independently of the base, with their own update channel. Without this, GPU nodes — the stated primary market (:307) — cannot be patched.
10. **Supply-chain provenance is conflated with boot measurement in one enum** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md:466 — 'secure_boot | tpm_quote | reproducible_image |'
   - Recommendation: reproducible_image is a build-provenance property (SLSA/in-toto: this artifact was built from these sources, reproducibly) while secure_boot/tpm_quote are runtime measurement properties (this machine booted these hashes). Listing them as alternatives in measurement_method lets a node substitute build provenance for boot evidence. Split them: put image_provenance (SLSA level, rebuild attestations, Sigstore signatures) on HypervisorOSBootProfile as reference-value pedigree, and keep measurement_method strictly runtime (secure_boot, tpm_quote, provider_attestation, declared). Conformance check 7 should require both provenance and measurement for settlement-qualifying nodes — they answer different questions and SOTA (Bottlerocket TUF + TPM) treats them as orthogonal.
11. **Mandatory per-event denial receipts create an unbounded receipt-storm channel into Agentgres** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md:711 — 'ExecutableDeniedReceipt'
   - Recommendation: ExecutableDeniedReceipt and EgressDetectionReceipt (:711-712) are required receipt families with no aggregation or rate policy, and 'treat datawall detections as optional debug logs' is an anti-pattern (:754) — so an adversarial or merely buggy workload spamming denied execs/egress forces unbounded receipt admission into the node's operational-truth store, a self-inflicted DoS on the very evidence plane disputes depend on. Add a receipt aggregation contract: per-window summarized incident receipts (first occurrence verbatim, then counted rollups with representative samples), mirroring auditd rate-limiting practice, with the rollup itself receipted so evidence remains complete without being volumetric.
12. **Daemon-as-PID-1 lacks an init/supervision contract; specify the fence and watchdog instead of the mechanism** — `low` · Better way *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md:233 — 'PID 1 or root control service'
   - Recommendation: The doc already hedges — the daemon is 'PID 1 or root control service' (:233), so PID 1 is optional not mandated, and the Execution Model (:256-267) already fixes boot ordering (measured boot → OS → daemon → workers). What is missing is the supervision contract behind either option: as PID 1, a daemon crash panics the node and a daemon upgrade forces a reboot; as a service, nothing specifies what may start before the daemon signals admission-ready. Specify: (a) an explicit 'admission fence' — no workload-shaped process starts before daemon admission-ready — preserving the execution-root rule (:515-541), (b) daemon supervision with a hardware watchdog wired to reboot-into-rollback, and (c) in-place daemon restart/upgrade without image reboot. Keep 'root control service' as the conformance property; treat 'PID 1' as one permitted mechanism, not a doctrine claim.
13. **'Type 1' / 'Type 3' overloads the canonical hypervisor taxonomy and invites credibility attacks** — `low` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md:63 — 'HypervisorOS is Hypervisor's Type 1 substrate mode for autonomous systems'
   - Recommendation: v1 is admitted to be Linux/KVM with a root daemon (:122) — infrastructure audiences will read 'Type 1 bare-metal substrate' as a claim to Goldberg Type-1 status and dismiss it as a Linux appliance; worse, 'Type 3 autonomy mode' (:113) is not a machine posture at all, breaking the borrowed taxonomy's own axis. The doc does self-explain the generalization (:63-69) and vocabulary.md explicitly says the three modes 'are not three products', so this is a deliberate stretch, not an accident — but deliberateness does not defuse the credibility risk with the stated infra audience. README Non-Negotiable 2 wants one product with three postures — preserved by renaming, e.g. 'node-root profile' (T1), 'hosted profile' (T2), and 'governed-autonomy plane' for Type 3, keeping 'bare-metal profile' as the marketing noun. Note the true cost: the taxonomy is hard-coded as HypervisorSubstrateMode enum slugs (type1_hypervisoros, type2_desktop_workstation, type3_autonomy_plane) in vocabulary.md and appears in README Non-Negotiable 2 plus 10+ other files, so this is a coordinated docs-wide rename, not a one-file edit — still far cheaper now than after implementation.
14. **Concretize or cut the 'Firmware Hypervisor' profile — as written it restates measured boot** — `low` · Refactor
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md:117 — 'Firmware Hypervisor: optional future profile using UEFI / TPM / pre-OS hooks'
   - Recommendation: UEFI/TPM measurement is exactly what the bare-metal profile's measured-boot layer (:228-230) already uses, so the firmware profile currently adds a name without a capability. If it means anything beyond that, it means a dynamic root of trust for measurement — Intel TXT/AMD SKINIT via TrenchBoot, or DICE-rooted early attestation. Either name that concretely (DRTM profile: late-launch measured environment that re-measures the daemon independent of the BIOS/bootloader chain) or delete the section and fold 'higher-integrity attestation later' into the Node Measurement Doctrine. Vague future profiles in canonical docs become drift anchors that other files cite as if specified.
15. **Split the runtime contract from product posture; the placement note institutionalizes the mixture** — `low` · Refactor *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/hypervisoros.md:21 — 'deliberately not moved to avoid churn on cross-references.'
   - Recommendation: The file self-diagnoses that it mixes daemon-runtime contract with deployment/product posture (:17-21) and then resolves the smell by inaction — while carrying marketing copy ('Rent the node. Keep the alpha.', :792) inside canonical substrate doctrine. Split: keep objects, threat model, execution-root rule, enforcement profile, and conformance checks here as the node contract; move Product UX, product language blocks (:30-36), and deployment-posture narrative to an estate/product doc that cites this one. Cross-reference churn is a one-time grep-fix; a canon file whose own header says its content is in the wrong place is a standing drift engine for every future alignment pass.

### Embodied runtime: units, fleets, two-speed boundary

**Files reviewed:** `components/daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md (2188) — read fully in chunks`

**Current shape.** embodied-runtime.md (2,188 lines, target canon, explicitly unimplemented) is the single owner for IOI's physical-world runtime: identity objects (EmbodiedRuntimeDomain, RobotFleetRecord, EmbodiedUnitIdentity, RobotControllerBinding, sensor/actuator registries, versioned EmbodiedResourceGroups with closure hashes), an immutable compiled EmbodiedRuntimeGraphManifest whose component contracts carry WCET/deadline/criticality and whose PhysicalStreamContracts fix semantics (units, frames, clocks, QoS, security) before any transport is chosen, and composable micro/edge/site deployment footprints. Execution is "two-speed": a slow governance plane (Goal Kernel, policy, wallet authority, Agentgres) admits a bounded, expiring PhysicalMissionControlEnvelope, while all deadline-sensitive work runs locally in three isolated strata — nondeterministic autonomy (planners/VLAs emitting expiring, watermark-bound EmbodiedActionChunk proposals), deterministic motion, and an independent runtime-assurance/safety domain owning monitors, command switch, clipping/veto, watchdogs, and minimum-risk control. A LocalControlSupervisor enforces the frozen admitted snapshot near the body — validating envelopes, leases, world state and clocks, fencing one exclusive actuator writer, arbitrating commands, and holding "the final local veto" — while external stacks (ROS-like, autopilots, PLCs, vendor clouds) attach only through subordinate LocalControlBridge compatibility bindings that can never arm graphs or grant authority. Activation (EmbodiedGraphActivationTransaction) is deliberately separated from arming; restart always returns graphs inactive and unarmed. Same-system fleet work uses epoch-fenced FleetMissionAllocationLeases plus separate SpacetimeReservationLeases, declared partition/rejoin behavior, and duplicate/ambiguous-effect reconciliation; cross-system cooperation must cross AIIP. Telemetry, raw robot logs vs normalized episode datasets, physical replay bundles, sim-to-real promotion gates (SIL/HIL/shadow/limited-live), deployment-bound assurance cases, incidents, operator handoff, and fleet policy complete the lifecycle, while safety policy itself (envelopes, e-stop, supervision) is owned by physical-action-safety.md.

**What it already gets right:**

- The three-strata local decomposition (autonomy / deterministic_motion / runtime_assurance_safety) with the requirement that 'a process, GPU, network, or model failure may not remove the runtime-assurance stratum's final local veto' is a correct, standards-aware generalization of Simplex-style runtime assurance — no agent-framework competitor has anything comparable, and most robot fleet products don't state it this precisely.
- PhysicalStreamContract is a strict superset of DDS QoS: it adds units, frames, clock domains, timestamp uncertainty, producer/consumer identity, anti-replay/confidentiality, criticality, and mandatory degrade/fail/stop actions, and the stance that 'matching topic names, tensor shapes, or successful transport negotiation does not prove contract compatibility' fixes ROS's single weakest contract point.
- EmbodiedActionChunk + EmbodiedActionPolicyContract (nominal control frequency, action horizon, max inference latency, jitter, late-result behavior, OOD thresholds, fallback, watermark binding, expiry, never-authority) matches pi-0/RT-2-era VLA chunked-deployment practice and governs it better than any shipping stack.
- Activation-vs-arming separation, restart-unarmed, safety-partition immutability while armed, one-exclusive-actuator-writer fencing, and epoch-fenced allocation plus separate spacetime reservation leases (with explicit refusal to claim cross-controller atomicity) are more rigorous than commercial fleet orchestration (Formant-class) and on par with Open-RMF's best ideas while adding fencing and receipts.
- Evidence honesty is real: the proof matrix demands p50/p99/p999 latency and jitter, fault injection, partition/rejoin, intervention rates, and replay fidelity; the sim-to-real ladder cleanly separates offline eval, SIL, HIL, shadow, limited live, and promotion; and the anti-pattern list ('treating a model response as heartbeat', 'simulation success as live deployment authority') encodes hard-won practitioner knowledge.

**SOTA verdict: `near-sota`** — compared against: Simplex runtime assurance (Sha) and ASTM F3269-style run-time assurance for bounded complex functions; ROS 2 / DDS QoS, ros2_control, lifecycle nodes; Nav2 + BehaviorTree.CPP reactive behavior semantics; pi-0 / RT-2-era VLA action-chunking deployment practice (inference deadlines, chunk horizons, fallback); IEC 61508 / ISO 13849 / ISO 10218 / ISO 3691-4 functional-safety practice and certification economics; ISO 34503 / BSI PAS 1883 ODD formalization; UL 4600 / GSN structured safety cases; Open-RMF traffic negotiation and VDA 5050 AGV interop for mixed-vendor fleets; Formant / Foxglove fleet observability and teleoperation practice (link budgets, deadman, advisory vs direct control); MIL/SIL/HIL validation ladders (dSPACE-class HIL, NVIDIA Isaac Sim)

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No typed runtime-assurance switching contract: detection-to-switch latency, monitor period, and recoverable-region margin derivation (the core of Simplex/ASTM F3269) live in opaque timing_and_fault_assumption_refs
- No distinction between safety-rated sensing channels (ISO 13849/3691-4 certified devices) and learned perception as inputs to the runtime-assurance monitors
- ODD is an unreviewable artifact://|policy:// ref; no ISO 34503-style typed ODD with measurable attributes and monitored exit triggers
- No concrete middleware mapping profiles — ROS 2 QoS/lifecycle/action mapping, MAVLink offboard/failsafe, VDA 5050 order/state — behind the LocalControlBridge's three opaque artifact refs
- No reference behavior-execution semantics (tick/preemption/contingency ordering) between the mission envelope and action-chunk sources
- No teleoperation session contract (link class, latency gates, deadman, shared-control arbitration) despite manual_control being an allowed remote-operator action — teleoperators are governed as action-chunk sources, but OperatorHandoff.manual_control is not bound to that path
- No certification-economics story for the native path: canon permits deferring to OEM-certified controllers and safety PLCs, but the mandated native reference LocalControlSupervisor as specced is far too large to be the assessed safety item under IEC 61508/ISO 13849
- No end-to-end timing chain budgets composing per-component WCET and per-stream QoS into the sensor-to-actuator and violation-to-stop paths that a builder must actually verify

**Findings (14, severity-ordered):**

1. **No runtime-assurance switching contract (detection-to-switch timing, recoverable region)** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md:968 — 'safe-set and operating-envelope monitor, command switch, clipping/veto,'
   - Recommendation: Simplex/ASTM F3269 practice derives the switching boundary from plant dynamics plus monitor evaluation period plus switch WCET; without that, the veto provably fires too late for fast plants (drones, arms near humans). Partial coverage exists — per-component WCET/deadline in the manifest, domain-level max_estop_latency_ms, and the assurance case's timing_and_fault_assumption_refs (common-objects-and-envelopes.md:6158) — but none of it types detection-to-switch latency, monitor period, or margin derivation. Add a typed RuntimeAssuranceSwitchContract required per actuator-bearing graph: monitored invariant refs, monitor period, worst-case detection-to-switch latency, margin-derivation evidence ref, bound recovery controller, and switch-test receipts; reference it from EmbodiedDeploymentAssuranceCase.safety_argument and check it at activation alongside WCET budgets. This strengthens INV-34 rather than changing it.
2. **No safety-rated sensing channel distinction for RTA monitor inputs** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md:512 — 'public | internal | private | regulated | safety_critical' — SensorRegistryEntry.data_class is a privacy/data axis, not an assurance axis
   - Recommendation: ISO 3691-4/ISO 13849 practice: safety functions consume safety-rated devices (certified laser scanners, safety encoders), never learned perception. The doc has adjacent hooks — data_class includes safety_critical, PhysicalStreamContract carries criticality, sensors can be marked untrusted, and the anti-pattern at :2136-2137 forbids colocating learned perception with the only watchdog — but these are data/priority/failure-domain axes, not assurance provenance: nothing forbids the RTA monitor from logically depending on autonomy-stratum or unassured learned-perception outputs, which collapses the independence argument even with perfect process isolation. Add an assurance-channel posture to SensorRegistryEntry and PhysicalStreamContract (safety_rated_ref | independently_assured | unassured) and a conformance check: runtime_assurance_safety components bind only assured channels, or the deployment assurance case records the explicit justified exception.
3. **The final local veto is owned by two different objects** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md:228 — 'monitors, command switch, clipping/veto, watchdogs, recovery/minimum-risk control, interlocks, and final local veto' (runtime_assurance_safety stratum) vs :1304 — 'command arbitration, recovery-controller switching, watchdogs, and the final local veto' (LocalControlSupervisor)
   - Recommendation: State explicitly whether the runtime_assurance_safety stratum executes inside the LocalControlSupervisor or beneath it — today both claim to own the final veto and the containment relation is never stated. The doc partially defuses this: :1262-1266 declares the supervisor 'a logical local trust boundary' whose runtime-assurance monitor, switch, and recovery path must retain independent isolation, and :1369-1373 explicitly resolves on_supervisor_gap (continue_under_separately_assured_boundary is valid only when another locally available safety controller retains monitor/switch/recovery/e-stop after supervisor loss), so the failsafe policy is not incoherent. But the ownership ambiguity remains in a pack whose whole ethos is single ownership. Cleanest fix: give the RTA stratum its own admitted identity (RuntimeAssuranceKernel) owning monitor, command switch, watchdog, minimum-risk control, and the veto; LocalControlSupervisor becomes the mission-side validator/scheduler that binds it (the existing runtime_assurance_component_keys field is the seam) — or add one sentence fixing the containment relation. Note README.md's decision text ('The native LocalControlSupervisor or a separately assured local controller holds the final veto') would need the same clarification.
4. **The certifiable safety item is too large — split the supervisor Simplex-style** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md:1262 — 'It is a logical local trust boundary, not permission to collapse every duty into one process or failure domain.'
   - Recommendation: The disclaimer exists, and canon already has an escape valve: deployments may use OEM-certified controllers or safety PLCs inside the local domain (:983-985), and EmbodiedRuntimeGuarantee.control_loop_owner may be a separately_assured_local_controller (:1387-1388). But the mandated native reference runtime still bakes graph scheduling, stream/time-health, lease fencing, command arbitration, evidence emission, and the veto into one LocalControlSupervisor object with only deployment-discretion isolation evidence. Under IEC 61508/ISO 13849 the item implementing a safety function is assessed as a whole, so the native assessable item is a huge mixed-criticality program — certification economics that killed many robotics platforms. Follow Simplex: a minimal decision module (safe-set monitor, command switch, watchdog, minimum-risk controller — small, formally analyzable, optionally on a safety MCU) is the only high-integrity item; everything else is QM software that may fail without violating the case. Encode the split as separate canon objects with a one-way typed interface, not deployment discretion.
5. **Remote manual_control on degraded networks vs the remote-loop prohibition** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md:1789 — 'observe | pause | safe_stop | manual_control' offered for reason :1781 — 'degraded_network | stale_sensor | high_risk_action' vs :1389 — 'remote_round_trip_in_control_loop: false'
   - Recommendation: This is an unresolved ambiguity more than a flat contradiction — :1389 governs the deterministic control loop, offline_mode already includes local_manual_only, and the doc already treats teleoperators as governed action-chunk sources (:743) — but OperatorHandoff.manual_control is bound to none of that. An OperatorHandoff to a user:// granting manual_control, triggered by degraded_network, is exactly a remote round trip in the control loop, invoked precisely when the link is least trustworthy, and the doc never distinguishes a local pendant from a remote link. Add a TeleoperationSessionContract: link class (local_pendant | assured_low_latency | best_effort_remote), measured latency/loss gates, deadman and operator-input stream contract, shared-control arbitration, and automatic degradation to safe_stop on link-budget violation; permit manual_control only under a link class the active assurance case admits, and route remote manual control through the governed action-chunk path rather than an unconstrained handoff action.
6. **Maturity bar demands a full native robotics platform before any claim** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md:1934 — 'native implementation may claim the target only after the same contract family passes all of the following reference systems'; :1865 — 'The target must therefore ship a native reference runtime'
   - Recommendation: The 7-family component library plus workcell+drone+humanoid+heterogeneous-site+MCU proof matrix is a ROS 2 + PX4 + MoveIt + Open-RMF-scale effort (decade, large team). The pack's implementation-status axis and _meta/implementation-matrix.md already permit honest partial/built claims below 'the target', so credibility is not entirely deferred — but within embodied canon itself the bar is all-or-nothing, which pushes adopters to treat bridges as the real product while canon frames them as shims. Restructure into named claimable conformance levels: L0 bridge-governed (external stack executes; native supervisor, envelopes, receipts, replay), L1 native RTA kernel, L2 native deterministic motion, L3 native micro/site footprints — each with its own subset of the reference matrix and reportable evidence. This preserves the competitive boundary (IOI owns semantics at every level) and the pack's evidence-honesty ethos without deferring all claimable status to L3.
7. **Bridge mappings are three opaque artifact refs; no ROS 2/MAVLink/VDA 5050 profiles** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md:1333 — 'component_mapping_ref: artifact://...' (likewise stream_mapping_ref :1334, action_mapping_ref :1335)
   - Recommendation: The bridge is the adoption-critical path — virtually every real deployment starts ros_like or drone_autopilot. The generic EmbodimentAdapter wire shape in common-objects-and-envelopes.md does own mapping into IOI identities, frames, streams, actions, lifecycle, health, and receipts, and the bridge pins a mapping_hash; but there are no canonical per-external_runtime_kind profiles, so each mapping's entire semantics remains an unreviewable artifact ref. Define typed ExternalRuntimeMappingProfiles per external_runtime_kind: for ros_like, a PhysicalStreamContract.qos↔DDS-QoS table, lifecycle-node↔component-lifecycle mapping, action-server↔PhysicalCommand mapping, TF↔frame refs, /clock↔time_sync binding; for drone_autopilot, a MAVLink offboard/failsafe/geofence profile; for industrial AGVs, VDA 5050 order/state mapping. Without canonical profiles every integrator reinvents them, semantic drift is guaranteed, and 'a binding may never silently change semantic, timing, evidence, or authority behavior' (:268) is unverifiable.
8. **EmbodiedRuntimeGuarantee is domain-scoped and disconnected from component/stream budgets** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md:1383 — 'domain_ref: embodied_domain://...' — the sole scope for max_command_latency_ms, max_observation_latency_ms, max_estop_latency_ms
   - Recommendation: One max_estop_latency_ms per embodied domain is the wrong granularity: a domain mixing drones and door locks has no single meaningful bound, and nothing composes these maxima from the per-component WCET/deadline and per-stream QoS values the manifest already freezes. Replace with named end-to-end TimingChainBudget entries on EmbodiedRuntimeGraphManifest (sensor→perception→policy→queue→supervisor→actuator, and violation→stop paths, each with budget decomposition) validated at activation; keep domain-level values only as defaults for admission when a graph declares nothing stricter. Also rename — the object declares requirements, not guarantees, and 'guarantee' invites exactly the claims-without-evidence anti-pattern the pack polices.
9. **ODD is an opaque ref; no typed ODD object or exit monitors** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md:1710 — 'operational design domain, hazards, timing/fault assumptions, monitor and'; the wire field is operational_design_domain_ref: artifact://... | policy://... (common-objects-and-envelopes.md:6155)
   - Recommendation: ISO 34503 / BSI PAS 1883 give measurable ODD taxonomies; here the ODD is an artifact://|policy:// ref inside the assurance case, invisible to the runtime. Add a typed OperationalDesignDomain object — environmental attributes (illumination, weather, surface, human density, GNSS/localization quality) each with measurable bounds and a monitoring source ref — bound to PhysicalZones and EnvironmentState. Require odd_exit as a first-class monitor class in the runtime_assurance stratum and as an EmbodiedIncident trigger (:1744-1747 has no such trigger), so leaving the validated envelope forces the declared mode degradation rather than remaining an unreviewable document assumption.
10. **No reference behavior-execution semantics between envelope and action chunks** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md:187 — 'References GoalRun, workflow, skill, policy, and supervision owners without taking over their state.'
   - Recommendation: On-unit sequencing — which contingency preempts which skill, what happens when a chunk expires mid-grasp, recovery-path ordering, where human interventions insert — is scattered across opaque fallback_and_interruption_policy_ref policies with no execution model, while the behavior graph remains a doc-level 'view' deferring to slow-plane owners. Nav2/BehaviorTree.CPP standardize exactly this with tick/halt/priority semantics. Define one reference BehaviorExecutionContract (tick rate, preemption ordering, contingency priority classes, halt-and-recover semantics, intervention insertion points) as an autonomy-stratum component kind so authored behavior graphs compile to defined reactive semantics; without it, two conforming implementations will sequence the same admitted mission differently.
11. **'Validate schedules and WCET budgets' hides an unbuilt hard problem** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md:279 — '-> validate schemas, frames, units, clocks, QoS, schedules, and WCET budgets'
   - Recommendation: Cross-placement schedulability analysis over heterogeneous MCU/edge/site partitions is research-grade, and WCET for GPU perception is practically unobtainable — as written, activation either blocks on vaporware analysis or rubber-stamps opaque static_analysis_and_schedule_evidence_refs. Tier the admission evidence by criticality and say so in the conformance checks: analytic response-time/schedulability analysis required only for deterministic_hard_realtime and independent_safety partitions on RTOS-class placements; measured p99.9 latency conformance against declared budgets (with regression receipts) suffices for bounded_soft_realtime autonomy components. Naming the evidence class per criticality tells a builder what 'validate' actually means and keeps the fail-closed gate buildable.
12. **Physical action-kind enum drifts across objects in the same file** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md:858 — '- navigation | inspection' vs :399-400 — 'navigation | manipulation | facility_control | access_control | sensor_capture | inspection | emergency_stop_test' vs :1853 — '- sensor_override' (defined only in foundations/physical-action-safety.md:182)
   - Recommendation: Unit identity, actuator/sensor registries, zones, resource groups, and fleet policy each list a different hand-rolled subset of the action-kind axis. FleetPolicy forbids 'sensor_override', a kind appearing nowhere else in this file; it is defined in physical-action-safety.md's PhysicalActionIntent.action_kind (:182), whose enum itself diverges from every embodied list (it lacks sensor_capture and inspection, while the embodied lists lack tool_use, drone_flight, and vehicle_adjacent). Queue admission compares shared actuators, zones, and safety envelopes (:1480-1481), and FleetPolicy's mutually_exclusive_action_kinds (:1840-1841) operates directly on action kinds, so divergent vocabularies weaken the fleet-level conflict checks. Move one PhysicalActionKind enum into foundations/canonical-enums.md — the pack's designated owner for shared values (README.md:30), which currently defines no such enum — reference it from physical-action-safety.md and every embodied object, and mark per-object lists as explicit constrained subsets of that enum.
13. **PhysicalMissionControlEnvelope needs a disclaimer paragraph to survive its own taxonomy** — `low` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md:1046 — 'The domain term *mission* in this envelope names the slow-plane physical safety/control scope, not a generic `HypervisorMission` record.'
   - Recommendation: When canon must disclaim a name inline, rename it. 'Mission' now also names a product surface (Missions over OutcomeRooms) and a retired generic HypervisorMission record, so PhysicalMissionControlEnvelope, FleetMissionCoordinationRecord, and FleetMissionAllocationLease collide three ways for every new reader. Note this collision is a deliberate, recent decision, not an oversight: README.md Non-Negotiable 33 explicitly states 'Typed physical mission contracts remain canonical', so this rename argues against that decision and would require amending NN33 and the Missions-taxonomy convergence alongside this file. If accepted: rename to PhysicalControlEnvelope, FleetWorkCoordinationRecord, and FleetWorkAllocationLease — 'work' already matches the TypedWorkSubjectBinding these objects carry — and delete the disclaimer paragraph. Low mechanical cost now (nothing is implemented); permanent vocabulary tax later.
14. **One 2,188-line owner for ~25 subjects is a drift engine** — `low` · Refactor
   - Evidence: components/daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md:11 — 'queues, spacetime reservations, fleet policy, recovery, and operator handoff.' — the end of an eight-line ownership list
   - Recommendation: This file owns identity, resource groups, graph compilation, world model, the two-speed contract, supervisor/bridge, fleet coordination, telemetry/replay, sim-to-real, deployment assurance, incidents, and fleet policy; its conformance checklist alone runs ~140 lines. Every alignment pass must touch all of it, and unrelated edits will silently skew the safety-critical sections. Split into four owners under the existing daemon-runtime pattern — embodied-identity-and-resources.md, embodied-execution-and-supervision.md (profiles, strata, manifests, activation, supervisor, two-speed), embodied-fleet-coordination.md, embodied-evidence-and-lifecycle.md — keeping this file as the domain index, and update _meta/source-of-truth-map.md so single-ownership per subject is preserved.


## Part III.11 — Hypervisor product surfaces

### Core clients & surfaces: shell taxonomy, applications, packages, sessions

**Files reviewed:** `components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md (4398) — read fully in chunks`

**Current shape.** The file is the canonical owner for Hypervisor's client/surface estate (with Improvement, Evaluations, and Foundry owner detail delegated to sibling docs it links). First-class clients (App, Web, CLI/headless, SDK/ADK/ODK) sit over one Hypervisor Core; the default shell is five core workspaces (Home, Systems, Projects, Applications, Work) plus Automations as an owner application with permanent shell placement and a singular "Open Application" slot. Every other durable surface must take exactly one of four registration classes — twelve baseline owner applications (Studio, Automations, Ontology, Data, Governance, Provenance, Evaluations, Improvement, Foundry, Packages, Developer Workspace, Developer Console) plus a conditional planned Embodied Systems owner registration, two substrate applications (Environments, Operations), owner-bound tool surfaces, and extension applications — with eleven deliberately independent axes (publisher, origin, creation method, distribution, availability, admission, installation, package disposition, enablement, capability depth, operational state). A normalized record family (stable registration → immutable release → installation binding → System-interface binding → serving binding, plus typed fail-closed route aliases) feeds one product-surface compiler that produces all navigation/catalog/command-palette/search projections, policy-filtered before caching and failing safe to static first-party inventory. Packages is the mandatory local lifecycle owner (Marketplace an optional mode); recall/revocation removes launch eligibility immediately but never silently mutates live Systems. Sessions are bounded interactive/headless/supervisory execution contexts; the file also specifies external harness adapters down to a six-step daemon admission chain with concrete codex argv/PTY-attach contracts, local-agent pairing, environment ops, and the retirement of generic Mission ids via typed fail-closed legacy aliases. All surfaces are projections; daemon/wallet.network/Agentgres retain truth.

**What it already gets right:**

- The registration/release/installation/System-binding/serving normalization with explicit join invariants (core-clients-surfaces.md:3382-3419) is genuinely stronger than shipping SOTA: VS Code Marketplace and Chrome Web Store conflate listing, admission, and package disposition, and while both have remote kill switches, those hard-disable installed code; here recall is receipted, carries typed affected-install/System impact, and is explicitly forbidden from silently mutating live Systems.
- The migration story is unusually disciplined: typed fail-closed route aliases that preserve query/hash/embed/back-stack context, and Mission retirement through HypervisorLegacyWorkSubjectAlias with needs_review/rejected states instead of a lossy redirect.
- The shell resists nav sprawl by construction: five core workspaces, one Open Application slot, query-first catalog, and an explicit rule against promoting platform primitives (models, connectors, receipts) into permanent rail — the correct lesson from Backstage and Palantir Foundry scale products.
- Candor about weak isolation: 'Docker and Podman are execution lanes, not privacy guarantees' and prompt-only local agents labeled proposal-only/low-assurance is above industry norm.
- Degraded-mode behavior is specified: partial daemon/preference failure preserves safe static first-party inventory without leaking cached org/user/System state.

**SOTA verdict: `near-sota`** — compared against: VS Code workbench + extension host (process isolation, engines.vscode semver compatibility, contribution points); Figma plugin sandbox (iframe + realm/Wasm isolation, typed plugin API bridge); MCP Apps / OpenAI Apps SDK pattern (iframe + postMessage for tool-rendered UI); Backstage plugin architecture (build-time plugin compilation, versioned core plugin API); Chrome Web Store / VS Code Marketplace admission economics (publisher verification tiers, automated scanning, permission-diff re-review on update); Raycast / command-palette contribution models; Electron security baselines (contextIsolation, sandboxed renderers, CSP); Micro-frontend composition (module federation, single-spa)

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No specified UI isolation mechanism for extension_application code — 'sandboxed' is asserted here and in the connectors-tools owner docs, never mechanized (no iframe/Wasm/process contract, no bridge schema, no CSP baseline), where Figma, VS Code, and MCP Apps all specify one
- No host-API/engine version compatibility field on releases (VS Code engines, Backstage core-plugin-api versions), so shell evolution vs installed extensions is unmanaged
- No contribution-point model: an extension can register as a launchable surface (including in the command palette), but cannot contribute typed commands, context widgets, or object-detail tabs into owner surfaces the way VS Code contribution points or Raycast commands allow
- No publisher trust tiers, automated scanning, or permission-escalation re-review in the admission ladder (publisher admission and release evidence_refs exist, but no tiering or re-review triggers)
- No performance/caching/invalidation model for the single product-surface compiler that must serve every navigation render

**Findings (15, severity-ordered):**

1. **Extension UI sandboxing is asserted but never mechanized — a security hole at the shell boundary** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md:1587 — 'An imported MCP App is a sandboxed extension surface, not a new runtime or truth owner.'
   - Recommendation: Add a SurfaceRuntimeContract to HypervisorSurfaceReleaseRecord that names the isolation lane (sandboxed iframe with locked-down CSP, Wasm realm, or separate process a la VS Code extension host), a typed postMessage/RPC bridge compiled from the release's declared action_contract_refs (the only APIs extension JS receives), and forbids DOM/shell-global access. Without this, all the authority-side rigor (daemon admission, MCP gateway) is bypassable by extension JS reading the shell's DOM, tokens, or sibling-surface data. Verified pack-wide: README Non-Negotiable 13 and connectors-tools/doctrine.md:178 / contracts.md:247 also say only 'sandboxed' with no mechanism, so no linked owner doc closes this. Figma (iframe+realm), MCP Apps (iframe+postMessage), and Electron contextIsolation are the anchors; today the word 'sandboxed' carries the entire client-side security story.
2. **Product-surface compiler demands both caching and instant revocation with no invalidation mechanism** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md:1774-1777 — 'Policy filtering occurs before aggregation and caching. … Disable, recall, and revocation remove launch eligibility immediately.'
   - Recommendation: Policy-before-cache means cache entries are per-principal-per-context (request_context_hash), so 'immediately' plus caching needs an invalidation contract the doc never states. Add a monotonic catalog_epoch (like Agentgres state roots or Kubernetes resourceVersion) bumped on any admission/enablement/recall/policy change; HypervisorProductSurfaceProjection carries the epoch and clients revalidate by cheap epoch compare. Also state the layering explicitly: static registration layer compiled once per release (VS Code contribution model), dynamic eligibility joined per request — as written, every nav render is a seven-record policy join with no performance doctrine on the shell's hottest path. Note the exposure is bounded rather than a full bypass: effectful work independently crosses fail-closed daemon admission and effectful System launches require an admitted System-interface binding, so a stale cached row yields stale catalog visibility and a failed launch, not ungoverned effects — which is why this is a spec-precision and performance gap, not a critical hole.
3. **One 4398-line file owns taxonomy, compiler, sessions, pairing, PTY mechanics — a drift engine with one alignment date** — `medium` · Refactor *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md:2697 — 'codex --oss --local-provider ollama' (implementation-grade argv inside the product-taxonomy owner doc)
   - Recommendation: Split by owner altitude: (1) shell taxonomy + registration/compiler contract stays here; (2) the harness admission chain (HarnessSessionLaunch/Spawn/Readiness/TerminalAttach, container lane plans, codex argv, lines ~2655-2830) moves to daemon-runtime/default-harness-profile.md or api.md, which already own harness execution; (3) the composition-primitive and surface-mode inventories become a design-system doc. The Connect-local-agent section is already partially delegated — lines 571-576 name common-objects-and-envelopes.md for the schema, identity-access-and-metering.md for lifecycle, and connectors-tools/contracts.md for gateway binding — so what remains here is product-boundary prose and can stay, but the daemon-grade PTY/argv contracts have no such delegation. A product-IA doc that also freezes PTY custody semantics guarantees the two audiences (product, daemon implementers) desynchronize; the stale reference wall (see supported_surfaces finding) is proof it already happens.
4. **HypervisorClient.supported_surfaces still lists retired taxonomy-v1 surfaces the same file bans from the shell** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md:3079-3083 — '- agents - services - models - privacy - receipts' vs :642-643 — 'Avoid turning platform primitives into permanent top-level nav. Agents, Workers, Models, Connectors, MCP servers, Ontology, Policies, Receipts…'
   - Recommendation: Regenerate the enum to the v2 estate: the five core workspace kinds plus surface://… refs for owner/substrate applications ('receipts' is now Provenance, 'models' lives under Foundry, 'agents' has no surface identity at all). Better: replace the hard-coded list with supported_workspace_kinds + supported_surface_classes and let the compiler resolve concrete surfaces, so client capability declarations can never re-fossilize a retired taxonomy. The reference-wall notice (lines 3050-3057) does disclaim doctrine authority for this section, which softens the contradiction — but the same notice instructs 'Do not narrow it,' which actively entrenches the retired list until it is regenerated.
5. **No host-API/engine compatibility contract on extension releases** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md:3214 — 'dependency_release_refs: [package://.../release/...]' (peer packages only; no shell/host compatibility range)
   - Recommendation: Add required_host_contract_range (semver or contract-revision refs against a named, versioned Hypervisor Surface Host API) to HypervisorSurfaceReleaseRecord, and make the compiler emit a typed disabled_reason_code host_incompatible (the current disabled_reason_codes enum at :3362-3365 has no such value). VS Code (engines.vscode), Backstage (core-plugin-api versions), and Figma (plugin API versions) all learned that an extension estate without declared host compatibility turns every shell upgrade into silent breakage; for a canon that promises recall/impact analysis per release, omitting the one dependency every extension shares (the shell itself) is the odd gap.
6. **Extensions are whole-frame apps only — no contribution-point model for commands, widgets, or menus** — `medium` · SOTA gap *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md:1758-1759 — 'Navigation, Applications, command-palette, contextual launch, search, recent, favorite, and recommendation projections must be produced from one compiler'
   - Recommendation: The command palette is compiler output and an extension can register launch_modes including command_palette, Developer Console names 'function, widget, and extension registries' (:1213-1214), and extensions compile into the same composition contract as first-party surfaces — but nothing lets an admitted extension contribute a typed command, a context-panel widget, or an object-detail tab into an owner surface. Add a declared-contribution block to HypervisorSurfaceReleaseRecord (contribution kinds: command, object_view_tab, context_widget, palette_action) bound to the same object/action contract ceilings and policy filtering. VS Code contribution points and Raycast commands show this is where extension ecosystems actually get their leverage; whole-surface-only extensibility reproduces the weakest part of the Backstage v1 model and undercuts the 'tools for this context' catalog group.
7. **Two overlapping capability axes: effect_boundary vs surface_capability_depth — and depth is self-declared** — `medium` · Better way *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md:3182 — 'effect_boundary: inspect_only | propose_only | effectful' vs :3206-3207 — 'surface_capability_depth: browse | inspect | propose | act | workflow_complete'
   - Recommendation: Both ladders encode 'how consequential can this surface be' with overlapping rungs (inspect, propose). Keep one declared ceiling (effect_boundary on the registration) and make capability depth a derived, evidence-backed value: browse/inspect from read contracts, propose from proposal contracts, act only when the release binds ≥1 effectful action contract with an admitted receipted execution, workflow_complete from a verified end-to-end path. canonical-enums.md:358-360 already demands this evidence ('act requires at least one real, admitted, receipted mutation'), so depth is doctrine-verifiable today — the field is just still declared rather than compiled. Caveat: capability depth is one of the independent axes mandated verbatim by README Non-Negotiable 35 and this file's own conformance checks, so deriving it is an explicit amendment to that invariant (justified here: a verifiable value should not be a declarable axis), not a compliant tweak. Deriving depth removes an axis clients must render and prevents the maturity inflation canonical-enums itself warns about.
8. **Developer Workspace vs Developer Console — near-identical names for disjoint jobs** — `medium` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md:690-693 — 'Developer Workspace (code, files, terminal, ports inside environments…)' / 'Developer Console (connectors, MCP, APIs, OAuth clients, SDK on-ramps, conformance —'
   - Recommendation: Two of twelve owner applications share the 'Developer' prefix while doing unrelated jobs (IDE vs integration registry); every user test of similar pairs (GCP 'Console' vs 'Cloud Shell', Azure portal blades) shows constant misnavigation, and search/palette results will interleave them. Rename Developer Console to Integrations (or restore its own alias Connections, core-clients-surfaces.md:738 — '/__ioi/connections'): the doc already describes it as the 'extension surface' for connectors/connected apps, and 'integration surfaces' is the term the Admin/builder vocabulary layer (line 241) already uses. Keep developer-console as a route alias.
9. **session_kind flattens placement, interface, and purpose into one enum, violating the file's own axis-independence doctrine** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md:3536-3540 — 'local_workspace | remote_vm_workspace | browser_sandbox | hosted_worker | hypervisoros_node | terminal | editor | computer_use | foundry_eval_training | provider_management | environment_management'
   - Recommendation: local_workspace/remote_vm_workspace/hypervisoros_node are placements (already owned by the session's environment_class_ref, line 3562, whose class_kind at :3620-3623 duplicates them); terminal/editor/computer_use are interface kinds; foundry_eval_training/provider_management are workload purposes. A 'computer-use session on a remote VM doing eval work' has no legal value. Apply the file's own medicine ('The following dimensions remain independent', line 1595): replace session_kind with interface_kind (terminal|editor|browser|computer_use|api) + workload_kind (general|eval_training|provider_management|environment_management), and derive placement from environment_class_ref (with a declared local/none default, since environment_class_ref is nullable for environment-less sessions). This also stops the enum growing a new value for every future substrate.
10. **Routing Work reviews/incidents on {facet_projection_id} makes URLs unstable and unshareable** — `medium` · Better way *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md:1519-1520 — '`/work/reviews/{facet_projection_id}`, and `/work/incidents/{facet_projection_id}`' vs :3915 — 'facet_ref: string # canonical typed ref owned by the source domain'
   - Recommendation: HypervisorWorkFacetProjection is a policy-filtered read_model_only pointer; nothing guarantees its projection id survives recomputation or policy changes, or is stable across users, so bookmarks, notifications, receipts, and cross-user links to a review/incident detail can break. The doc itself says (line 1517) typed details resolve 'by canonical subject identity' — then routes on a read-model id, an internal tension. Route on the canonical typed facet_ref the projection already carries (encoded, e.g. /work/reviews/{facet_kind}/{urlencoded_facet_ref}) and resolve to a fresh projection at request time — same pattern the file already mandates for legacy Mission aliases (typed resolver, fail closed). Projection ids stay internal.
11. **Eleven 'independent' axes exceed any shipped platform's classification budget and tax every client** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md:1540-1543 — 'Publisher identity, origin, creation method, distribution channel, availability, admission state, installation state, package disposition, enablement state, capability depth, and operational state are independent dimensions'
   - Recommendation: VS Code ships a world-scale extension ecosystem on roughly four user-visible axes (installed, enabled, compatible, running). Here every catalog row, conformance check, and disabled_reason_code path must reason over eleven, and the projection schema (lines 3315-3345) forces all of them nullable onto every entry. Be explicit that this is an invariant-change proposal: the eleven axes are mandated verbatim by README Non-Negotiable 35 and this file's conformance checks, so any trim must amend that invariant, with the argument that a smaller axis set survives client implementations better. Concretely: derive capability_depth (see separate finding); demote surface_creation_method to release evidence/conformance-profile refs — the enums doc justifies it only by differing admission and conformance obligations, which named conformance profiles express better than a permanent axis. On `planned` in end-user catalogs (disabled_reason_codes includes 'planned', line 3363): this is a deliberate design for the Embodied Systems registration (canonical-enums allows planned registrations roadmap and dependency inspection), so the challenge is to a conscious choice — but the credibility cost of a permanently greyed tile in an end-user store is real and no SOTA store pays it; roadmap inspection could live in an admin/roadmap view instead.
12. **The hand-maintained 1000-line YAML reference wall is already drifting; generation must be canon, not a TODO** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md:3056-3057 — 'Generator TODO: emit this section from the daemon schema registry instead of maintaining it by hand.'
   - Recommendation: The wall's staleness is demonstrated in-file (supported_surfaces contradiction). Either (a) promote generation to a conformance check — the architecture-docs gate fails when the wall diverges from the daemon schema registry — or (b) cut the wall to the ~8 doctrine-bearing shapes this file actually owns (registration family, projection, route alias, legacy alias) and move session/environment/work-run object walls to their runtime owner docs with pointers. A 'reference-wall notice' disclaiming authority while sitting inside the canonical authority file is exactly the ambiguity the pack's source-of-truth-map exists to prevent.
13. **Canvas's permitted hosts disagree three ways within the same file** — `low` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md:1219 — 'visual builder/editor inside Studio, Automations, Developer Workspace, or Foundry where useful' vs :2180 — 'Canvas may appear inside Automations, Developer Workspace, or Foundry.' and :3523 — 'owner_surface: automations | developer_workspace | foundry'
   - Recommendation: The owner-application block includes Studio as a Canvas host; the Hypervisor Canvas section and the HypervisorCanvasView.owner_surface enum both omit it — yet Studio is the 'typed canvas over real substrate objects' (line 1120) and Surface Generate lives there, so Studio is the most likely Canvas consumer. Pick one truth: add `studio` to the owner_surface enum and to line 2180, or strike Studio from line 1219 and say Studio has its own native canvas that is not Hypervisor Canvas. Also note line 99 says only 'Canvas visually edits automations', a third narrower scope.
14. **tool_surface collides with RuntimeToolContract/MCP 'tools' in a doc where both are load-bearing** — `low` · Rename
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md:3152-3155 — 'tool_kind: editor | inspector | picker | inbox | graph | wizard | report | dashboard | comparison | console'
   - Recommendation: In the same file, 'tool' means an agent-callable capability (RuntimeToolContract, tool:// refs, 'tool/MCP nodes', MCP gateway tools) and a class of owner-bound UI panels (tool_surface, tool_kind, 'Tool / Function Builder'). Sentences like a registration declaring 'operator-plane tool or MCP contract refs' next to 'tool registrations' force readers to disambiguate constantly. Rename the UI class to panel_surface or utility_surface (tool_kind → panel_kind), keeping tool:// exclusively for callable capabilities. This is a mechanical rename with high comprehension payoff in a pack that is otherwise obsessive about name collisions (Mission, Recipe, Workbench) — it does touch canonical-enums.md and Non-Negotiable 35's 'surface class' axis values, so it lands as a coordinated enum rename, not a local edit.
15. **Admission ladder has states but no review economics: no publisher tiers, scanning, or permission-diff re-review** — `low` · SOTA gap *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/components/hypervisor/core-clients-surfaces.md:1613-1614 — 'surface_admission_state: not_applicable | candidate | under_review | admitted | rejected | revoked'
   - Recommendation: under_review is a state with limited contract behind it. Raw material exists — releases carry admission_decision_ref and evidence_refs, and an external publisher is itself admitted (ioi://publisher/... = 'an admitted external publisher') — but nothing specifies what re-triggers review. Chrome Web Store and VS Code Marketplace both learned that update-time permission escalation is the main supply-chain attack, so add: (a) publisher assurance tiers on ioi://publisher refs (unverified/verified/audited) surfaced in the catalog; (b) an admission rule that a successor release widening object/action contract ceilings relative to its predecessor re-enters under_review rather than inheriting admitted (the join invariants only bound each release against the definition ceiling, not against its predecessor); (c) required automated conformance/scan evidence_refs on the release. The join-invariant machinery (release ceilings ⊇ install ⊇ System binding) is already the perfect place to hang (b).

### Providers & environments + BYO provider plane + identity/access/metering

**Files reviewed:** `components/hypervisor/providers-and-environments.md (1810)` · `components/hypervisor/byo-provider-plane.md (319)` · `components/hypervisor/identity-access-and-metering.md (376)`

**Current shape.** The facet defines Hypervisor as the single governed control plane for sessions, environments, and providers: environments are VM-like governed units behind sessions, providers are cross-session resources, the daemon executes lifecycle, wallet.network authorizes consequential crossings, Agentgres records admitted truth, and provider state is always evidence, never truth. providers-and-environments.md canonizes roughly 55 environment-ops objects — development-environment recipes and resolutions, readiness gates, isolation/connectivity/substrate profiles, a component-phased status projection streamed over SSE, append-only lifecycle observations, provider-failure incidents with recovery candidates/attempts and per-WorkRun effect-recovery classes, capacity pools/budgets/allocation decisions, and a full Change Plane (target/observed state, change plans, release channels, canary, recall, SBOM/deployed-where evidence). byo-provider-plane.md is the implemented ProviderAccount plane: four user-facing placement venues, sealed credential binding riding the CapabilityLease spine, budget-discovery-before-provider-mutation plus a per-operation wallet grant, receipts for success and refusal, daemon-custody snapshot/restore validated against admitted sha256 state roots, and nine adapter lanes (SSH, Vast, RunPod, Lambda, Akash, AWS, GCP, Azure, plus a k8s lane the status header scopes to credential+preflight while the lane table describes a full cluster adapter) that preserve provider-real billing semantics rather than a flattened generic cloud. Optimized placement is delegated to an optional decentralized.cloud candidate plane with an explicit no_eligible_candidate → run_local fallback. identity-access-and-metering.md defines the deployment-local identity plane — principals (admin|member), sessions, OIDC SSO with full id_token verification, SCIM 2.0, invites, domain verification, inbound API tokens, sealed secrets, and local-agent pairing — under the strict rule that identity and roles never authorize a crossing; metering is a coarse receipt-derived OCU projection with a wallet-backed auto-funding budget, with invoice-grade reconciliation explicitly planned, not claimed.

**What it already gets right:**
