- The per-WorkRun effect-recovery-class taxonomy (replayable | checkpointable | compensatable | reconciliation_required | non_retryable) and the explicit split between environment recovery and external-outcome recovery (providers-and-environments.md:928-943) is genuinely ahead of SkyPilot/Crossplane-class systems, which model infrastructure recovery but not effect ambiguity; it imports Temporal/saga semantics into infrastructure canon correctly.
- Provider-semantics honesty is stronger than industry norm: adapters must preserve real billing/interruption/custody semantics (Azure stop DEALLOCATES, EBS bills after stop, Akash leases bill until CLOSED), fail closed with named gate codes, and never simulate a cloud (byo-provider-plane.md:170-199). Most multi-cloud abstractions actively hide these differences.
- The identity/authority layering doctrine is clean and correctly stated: roles only narrow, never grant; a wallet-assigned crossing 403s even for an authenticated admin (identity-access-and-metering.md:50-59); OIDC verification detail (JWKS signature, issuer/audience/expiry, per-login nonce) is specific and correct; provider secrets are sealed, daemon-held, and never reach agents (byo-provider-plane.md:253-260).
- Budget-discovery-before-provider-mutation with receipted refusals (byo-provider-plane.md:264-270, 273-280) inverts the usual cloud pattern where budget alarms lag spend; refusals being receipted is rare and valuable for audit.
- Fee legitimacy bound to receipts — RoutingDecisionReceipt minted only when routing/procurement value is actually delivered, no percentage fee on self-managed BYO spend (byo-provider-plane.md:24-32) — is a credible, checkable answer to the hidden-markup problem that GPU brokers and PaaS resellers routinely fail.

**SOTA verdict: `behind`** — compared against: SkyPilot (typed multi-cloud resource specs, price/availability-optimized placement, managed spot jobs with checkpoint-recover, quota-error failover); Karpenter (capacity/quota-aware provisioning, consolidation/bin-packing); Crossplane/Terraform providers (declarative reconciliation as truth — IOI deliberately rejects this; divergence is defensible for agent workloads); Devcontainers/Gitpod/Codespaces (the environment status object is an explicit mapping from these shapes, providers-and-environments.md:544-563); E2B/Modal/Fly Machines (microVM sandbox lanes — IOI's substrate policy matches their isolation doctrine); SPIFFE/SPIRE and OIDC workload federation (GCP lane uses oidc-workload; AWS/Azure lanes still use sealed static credentials); FinOps FOCUS billing spec (no named export/ingest format for SpendReceipt/pse_* rows); EC2 Spot interruption notices / GCP preemptible semantics (unmodeled)

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No spot/preemptible market strategy at all: 'spot' never appears; capacity_eviction is only a failure kind, never a priced, planned-for placement input with checkpoint cadence and auto re-placement (SkyPilot managed jobs).
- Typed placement request is unbound: cloud.md's CloudResourceIntent (compute/GPU/storage/network requirements, custody, privacy, region, budget refs) exists in the optional decentralized.cloud plane, but it is never echoed into PlacementDecision, the venue contract, or the placement receipt in this facet's files, and it carries no price ceiling or interruption tolerance — so 'Let Hypervisor choose' still has no challengeable contract for what was asked.
- No provider quota discovery/preflight: quota_exhausted exists as a decision reason but nothing syncs AWS Service Quotas/GCP regional quotas into HypervisorResourcePool before placement; Karpenter/SkyPilot treat quota as a first-class failover trigger.
- No typed workload-identity object: node attestation exists as doctrine flow in runtime-nodes-tee-depin.md (TEE flow, attestation receipts), but node/environment/agent-service identity in this facet is one unspecified join-sequence line; SPIFFE/SPIRE-class unified attested identity is absent.
- Long-lived static cloud credentials (aws-sigv4 sealed keys, azure service-principal secret) where SOTA is short-lived federated credentials (AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity, IAM Roles Anywhere, Azure federated credentials).
- No FOCUS-conformant cost export or provider cost-report (CUR/billing-export) ingestion named for reconciliation.
- Counterweight: failure semantics (effect-recovery classes, incident→candidate→attempt spine) and billing honesty exceed the named SOTA; the deficit is concentrated in the optimizer core the facet angle asks about.

**Findings (16, severity-ordered):**

1. **Spot/preemptible capacity has no strategy object — eviction is only a failure, never a plan** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/byo-provider-plane.md:216 — 'preserve offer/ask, availability, interruption, data-movement, and custody semantics'
   - Recommendation: The word 'spot' appears nowhere in either file; providers-and-environments.md:979 lists capacity_eviction only as a failure_kind. For a stack whose adapter ladder is dominated by interruptible GPU markets (Vast, Akash, RunPod community tier), define a HypervisorInterruptionPolicy: interruption_tolerance on the allocation request, eviction-probability evidence on candidates, checkpoint cadence derived from the WorkRun's declared recovery class, and automatic re-placement via the existing HypervisorEnvironmentRecoveryCandidate failover_provider mode. This is SkyPilot managed-jobs / EC2 interruption-notice territory and it composes cleanly with objects the canon already has (the capacity plane's preempt/checkpoint_and_pause/shift_provider machinery handles internal pressure but never provider-side eviction as a priced input) — it is the cheapest big win in this facet.
2. **Wallet challenges bind (account, op, environment) but not the requesting principal — the identity/authority seam is unbound** — `high` · Better way
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/byo-provider-plane.md:269 — 'request facets (account, op, environment), lease descriptor persisted without secrets.'
   - Recommendation: The three identity planes (principals, wallet authority, provider credentials) are cleanly layered doctrinally, but nothing binds a wallet grant to the principal who requested it: identity-access-and-metering.md:96-101 makes principal-scoped leases a separate optional ring, and receipts attribute the principal only after the fact. Add principal_ref to the wallet challenge request facets and to the persisted lease descriptor, plus a composition-order rule that a lease minted under one principal is unusable by another authenticated principal. This informs and pins — it does not make identity grant authority, so the roles-never-authorize invariant is preserved. Without it, the wallet approver cannot see who is asking, and lease replay across principals inside one deployment is undefined rather than refused.
3. **Typed placement request exists only in the optional candidate plane — PlacementDecision and the venue contract never echo what was asked** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/byo-provider-plane.md:112 — 'why this substrate was selected'
   - Recommendation: PlacementDecision is defined only as a prose 'why', and providers-and-environments.md:177-180 says placement 'must be driven by policy, authority, budget, privacy posture, receipts, and user preference' without binding a typed request in this facet. The typed shape does already exist in the linked candidate-plane doctrine: cloud.md's CloudResourceIntent carries compute/GPU/storage/network requirements, runtime_class, custody_posture, privacy requirements, region preferences, and budget refs, and HypervisorResourceAllocationRequest carries required_capabilities and privacy_and_residency_constraints. What is missing is the binding: neither object is echoed into PlacementDecision, the venue-policy/preview contract, or the placement receipt, and neither carries a price ceiling or interruption tolerance. Adopt CloudResourceIntent (or a PlacementConstraintSpec profile of it) as a required, verbatim-echoed facet of every PlacementDecision across ALL venues — not just optimized — so a placement receipt is challengeable against what was asked, matching the doc's own rule that routing fees are legitimate 'only when a challengeable routing or placement receipt is emitted'.
4. **providers-and-environments.md owns at least eight distinct planes — split the Change Plane and capacity plane out** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/providers-and-environments.md:4 — 'this file for Hypervisor-managed providers, environments, cross-session infrastructure posture, zero-to-idle, archive/restore posture, development environment recipes, lifecycle observability, and provider integration doctrine'
   - Recommendation: The file additionally owns a full release-management plane (HypervisorChangePlan, ReleaseChannel, MaintenanceWindow, CanaryRun, RecallPolicy, RemediationPlan, lines 1282-1541), supply-chain/SBOM evidence, capacity/allocation (lines 1111-1280), and autonomous-system membership (1076-1109). Split: (1) change-plane.md owning Change Plane + recall + DeployedWhereIndex/SBOM with its own status header; (2) move ResourcePool/Budget/AllocationRequest/Decision/CatchupPolicy to the Operations canon the text itself assigns them to ('Operations owns the cross-workload resource posture', line 1119). Single-owner-per-concern is this pack's own convention, and the pack has already executed exactly this split once (byo-provider-plane.md was carved out of this file with its own status header); one 1810-line file with one shared 'partial' status makes per-plane drift invisible.
5. **55 canonical objects with fully-fielded schemas under one 'partial' status is a drift engine** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/providers-and-environments.md:367 — 'Canonical environment ops objects:'
   - Recommendation: Lines 369-425 enumerate 55 objects; many get 20-40-field schemas (the recipe at 582-627 has ~40 fields) while implementation refs name only two route files and status is 'partial' (line 13). The pack already built the right mechanism in _meta/canon-to-code-delta.md (per-object built/partial/planned rows with honest 'not started' states), but that file's declared scope is the bounded-DAS / learning-boundary / collaborative-pursuit / federated-ontology object families — the environment-ops census is not covered by it today. Extend the same mechanism to this census (or add a per-plane implementation-state table here) and mark unbuilt schemas as target-shape with a named minimum viable field set. Otherwise every review cycle re-litigates whether ChangePlanGate or SuppressionWindow 'exists', and code that ships a subset silently diverges from a 40-field canonical shape nobody audits field-by-field.
6. **Azure is simultaneously a proven adapter lane and a 'future adapter class' whose account kind must not be presented** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/byo-provider-plane.md:243 — 'may name future adapter classes (clusters, Azure) before their account kinds exist'
   - Recommendation: Line 179 lists Azure as a proven lane (azure-service-principal, azure_* gates, IOI_AZURE_LIVE=1, a done-bar verifier), yet line 241 omits azure from admitted ProviderAccount kinds, line 5's status header omits it from the lane list, line 257's credential-kind list omits azure-service-principal, and line 296's metered-kinds list (vast | runpod | lambda_cloud | akash | aws | gcp) omits it too. Fix all four: add azure to the admitted kinds, status header, and metered-kinds list, add azure-service-principal to the CapabilityLease resolver kinds, and re-scope the line 243 caveat to clusters/k8s-lifecycle only. This is exactly the class of drift the pack's implementation-audit headers exist to catch; a reader auditing supported kinds today gets a wrong answer from the doc's own normative list.
7. **'Four placement choices' header maps to five economic modes, and the managed venue is missing from the venue-card contract** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/byo-provider-plane.md:40 — 'Users should see four placement choices, not provider-mode plumbing:'
   - Recommendation: The economics block (lines 51-84) lists five entries — Run local, Use my infrastructure, Pick a cloud, 'Hypervisor managed infrastructure / managed option' (line 73), Let Hypervisor choose — and GET /placement/venues (line 145) returns 'the four venue cards', leaving the managed mode with no UX home even though it is the placement_source 'managed' axis value (line 95). Either declare managed a fifth card, or state explicitly that managed capacity is reached through 'Pick a cloud' / 'Let Hypervisor choose' with IOI-as-provider-of-record, and update the venues contract text to match. The linked candidate-plane doctrine already supports the second resolution: cloud.md's CloudResourceIntent keeps user_placement_choice as exactly the four values with placement_source (connected | managed | optimized) as a separate axis. As written, this doc's product surface cannot express one of its own declared placement sources.
8. **no_eligible_candidate → run_local fallback is a silent capability downgrade, not a fail-closed decision** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/byo-provider-plane.md:154 — '`no_eligible_candidate` → `run_local` fallback.'
   - Recommendation: If a user delegated placement for an 8xA100 job and no candidate exists, falling back to the local machine changes GPU capability, isolation substrate, and custody posture without a stated constraint re-check — the placement analogue of the 'container started = environment ready' anti-pattern the pack forbids, and in tension with INV-14 honest-posture. Partial mitigations exist but are not bound to the fallback: GET /placement/preview names receipts_expected before launch, and EnvironmentClass honesty computes local enablement at read time — yet the fallback rule itself re-checks nothing and names no unmet constraint, and neither this file nor cloud.md states that run_local must satisfy the resolved environment class. Change the fallback to placement_blocked naming the unmet constraints (once the typed placement request is bound), with run_local admitted automatically only when local capacity satisfies the resolved environment class, substrate policy, and resource class; otherwise run_local is offered as an explicit user choice on the preview surface, receipted as a downgrade.
9. **Snapshot custody streams whole workspaces through the daemon — add a provider-native snapshot custody lane** — `medium` · Better way *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/byo-provider-plane.md:284 — '`snapshot` streams the remote workspace back into daemon custody'
   - Recommendation: Daemon-custody streaming is what makes restore-elsewhere work, but as the only lane it makes the daemon a data-plane chokepoint and generates hyperscaler egress cost that the pse_* spend plane never models (large model-cache workspaces make this real money per snapshot). providers-and-environments.md:779-780 already permits snapshot bytes in 'local disk, object storage, CAS/Filecoin, or provider storage'. Canonize a provider_native_snapshot custody mode: adapter triggers EBS/PD/managed-disk snapshot, daemon streams once to compute and admit the sha256 state root and content manifest but leaves bytes provider-side, and the receipt names the weaker custody posture (provider-custodial, not failover-portable) honestly. Also add snapshot egress to spend exposure rows.
10. **Static sealed cloud keys where SOTA is short-lived federated credentials** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/byo-provider-plane.md:257 — '`ssh-key` (new), `aws-sigv4`, `oidc-workload` (gcp), `bearer` (k8s/vast/akash)'
   - Recommendation: The plane already proves oidc-workload for GCP, but AWS binds long-lived SigV4 keys and Azure a service-principal secret. 2026 SOTA (and enterprise security review reality) is federation: AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity / IAM Roles Anywhere for AWS, workload-identity-federated credentials for Azure, with static keys as the explicit fallback kind. Extend the credential-kind table with aws-oidc-federation and azure-federated, have preflight verify the trust-policy binding, and have the ProviderAccount capabilities honestly label static-key accounts as lower posture. This removes the highest-value secret at rest in the whole facet and is a named enterprise-adoption gate ('no long-lived cloud keys' is a common procurement rule).
11. **No typed workload-identity object — node/environment/agent-service identity fragments stay disjoint** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/providers-and-environments.md:1084 — '-> establish node identity and attestation'
   - Recommendation: The join sequence names node identity and attestation with no object. Node attestation is covered doctrinally in the linked runtime-nodes-tee-depin.md (TEE flow: node produces attestation → wallet.network verifies measurement; attestation/execution receipts), and principals can be service-account/agent kinds — but there is no typed identity object, and identity-access-and-metering.md's principal sources are all human-shaped flows (local, sso, scim, invite); environment-resident agent services authenticate back to the daemon with what — an injected API access token? Define a SPIFFE/SPIRE-class workload identity object: attested node/environment identity document bound to the provisioning receipt and state root, used by agent services, runtime nodes, and the pairing candidate key path (which already has the right possession-proof shape at identity-access-and-metering.md:158-163). Make injected API tokens for services an anti-pattern. This unifies three currently disjoint machine-identity fragments: pairing candidate keys, node attestation, and service ingress auth.
12. **Wallet auto-funding replenishes real money against a knowingly coarse flat-rate meter** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/identity-access-and-metering.md:206 — 'flat `0.1` OCU per model-backed receipt. That is honest coarse telemetry, not'
   - Recommendation: Line 208 wires a 'wallet-backed auto-funding policy (replenish from wallet.network when the balance crosses a threshold)' to that same coarse OCU projection. Labeling the meter coarse is honest, but moving wallet funds automatically on fabricated-granularity units is an economic footgun: a receipt-storm (retry loop, chatty automation) drains a wallet at 0.1 OCU per receipt with no relation to supplier cost. Gate auto-funding on reconciled spend classes only (pse_* provider exposures, route-attempt receipts once built), or require a hard per-window replenishment cap plus a receipted operator approval above it. The conformance line 292 stops auto-funding from authorizing crossings but not from over-draining on bad telemetry.
13. **Provider quota is a failure reason, never a discovered pre-placement fact** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/providers-and-environments.md:1230 — 'budget_exhausted | quota_exhausted | rate_limited |'
   - Recommendation: HypervisorResourcePool carries quota_refs (line 1154) and allocation decisions can say quota_exhausted, but nothing states that provider quotas are discovered, synced, or preflighted — so quota surfaces only as a post-mutation provider error. SkyPilot and Karpenter treat vCPU/GPU regional quota as a first-class placement input and failover trigger. Extend the existing ProviderAccount preflight (byo-provider-plane.md:240-250) to fetch provider quota/limit evidence (AWS Service Quotas, GCP regional quotas; the k8s lane already reads namespace quota) into ResourcePool rows, and mirror the 'budget discovery BEFORE provider mutation' rule as 'quota discovery before placement' so quota exhaustion fails closed pre-mutation with a named provider/region/limit.
14. **'Type 1 / Type 2 / Type 3 substrate modes' collide with the canonical hypervisor Type-1/Type-2 taxonomy — in a product named Hypervisor** — `medium` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/providers-and-environments.md:45 — 'Environments are the bridge between Hypervisor\'s Type 1, Type 2, and Type 3'
   - Recommendation: In industry, Type 1/Type 2 hypervisor means bare-metal vs hosted VMM (Popek-Goldberg lineage). Here Type 1 = capacity supply, Type 2 = hosted developer environments, Type 3 = autonomy (lines 66-69) — unrelated meanings a virtualization-literate reader will confidently misread, and the product name maximizes the collision (their 'Type 1' governs bare-metal, adjacent to but not meaning 'Type 1 hypervisor'). The fix is smaller than a rename: _meta/vocabulary.md's HypervisorSubstrateMode entry already pairs each mode with a descriptive machine name (type1_hypervisoros | type2_desktop_workstation | type3_autonomy_plane) — lead with those descriptive names (or capacity/workspace/autonomy posture) in prose and keep 'Type N' as the parenthetical alias. This term also appears in README Non-Negotiable #2, so it needs a vocabulary.md decision and a pack-wide alias pass rather than a local edit — but the collision damages exactly the infrastructure-buyer audience this facet addresses.
15. **SCIM Groups are provisioned but semantically dangling** — `low` · Refactor
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/identity-access-and-metering.md:88 — 'provision/deprovision Users (mapped onto principals) and Groups, authenticated'
   - Recommendation: Groups are ingested via SCIM but no sentence anywhere assigns them meaning: they map to no role, no principal-scoped lease bundle, no surface access, no project membership. An IdP administrator pushing groups will assume group-based access works — a silent-expectation failure. Either (a) bind Groups explicitly: a Group may carry principal-scoped (connector, tool) lease-grant bundles and org-surface scoping, applied on membership change with receipts (still never machine authority, consistent with the roles-only-narrow rule), or (b) add one line stating Groups are recorded-only with no access semantics, plus a conformance check. Option (b) is one sentence and removes the trap today; (a) is the eventual enterprise need.
16. **'Lease' is five different objects across this facet — reserve the bare word** — `low` · Rename
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/providers-and-environments.md:108 — 'session access leases'
   - Recommendation: Within these three files 'lease' means: wallet CapabilityLease (identity-access-and-metering.md:96-101), HypervisorSessionAccessLease (providers-and-environments.md:108), IP leases (line 228), Akash provider leases (byo-provider-plane.md:176), and participant leases in pairing (identity-access-and-metering.md:170-173). The TTL/revocation semantics are intentionally shared, but the authority weight differs enormously — one grants crossings, another is a DHCP-adjacent provider artifact. _meta/vocabulary.md already defines CapabilityLease but has no reservation rule for the bare word. Add one there: bare 'lease' is reserved for wallet CapabilityLease; every other use must be a qualified compound (session-access lease, provider lease, participant lease), and audit these files for unqualified uses. Cheap change that prevents the worst misreading (provider lease ≈ authority).

### Foundry (tuning/derived capability) + Evaluations

**Files reviewed:** `components/hypervisor/foundry.md (2282) — read fully in chunks` · `components/hypervisor/evaluations.md (414)`

**Current shape.** Foundry (foundry.md, 2282 lines) is canon's "capability factory": it builds immutable candidate assets — datasets, tuned/distilled models, workers, verifiers, route policies, conductor advisors, eval suites/worlds, embodied capability packages — and executes admitted experiments/training/eval jobs through the daemon, while explicitly not owning judgment, campaign nomination, or release. It specifies four planes (control/data/execution/serving), a persistent training-pipeline lifecycle (FoundrySpec → DatasetSnapshot → RunPlan → Trial/Checkpoint → ModelArtifact → PackageArtifact → RegistryVersion → RouteBinding → PromotionBundle), teacher-distillation with candidate-data quarantine, an autonomous experiment optimizer, executable eval worlds, interactive-world/world-model training, and a full robotics sim-to-real lane, backed by a ~900-line hand-maintained wall of ~38 object schemas. Evaluations (evaluations.md, 414 lines) is the independent judgment surface: suite revisions are released then frozen into an immutable EvaluationEpoch (exact target, incumbent, evaluators, thresholds, statistical policy); sealed holdouts are a custody/information-flow boundary with an append-only EvaluationExposureLedger charging what adaptive search learned from protected tests; evaluators have a validity lifecycle (challenged → degraded → invalidated) with VerifierChallenge-driven discovery and re-verification of dependent claims and releases. A portfolio taxonomy (visible/sealed/transfer-OOD/adversarial/cross-play/external-reality/production/independent-reproduction lanes) must be declared per claim. The two docs draw a strict boundary — Foundry builds and executes, Evaluations judges, Improvement nominates, Governance activates — and both are largely unbuilt: Foundry is a deliberately inert draft object plane, Evaluations a planned owner-application contract whose wire shapes (EvaluationEpochEnvelope, EvaluationExposureLedgerEnvelope, VerifierChallengeEnvelope) live in foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md.

**What it already gets right:**

- The EvaluationExposureLedger is genuinely ahead of named SOTA: no shipping eval platform (Inspect, Braintrust, LangSmith, OpenAI Evals) has receipted, append-only, inheritance-aware accounting of what adaptive search learned from protected holdouts. It operationalizes the Dwork reusable-holdout insight as an institutional object, and the anti-reset rules (child campaigns inherit ancestor spend) close the classic 'new project, fresh benchmark' laundering loophole.
- Epoch freezing discipline — no mutable 'latest' suite/judge/threshold can supply promotion evidence, and any evaluator or rubric change creates a successor epoch rather than silently reinterpreting old passes — is stricter versioning than HELM, OpenAI Evals, or any commercial eval product enforces.
- Rights-aware training governance is well beyond Together/Modal/Axolotl-class platforms: teacher-route rights ('inference access is not training permission'), TrainingEvidenceEligibility per input, most-restrictive-intersection learning boundaries, transitive derivative constraints, and revocation impact graphs — including the honest fail-closed stance that deletion receipts are not verified unlearning.
- Training artifacts vs deployable package artifacts as separate lineage nodes with validation-gated conversion (checkpoint ≠ GGUF ≠ endpoint package) matches SageMaker/Vertex registry best practice and exceeds it in evidence binding; the model-swap continuity test (rerun with incumbent provider-state hard-disabled) is a portability proof no mainstream platform offers.
- Evaluator validity as a first-class lifecycle with challenge-driven discovery of affected epochs, claims, and live deployments treats the judge as a fallible versioned dependency — a real gap in Inspect/HELM-class practice where a broken scorer silently invalidates history.

**SOTA verdict: `near-sota`** — compared against: UK AISI Inspect (task QA, EvalLog format); HELM / HELM-lite (scenario taxonomy, multi-metric reporting); OpenAI Evals + RFT services; Braintrust / LangSmith (eval ops, judge management); W&B / MLflow (experiment tracking, lineage); DVC / LakeFS (dataset versioning); Together AI / Modal / Axolotl (tuning platforms); SageMaker & Vertex model registries (aliases, approval, cards); Dwork et al. reusable holdout / Thresholdout (adaptive overfitting control); GSM1k / LiveBench private-split contamination practice; METR task standards (task validity, human baselining); NVIDIA Isaac Lab (embodied sim-to-real)

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No train/eval decontamination machinery anywhere in the pack: no n-gram/embedding overlap audit between DatasetSnapshot lineage and sealed-holdout commitments, no canary strings in protected suites, no base-model pretraining-contamination probes at epoch freeze — standard practice in Llama/GPT-class training reports and GSM1k/LiveBench methodology.
- Suite-release 'validation' is a named checkpoint with no content: nothing like Inspect task QA or METR task standards (solvability/oracle checks, human baselines, judge inter-rater agreement, error bars on the eval itself).
- No portable eval-log/attestation interchange format (Inspect EvalLog is the de facto standard); replay bundles are IOI-internal refs, and the independent-reproduction lane never binds to AIIP for genuinely external judgment.
- The exposure-spend function — the flagship primitive — has no reference semantics (units, return-class charges, a Thresholdout-style default), so independent implementations cannot produce interoperable or comparable ledgers as specified.
- No metric-stream/experiment-tracking contract (W&B/MLflow step-series, system metrics) under FoundryTrial/Checkpoint, and no annotator-QA machinery (gold tasks, agreement stats, qualification) behind the feedback/annotation queues.

**Findings (12, severity-ordered):**

1. **The direct (non-campaign) change path has no valid judgment-contract object: epochs structurally require a campaign, and 'direct evaluation contract' is defined nowhere** — `high` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/evaluations.md:347 — 'Every decision-bearing result must reference a frozen EvaluationEpoch' vs foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:7335 — 'campaign_ref: improvement-campaign://...' (non-nullable); components/hypervisor/foundry.md:237 — 'under an EvaluationEpoch or direct evaluation contract'
   - Recommendation: foundry.md:489 says a direct UpgradeProposal 'remains sufficient for a one-shot change', README Non-Negotiable 5 makes the direct path the common case, and INV-33 plus improvement.md:361 both guarantee the direct path stays valid — yet EvaluationEpochEnvelope hard-requires campaign_ref, campaign_contract_root, and pursuit-profile fields, and the phrase 'direct evaluation contract' occurs only twice in the pack (foundry.md:237 and, line-wrapped, foundry.md:307-308) with no owner, envelope, or lifecycle. improvement.md:44 names 'target-specific evaluation and simulation requirements' for the direct path but binds no judgment-contract object either. Either make campaign_ref nullable with an alternative direct_change_ref/upgrade_proposal_ref binding, or define a DirectEvaluationContract envelope in common-objects-and-envelopes.md owned by Evaluations, and state in evaluations.md which epoch fields collapse for the one-shot case.
2. **No decontamination contract: exposure accounting controls queries, but nothing stops holdout content from arriving via training data or base-model pretraining** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/evaluations.md:189 — 'Every protected query or execution appends an exposure entry binding the candidate commitment and family, case commitment, information returned, evaluator versions, access/execution receipts, contamination flags'
   - Recommendation: Canon does carry contamination flags on exposure entries, leakage_rotation_and_challenge_policy_refs on the epoch, and dedupe/provenance gates in candidate-data quarantine — but all of it polices the query path. The blunt paths are unhandled: (a) sealed cases leaking into scraped traces/DatasetSnapshots, and (b) base models having memorized public benchmark derivatives during pretraining (the dominant real-world eval-integrity failure; cf. GSM1k, LiveBench). Add decontamination_refs (n-gram/embedding overlap audit vs sealed_suite_and_world_commitment_refs) to FoundryDatasetSnapshot and a foundry.md conformance check requiring it before training; add canary-string policy to sealed suite releases; require the epoch to record a base-model contamination posture (public-derivation status of tasks, memorization probes) at freeze.
3. **The exposure-spend function — the pack's flagship eval-integrity primitive — has no reference semantics, making the ledger unfalsifiable** — `high` · Better way
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/evaluations.md:183 — 'the exposure-spend function and inherited ancestor exposure posture'
   - Recommendation: Method-neutrality is stated doctrine, but with zero worked defaults every implementation invents incompatible units and the ledger degenerates to a query counter. Publish one named default policy doc (e.g., foundations/default-exposure-spend-policy.md): information-return classes (pass/fail bit, noisy aggregate, per-case labels, full transcripts) mapped to declared bit-cost charges; Thresholdout-style noisy thresholded returns for aggregates; per-candidate-family charging with the family-commitment rule already in evaluations.md:184-185; a worked example showing budget exhaustion forcing holdout rotation. Mark it default-not-mandatory to preserve the stated neutrality.
4. **Foundry canon is the union of ~8 commercial product categories, a scope that dilutes an otherwise credible tuning-platform core** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/foundry.md:4 — 'the model, worker, eval, training, registry, endpoint, experiment, pipeline, metadata, monitoring, simulation-training, interactive-world training, durable training lifecycle, artifact packaging, promotion-bundle construction'
   - Recommendation: The core pipeline (FoundrySpec → DatasetSnapshot → RunPlan → Trial/Checkpoint → ModelArtifact → PackageArtifact → RegistryVersion → PromotionBundle) is a credible Together/Modal-class spec. The interactive-world, gameplay-trajectory, world-model, spatial-temporal-policy, and embodied/robotics lanes (roughly lines 313-469 and 904-1069 plus ~10-12 object schemas) add on the order of half the doc's bulk, and each is a company-sized product (Isaac Lab, Scale, METR). The reference-wall notice (line 1133), the 'deliberately inert' implementation status, and canon-to-code-delta gating do fence implementation risk, but they do not reduce review cost or the chance that future implementations contradict wall entries. Split the world/embodied lanes into a separate horizon-gated doc (e.g., components/hypervisor/foundry-worlds-and-embodied.md, co-owned with daemon-runtime/embodied-runtime.md) and declare a minimal-viable-core object subset in foundry.md.
5. **Suite-release 'validation' is a named checkpoint with no content — no task QA, human baselines, or judge-agreement requirements** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/evaluations.md:37 — 'validation, custody, affiliation, rights, and independence checks'
   - Recommendation: Every epoch, exposure charge, and challenge presumes the released suite is itself sound. evaluations.md names 'validation' as a release checkpoint and owns per-suite thresholds, estimands, and power/inconclusive policy, but canon never says what release validation of the suite itself requires — the exact place Inspect task review and METR task standards set the bar. Add a 'Suite Release Validation' section to evaluations.md defining minimum release evidence for a suite revision: solvability/oracle or reference-solution checks, declared human baseline or expert pass where applicable, judge/scorer agreement and calibration stats for model-graded tasks, task provenance (public-derivation status), and error bars on the suite's own discriminative power. Bind these as refs on the released revision so a VerifierChallenge can target them.
6. **FoundryRouteBinding carries post-promotion runtime lifecycle that Foundry explicitly disclaims, and reuses the promotion_record:// URI scheme** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/foundry.md:166 — 'rollout, rollback, recall, kill-switch, or remote-config truth after a candidate leaves Foundry' vs foundry.md:1372 — 'proposed | active | paused | rolled_back | recalled | superseded'
   - Recommendation: The Does-Not-Own list assigns rollout/rollback/recall truth to Governance release controls, and the reference-wall notice disclaims the wall as doctrine — yet the Foundry-owned FoundryRouteBinding object models active/paused/rolled_back/recalled states, its ID scheme (route_binding_id: promotion_record://..., line 1362) conflates two concepts the prose treats as one stage ('Route binding and PromotionRecord', line 677), and no Governance-owned PromotionRecord object exists anywhere in the pack. Terminate Foundry's object at status: proposed (or mark the later states as projections of Governance-admitted transitions); move active/paused/rolled_back/recalled into a Governance-owned PromotionRecord that references the binding; give RouteBinding its own route_binding:// scheme or explicitly merge the two objects under one name.
7. **Object-shape imbalance: Foundry hosts a 900-line hand-maintained wall already drifting internally, while Evaluations defines zero shapes and never anchor-links its own envelopes** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/foundry.md:1133 — 'The object listing below is hand-maintained reference material, not additional doctrine' ; foundry.md:1945 — 'job_ref: foundry_job:...' (vs foundry_job:// elsewhere)
   - Recommendation: The wall already shows the drift its own notice fears: mixed ':' vs '://' schemes (foundry_scorecard: at 1944 vs foundry_scorecard:// at 1592; foundry_project: vs foundry_spec://), and the promotion_record:// reuse. Meanwhile evaluations.md — the facet everything depends on — has no inline shapes and its Related Canon links common-objects-and-envelopes.md only generically, unlike foundry.md's per-envelope anchors (lines 2041-2044). Execute the stated generator TODO or trim the wall to the core-pipeline objects, and add to evaluations.md an anchored envelope index (EvaluationEpochEnvelope, EvaluationExposureLedgerEnvelope, VerifierChallengeEnvelope) plus the missing EvaluationRun/Finding/released-suite-revision shapes.
8. **Independence is only intra-instance role separation; the independent-reproduction lane should bind to AIIP with a portable eval-log format** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/evaluations.md:162 — 'independent reproduction | reruns under declared separation, resources, and environment'
   - Recommendation: Custodian/affiliation/collusion posture is tracked and INV-18 already canonizes that multiplicity under one principal is not independence — but every actor (builder, executor, judge, custodian) runs on the same Hypervisor under the same operator, so 'declared separation' is organizationally hollow; SOTA independence (AISI, METR third-party evals) is inter-institutional. Neither facet file mentions AIIP, the pack's own cross-institution protocol, and aiip.md defines no evaluation-attestation lane. Define one: export epoch + sealed-suite commitments to a counterparty institution, whose Evaluations executes judgment and returns signed scorecards and exposure entries. Prerequisite: a portable eval-log interchange projection of the replay bundle (Inspect EvalLog-compatible) so external evidence is verifiable rather than trust-me refs.
9. **Foundry-owned scorecards emit verdicts and promotion recommendations — the judgment vocabulary the boundary reserves for Evaluations** — `low` · Contradiction *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/foundry.md:1971 — 'promote | hold | reject | needs_review' and foundry.md:1492 — 'pass | fail | needs_review | invalid' vs evaluations.md:262 — 'Foundry is the builder... Evaluations is the judgment owner.'
   - Recommendation: The doctrine already guards this boundary in prose: 'Foundry scorecard = EvaluationEpoch truth' is a named anti-pattern (foundry.md:2221), a conformance check (foundry.md:2133-2135) says the experiment-local best and promotion recommendation are not epoch truth, and foundry.md:155-157 explicitly permits Foundry to 'rank experiment-local candidates and emit a recommendation' — so promotion_recommendation is arguably within the stated allowance. The residual issue is the verdict: pass | fail field on FoundryTrajectoryScorecard, which feeds FoundryCertificationRunCandidate: schema vocabulary outlives prose disclaimers once receipts bake it in, and downstream consumers will treat a pass verdict as judgment, recreating the anti-pattern evaluations.md:383 forbids. Rename that field to a declared builder-local projection (builder_assessment / observed_outcome) and add a conformance check in both files: a verdict-bearing decision object must be Evaluations-released under an epoch.
10. **'EvaluationEpoch' collides with training epochs inside the very platform that runs training loops** — `low` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/evaluations.md:119 — 'EvaluationEpoch says exactly which immutable judgment contract governs a particular bounded comparison'
   - Recommendation: Foundry docs and objects speak SFT, checkpoints, global_step — a context where 'epoch' universally means one pass over the training data — and the pack already uses 'epoch' a third way ('writer epoch' in INV-24 failover semantics). Operators reading 'the run is admitted under epoch 3' will misparse it, and searchability across logs/receipts is poor. The concept is a frozen judgment contract version, so name it that: JudgmentContract or EvalContractFreeze (envelope: ioi.judgment-contract.v1), keeping 'epoch' only as informal prose. The rename is feasible pre-implementation (implementation status: planned, nothing shipped) but not free: the string is baked into common-objects-and-envelopes.md schema ids, decision 0018, canon-to-code-delta rows, and bounded-recursive-improvement.md, so it is a multi-file canon sweep — worth doing only before receipts and AIIP attestations freeze the string permanently.
11. **'Foundry' collides with Palantir Foundry and Microsoft Azure AI Foundry, two major products with different meanings in adjacent spaces** — `low` · Rename
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/foundry.md:25 — 'Hypervisor Foundry is the capability factory for governed autonomous systems.'
   - Recommendation: The suite deliberately courts a Palantir-literate audience (Ontology, Missions), for whom 'Foundry' means the data/operations platform — here it means the opposite half (training/tuning), while Azure AI Foundry is a model/agent platform, guaranteeing persistent cross-talk in sales and hiring conversations. Cheapest fix short of a rename: standardize the two-word form 'Capability Foundry' on first use in every doc and add a vocabulary.md disambiguation entry explicitly contrasting Palantir Foundry and Azure AI Foundry. If renaming, the doc's own phrase 'capability factory' is more ownable.
12. **Feedback and annotation queues have no annotator-quality machinery — no gold tasks, agreement statistics, or qualification gates** — `low` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/hypervisor/foundry.md:117 — 'feedback and annotation queues that turn corrections, acceptance/rejection reasons, reviewer judgments, and quality labels into evaluation or training candidates'
   - Recommendation: Human labels flow into preference datasets, verifier tuning, and evaluation candidates, yet nothing in either file requires inter-annotator agreement thresholds, seeded gold tasks, annotator qualification/calibration, or per-annotator provenance on labels — the standard Scale/Surge-class QA layer, and a prerequisite for the rubric/preference versioning foundry.md:582 already demands. Extend FoundryCandidateDataRecord (or the quarantine gates) with annotator_refs, gold-task audit refs, and agreement-statistics refs, and add a conformance check: human-labeled records cannot leave quarantine without declared annotation-QA evidence.


## Part III.12 — Supply planes: model router, connectors, storage

### Model router + BYOK, connectors/tools contracts, storage backends + Filecoin CAS

**Files reviewed:** `components/model-router/doctrine.md (638)` · `components/model-router/api-byok-mounting.md (322)` · `components/connectors-tools/doctrine.md (531)` · `components/connectors-tools/contracts.md (506)` · `components/storage-backends/doctrine.md (210)` · `components/storage-backends/filecoin-cas.md (210)`

**Current shape.** This facet defines the three supply planes of the IOI stack under one fail-closed authority physics. The model router selects cognition backends (provider APIs, aggregators, BYOK/BYOA, local servers, self-hosted weights) as ModelRoutes, and every candidate must resolve an admitted, versioned ModelRouteRightsContract carrying a bidirectional, purpose-specific rights matrix — provider use of customer material (logging, human review, training, retention/ZDR) and customer use of outputs (retain, replay, RAG, distillation, resale) — where missing or ambiguous members fail closed and fallback is a receipted "semantic substitution" that must re-run verification; model-weight custody (ModelWeightCustodyProfile) is declared separately from execution privacy, and BYOK keys live in wallet.network with runtimes receiving only brokered grants. The connectors/tools plane makes every effectful tool an immutable, content-addressed RuntimeToolContract with a canonical risk class, two-tier prim:*/scope:* capabilities, approval and receipt obligations; MCP is a replaceable transport normalized into canonical IOI owners, exposed only through immutable MCPGatewayRequirementEnvelopes resolved into subject-scoped, expiring gateway profiles whose widening requires fresh admission, with a local-agent pairing flow that authenticates candidates without granting any authority. The storage plane declares that backends (local disk, S3, Filecoin/CAS/IPFS, provider blobs) hold only payload bytes while Agentgres owns artifact identity, lifecycle, and restore validity; retrieval failures open ArtifactAvailabilityIncidents, repairs require ArtifactRepairReceipts and re-admission, and archive restore admits only after fetch + commitment hash + decrypt + admitted state-root all verify.

**What it already gets right:**

- The bidirectional purpose-specific rights matrix (provider-use of inputs vs customer-use of outputs, with no inference between directions, and intersection semantics for Compare so synthesis cannot launder restricted outputs into reusable datasets) goes beyond every named LLM gateway — LiteLLM, Portkey, and OpenRouter track at most coarse logging/training flags.
- Treating provider/model fallback as a receipted semantic substitution that must stay inside the admitted contract and re-run verification names and fixes a real industry hazard: OpenRouter-style provider routing does silently swap providers/quantizations under one model label today.
- ModelWeightCustodyProfile separated from ExecutionPrivacyPosture correctly identifies that cTEE workspace custody says nothing about proprietary weights mounted as plaintext on a root-owned rented GPU — a distinction most confidential-compute positioning conflates.
- The MCP requirement-vs-profile split (immutable package-safe MCPGatewayRequirementEnvelope vs subject-scoped, expiring, deny-only-mutable admitted profile where any widening repeats admission, and the pairing device code can never become the bearer credential) is ahead of current MCP registry/gateway practice, where manifests are mutable and bearer-scoped.
- The storage layer's availability-is-not-restore-truth discipline (ArtifactAvailabilityIncident, ArtifactRepairReceipt, restore only after fetch + hash + decrypt + state-root re-admission) is an honest rejection of the CID-as-truth cargo cult common in Filecoin/IPFS-adjacent architectures.

**SOTA verdict: `divergent-by-design`** — compared against: LiteLLM Router / Portkey AI Gateway / OpenRouter (fallback semantics, cooldowns, budgets, virtual keys, caching); Vault/KMS-class secret custody and envelope-encryption BYOK (per-object DEK wrapped by rotatable KEK); MCP specification, registry, and gateway products plus prompt-injection information-flow research (CaMeL/dual-LLM patterns, lethal-trifecta analyses); Sandboxed tool runners with per-tool network egress pinning (gVisor/E2B-class isolation); S3/Glacier durability contracts and explicit retrieval tiers (expedited/standard/bulk); Filecoin deal mechanics: sealed sectors, PoSt proofs, unsealing latency, multi-miner replication, hot-copy pinning services (Lighthouse/web3.storage pattern), erasure coding

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- Canonical failure-class taxonomy with per-class fallback eligibility, endpoint cooldown/circuit-breaking, and weighted/latency-aware load balancing (LiteLLM/Portkey table stakes)
- Semantic/prompt caching modeled at all — including cache-as-retention rights implications and provider prompt-cache affinity routing
- Envelope encryption with per-artifact DEKs, KEK rotation, and crypto-shredding as the deletion mechanism on immutable backends
- Per-tool network egress allowlists bound in RuntimeToolContract (currently prim:net.request is ambient network)
- Taint/information-flow control on tool outputs and connector payloads feeding later effectful calls (CaMeL-class injection defense)
- Signed-webhook ingestion contract (HMAC verification, replay windows, idempotent delivery) for connector subscriptions
- Retrieval-latency tiers and declared RTO for restore-critical payloads on Filecoin (Glacier-style tiering)
- Proactive durability policy: replication factor, multi-miner deals, scheduled renewal and scrub cadence
- Streaming and mid-stream cancellation/budget semantics in the model invocation contract

**Findings (16, severity-ordered):**

1. **Route-rights contracts have no production supply chain; kill-on-terms-change plus fail-closed will dark most of the portfolio** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/model-router/doctrine.md:295 — 'The route is killed or quarantined until a reviewed contract version is admitted; availability alone never re-enables it.'
   - Recommendation: Name the missing pipeline as canon objects: a ProviderTermsSnapshot (source URL, content hash, retrieved_at, monitor cadence) and a RouteRightsCompiler admission path with a named reviewing authority — the compiler is implied at api-byok-mounting.md:182 but unowned. Add materiality triage: a detected terms change quarantines only the matrix members/purposes it touches, leaving the admitted contract valid for unaffected purposes; whole-route kill only for material changes. Otherwise dozens of providers × models × ~17 matrix members × frequent silent ToS edits makes fail-closed equal permanently-dark, and operators will route around the contract system entirely — the classic drift engine. Real provider ToS are prose, unversioned, and changed without notice; canon must own who converts them into exact versioned refs.
2. **Tool outputs are never tainted: no information-flow control between untrusted content and subsequent effectful calls** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/connectors-tools/contracts.md:244 — 'always untrusted/tainted until schema, policy, and provenance checks pass' (applies to MCP Prompts only)
   - Recommendation: The normalization table taints MCP prompts, doctrine taints prompt-only local-agent submissions, and INV-17 taints participant input — but tool results, resources, and connector payloads flowing into agent context are covered only by output schema validation (doctrine invariant 4) and the source-material-is-not-domain-truth rule, neither of which is flow control. That leaves the primary 2025-26 exfiltration vector open: injection via fetched email/web/issue content steering a later gmail.send. Add taint labels to tool outputs and context (origin class: operator | admitted_artifact | external_untrusted); require that an effectful call whose arguments derive from external_untrusted content escalates approval or blocks, and adopt a lethal-trifecta rule: private-data read + untrusted content + egress-capable tool never co-resident in one run without step-up. CaMeL-style capability-flow control and gateway guardrail products are ahead here; IOI's risk ladder gates the tool, not the data flow into it. This extends INV-17's admission discipline from room participants to connector-fetched content.
3. **RuntimeToolContract has no network egress binding; prim:net.request is ambient network access** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/connectors-tools/contracts.md:44 — '"primitive_capabilities_required": ["prim:net.request"]'
   - Recommendation: Add an egress_policy field to RuntimeToolContract: allowed host/endpoint patterns per tool revision (gmail.send pins googleapis.com), enforced by the daemon sandbox, plus an admission rule that a connector tool's egress set covers only its declared provider — the connector_covers_endpoint bind already proven in the built connector adapter should be promoted to canon here. Without host pinning, any injected call to a net.request tool can exfiltrate to attacker infrastructure, defeating the approval gates on every other tool. Per-tool domain allowlists are table stakes in gVisor/E2B-class sandboxes and MCP gateway products; the node-level 'egress policy' in task-capsule-protocol.md is never bound at tool-contract granularity.
4. **No canonical failure-class taxonomy: free-string fallback classes and no refusal-vs-availability distinction enable safety-refusal laundering** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/model-router/doctrine.md:377 — 'fallback_classes: [string]'; doctrine.md:581 — 'The receipt must preserve each attempted route, failure class, fallback or escalation'
   - Recommendation: Add a canonical enum to foundations/canonical-enums.md: rate_limited, provider_unavailable, timeout, contract_ineligible, budget_exceeded, content_policy_refusal, malformed_output, quality_rejected. Make fallback_classes reference it and make fallback eligibility per-class policy: availability-class failures may fall back inside the admitted contract, but content_policy_refusal must not silently fall back to a more permissive provider — that is a policy bypass the current 'semantic substitution' rule does not catch because the substitution stays inside the contract. LiteLLM/Portkey distinguish retryable vs non-retryable classes with cooldowns; IOI receipts already record 'failure class' without ever defining its members, which guarantees implementation drift on a fail-closed surface.
5. **BYOK 'short-lived operation-scoped tokens' do not exist at major providers; commit to a wallet-guarded key-injection egress proxy** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: components/model-router/api-byok-mounting.md:288 — 'Runtimes receive brokered authority grants or short-lived operation-scoped tokens, never raw long-lived provider keys by default.'
   - Recommendation: OpenAI/Anthropic API keys (and Gemini API-key lanes) are long-lived bearer credentials; no major provider mints per-operation tokens from a customer key (Vertex-style short-lived OAuth is the exception, not the rule), so the 'short-lived operation-scoped tokens' half of the stated default is unimplementable for the top routes as written. The compliant half already exists in outline — 'brokered authority grants', the doctrine BYOK flow (wallet.network grants operation-scoped authority, then the model router invokes the provider, doctrine.md:263-269), and connectors doctrine's 'internal execution by wallet/guardian when possible' — but no model-router file specifies which component holds the plaintext key at request time. Commit to the actual mechanism: a daemon/wallet-guarded invocation proxy that terminates the provider call, injects the sealed key per admitted grant, and binds request-hash → grant → ModelInvocationReceipt so the worker runtime never sees plaintext. State the rotation/revocation verification path and the BYOA session-credential variant. This is Vault/KMS-gateway SOTA (Portkey/LiteLLM virtual keys) expressed in IOI's lease language — INV-2 already demands it.
6. **Filecoin retrieval latency and unsealing reality are absent from the restore path** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: components/storage-backends/filecoin-cas.md:158 — 'replica, pin, deal renewal, archive fallback, or replacement payload is tried'
   - Recommendation: The docs handle restore validity honestly but treat retrieval as a boolean availability event; backend selection lists only generic 'cost and latency' and 'restore requirements' inputs (storage-backends/doctrine.md:149-163) with no retrieval tiers. Real Filecoin: cold sealed-sector retrieval requires unsealing — hours to days, sometimes refused — which is why production users keep hot unsealed copies (Lighthouse/web3.storage pattern). Add retrieval_class (hot_replicated | warm_pinned | cold_sealed_deal) to the backend selection policy with a declared RTO per class, and a canon rule: restore-critical payloads (AgentStateArchive, disputed delivery bundles) require at least one hot/warm replica outside sealed deals — a Filecoin deal alone may satisfy durability posture but never restore-latency posture. This mirrors S3 Glacier's explicit expedited/standard/bulk retrieval tiers.
7. **No durability policy object: replication factor, multi-miner deals, and proactive renewal are unspecified; the incident flow is repair-after-failure only** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/storage-backends/filecoin-cas.md:37 — 'durability posture for immutable payloads;'; doctrine.md:132 — 'expired storage lease'
   - Recommendation: Define a DurabilityPolicy consumed by backend selection: replication_factor (Filecoin practice is 3-5 independent miners/regions), erasure-coding profile where supported, deal_renewal_lead_time driving a scheduled, receipted renewal automation, and a scrub cadence that verifies commitments before a consumer fails. Today an expired deal is discovered only as an ArtifactAvailabilityIncident after a dependent read. S3 earns its 11-nines by continuous internal replication and scrubbing; Filecoin gives per-deal PoSt proofs that canon should consume as scheduled availability evidence, not only as failure evidence. Fits the existing physics: proofs remain evidence, renewal receipts remain admitted facts.
8. **Single passphrase-derived sealing key: specify envelope encryption with per-artifact DEKs and crypto-shredding as deletion on immutable backends** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: components/storage-backends/filecoin-cas.md:188 — 'sealed bytes (Argon2id KDF + AEAD under the wallet-secret passphrase) before every backend write'
   - Recommendation: Wallet-controlled decryption and decryption leases already exist in canon (filecoin-cas.md:122-126), and the cited seal describes the built implementation, but target canon nowhere requires envelope encryption: a per-artifact DEK wrapped by a wallet-custody KEK, recorded on the ArtifactRef. Under passphrase derivation, (a) decryption leases scoped to one artifact are possible only if per-artifact key derivation is specified — which the canon does not do; and (b) KEK rotation without re-encrypting payloads and (c) crypto-shredding are impossible while any passphrase holder can re-derive every key. Destroying a DEK is the only honest deletion on Filecoin/IPFS where bytes are immutable and replicated, yet the selection policy lists 'deletion or redaction needs' (doctrine.md:159) with no mechanism that can satisfy them on those backends. Standard Vault/KMS practice; also bounds blast radius of a single wallet-secret compromise.
9. **Caching is never addressed, yet a response cache is retention plus replay under the facet's own rights matrix** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/model-router/doctrine.md:386 — 'Customer permission to receive an output does not imply permission to retain, replay, evaluate'
   - Recommendation: Gateway-level semantic/prompt caching is a core LiteLLM/Portkey feature and the dominant 2025-26 cost lever, but under this doctrine a cache hit IS retain + replay — an unmodeled cache silently violates fail-closed rights. Add cache_policy to ModelRoute bound to the customer_use_of_outputs matrix (cacheable only when retain and replay resolve; cache-entry refs receipted). Add provider prompt-cache affinity to Routing Inputs: Anthropic/OpenAI prompt caching only pays when consecutive calls hit the same provider, so Auto/fallback routing that ignores affinity destroys the optimization and changes cost lineage. Cache TTL must also reconcile with the provider-side retention/ZDR posture the contract declares.
10. **Connector subscriptions API is an orphan: no inbound webhook trust contract (signature, replay, taint, admission path)** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: components/connectors-tools/contracts.md:418 — 'POST /v1/connectors/{connector_id}/subscriptions'
   - Recommendation: Subscriptions appear only as three HTTP routes; ConnectorMapping nominally covers provider 'events' (doctrine.md:382-383) and INV-25/INV-17 already establish at the foundations level that external observations remain evidence until admitted, but nothing defines how an inbound provider event is verified (per-provider HMAC/signature, timestamp/replay window, idempotent delivery), what taint class it carries, or how it becomes an Agentgres fact — the ConnectorMapping/DataRecipe chain is exercised only on the pull side. Define a ConnectorSubscriptionContract: verification-method ref, delivery receipt obligation, taint = external_untrusted, and the rule that a webhook may only propose events for admission and may never directly trigger an effectful tool. Signed-webhook verification (Svix-style) is industry baseline; an unverified webhook that can start an Automation is a spoofable trigger into the effectful plane.
11. **Gateway profile inlines ten nullable pairing/room fields; extract a tagged AdmissionBasisEnvelope** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: components/connectors-tools/contracts.md:149 — '"local_agent_pairing_session_ref": "local-agent-pairing://... | null"'
   - Recommendation: The profile (contracts.md:136-209) mixes its core exposure contract with ~10 nullable pairing/room/registration fields whose valid combinations (room_guest requires a participant lease; registered_worker requires registration plus invocation refs; not_applicable requires all null) exist only in prose (contracts.md:277-291). Extract a discriminated admission_basis envelope — not_applicable | room_guest{pairing_ref, lease_ref, decision_ref} | registered_worker_invocation{registration_ref, invocation_scope_refs} — so illegal combinations are unrepresentable and validators enforce what the prose demands. profile_content_hash semantics are unchanged; pairing lineage stays referenced rather than inlined. This mirrors the discipline the file already applies well in its requirement-vs-profile separation.
12. **model-router/doctrine.md carries a second, stale ModelEndpoint schema that the API file owns** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: components/model-router/doctrine.md:226 — 'Model records should include:'; api-byok-mounting.md:4 — 'Canonical owner: this file for model provider, endpoint, route-rights contract'
   - Recommendation: The doctrine's Model Registry YAML (doctrine.md:228-252) diverges from the owned JSON shape (api:57-94): it lacks mount_mode, auth_mode, key_ref, route_contract_ref, execution_privacy_posture, and run_to_idle, and represents models as bare strings instead of capability objects. Under the pack's single-owner rule this is exactly the restatement drift the header disclaims (doctrine.md:5-6 delegates the low-level API to the api file). Replace the YAML block with a pointer plus only the doctrine-relevant rationale fields (architecture_profile, context_mutability), or move those into the api schema and delete the block — the same treatment connectors-tools/doctrine.md:336-344 already applies correctly to RuntimeToolContract.
13. **goal_execution_policy on model-route selection collides with GoalRun execution; rename to model_selection_mode** — `medium` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/model-router/api-byok-mounting.md:104 — '"goal_execution_policy": "auto | pinned | compare"'
   - Recommendation: Auto/Pinned/Compare selects among model endpoints, but GoalRun/GoalRunProfile own goal execution in the pack's work spine, and a field literally named goal_execution_policy inside ModelRoute.selection_policy — repeated on ModelConfiguration (api:204) and the invocation body (api:222) — invites implementers to bind it to GoalRun policy objects. Rename to model_selection_mode (or route_selection_mode), keep Auto/Pinned/Compare as the user-facing labels per the doctrine's own naming rule (doctrine.md:49-52), and add the old name to vocabulary.md's alias table so existing WIP maps cleanly.
14. **Run-to-idle lifecycle disagrees between the two owner files: nine states vs a seven-state linear chain** — `low` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: components/model-router/api-byok-mounting.md:283 — 'cold → warming → ready → busy → draining → idle → sleeping'; doctrine.md:490-498 lists nine states including 'unavailable' and 'failed'
   - Recommendation: Give the state set one owner (foundations/canonical-enums.md is the pack's enum home) with transition semantics: where failed may be entered from, whether unavailable is distinct from sleeping, and which states may accept queued invocations. The API file's arrow chain also implies a linear topology with no failure or eviction edges — wrong for GPU serving, where ready→failed, warming→failed, and sleeping→unavailable are exactly the edges that matter for receipts and workflow determinism (doctrine invariant 6). Low severity today, but this enum will be encoded in daemon state machines and two published shapes guarantee drift.
15. **Commerce examples contradict the risk ladder: prose says cart drafts are medium risk, yaml gives both stages the same commerce class; example lists drifted across files** — `low` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: components/connectors-tools/doctrine.md:477 — 'cart/search/draft     = medium risk'; doctrine.md:485 — 'risk_class: commerce   # cart/draft stage; approval preview binds cart contents'
   - Recommendation: Either map cart/draft operations to the canonical draft (or write_reversible) class and reserve commerce for order placement, or drop the medium/high prose split and let approval_required alone carry the distinction — as written, a policy engine keying on risk_class cannot express the stated split. Also reconcile the example lists: doctrine.md:328-329 genericized to commerce.create_cart_draft/submit_order while contracts.md:458-459 still ships instacart.*, and contracts.md:460 adds blender.run_python — arbitrary code execution — as a casual example with no risk annotation; annotate it or use the doctrine's blender.render_preview.
16. **filecoin-cas.md claims Implementation status: built while admitting the live ipfs/filecoin lanes have never run** — `low` · Contradiction *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: components/storage-backends/filecoin-cas.md:10 — 'Implementation status: built (sealed archive custody with preflight probes and done-bar)'; filecoin-cas.md:196-197 — 'ipfs/ filecoin live modes block named without credentials/config'
   - Recommendation: The status parenthetical does scope the claim to sealed archive custody, and the body honestly discloses that live lanes block without credentials — so this is overreach at the headline, not concealment. Still, restate as partial (local CAS fixture and sealed archive custody built; ipfs/filecoin live network lanes unexercised). The pack's own discipline routes built/partial/planned claims through the implementation matrix, and this profile's central subject — actual Filecoin behavior — is precisely what has never executed: no real deal lifecycle, renewal, or retrieval has run, which is where this backend's risk lives (see the retrieval-latency and durability findings). Honest status here protects the credibility of every other status line in the pack.


## Part III.13 — Product domains: Goal Space, workers, services, decentralized

### ioi.ai Goal Space: collaborative outcome pattern + control plane

**Files reviewed:** `domains/ioi-ai/collaborative-outcome-pattern.md (1285)` · `domains/ioi-ai/control-plane.md (645)`

**Current shape.** ioi.ai is defined as the first-party "outcome conductor" built on Hypervisor, and a Goal Space is its durable per-outcome product container; simple goals collapse to one GoalRun, while goals that justify collective pursuit materialize an OutcomeRoom — a bounded-DAS instance with a CollaborativeWorkGraph of WorkFrontierItems, terms-bound RoomParticipantLeases, WorkClaimLeases, Attempts (positive/negative/inconclusive/invalid/exploit/superseded classes), Findings, and VerifierChallenges, all recorded as projections over daemon/Agentgres-owned truth. Cross-party rooms form only under an exact CollaborationTermsEnvelope root; external workers discover rooms via a signed OutcomeRoomDiscoveryEnvelope, join over AIIP, and exit with a portable ParticipantStateBundle; every room declares hosted (first target) or federated (later AIIP profile) shared-state admission. Claims advance through an explicit assurance ladder (attested→evidenced→verified→accepted→adjudicated→settled), and local agents enter as room-scoped guests via LocalAgentPairingSessionEnvelope with three independently reversible elevations (room guest → private worker → public listing). Orchestration is made auditable through OrchestrationPolicy/ConstraintEnvelope/VerifierPath/DecisionReceipt and three execution policies (Auto/Pinned/Compare), with ioi.ai's own implementation objects declared read-model-only projections. control-plane.md keeps ioi.ai's backend deliberately thin: it owns accounts, Goal Space subscription/entitlement records, Work Credit grant pointers, devices, restore-routing metadata, publishing flows, console.ioi.ai Environments views, and an optional Intake Worker, while execution, authority, secrets, marketplace, and settlement truth stay with Hypervisor, wallet.network, Agentgres, aiagent.xyz, and sas.xyz. On the facet question of duplication: the control plane is mostly disciplined about not re-owning Hypervisor surfaces, with residual overlap in its restated Environments inventory and "node registry" language.

**What it already gets right:**

- 'Collective pursuit is conditional, not the default' (line 48) with an explicit expected-value test is ahead of the 2025-26 multi-agent products that default to spawning swarms; it encodes the empirical lesson that multi-agent overhead usually exceeds benefit.
- The staged assurance ladder (attested→evidenced→verified→accepted→adjudicated→settled) and 'workgraphs verify bounded contributions, not minds' is unusually honest epistemics — it explicitly bans the 'verified agent' badge that every agent marketplace pitch abuses.
- Durable, creditable negative and inconclusive attempts (lines 713-718) treat eliminated paths as first-class contributions; no shipped bounty platform (Kaggle, Gitcoin) does this, and it is the right lesson from replication economics.
- The three-elevation local-agent model (room guest → Save to My workers → Publish) with 'no elevation is automatic' is a genuinely well-designed consent boundary, better than typical marketplace onboarding funnels.
- Scoping the mechanism to domains 'where verification is materially cheaper than generation' (line 412), plus a deliberately narrow first implementation target (one room, one guest, one deterministic verifier), shows real restraint about where the loop can work.

**SOTA verdict: `near-sota`** — compared against: GitHub PR review economics (review capacity as the throughput bottleneck; CODEOWNERS routing); Kaggle competition mechanics (sponsor-funded automated scoring; public/private leaderboard split against overfitting); Gitcoin Grants sybil/collusion lessons (Passport identity scoring; pairwise-bounded quadratic matching after collusion attacks); UMA optimistic oracle and Kleros dispute economics (bonded assertions, challenge windows, staked jurors, escalating appeal fees); Linear/Notion/Figma multiplayer presence and soft-intent awareness (CRDT presence, live cursors); Agent-inbox UX SOTA (LangGraph agent-inbox, Devin-style session review queues treating human attention as a metered resource)

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No verification-funding mechanism at room level: economics canon funds independent verifiers through the Network/Open goal budget, bounties, and sas.xyz service orders, and CollaborationTermsEnvelope carries a verifier party role plus funding_and_budget_refs, but nothing earmarks a verifier budget or fee schedule that scales verification capacity with attempt volume (Kaggle funds scoring from sponsor prize pools; UMA pays challengers from forfeited bonds).
- No bonded-challenge economics: VerifierChallengeEnvelope is admission-gated and adjudicated but carries no stake, liveness window, or escalation fee, which Kleros/UMA solved years ago.
- Sybil resistance is named but not mechanized: conformance requires identity/eligibility policy, rate limits, and 'Sybil/collusion signals', and the terms envelope can exclude or attribution-only the cheap contribution classes, but no default mechanism is specified; Gitcoin needed Passport plus mechanism changes after real attacks.
- Holdout discipline exists in Evaluations canon (sealed holdouts, exposure accounting) but benchmark-shaped rooms are not required to bind their scorecards and verifier paths to it, leaving room-level verifier overfitting unmanaged relative to Kaggle's public/private split.
- No pre-claim presence or soft-intent signal on the frontier, so duplicated grounding cost is unmanaged relative to Figma/Linear-grade multiplayer awareness.
- Human review is a named VerifierPath stage with no explicit capacity binding: the ResourceOffer/CapabilityOffer primitive already carries queue/availability/cost fields, but nothing requires human review to be modeled and priced through it; agent-inbox SOTA meters human attention explicitly.

**Findings (14, severity-ordered):**

1. **Verification is the load-bearing mechanism but no mechanism scales verifier funding with attempt volume** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: domains/ioi-ai/collaborative-outcome-pattern.md:558 — 'Verifiers reproduce, compare, reject, accept, or challenge attempts under a named rule/version.'
   - Recommendation: The whole loop (step 16, assurance ladder, acceptance) depends on verifiers doing expensive reproduction work. Canon does provide funding lanes: economic-flywheel-and-pricing-boundaries.md funds independent verifiers through the separately bounded Network/Open goal budget, bounty, procurement limit, or sas.xyz service order; verifier work consumes Work Credits in the subscription lane; and CollaborationTermsEnvelope carries a verifier party role plus economics.funding_and_budget_refs and payout_condition_refs. What no owner specifies is the mechanism that keeps verification capacity proportional to attempt volume: no verifier-budget earmark or per-verification fee schedule inside the terms envelope, and no challenge-funded payout source for judgment lanes (Kaggle funds scoring from sponsor prize pools; UMA pays challengers from forfeited bonds). Add a VerificationFundingPolicy to CollaborationTermsEnvelope — a named verifier budget line within funding_and_budget_refs, a fee schedule for deterministic lanes, and bonded-challenge payouts for judgment lanes, with the economic owner named — so attempts cannot pile up in 'verifying' when the generic goal budget is exhausted or never allocated to verification.
2. **VerifierChallenge carries no challenger-side cost, leaving re-verification economics unpriced** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: domains/ioi-ai/collaborative-outcome-pattern.md:706 — 'challenge to a metric, rule, verifier, evidence, eligibility decision, or result; rule changes identify affected attempts for re-verification'
   - Recommendation: A challenge that succeeds can trigger re-verification of every affected attempt — the most expensive operation in the room. Canon already blunts the crudest griefing path: VerifierChallengeEnvelope (common-objects-and-envelopes.md) gates challenges through room admission (status proposed→admitted), routes them to a named adjudicator_policy_ref, and makes re-verification an adjudicated flag (reverification_required) rather than automatic, and conformance (collaborative-outcome-pattern.md:1149-1152) requires rate limits and queue backpressure for open rooms. But nothing prices a challenge to the challenger: no challenge_bond_ref, liveness window, or escalation fee, which UMA/Kleros use to make frivolous challenges self-defeating. Adopt the optimistic-oracle shape: a failed challenge forfeits a bond to the verification budget, an upheld one earns a fee share; cap challenge-triggered re-verification per participant per epoch in the room constitution.
3. **Creditable contribution classes include cheap-to-manufacture work with no default anti-sybil pricing** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: domains/ioi-ai/collaborative-outcome-pattern.md:717 — 'independent replication, integrity reporting, resource provision, negative information, curation, and synthesis—not only the winning output or top score.'
   - Recommendation: Negative results, curation, and replication are trivially manufacturable at agent scale. CollaborationTermsEnvelope.contribution_terms already blunts the crude attack: negative_and_inconclusive_result_eligibility can be set to excluded or attribution_only, reward eligibility requires acceptance (reward_eligible_when_accepted), minimum_reward_assurance_stage gates payout, and self_report_creates_payout_right is pinned false — so a sybil fleet cannot farm payout from unaccepted spam. What canon does not give is a default anti-sybil posture for rooms that do reward these classes: no claim-before-credit rule (credit only for attempts against an open WorkFrontierItem the participant held a WorkClaimLease on, priced by the item's declared prior value), no per-participant unclaimed-attempt cap per epoch, and no requirement that benchmark-shaped rooms bind their scorecards to the sealed holdouts and exposure accounting that Evaluations canon (components/hypervisor/evaluations.md) already owns. Name those as the recommended defaults for reward-bearing rooms.
4. **No verifier-assignment mechanism: who verifies a given attempt is never chosen by anything** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: domains/ioi-ai/collaborative-outcome-pattern.md:1150 — 'queue backpressure, fair resource allocation, Sybil/collusion signals, reviewer-independence checks, separation of duty where risk requires it'
   - Recommendation: Reviewer independence is demanded, and CollaborationTermsEnvelope reserves a slot for it (cooperation_conditions.verifier_and_independence_policy_refs) while VerifierChallengeEnvelope can challenge independence or collusion after the fact — but no canonical semantics define how a verifier is assigned to an attempt, how competitors are excluded from verifying rivals, or how assignments rotate.
