Concrete mechanism: define a canonical VerifierAssignmentPolicy behind that policy slot with three lanes — (1) deterministic checks auto-assigned to the named rule engine; (2) judgment verification drawn randomly from a disclosed verifier pool (Kleros-style draw), recorded in the attempt's verifier_refs; (3) conflict-of-interest exclusion: a principal holding a WorkClaimLease on a competing frontier item cannot verify that item's rivals. This makes 'reviewer-independence checks' an enforceable admission gate rather than aspiration.
5. **Cold-start circularity is prohibited but not staged: early rooms will be IOI on all five sides** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: domains/ioi-ai/collaborative-outcome-pattern.md:284 — 'IOI must not circularly act as coordinator, paid worker, sole verifier, ranking authority, and settlement judge for the same outcome.'
   - Recommendation: Lines 296-297 concede a Network/Open room of only seed workers 'remains one party' — which is exactly what every early room will be. Canon already carries partial defenses: seed workers must disclose IOI ownership, subsidy, and real cost (lines 282-289), and conformance plus the economics owner forbid presenting same-owner execution as multi-party. But the flat five-role prohibition is still either violated or vacuous in the seed phase, because nothing makes single-party status a checkable, rendered room property. Add a disclosed single_party_mode room state: while active, the room may not label anything 'independent verification', may not charge multi-party premiums, and renders an explicit one-party banner; define a measurable graduation criterion (>=1 non-IOI principal holding verifier or worker leases) that flips the mode. This turns an unenforceable ethic into a checkable room property.
6. **Federated admission is unspecified while conformance asserts contract-identity with it** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: domains/ioi-ai/collaborative-outcome-pattern.md:727 — 'and failover. This is a later AIIP profile.'
   - Recommendation: Lines 1134-1137 require hosted and federated admission to 'accept the same discovery, request, eligibility… contracts. Only the declared ordering/admission owner and watermark differ.' MultiPartyCollaborationEnvelope does already carry the object-level surface (coordination_topology, shared_state_admission_owner_ref, conflict_failover_and_adjudication_policy_refs), so this is more than two sentences of prose — but the semantics behind those refs are entirely unspecified: who admits a participant lease during partition, what happens to WorkClaimLease heartbeats across a failover, how quorum/merge conflicts resolve. Asserting API-compatibility with an unspecified consensus protocol is a future breaking-change guarantee. Either downgrade the same-contract sentence to an explicit design goal with named open questions, or specify the minimal federated profile now (single rotating adjudicator, no quorum) so the identity claim is checkable.
7. **read_model_only projection carries inline policy enums with no owner ref** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: domains/ioi-ai/collaborative-outcome-pattern.md:1014 — 'training_posture: never_train | synthetic_only | redacted_opt_in | full_private_opt_in | org_policy'
   - Recommendation: IoiAiCrossSessionOutcomeGraph declares read_model_only: true (line 1019) yet embeds training_posture and blocked_context_classes as inline enums rather than refs, while sibling fields (training_consent_refs, training_evidence_eligibility_refs) correctly point at owners. The training_posture enum is defined nowhere else in canon — this projection is currently its only definition site, which is exactly how a projection becomes de facto policy truth. control-plane.md:141-144 forbids ioi.ai becoming the 'attempt truth source', and the pack's projections-never-truth physics applies equally to policy vocabulary. Replace both fields with refs to the owning policy objects (training-consent authority ref per institutional-learning-boundary.md, collaboration-envelope disclosure-policy ref), or annotate them as denormalized copies of a named owner field with the owner ref alongside.
8. **'Goal Space' is overloaded across subscription SKU, per-outcome container, and room render** — `medium` · Rename
   - Evidence: domains/ioi-ai/control-plane.md:92 — 'The same OutcomeRoom is rendered as Goal Space in ioi.ai and Work / Rooms in Hypervisor'
   - Recommendation: control-plane.md:69 makes 'Goal Space subscription' the account-level plan (seats, Work Credit grants), while collaborative-outcome-pattern.md:22-23 makes a Goal Space one outcome's container — a fresh reader cannot tell whether an account has one Goal Space or many. The line-92 render usage is reconcilable with the container reading (the room is projected into the Goal Space) but the phrasing 'rendered as Goal Space' invites reading them as the same object; rephrase to 'rendered inside the Goal Space'. The subscription/container collision is the load-bearing confusion: reserve 'Goal Space' strictly for the per-outcome container and either rename the plan (noting that 'Goal Space subscription' is anchored in README's Decisions section and economic-flywheel canon, so the rename must be one coordinated pass across those owners) or state explicitly in both files that one subscription covers many Goal Spaces. Do not delete the Mission-label sentence: vocabulary.md retires CollaborativeMission but sanctions MissionProfile as an optional label over exactly one GoalRun or OutcomeRoom (README Non-Negotiable 33), so the sentence is deliberate alignment — at most, link the MissionProfile vocabulary entry.
9. **No pre-claim presence or soft-intent signal on the frontier; duplicated grounding cost is unmanaged** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: domains/ioi-ai/collaborative-outcome-pattern.md:695 — 'heartbeat, renewal, release, reassignment, and duplicate-work policy'
   - Recommendation: WorkClaimLease handles post-claim concurrency, but two participants can both spend tokens grounding the same WorkFrontierItem before either claims it — the exact waste Figma/Linear-grade presence (live cursors, 'someone is viewing') eliminates for humans. Add a FrontierInterestSignal: a non-authoritative, TTL-seconds soft-intent projection a participant emits when it begins evaluating an item, rendered on the frontier graph and visible to the conductor's routing. Because it is explicitly a projection with no admission consequence, it fits projections-never-truth physics while cutting the dominant duplicated-cost path in multi-agent rooms (deliberate independent replication, which the room credits separately, remains a claimed activity and is unaffected).
10. **Human review is a named verifier stage with no explicit queue, capacity, or SLA binding** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: domains/ioi-ai/collaborative-outcome-pattern.md:409 — 'named human review can advance the specific supported claim. They do not'
   - Recommendation: VerifierPath includes human review (line 639) and the Goal Space projection lists approvals (line 807), but no text represents reviewer capacity, queue depth, or latency for the human lane — so human-gated attempts will silently pool in 'verifying', the GitHub-PR failure mode. The primitive already exists: ResourceOffer/CapabilityOffer carries capacity, availability, queue, and cost fields (lines 686-687), and conformance demands queue backpressure (line 1150). Close the gap by binding them: state that human review enters a room as a ResourceOffer/CapabilityOffer variant (reviewer pool ref, queue depth, latency SLO, cost) so the conductor can price it into VerifierPath selection, and require frontier items in human-verifying state to render queue position and expected wait in the Goal Space projection — the agent-inbox SOTA (LangGraph agent-inbox, Devin review queues) meters human attention explicitly.
11. **Compare is the flagship product promise but attempt comparison has no contract** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: domains/ioi-ai/collaborative-outcome-pattern.md:123 — 'the evidence and attempts stay comparable'
   - Recommendation: Comparability is the stated core analogy (lines 121-125) and Compare must 'apply a named comparison, verifier, or synthesis rule' (line 655), yet no object defines what a comparison is — IoiAiAttemptSummary carries per-attempt refs only. Eval SOTA (LMSYS side-by-sides, Braintrust, OpenAI evals) has concrete pairwise schemas. Define AttemptComparisonProjection: compared attempt-summary refs, dimension scores with verifier refs per dimension, the named comparison-rule ref/version, cost/latency deltas, synthesis-choice receipt, and human-override receipt. Read-model-only, owned by ioi.ai like its sibling projections — this is the one place the product's value-add lives and it is currently unspecified.
12. **Room-object lifecycle prose is restated across co-owner doctrine files while schemas live in a third** — `low` · Refactor
   - Evidence: domains/ioi-ai/collaborative-outcome-pattern.md:680 — 'Its minimum shared-frontier lifecycle is:'
   - Recommendation: Lines 682-708 restate field-level semantics for RoomParticipantLease, ResourceOffer, WorkFrontierItem, WorkClaimLease, Attempt, Finding, and VerifierChallenge (states, TTL, heartbeat, duplicate-work policy), and foundations/governed-autonomous-systems.md restates the same family with its own lifecycle prose. This is not an ownership violation — source-of-truth-map.md deliberately co-owns the room-object family between the two files, and the envelope schemas (e.g., WorkClaimLeaseEnvelope) live in common-objects-and-envelopes.md — but sanctioned joint ownership of the same lifecycle prose in two doctrine files plus a schema file is still a three-site drift risk on the first field change. Tighten the split: governed-autonomous-systems.md keeps system/lifecycle semantics, this file keeps product-behavior consequences, and both point field semantics at the common-objects envelopes instead of restating states and TTL/heartbeat policy inline.
13. **control-plane.md restates the Environments surface inventory it explicitly does not own** — `low` · Refactor
   - Evidence: domains/ioi-ai/control-plane.md:335 — 'VM/container/microVM/WASM inventory'
   - Recommendation: Lines 328-344 and 349-366 enumerate node registry, VM/container/microVM/WASM inventory, images, volumes, GPU pools, attach/detach verbs — then line 368 assigns ownership to providers-and-environments.md. This is the one real answer to 'does the control plane duplicate Hypervisor surfaces': the boundary prose is disciplined, but the concrete inventory lists mean every Environments-canon change needs synchronized edits here. Collapse both blocks to: 'console.ioi.ai renders the Environments read projection defined by providers-and-environments.md, overlaid with account/org billing, entitlement, and policy-visibility fields owned here', and delete the itemized lists.
14. **'node registry' and 'runtime profile registry' in the ioi.ai Owns list imply identity ownership the file elsewhere denies** — `low` · Rename
   - Evidence: domains/ioi-ai/control-plane.md:271 — 'node registry and runtime-node inventory metadata;'
   - Recommendation: 'Registry' connotes minting and owning identity, but lines 587-589 give infrastructure-manager semantics to Hypervisor Environments/Providers canon and Non-Negotiable 7 says environment status here is 'coordination metadata'. Rename the owned records to what they are: 'node attachment pointers and runtime-node inventory metadata' and 'runtime-profile selection pointers for a user or org', with node/profile identity explicitly minted by the Hypervisor daemon/Environments owner. Small change, but it closes the last textual door to ioi.ai becoming a second source of node identity truth.

### aiagent.xyz: worker marketplace, ontology, packs, lifecycle, console, endpoints

**Files reviewed:** `domains/aiagent/worker-marketplace.md (1153)` · `domains/aiagent/digital-worker-ontology.md (277)` · `domains/aiagent/vertical-ontology-packs.md (157)` · `domains/aiagent/integration-surface-taxonomy.md (173)` · `domains/aiagent/managed-worker-instance-lifecycle.md (433)` · `domains/aiagent/managed-agent-console-contract.md (234)` · `domains/aiagent/worker-endpoints.md (831)`

**Current shape.** Seven canon docs define aiagent.xyz, IOI's planned first-party marketplace for "ontology-bound" autonomous workers — benchmarked, installable, attributable capability rather than a persona store. A WorkerPackage is a portable template; a WorkerComposition (model route + harness + tools + runtime + policy version) is the benchmark and listing identity; a ManagedWorkerInstance is a buyer-bound initialization whose post-hire changes flow through ConfigRevisions and risk-classed ChangePlans with dry-run/canary/rollback gates. A base DigitalWorkerOntology plus installable VerticalOntologyPacks and an IntegrationSurface taxonomy replace hardcoded marketplace verticals and extend to embodied work via physical-action safety objects. Sparse Worker Categories are narrow benchmarked labor markets that gate MoW routing eligibility; submission fees pay for benchmark compute but cannot buy ranking. A private "My workers" registry lets a local agent join as a one-room ioi.ai guest, be saved privately via a fail-closed pairing envelope, and only later be explicitly promoted to public listing through disclosure, admission, and benchmark gates. The managed-agent web console is projection-only: execution stays with Hypervisor Daemon runtime nodes, truth with Agentgres, authority with wallet.network/policy providers. A low-level endpoints reference specifies discovery manifests (/.well-known/ai-agent.json), task/thread APIs, OpenAI-wire and MCP compatibility faces, inter-agent routes, and marketplace admission/install/instance APIs; only a draft listings/candidates/reviews/offers plane is implemented today. Neutrality, routing-receipt, and marketplace-economics doctrine for this domain is split across three foundations/domains owners (marketplace-neutrality.md, mixture-of-workers.md, economic-flywheel-and-pricing-boundaries.md) that the seven aiagent docs never link.

**What it already gets right:**

- Composition-bound benchmark identity is genuinely ahead of shipped marketplaces: scores attach to the exact model+harness+tools+runtime+policy hash, with staleness and rebenchmark rules (worker-marketplace.md:381-388, 952-983). GPT Store, Poe, and current agent stores rank names and personas; this ranks reproducible compositions.
- Package-vs-instance separation with default-deny seller/platform/cross-tenant learning reuse and buyer-bound portable memory (worker-marketplace.md:397-409, managed-worker-instance-lifecycle.md:216-247) is a stronger data-rights posture than any SaaS-agent vendor publishes, and directly answers enterprise 'your usage trains their agent' objections.
- Lifecycle honesty is first-class: payment lapse cannot delete memory, archive/restore/export/delete/forget are named states with receipts, provider exit produces migration receipts (managed-worker-instance-lifecycle.md:64-137, 371-407). This fixes real failure modes of hosted-agent products rather than hand-waving billing.
- The private-first supply funnel (one-room guest -> Save to My workers -> explicit promotion) with a closed bootstrap action set and fail-closed pairing lifecycle (worker-marketplace.md:283-357, worker-endpoints.md:157-259) is a structural spam and quality defense the GPT Store never had — nothing becomes public supply by accident.
- Explicit platform-neutrality and seed-fleet disclosure doctrine (worker-marketplace.md:1017-1059), backed by a canonical RoutingDecisionReceipt and anti-cannibalization invariants in the linked neutrality owner (marketplace-neutrality.md:87-135) — no fiat first-party ranking, seed fleet must be ordinary disclosed packages, no silent cloning of worker internals — pre-empts the platform-competes-with-suppliers trap that poisons staffing marketplaces and app stores.

**SOTA verdict: `divergent-by-design`** — compared against: GPT Store failure post-mortem: no quality signal, persona spam, no discovery demand, no builder monetization; Google A2A / Linux Foundation Agent Cards at /.well-known/agent-card.json as the de facto agent discovery standard; MCP server registries and manifest conventions; Harvey / Sierra / Decagon vertical-agent go-to-market: pilot evals on the customer's own data, outcome-based pricing, tau-bench-style task evals; Upwork/Fiverr staffing-marketplace economics: category liquidity, take-rate defensibility, disintermediation; Salesforce AgentExchange / Agentforce as the enterprise agent-marketplace incumbent pattern; SWE-bench/GAIA-class agent benchmarks and eval-before-hire procurement patterns

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No benchmark-cost amortization: SOTA eval practice uses delta/subset evals and score inheritance; canon's per-composition material-change rebenchmark rule has no sensitivity classes, score inheritance, or production-receipt refresh anywhere in the pack
- Reputation updates are governed by marketplace-neutrality invariants (usage/quality evidence required, sybil/reciprocal-review visibility), but the already-built 'reviews' draft plane and the aiagent Quality and Reputation section have no receipt-binding ReviewRecord doctrine — the GPT Store quality-signal hole at the object level
- Pre-hire trial exists only as informal try-in-browser / Ephemeral run; no typed buyer-environment trial-eval object with buyer-private receipts, though pilot evals are how Harvey/Sierra/Decagon actually convert enterprise buyers
- Take-rate legitimacy per surface and an explicit no-fee-on-self-host disintermediation stance live in the economics owner (economic-flywheel-and-pricing-boundaries.md), but the aiagent docs never link them, and no per-invocation-mode fee basis or publisher payout-accrual mechanics beyond MoW's reference formula are declared
- No category lifecycle/liquidity governance for Sparse Worker Categories — narrow markets fragment thin liquidity with no creation criteria, curation ownership, or merge/retirement doctrine in either the domain doc or the mixture-of-workers owner
- AIIP already defines a versioned A2A binding, but worker-endpoints ships OpenAI-wire and MCP compatibility faces with no A2A agent-card discovery projection or import path at the marketplace surface
- The canonical RoutingDecisionReceipt / MoWRoutingDecision objects exist in marketplace-neutrality.md and mixture-of-workers.md but are never cited from the aiagent docs' neutrality section or receipt lists, leaving the domain pack's neutrality rules looking unaudited to a facet-local reader

**Findings (16, severity-ordered):**

1. **Composition-bound rebenchmarking is economically combinatorial and will strangle supply** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/worker-marketplace.md:384 — 'A material change to ModelRoute, HarnessProfile, AgentHarnessAdapter, tool set, runtime profile, privacy posture, verifier path, prompt/policy bundle, or package version'
   - Recommendation: The facet's core differentiator ('benchmarked capability') is priced out under the fresh/stale rule: model routes churn monthly, and each of N routes x M harness/tool variants demands a full re-run someone must pay for (worker-marketplace.md:971-983). Canon already softens the edges — the trigger is a 'material' change, waivers/subsidies/sponsored benchmark lanes exist (worker-marketplace.md:971-977), and unbenchmarked listings remain discoverable when labeled — but no doc in the pack defines amortization, so every material delta is a full re-run. Add amortization canon: (a) per-component sensitivity classes so a delta re-runs only the affected suite subset; (b) score inheritance across composition versions with declared confidence decay instead of binary staleness; (c) publish the benchmark compute cost model inside the Sparse Worker Category record; (d) let verified production ContributionReceipts partially refresh staleness. Without this, honest sellers list one composition or skip benchmarking entirely.
2. **Cold start is one supply-side paragraph; demand engine and category liquidity are unspecified** — `high` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/worker-marketplace.md:1044 — 'IOI may operate a first-party seed fleet to solve cold start, provide baseline quality, last-resort capacity, conformance fixtures, and initial Goal Space liquidity.'
   - Recommendation: GPT Store died demand-first: supply flooded in, nobody browsed. The seed fleet addresses supply only, and Sparse Worker Categories ('narrow labor markets', worker-marketplace.md:926-927) fragment whatever demand exists into thin markets. Neither the domain doc nor the category owner (foundations/mixture-of-workers.md:180-214, which owns Sparse Worker Category structure) defines creation criteria, curation, or retirement. Add category-lifecycle governance to the owner and its domain application: creation criteria (named demand source, minimum expected volume), curation ownership, merge/retirement rules, and a minimum-liquidity threshold before a leaderboard renders. Bind the primary demand channel explicitly: ioi.ai GoalRun/OutcomeRoom routing should be canon's declared demand engine for aiagent.xyz with pull-through expectations, not the current optional 'may route to aiagent.xyz workers' (worker-marketplace.md:1061-1064).
3. **/v1/interagent/* is a second inter-agent protocol while canon says aiagent.xyz is only an AIIP client** — `high` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/worker-endpoints.md:673 — 'POST /v1/interagent/message' vs domains/aiagent/worker-marketplace.md:50 — 'aiagent.xyz is not the protocol. It is a first-party protocol client, demand'
   - Recommendation: worker-endpoints.md defines a full bespoke inter-agent vocabulary (message, task-offer, task-accept, handoff, evidence-request, decision-request, patch-proposal) with no stated correspondence to AIIP envelopes, while the marketplace doc insists aiagent.xyz is an AIIP client and proof surface. The route names visibly mirror AIIP envelope vocabulary (TaskOffer, TaskAcceptance, handoff, evidence — foundations/aiip.md:77-78), which makes the fix cheap: define /v1/interagent/* as the HTTP binding of named AIIP message/handoff/evidence envelope types with a mapping table to the AIIP owner doc, or delete the route family and expose AIIP directly. As written, the marketplace's interop surface is guaranteed to drift from the protocol the whole pack claims aiagent.xyz exists to prove.
4. **Vertical packs author their own risk mappings with no tighten-only rule — a policy-downgrade hole** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/vertical-ontology-packs.md:90 — 'risk_mappings: external_message: high, policy_widening: high'
   - Recommendation: Packs are third-party-authorable extensions that define risk mappings, approval defaults, and receipt schemas — safety-relevant policy — yet nothing states that base-ontology and IntegrationSurface postures are a floor. integration-surface-taxonomy.md owns 'default risk classes' and 'approval defaults' per surface, and digital-worker-ontology.md treats missing coverage as 'unknown, not safe', but no doc forbids a pack from relaxing an existing mapping: a sloppy or malicious pack could map member.ban or funds-adjacent actions to low risk. Add a monotonicity invariant to vertical-ontology-packs.md and digital-worker-ontology.md conformance: packs may only tighten risk classes, evidence requirements, and approval defaults relative to base ontology plus surface defaults, never relax them; daemon/authority gates treat base+surface posture as the floor. Require pack publication to pass admission (conformance fixtures executed, risk-mapping diff reviewed) with the existing VerticalOntologyPackValidatedReceipt binding the reviewed diff.
5. **No A2A agent-card projection at the worker-endpoints surface despite shipping every other compatibility face** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/worker-endpoints.md:82 — 'GET /.well-known/ai-agent.json'
   - Recommendation: The doc provides OpenAI-wire (chat_completions, responses) and MCP compatibility projections on principle (worker-endpoints.md:392-421, 683-690) but no A2A face. The architecture is not A2A-blind — foundations/aiip.md:307-334 defines a versioned A2A binding that maps Agent Card/Task/Message to AIIP envelopes and names A2A Agent Cards as a resolver/registry option — but none of that surfaces in the marketplace endpoint spec, where external supply would actually arrive. Add a third compatibility face to worker-endpoints.md: serve a derived A2A card at /.well-known/agent-card.json projected from the worker manifest, and accept A2A cards as import material for My workers private registration (mapped to a WorkerComposition draft with unverified self-description posture, consistent with the existing prompt_only/attested ceiling). Keep ai-agent.json as the canonical superset, publish the field-mapping table, and cite the AIIP A2A binding for assurance non-equivalences.
6. **The built 'reviews' draft plane has no receipt-binding ReviewRecord doctrine** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/worker-marketplace.md:10 — 'listings/candidates/reviews/offers; private/local-worker registration,' and :1071 — 'Workers should accumulate measurable records:'
   - Recommendation: The Quality and Reputation section lists metrics but never governs review authenticity, and 'reviews' sits ungoverned in both the implemented draft plane and the Agentgres state list (worker-marketplace.md:812). The linked neutrality owner already covers reputation updates — marketplace-neutrality.md invariants 4 and 9 forbid reputation updates without usage/quality evidence and from a bare ContributionReceipt, and rule 12 requires sybil/reciprocal-review/collusion visibility — but no object-level doctrine binds a marketplace review to those invariants. Define ReviewRecord rules in worker-marketplace.md citing the neutrality owner: a public review or reputation entry must bind to an admitted WorkerInvocation or ContributionReceipt ref (verified-usage only), be weighted by dispute/refund outcomes, and disclose reviewer relationship (buyer, verifier, seed fleet). Receipt-backed reviews are this architecture's natural equivalent of Upwork's verified transaction history, and the one built marketplace surface is currently the one without integrity canon.
7. **Marketplace economics are unlinked and unmapped to invocation modes: no per-mode fee basis or publisher payout accrual in the domain docs** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/worker-marketplace.md:453 — 'Install / self-host — The user installs the package into their own Hypervisor node, local runtime, org cloud, customer VPC, or workflow.'
   - Recommendation: Economics doctrine does exist upstream: economic-flywheel-and-pricing-boundaries.md declares aiagent.xyz's legitimate fee surfaces including marketplace take-rate on paid usage/installs/subscriptions/managed instances, and takes an explicit disintermediation stance — no generic fee on self-hosted runs that skip marketplace matching, hosting, or settlement (echoed by marketplace-neutrality.md invariant 8) — while mixture-of-workers.md gives a reference worker_payout formula. But none of the seven aiagent docs links any of it, and nothing maps fee basis to the three declared modes (Ephemeral run / Install / Run managed) or specifies how publisher payouts accrue from subscriptions and ContributionReceipts. Add an economics section to worker-marketplace.md that cites the economics owner, maps take-rate basis per invocation mode, states publisher payout mechanics, and names what remains monetizable after accepted self-hosting (license-keyed update streams, rebenchmark access, managed-runtime margin). Discovering the per-mode dead-ends after the admission plane builds out is the expensive order.
8. **A marketplace-owned onboarding plan 'admits' the run mode the daemon must enforce** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/worker-marketplace.md:763 — 'A managed instance may run only in the readiness mode admitted by its completed plan' vs :195 — 'lifecycle/change-plan admission inside the daemon;' (listed under Does Not Own)
   - Recommendation: ManagedWorkerOnboardingPlan is an aiagent marketplace object, yet its readiness mode gates activation — execution admission — while the marketplace explicitly does not own daemon admission, and managed-worker-instance-lifecycle.md:115-116 repeats the same 'admitted by the completed onboarding plan' phrasing. The ontology already points the right way — digital-worker-ontology.md:57 tables the plan as 'requirement-to-step plan / readiness projection' — but the activation prose makes the projection sound like truth. State in both worker-marketplace.md and managed-worker-instance-lifecycle.md that the executing daemon recomputes readiness at activation from daemon-checkable facts (admitted grant refs, connector lease receipts, runtime assignment receipts); the plan is a projection of those facts and its readiness.mode is advisory. The compiler pattern is right; the enforcement locus needs to be named as the daemon.
9. **Managed-instance status enums drift between the endpoints reference and the lifecycle owner** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/worker-endpoints.md:617 — 'status: starting | running | idle | suspended | archived | failed' vs domains/aiagent/managed-worker-instance-lifecycle.md:185 — 'discover | installed | onboarding_planned | initializing |'
   - Recommendation: The lifecycle doc's 17-state enum and the endpoint's 6-state enum use different names for the same object (active vs running, initializing vs starting) and the endpoint's 'failed' has no lifecycle counterpart. The lifecycle doc's header ('Supersedes: plan prose and endpoint examples when lifecycle state conflicts', line 5) concedes the conflict exists and resolves it by precedence instead of removing it. Encode the mapping: declare the console/API status a named projection of canonical lifecycle state with an explicit mapping table in canonical-enums.md or the lifecycle doc, and add failure semantics to the lifecycle enum. Precedence rules are how drift persists; mapping tables are how it ends.
10. **agent:// as the canonical URI scheme contradicts the pack's own 'it is a worker instance' doctrine** — `medium` · Rename
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/managed-worker-instance-lifecycle.md:16 — 'call the instance an agent, but canonical state remains a managed worker' and :143 — 'worker_instance_id: agent://...'
   - Recommendation: Three docs repeat that 'agent' is product UX and the canonical object is a ManagedWorkerInstance, yet every canonical example — lifecycle records, onboarding plans, endpoint payloads — mints agent:// identifiers, which will be baked into receipts and Agentgres refs forever. Identifiers outlive prose. Either change the scheme to worker-instance:// (keeping agent:// as a product-facing alias), or promote 'agent' to the canonical noun and drop the repeated disclaimers. The current split guarantees that the built system's durable identifiers argue with its doctrine on day one.
11. **'Sparse Worker Categories' is ML jargon that collides with 'sparse settlement' and won't survive seller contact** — `medium` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/worker-marketplace.md:926 — 'Sparse Worker Categories are aiagent.xyz's category-level market structure for MoW. They are narrow labor markets'
   - Recommendation: Rename to WorkerCategory or BenchmarkedLaborCategory, carrying narrowness as a market_depth or design-intent attribute. 'Sparse' already means something else inside this pack (INV-11 'sparse settlement' and IOI L1 sparse commitments) and something else again to the ML-literate seller audience (sparse models/MoE — note listings may declare 'MoE-backed' cognition at worker-marketplace.md:1004-1005, inviting direct confusion). Category identifiers themselves (std:code:runtime_audit.v1) do not embed the word, but the wire field `sparse_worker_category` appears in discovery manifests, promotion proposals, and admission submissions (worker-endpoints.md:139, 323, 377), and the term is owned by foundations/mixture-of-workers.md, so the rename touches the foundations owner too — cheap now, expensive after the admission plane builds out.
12. **worker-marketplace.md is a 1153-line mega-doc that restates the lifecycle, memory, and console canon it links to** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/worker-marketplace.md:490 — 'delivery cadence, quiet hours, notification-only channels, supported model route selection, budget caps, memory-retention posture within policy'
   - Recommendation: The change-risk taxonomy (safe live / dry-run-canary / package revision) is spelled in four places — worker-marketplace.md:486-520, managed-worker-instance-lifecycle.md:99-116 and 249-322, managed-agent-console-contract.md:168-175, plus worker-endpoints.md:627-652 — each with slightly different enumeration granularity. The pack's own invariant registry exists precisely because 'the same boundary invariants used to be restated, with drifting wording, in four to seven files each' (foundations/invariants.md). Cut worker-marketplace.md to marketplace-owned doctrine (definition, listings, admission, categories, neutrality, supply, economics) and replace 'Managed Instance Dashboard And Change Lifecycle' and 'Portable Managed-Instance Memory' with pointers to the lifecycle owner. Seven docs is roughly the right factoring; the defect is that the flagship doc duplicates the other six, which is this pack's own definition of a drift engine.
13. **worker-endpoints.md mixes three ownership planes and lacks a testable worker conformance profile** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/worker-endpoints.md:63 — 'A worker may implement these directly, or a compatible Hypervisor Daemon may expose them on the worker's behalf.' and :162 — 'cross-domain product-flow reference, not duplicate endpoint ownership or'
   - Recommendation: The doc disclaims ownership of the daemon pairing routes, then re-specifies their request shapes and projection payloads in ~100 lines. The lifecycle states and failure codes are at least declared as the shared LocalAgentPairingSessionEnvelope's canonical closed set ('Product-specific explanations must map to one of those codes rather than extending the envelope locally'), which bounds that drift — but the duplicated request/response shapes will still diverge from the daemon API owner, and 'may implement these directly' with no conformance levels means third-party workers will implement incompatible subsets. Split into (a) a WorkerEndpointConformanceProfile defining required-vs-optional endpoint classes per worker_type with named levels (discovery-only, invocable, managed-instance) so compatibility is testable, and (b) the marketplace-hosted API; replace pairing-route detail with a pointer plus the product-flow narrative only.
14. **No governed buyer-environment trial eval before hire — the mechanism that actually sells vertical agents** — `medium` · Better way *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/worker-marketplace.md:889 — 'console: browse worker, try in browser, chat or submit a form, approve authority'
   - Recommendation: Benchmarks are seller-side and category-level; dry_run is a post-hire onboarding step (worker-marketplace.md:738). An informal pre-hire trial does exist — 'try in browser' and the Ephemeral run mode with 'Run once'/'Try it' listing copy (worker-marketplace.md:447-451, 470-472) — but it produces no typed eval evidence a buyer can gate a hire on. What converts enterprise buyers in practice — Harvey, Sierra, Decagon all sell this way — is a pilot eval on the buyer's own data. Add a BuyerTrialRun object between listing and hire: a bounded, sandboxed invocation of the exact WorkerComposition over buyer-supplied fixtures or a policy-filtered slice of buyer environment, producing buyer-private eval receipts that gate the hire decision but never become public benchmark claims. Reuse the Ephemeral-run plumbing and price per trial. This turns the pack's eval machinery into a conversion mechanism instead of only an admission tax.
15. **The canonical RoutingDecisionReceipt backing the neutrality rules is never cited from the aiagent docs** — `low` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/worker-marketplace.md:1026 — 'Routing decisions are explainable and user-controllable.' — while the receipt this rule depends on is defined outside the facet: domains/marketplace-neutrality.md:87-98 mandates a canonical RoutingDecisionReceipt over RoutingDecisionEnvelope binding candidate set, affiliations, policy hash, and reason codes, and foundations/mixture-of-workers.md:240-277 specifies its required routing inputs/outputs.
   - Recommendation: The routing-audit object the neutrality claims need already exists and applies to first-party seed-fleet selections ('routed through the same contracts', marketplace-neutrality.md:105-111), so no new MoWRoutingDecisionReceipt should be minted. The residual defect is facet-local discoverability: none of the seven aiagent docs links marketplace-neutrality.md or mixture-of-workers.md, worker-marketplace.md's Marketplace Neutrality section restates rules 4/6/7 without citing the receipt that makes them falsifiable, and digital-worker-ontology.md's receipt list (214-230) omits routing entirely. Per the pack's own citation rule (invariants.md: cite the owner, don't re-derive), add RoutingDecisionReceipt / MoWRoutingDecision citations to the neutrality section and the ontology receipt list, and add the neutrality and MoW owners to Related Canon.
16. **DigitalWorkerOntology apologizes for its own name in its second sentence** — `low` · Rename
   - Evidence: domains/aiagent/digital-worker-ontology.md:16 — 'autonomous labor. The name is historical: it covers software workers by'
   - Recommendation: A canonical vocabulary owner that annotates its own name as historical bakes vocabulary debt into every downstream ref (ontology:aiagent.base.v1, DigitalWorkerOntologyProfile). Rename to WorkerOntology or AutonomousLaborOntology at the next vocabulary pass, minting ontology:aiagent.base.v2 with a recorded historical alias, before implementation begins (status is still 'planned'). The pack's own discipline — single owners, canonical enums, names that carry meaning — argues against baptizing a greenfield object plane with a name the canon concedes is wrong for the embodied half of its scope.

### sas.xyz service marketplace + decentralized exchange/trade/cloud route intelligence

**Files reviewed:** `domains/sas/service-marketplace.md (339)` · `domains/sas/service-endpoints.md (328)` · `domains/decentralized/README.md (206)` · `domains/decentralized/exchange.md (293)` · `domains/decentralized/trade.md (538)` · `domains/decentralized/cloud.md (439)`

**Current shape.** This facet defines two lanes. sas.xyz is a fully-planned first-party marketplace that sells delivered, verifiable service outcomes (flagship wedge: Worker Training) rather than workers or software: an order binds an explicit outcome contract, escrow/SLA on a declared settlement rail (local_domain | bilateral | invoice | external_escrow | external_chain | ioi_l1), an OutcomeWorkspace/RuntimeAssignment/ComputeSession execution spine on Hypervisor daemon nodes, delivery bundles binding artifacts + receipts + composition/contribution evidence, and an acceptance/revision/dispute loop; service-endpoints.md supplies the HTTP surface. The decentralized.* pack defines three "candidate-intelligence engines" — decentralized.exchange (asset-conversion route candidates), decentralized.trade (perps/spot/prediction-market exposure candidates), and decentralized.cloud (infrastructure-capacity candidates) — that may only propose: every candidate carries CandidateEvidence (source, adapter, observed/expiry timestamps, coverage state, evidence refs) and cannot execute until wallet.network approves an exact ExchangeIntent/TradeIntent/PredictionIntent or Hypervisor admits a PlacementDecision; venues/providers execute, Agentgres records admitted truth, and IOI L1 is optional selected settlement. Agent trading is default-deny with paper mode, max-loss caps, and category allowlists. Implementation reality is sharply uneven: decentralized.cloud is built (candidate plane, deterministic placement decisions, wallet-gated cross-provider failover with verifier done-bars), exchange/trade exist only as wallet-SDK source-client seams, and sas.xyz is entirely specification. On the facet question: cloud.md is load-bearing, exchange.md is load-bearing as the contract behind wallet's exchange-risk owner, while trade.md and the sas.xyz dispute/arbitration core are specification-only surface today — the pack's status axis (doc-classes.md) deliberately permits canonical doctrine over planned implementation, but that makes their doctrine binding on implementers before any of it has been exercised.

**What it already gets right:**

- The candidate→intent→authority→receipt discipline is genuinely stronger than production DEX-aggregator practice: CandidateEvidence with expiry and coverage_state, typed rejection ladders ('missing → reject', 'stale → cannot execute silently'), and a structural rule that route intelligence can never become execution authority. No major aggregator (1inch, LI.FI) publishes an equivalent fail-closed quote-handling contract.
- The agent-trading policy in trade.md is ahead of 2026 industry practice: default-deny live authority, paper/sandbox default, max collateral/leverage/daily-loss caps, isolated-margin default, market-category allowlists excluding insider-risk event markets, lease expiry and step-up. Most agent-trading stacks shipping today have nothing comparable.
- decentralized.cloud's economic honesty is real and implemented: candidates from local facts only, `candidate_source_unavailable` with evidence instead of fabricated prices, spend exposures reconciled against admitted creates, and routing-fee eligibility withheld until ≥2 real candidates were actually compared — an unusually honest brokerage-fee doctrine, backed by executable verifiers.
- Correct risk-taxonomy separations that SOTA products blur: prediction markets modeled as event exposure with resolution_source and market_rules_hash bound into the intent (not 'just swaps'); bridge routes vs same-chain routes as distinct risk classes; and a crisp outcome-vs-worker marketplace split between sas.xyz and aiagent.xyz.
- sas.xyz delivery bundles binding execution receipts, composition/contribution receipts, private-data posture, and dispute-ready evidence give outcome provenance materially stronger than Upwork/Fiverr deliverable handling, where dispute evidence is reconstructed after the fact from chat logs.

**SOTA verdict: `behind`** — compared against: CoW Protocol (batch auctions, bonded solvers, uniform clearing prices); UniswapX / 1inch Fusion (intent-based fills, filler competition); Flashbots Protect / MEV Blocker (private orderflow, MEV protection as settlement mechanism); LI.FI / Socket (cross-chain routing with stuck-transfer recovery and refund lanes); Upwork (milestone escrow, proposal/offer negotiation, Job Success Score, mediation→AAA arbitration ladder); Fiverr (milestone escrow, structured revision/dispute deadlines with defaults); Kleros / UMA optimistic oracle (named on-chain adjudication with bonds, deadlines, appeals); Polymarket (UMA resolution, negRisk correlated-market structure) / Kalshi (CFTC-regulated event contracts); Akash Network / io.net (decentralized cloud brokerage market depth); OpenRouter / SkyPilot (meta-routing and multi-cloud placement)

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No MEV-protected execution path: signing an exact TxIntent then broadcasting publicly leaves users sandwich-exposed; CoW/UniswapX/Fusion make protection a settlement mechanism, here it is at most a disclosure label in the wallet owner doc.
- No solver/filler accountability or realized-vs-quoted execution-quality feedback loop; CoW bonds and ranks solvers, aggregators score realized fill quality into routing.
- No cross-chain partial-failure recovery lane for multi-hop bridge routes (LI.FI/Socket table stakes), despite the pack's own typed-effect-recovery invariant.
- No named arbitration mechanism: adjudicator identity, evidence deadlines, non-response defaults, appeals, and fee/bond allocation are all absent while 'deterministic arbitration' is asserted; Upwork/Kleros/UMA all specify these.
- No milestone object, proposal/counter-offer, or quote-negotiation flow despite pricing.type=quote and milestone_ref fields; milestone escrow is the core Upwork/Fiverr protection primitive (partial delivery/payout states exist, but no first-class milestone contract).
- No sybil-resistance, cold-start, or review-integrity mechanics behind the owned 'service quality ledgers / service reputation'.
- No per-settlement-rail buyer-protection matrix: refund/slash semantics are claimed uniformly across rails that cannot enforce them.
- No verifier-independence or sealed-holdout doctrine for Worker Training acceptance, leaving escrow release gameable by provider-run benchmarks.
- Nuance: the authority/receipt boundary layer (candidates never authority, wallet fail-closed approval) is divergent-by-design and arguably ahead of SOTA; the verdict is 'behind' because the settlement, dispute, and execution-mechanism layers that SOTA systems actually compete on are unspecified.

**Findings (15, severity-ordered):**

1. **Escrow release hangs on an arbitration mechanism that exists nowhere in the pack** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/domains/sas/service-marketplace.md:148 — 'deterministic arbitration path as other service outcomes.'
   - Recommendation: This phrase appears exactly once in the whole corpus (verified repo-wide) and nothing defines it. The Dispute API exposes POST /v1/disputes/{id}/resolve (service-endpoints.md:304) with no stated adjudicator, deadlines, non-response defaults, bonds, appeals, or fees; the IOI L1 dispute service is optional ('selected challenge/adjudication service', service-marketplace.md:159) and absent for the default local_domain rail. Create an arbitration owner (domains/sas/dispute-and-arbitration.md): who may call resolve (platform arbiter, mutually named third party, enrolled L1 dispute service), evidence deadlines, default judgment, remedy bounds tied to the action enum at service-endpoints.md:309-310, appeal path, fee/bond allocation, per-rail enforceability. Anchor on Upwork's mediation→AAA ladder and Kleros/UMA. Until then delete 'deterministic arbitration' — it overstates the design. Severity is high rather than critical only because sas.xyz is Implementation status: planned — this is a spec-integrity hole gating the flagship wedge's economic loop, not a live-system failure.
2. **Same-lifecycle claim across settlement rails hides a buyer-protection cliff** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/domains/sas/service-marketplace.md:169 — 'Local invoice, bilateral, external-escrow, and external-chain modes implement the same lifecycle'
   - Recommendation: On local_domain/invoice/bilateral rails there are no locked funds, so 'refund, or slashing disposition' (service-marketplace.md:106) is unenforceable — it degrades to 'don't pay the invoice'. service-endpoints.md:256-258 already concedes the declared rail is 'authoritative for monetary movement', so the pack knows rails differ in power — but nothing states which protections each mode can actually enforce, and the order-create response returns 'escrow_status: pending_lock | locked' (service-endpoints.md:142) uniformly across all modes, implying a lock that most rails cannot provide. Add a per-mode protection matrix to the Settlement/Escrow Mirror section of service-endpoints.md: for each settlement_mode, state which of {pre-funded lock, refund, partial payout, slash, SLA bond} is enforced vs advisory. Require listings with sla.bond_required=true to declare minimum acceptable settlement modes and fail order creation closed when the chosen mode cannot enforce listed protections. Also reword service-marketplace.md:40 ('a service escrow and SLA layer') to 'escrow-mirroring and SLA-contracting surface' — service-endpoints.md:254-258 already makes the rail authoritative.
3. **Worker Training escrow release keyed to benchmarks with no verifier independence or holdout secrecy** — `high` · Better way
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/domains/sas/service-marketplace.md:145 — 'Settlement releases only when the trained worker satisfies the acceptance rubric'
   - Recommendation: Nothing prevents the provider from running its own evaluation and gaming disclosed evaluation_dataset_refs — the exact overfit-to-eval failure Kaggle-style hidden test sets exist to stop. The worked example even gestures at the right idea (training_spec cites 'dataset://construction-estimate-holdout-v1', service-endpoints.md:125) and delivery bundles carry verifier_receipts, but nothing requires the holdout to be sealed, hash-committed, or evaluated by anyone other than the provider. Concretely: acceptance evaluation runs as an independent HarnessInvocation under a buyer- or third-party-selected HarnessProfile; split evaluation datasets into a disclosed dev set plus a sealed holdout, hash-committed in the order at creation and revealed only to the evaluator at submission; BenchmarkReceipt must bind an evaluator principal distinct from the provider principal plus the holdout commitment; the settlement gate consumes only receipts at assurance stage 'verified' by a non-provider evaluator. Add evaluator_ref and holdout_commitment to training_spec and to Non-Negotiable 7 in service-endpoints.md.
4. **No MEV-protected execution path — protection is disclosure-only while SOTA makes it a settlement mechanism** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/domains/decentralized/exchange.md:102 — 'wallet.network signs exact TxIntent records or denies execution'
   - Recommendation: Between signing and chain inclusion the doctrine says nothing: a signed exact TxIntent broadcast to a public mempool is sandwich bait, and exchange.md's risk-label list (:217-228) omits MEV entirely; wallet-network/product-exchange-risk.md has only 'MEV Exposure' (:1000) and 'MEV/protection mode, if any' (:1166) as disclosures. 2026 SOTA (CoW batch auctions, UniswapX/1inch Fusion filler fills, Flashbots Protect/MEV Blocker private orderflow) treats protection as the settlement path. Add SubmissionRoute to ExchangeIntent doctrine: public_mempool | private_relay | batch_auction | rfq_direct_fill; RouteCandidate declares supported routes and an mev_exposure label; wallet policy requires a protected route above a size threshold. Extend the 'best route' conformance check (exchange.md:270-271) to include execution-protection posture.
5. **Multi-hop bridge routes have no typed recovery/reconciliation semantics, violating the pack's own invariant** — `high` · Red flag
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/domains/decentralized/exchange.md:169 — 'path: Vec<RouteHop>,'
   - Recommendation: A bridge route that fails after leg N strands funds on an intermediate chain — precisely the 'ambiguous external effect' README Non-Negotiable 22 says must reconcile before retry — yet exchange.md never applies recovery posture to hops; bridges appear only as risk labels (:218-219). LI.FI/Socket ship explicit stuck-transfer refund lanes. Concretely: each RouteHop declares recovery posture (replayable | compensatable | reconciliation_required | non_retryable) and stranded-asset custody (which address holds funds if leg N+1 fails); add an ExchangeReconciliation flow and receipt for partially executed routes; wallet policy refuses multi-leg routes whose hops lack declared recovery. Without this the first stuck bridge leg becomes an unrecoverable support incident with receipts but no remedy.
6. **Three incompatible settlement-status enums inside one reference file, none owned by canonical-enums** — `high` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/domains/sas/service-endpoints.md:187 — 'not_requested | intent_created | pending | settled | paid | refunded | slashed | failed'
   - Recommendation: The delivery bundle's top-level settlement_status (:187), the nested settlement.status (:231 — 'pending_acceptance | accepted | accepted_partial | revision_requested | disputed | refunded | partially_refunded | paid | partially_paid | slashed'), and the escrow-mirror status (:286 — 'drafted | submitted | pending | settled | disputed | reversed | failed') are three divergent state machines for the same money. canonical-enums.md owns settlement_mode (:605) but no settlement status. Fix: canonical-enums.md owns one settlement_status enum; :231 conflates acceptance states (pending_acceptance, revision_requested) with monetary states (paid, slashed) — those belong to the already-existing acceptance_status (:185). Delete the hybrid; endpoints cite the canonical enum. This is the drift engine at the marketplace's economic core.
7. **trade.md canonizes a regulation-heavy lane with no regulatory posture for the engine itself** — `medium` · Refactor *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/domains/decentralized/trade.md:13 — 'Implementation status: partial (SDK candidate-source client exists; live venue service planned)'
   - Recommendation: Direct answer to the facet question: keep cloud.md (built, code-anchored) and exchange.md (it defines RouteCandidate/CandidateEvidence consumed by wallet-network/product-exchange-risk.md and a shipped SDK seam). For trade.md, note that the pack's own status axis explicitly permits canonical doctrine over planned implementation — doc-classes.md states 'Never demote doctrine because implementation is future' — so unbuiltness alone is not grounds for demotion, and 'demote to draft per doc-classes.md' would actually contradict that rule. The defensible gap is regulatory: trade.md canonizes order-ticket normalization plus venue routing for perps and event contracts — activity regulators plausibly treat as order reception/transmission under MiCA, and squarely CFTC-adjacent for US event contracts (Polymarket/Kalshi history) — with zero venue adapters and no regulatory-posture doctrine for the engine itself; candidate-level 'Jurisdiction Restricted' labels (:407, :424) cover candidates, not the engine's own exposure. Add a regulatory-posture section (operator exposure, jurisdiction gating of the engine's own API surface, venue-adapter licensing assumptions) as a conformance requirement before any live venue adapter ships, and mark the prediction-market family Implementation status: speculative (an allowed axis value that is 'a maturity statement, never a scope cut'); anyone still wanting doctrine-status demotion must explicitly argue a regulated-lane exception to the doc-classes rule.
8. **Listing pricing hardcodes IOI token currency against the doc's own rail-neutral asset model** — `medium` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/domains/sas/service-endpoints.md:49 — '"currency": "IOI"'
   - Recommendation: The canonical listing example prices services in the IOI token, while the order payment model is deliberately rail-neutral ('asset_kind: fiat | stablecoin | native_asset | service_credit', :99, example asset_identifier USD) and service-marketplace.md:170-171 insists no order is forced onto IOI L1. A token-denominated listing cannot settle on local_domain/invoice/bilateral rails and undercuts the pack's own 'token economics activate only with real verified network demand' posture (README Non-Negotiable 30). Replace listing pricing with the same {asset_kind, asset_identifier, amount} shape plus accepted_settlement_modes per listing, and make the worked example USD/stablecoin. The one place a skeptical reader samples pricing should not contradict the neutrality doctrine.
9. **CandidateEvidence is duplicated across three lane docs and has already diverged** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/domains/decentralized/trade.md:152 — 'struct CandidateEvidence {'
   - Recommendation: The same struct appears in exchange.md:126-139 (source: RouteSourceRef) and trade.md:152-164 (source: CandidateSourceRef) — field-type divergence already — and is restated as prose in cloud.md:224-226; the identical failure ladder ('missing → reject; expired → requote; stale/unknown/conflicting → cannot execute silently') is copied in all three. Move CandidateEvidence plus the failure ladder to foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md (the pack's stated owner for shared objects, which today does not define it) with a generic CandidateSourceRef; each lane doc keeps only its candidate delta (RouteCandidate, TradeCandidate/PredictionCandidate, CloudResourceCandidate). Alternatively the decentralized README could own the shared engine contract, since it already claims 'the route-intelligence / venue-intelligence / resource-intelligence engine boundary' (README.md:121).
10. **Seventy lines of implementation changelog narration embedded in cloud doctrine** — `medium` · Refactor
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/domains/decentralized/cloud.md:358 — '(27/27 — marker survives a vast → akash class move; corrupt custody heals'
   - Recommendation: cloud.md:318-387 carries verifier tallies (27/27, 20/20), env vars (IOI_FAILOVER_AUTO_EVALUATE_SECS), route paths, and per-feature narration inside a canonical doctrine file — exactly the volatile material the pack routes to implementation-matrix.md and already archived once (:385-387 points at the archived first-cut log). Compress to ~10 lines of durable implemented-contract invariants (local facts only, candidates never authority, no fee object minted, spend never faked) plus pointers to the matrix and archive; move tallies and narration into the archive log. Doctrine files embedding changelog counters go stale on every PR and train readers to distrust the rest of the pack's status claims.
11. **'decentralized.exchange' / 'decentralized.trade' name exactly what the doctrine disclaims** — `medium` · Rename *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/domains/decentralized/exchange.md:20 — '`decentralized.exchange` is a preferred first-party route-intelligence engine'
   - Recommendation: A first-party, centrally operated quote API that owns no liquidity, custody, or execution (exchange.md:63-73) is neither decentralized nor an exchange; the names invite the exchange/broker classification the doctrine spends pages disclaiming, and 2026 regulators (MiCA CASP definitions, CFTC front-end actions) read names and function, not disclaimers. The README already compresses public labels to Exchange/Trade/Cloud Routing, but deliberately keeps 'exact decentralized.* names' in APIs, receipts, SDKs, and adapter metadata (README.md:85-87) — the surfaces regulators and auditors actually read. Canon-fix without losing the domains: declare RouteIntelligence / VenueIntelligence / ResourceIntelligence services as the canonical object and receipt namespace (README.md:121 already uses these exact terms), and demote decentralized.* to brand/domain aliases that never appear in receipts, adapter IDs, or API object names — explicitly reversing the README.md:85-87 rule.
12. **Route scoring has no realized-vs-quoted execution-quality feedback loop** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/domains/decentralized/exchange.md:57 — 'route scoring and comparison;'
   - Recommendation: The engine scores routes and projects 'route-source health' (:59) but nothing closes the loop from execution back to scoring — the mechanism CoW uses to rank/bond solvers and aggregators use to score fill quality. Concretely: add ExecutionQualityReceipt binding quote_hash, expected_amount_out, realized amount out, realized fees/gas, submission route, and the delta; an Agentgres projection per route-source/adapter feeds route-source health and stamps CandidateEvidence risk_labels (e.g., 'Quote Reliability Low'); wallet policy may require N historical quality receipts before auto-approving a source. This stays inside the pack's physics — the engine gains evidence, never authority — and turns the receipt system from pure audit into a routing-quality asset no competitor aggregator publishes.
13. **TradeIntent mandates a simulation hash that off-chain venues cannot produce; PredictionIntent makes it optional** — `medium` · Contradiction *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/domains/decentralized/trade.md:244 — 'simulation_hash: Hash,'
   - Recommendation: TradeIntent requires simulation_hash (:244) and the conformance check demands binding 'simulation' (:501-503), while PredictionIntent declares 'simulation_hash: Option<Hash>' (:358) with no stated reason for the asymmetry. Off-chain CLOB perps venues (Hyperliquid-class) cannot produce EVM-style simulations, so the mandatory field either blocks the lane or gets stuffed with meaningless hashes — both worse than honesty. Replace both with a typed pre_trade_check: PreTradeCheck { kind: onchain_simulation | venue_quote_ack | margin_preview | none_with_reason, ref: Hash }, and require at least one non-none check for live (non-paper) intents in the conformance section.
14. **No milestone, proposal, or quote-negotiation objects despite quote pricing and milestone refs** — `medium` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/domains/sas/service-endpoints.md:188 — '"milestone_ref": "optional",'
   - Recommendation: Deliveries can reference milestones (:188) and listings can be pricing.type=quote (:47), but no endpoint creates a milestone and no flow gets a quote-priced service from listing to order — POST /v1/orders (:69) assumes a known max_amount. The spec does already carry partial-work plumbing (POST /v1/deliveries/{id}/submit-partial and accept-partial at :170-172, partial_payout/partial_refund dispute actions at :309-310, accepted_partial/partially_paid statuses), so this is not strictly all-or-nothing — but partial states without a first-class Milestone object (scope, amount, acceptance criteria, escrow-lock ref, deadline) leave nothing contractual for a partial acceptance to bind against, and quote-priced listings have no proposal/offer path at all. Add a Milestone object with POST /v1/orders/{id}/milestones and per-milestone accept/release wired to the settlement mirror, and a negotiation flow for quote-priced listings (quote request → provider proposal → buyer acceptance materializes the order). Milestone escrow plus proposal negotiation is the core Upwork/Fiverr protection and procurement primitive; week-long Worker Training engagements need it most.
15. **Header status axis disagrees with itself across the decentralized docs: alpha vs canonical** — `low` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: docs/architecture/domains/decentralized/exchange.md:3 — 'Status: alpha canon architecture doctrine.'
   - Recommendation: All four decentralized docs — README.md, exchange.md, trade.md, and cloud.md — open with 'Status: alpha canon architecture doctrine' (:3) and five to eleven lines later declare 'Doctrine status: canonical' (exchange.md:12, trade.md:12, cloud.md:12, README.md:14). doc-classes.md defines the Doctrine status vocabulary as canonical | draft | reference | archived ('alpha' is not a value) and explicitly supersedes 'ad hoc document-type labels in architecture headers'. In a pack whose credibility rests on an honest status axis, an ambiguous maturity claim on the highest-risk lanes is not cosmetic — a reader cannot tell whether trade doctrine is binding on implementers. Drop or align the legacy 'Status:' line to the doc-classes vocabulary; sas files, by contrast, are internally consistent.


## Part III.14 — Protocol and network: AIIP and IOI L1

### AIIP inter-institution protocol + Web4/IOI stack framing

**Files reviewed:** `foundations/aiip.md (1005)` · `foundations/web4-and-ioi-stack.md (533)`

**Current shape.** aiip.md defines AIIP as an "RPC-shaped, receipt-native" interop protocol for voluntarily accepted, terms-bound autonomous work between independently governed "bounded execution domains," each keeping its own runtime, operational truth, governance, and settlement, with IOI L1 anchoring only explicitly enrolled commitments. It specifies 23 typed packet classes — capability discovery, task offer/acceptance, collaboration-terms proposal/response, handoff, semantic-profile negotiation, OutcomeRoom discovery/participation/frontier/claims/findings/challenges/admission, authority query/grant, receipt commitments, delivery, acceptance, settlement intents, disputes, and reputation queries — all riding one shared AIIPEnvelope/AIIPChannelEnvelope schema owned by common-objects-and-envelopes.md, plus seven protocol profiles from Local to Enterprise. External standards (A2A, MCP, AGNTCY/OASF, draft ERC-8004/8183) are composed via versioned AIIPExternalProtocolBindingEnvelope mappings rather than replaced, and a pre-AIIP LocalAgentPairingSessionEnvelope handles first-mile local-agent onboarding under a closed bootstrap scope. An assurance ladder (receipt → evidence → verification → acceptance → adjudication → settlement) and declared effect-recovery classes bound what any packet may claim; implementation status is honestly "planned (protocol design only)." web4-and-ioi-stack.md declares "canonical Web4" — Read + Write + Own + Act under machine authority — as IOI's category, enumerates the full IOI reference stack from L0 kernel through client surfaces and marketplaces, lists 26 requirements a canonical Web4 application should satisfy, and frames an edge-in topology where work stays local-first and only sparse commitments opt into L1 settlement.

**What it already gets right:**

- The assurance ladder (receipt -> evidence -> verification -> acceptance -> adjudication -> settlement, aiip.md:115-134) is genuinely ahead of A2A/MCP, which conflate 'task reported complete' with 'outcome accepted'; the doc's insistence that an A2A `completed` status proves only self-reported completion (aiip.md:313-315) is exactly the non-equivalence discipline most agent-interop writing lacks.
- Effect-recovery classes (replayable/checkpointable/compensatable/reconciliation_required/non_retryable) mandatory on every effectful profile, with 'restoring a transport is not proof the business outcome was restored' (aiip.md:559-566), reflects real distributed-systems maturity absent from A2A, MCP, and every agent framework's retry semantics.
- The pre-AIIP pairing design (aiip.md:183-272) — one-time atomically-consumed challenge, closed bootstrap action list, explicit 12-state lifecycle, prompt_only assurance ceiling — avoids the durable-bearer-token and implied-trust mistakes that plague most 'connect your agent' flows in production products today.
- INV-32 discipline (internal replication/failover is never 'federation'; AIIP starts only at an independently governed boundary) is a boundary most multi-agent architectures blur, and the doc repeats it consistently in doctrine, anti-patterns, and non-negotiables.
- Settle-locally-by-default with fail-closed enrollment checks for any public rail (common-objects-and-envelopes.md:2342-2346) is economically honest and directly encodes the IBC/relayer and chain-maximalist lessons rather than assuming chain demand.

**SOTA verdict: `divergent-by-design`** — compared against: Google A2A protocol (task lifecycle, Agent Cards, extension mechanism); MCP (initialize/version negotiation, capability negotiation, tools boundary); W3C DIDs/VCs (identity resolution, key rotation, verifiable attestation); UCAN (attenuated capability delegation chains); GNAP (grant negotiation across trust domains); Cosmos IBC (TAO core vs ICS app-layer split; 4-step channel handshake; unpaid-relayer failure history); SPIFFE/SPIRE trust-domain federation (cross-org workload identity + mTLS); ebXML/EDI canonical-data-model history (translation-hub maintenance burden); ERC-8004 / x402-style payment-intent patterns (cited by the doc itself)

**Where the state of the art is ahead (gaps):**

- No channel-establishment or protocol-version negotiation handshake anywhere in the 23-packet vocabulary (IBC's OpenInit/Try/Ack/Confirm, MCP initialize both have this).
- No identity resolution/rotation/revocation story for system:// peers — DIDs and SPIFFE federation solve exactly this; signature schemes are named but key lifecycle is not.
- No attenuated re-delegation semantics for multi-hop handoffs (UCAN proof chains); HandoffPacket transfers work but the authority-lease chaining rule is undefined.
- No canonical serialization, signature-coverage, or hash-agility decision (COSE/JCS-class), and it is absent from the open-workstreams list — the classic cross-org signing failure mode.
- No relay/router actor object despite a non-negotiable about relayer economics (acknowledged as a deferred workstream).
- Conformance core is monolithic (23 message types in one envelope enum) versus SOTA minimal-core-plus-profiles layering.

**Findings (15, severity-ordered):**

1. **The 'Local Profile' and the web4 stack line make AIIP same-system, directly contradicting INV-32 and AIIP's own boundary doctrine** — `high` · Contradiction
   - Evidence: foundations/aiip.md:574-575 — 'Local Profile / Same-node HarnessInvocation and Worker routing, local receipts, optional settlement.' vs foundations/web4-and-ioi-stack.md:378 — 'AIIP = semantic work interop for local and cross-system autonomous handoffs'
   - Recommendation: aiip.md:296-297 says 'reserve AIIP for independently governed system boundaries' and Non-Negotiable 16 (aiip.md:962-965) forbids calling same-system_id routing AIIP — yet the protocol ships a 'local' value in the normative AIIPEnvelope.profile enum, an in_process/daemon_ipc/unix_socket transport enum (common-objects-and-envelopes.md:2426), and web4's stack line says 'local and cross-system.' The 'Shared Conventions, Different Sovereignty Boundaries' section (aiip.md:274-297) already sanctions envelope-convention reuse without it being AIIP, but the Local Profile is listed under 'Protocol Profiles' as an AIIP profile — which is not that sanctioned framing. Resolve one way: remove 'local' from AIIPEnvelope.profile and the Local Profile from aiip.md, renaming that reuse as what the doc already sanctions — 'shared envelope conventions' (a distinct LocalWorkEnvelope profile) — and fix web4-and-ioi-stack.md:378 to cross-system only. Otherwise every gate and implementer inherits a definitional landmine.
2. **No channel-establishment or protocol-version negotiation handshake exists in the packet vocabulary** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: foundations/aiip.md:394 — 'AIIP carries typed work packets, not arbitrary undifferentiated bytes.'
   - Recommendation: The packet list begins at CapabilityDiscoveryPacket and AIIPChannelEnvelope carries 'status: opening | active | paused | closing | closed | disputed' (common-objects-and-envelopes.md:2440) with no packets that drive those transitions or negotiate schema_version/profile/settlement-mode compatibility between two sovereign peers. IBC needs a 4-step handshake precisely because sovereign peers must mutually verify identity, version, and ordering before traffic; MCP's initialize negotiates protocol version. Add ChannelOpenPacket/ChannelAcceptPacket (or a handshake profile of CollaborationTerms) binding: supported envelope versions, profiles, transports, signature schemes, settlement modes, and both parties' identity proofs. 'AIIP version, schema, and profile governance' (aiip.md:978) covers governance, not the wire handshake.
3. **No identity resolution, key rotation, or revocation story for sovereign peers; DIDs/SPIFFE solve this and are not even bound** — `high` · SOTA gap
   - Evidence: foundations/aiip.md:331-333 — 'They remain identity and capability evidence—not wallet authority, a canonical IOI Domain Ontology, competence, independence, quality, or operational truth.'
   - Recommendation: Bindings cover AGNTCY/OASF directories and draft ERC-8004 registries as capability evidence, AIIPEnvelope names signature schemes (ed25519|secp256k1|ml-dsa|hybrid), the optional ai:// resolver/registry binding (aiip.md:333-335) covers discovery, and optional mainnet anchoring includes 'autonomous-system identity roots' (aiip.md:781) — but nothing says how a peer resolves system://x to current keys, detects rotation, or checks revocation before trusting a signed packet, which is the foundational question for cross-institution federation. W3C DID documents (verification methods, rotation, deactivation) and SPIFFE/SPIRE trust-domain federation are the two mature answers. Add either a DID-method binding (did:web-class minimum) as the canonical identity-resolution profile for system://, or a native SystemIdentityDocumentEnvelope with rotation/revocation semantics, and add it to Open Protocol Workstreams — identity/key lifecycle is currently absent from that list.
4. **Monolithic 23-packet vocabulary hardwired into one envelope enum inverts minimal-core-plus-profiles protocol design** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: foundations/common-objects-and-envelopes.md:2447-2454 — 'message_type: capability_discovery | task_offer | task_acceptance | handoff | semantic_profile_negotiation | collaboration_terms_proposal | ...' (23 members)
   - Recommendation: Every packet family — reputation, disputes, rooms, frontier, pairing-adjacent participation — is a first-class member of one closed message_type enum on ioi.aiip-envelope.v1, so adding or revising any family revs the single envelope schema for all implementers. IBC split TAO (transport/auth/ordering) from ICS app standards; MCP ships a tiny core plus negotiated capabilities. Restructure: define a mandatory conformance core (channel, terms, task offer/accept, receipt_commitment, dispute-hook — ~6 types) and move rooms/frontier/reputation/settlement into namespaced extension message types (e.g. ioi.aiip.ext.collaboration.v1) declared per AIIPChannelEnvelope. aiip.md's profile section is the right instinct, and the open workstream 'AIIP version, schema, and profile governance' (aiip.md:978) acknowledges governance is unfinished — but no extension/namespacing mechanism exists, and the closed enum defeats the profile layering. Cheap to fix while implementation status is still 'planned (protocol design only).'
5. **Multi-hop handoff authority chaining is undefined; UCAN-style attenuation is the known solution** — `medium` · Better way
   - Evidence: foundations/aiip.md:414-415 — 'HandoffPacket / Transfer this task or subtask to another bounded execution domain.'
   - Recommendation: AuthorityGrantPacket (aiip.md:458-459) validates a lease for 'scope, time, budget, subject, and policy,' and MultiPartyCollaborationEnvelope already forbids one party's grant authorizing another party's connector, worker, data, or physical system (aiip.md:644-645) — but when A hands B's task to C, nothing states whether C needs a fresh lease from the origin authority, whether B can attenuate its own lease, how budget subdivides, or how revocation propagates across hops. 'wallet.network authority lease types' is an acknowledged open workstream (aiip.md:975), but hop-chaining semantics are a protocol question, not just a lease-type question. UCAN solved exactly this with attenuated proof chains; GNAP with grant negotiation. Define on HandoffPacket: authority_chain_ref (ordered lease refs, each a strict attenuation of its parent), a rule that effective scope/budget = intersection/minimum along the chain, and revocation-propagation semantics. This preserves the wallet.network fail-closed invariant while making the flagship handoff scenario actually specifiable.
6. **Binding strategy is hub-and-crosswalk (ebXML pattern); overlay-first over A2A would cut the NIH surface** — `medium` · Better way *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: foundations/aiip.md:303-305 — 'A versioned AIIPExternalProtocolBindingEnvelope maps an external protocol's identities, messages, lifecycle states, artifacts, errors, and receipts into AIIP semantics'
   - Recommendation: AIIP's genuinely differentiated layer (exact-root terms acceptance, authority leases, assurance ladder, effect recovery, settlement selection) is semantic, not transport. Yet the design is a full native packet vocabulary plus N maintained crosswalks — the ebXML/EDI canonical-model burden: every external protocol rev forces a binding rev and grows assurance_non_equivalences. aiip.md:315-317 already says to use 'the standard's extension mechanism rather than forking its transport' — go further: make the primary v1 conformance target an AIIP overlay profile carried inside A2A-class remote-task transports (extension fields), with the native transport an optional profile for peers that speak neither. (MCP is explicitly declared to compose at a different layer, aiip.md:325-326, so the overlay argument applies to task transports, not the tools boundary.) This answers 'why not A2A+VC?' by construction instead of by parallel stack. A debatable strategy call, but one the spec should argue explicitly rather than default past.
7. **Claiming to define 'canonical Web4' is a credibility liability that undercuts the strong machine-authority thesis** — `medium` · Red flag *(confidence: medium)*
   - Evidence: foundations/web4-and-ioi-stack.md:528 — 'IOI does not define a proprietary Web4. IOI implements canonical Web4'
   - Recommendation: 'Web4' is a contested, repeatedly burned label (EU 'Web 4.0' policy usage, assorted crypto marketing); no standards body recognizes it, and declaring one vendor's stack 'canonical Web4' in a doc titled 'canonical architecture authority' pattern-matches to Web3-era category invention, inviting dismissal of the genuinely defensible content — machine authority as a legible primitive (web4-and-ioi-stack.md:139-149). Restructure: rename the owner doc machine-authority-stack.md; make machine authority the category claim; demote Web4 to one clearly-labeled positioning section ('a Web4 framing some audiences use'). The Read/Write/Own/Act ladder can survive as marketing; it should not be load-bearing doctrine that 28 mentions in this file and downstream docs must track. Note: this is a deliberate, consistently executed doctrinal choice rather than an internal inconsistency, so this finding is a strategic-risk flag on external credibility, not a defect in the doc's own logic.
8. **26 IOI-object-specific 'canonical Web4 requirements' make the category unadoptable by anyone else, contradicting the source-neutral claim** — `medium` · Red flag
   - Evidence: foundations/web4-and-ioi-stack.md:309-313 — 'a one-shot change may move directly to an evaluated UpgradeProposal; adaptive or multi-epoch work uses an optional ImprovementCampaign'
   - Recommendation: Requirements 10, 20, 23, 24 name IOI-proprietary objects (UpgradeProposal, ImprovementCampaign, OutcomeRoom genesis, writer epochs/fencing) — and 13, 15, 16 additionally name MoW, Hypervisor Nodes/IOI L1, and AIIP — so no third party can be 'canonical Web4' without adopting IOI's object model wholesale, yet web4-and-ioi-stack.md:166-169 claims the substrate 'is source-neutral: any model, runtime, connector, worker... can participate.' The list header is softened to 'should have' (web4-and-ioi-stack.md:298), but the requirement bodies remain object-specific.
